Absolute Regression
===================

Chapter 651: Don’t Tell Father Then
-----------------------------------
When Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King returned to their quarters, Geom Muyang and Mabul were drinking tea with the windows thrown wide open.

Geom Mugeuk stopped briefly at the entrance and looked at them.

He could sense trust in the gazes the two men exchanged.

Geom Mugeuk liked that sight very much. Mabul was filling the parts that he himself could not provide for his brother.

‘Thank you, Mabul.’

Of course, the words that came out of his mouth were entirely different from what was in his heart.

“How cruel of you all. While I was engaging in a terrifying, life-and-death blood battle of a full three hundred exchanges against those old demons, you two were relaxing!”

Geom Muyang and Mabul glanced at Geom Mugeuk, then greeted only the Poison King.

You’re back. Yes, we returned safely. You must have worked hard. As such greetings were exchanged, Geom Mugeuk raised his voice even more.

“I’m the Young Cult Leader, you know! I’m even the acting Cult Leader! If you keep this up, I’ll go back and assemble all the Demon Venerables!”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk had seen it. When his brother had glanced at him, an unconcealable relief had flickered across his expression.

You were worried, weren’t you? I made it back safely.

With that, Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King entered the room where the two were.

As soon as he stepped inside, the Poison King opened his leather satchel and showed Mabul the poisonous herbs first.

“Take a look at these.”

“Oh, those are the poisonous herbs you were searching for, Poison King.”

Mabul knew what poisonous herbs the Poison King had been looking for.

“Those herbs made the person the Poison King cherishes most change. First place has permanently changed hands.”

The Poison King corrected Geom Mugeuk’s words.

“It only changed for a very brief time.”

Geom Mugeuk pouted at Mabul. From that gesture, Mabul could tell. He had become first place again.

‘You need not tell me in such a way, Young Cult Leader.’

Still, even if it was only in words, it felt good to hear that someone cherished him most.

Geom Muyang could tell for certain from those poisonous herbs. So Jeongrak was already dead.

Seeing as he had exaggerated about fighting a life-and-death blood battle, it seemed he had personally killed the two old demons as well.

Just then, Mabul took something out of the small front pouch of the leather satchel.

The half-eaten thing he pulled out was none other than the Cheoneum Mandok Herb. The very thing Geom Mugeuk had been chewing like a bellflower root in front of So Jeongrak earlier!

‘Ah! Not that!’

Geom Mugeuk looked at Mabul and shook his head vigorously, telling him not to take it out.

Mabul tried to slip it back in, but it was already too late.

The Poison King saw it first and took it.

“I knew I smelled the Cheoneum Mandok Herb. So this was where it was.”

Regret flashed across the Poison King’s face.

“Who ate something this precious?”

“I know nothing about it.”

When Geom Mugeuk flatly denied it, the Poison King examined the bitten part closely.

“We can simply check whether it matches the shape of your teeth.”

But how could they really match it? He could have insisted it had been like that from the beginning, but was Geom Mugeuk the sort of person who would lie to the Poison King over something like this?

“Well… So Jeongrak wouldn’t believe that I was immune to all poisons. So I showed him as proof.”

“By eating something this precious?”

“…Yes. You have to chew something potent for people to believe you. That’s what makes it impressive.”

Geom Mugeuk blamed the innocent Mabul for no reason.

“No, really! It’s bad enough that you’re so good at finding poisonous herbs, but how could you even find what was in the front pocket of the satchel?”

“I just had a feeling.”

“You got a perfect feeling from that front pocket that the Young Cult Leader was going to be scolded by the Poison King, didn’t you? Right?”

Mabul smiled with an apologetic expression.

“Fine. I admit it. I concede the place of the Poison King’s most cherished person to you, Mabul!”

Geom Muyang listened to their conversation in silence.

Even when Geom Mugeuk made silly jokes, those jokes contained an effort to improve the relationship between Mabul and the Poison King.

Now he could clearly understand what sort of heart that came from.

There is no need for me to be the closest.

Because he had such a heart, he did not distort the relationships others formed with other people. Nor did he grow jealous.

Geom Muyang knew. Once one began forming relationships with people, just how difficult that was. The closer one became, the more difficult it became.

Only after that commotion that was not quite a commotion did Geom Mugeuk speak seriously.

“By the way, there is something Mabul and Brother need to know.”

Geom Mugeuk told the two of them what he had experienced on the way back. The story of the woman he had met in the marketplace.

“The other side has a master of the Soul-Capturing Art.”

At the mention of the Soul-Capturing Art, silence fell for a moment.

“I still do not know what their intentions are.”

Earlier, Geom Mugeuk had judged that it was not a simple warning for them to turn back, but that there must be some other intention.

That intention was important, but for now, this came first.

“We must prepare against the Soul-Capturing Art. We absolutely must not fall victim to it.”

The person here most versed in the Soul-Capturing Art was Mabul. Mabul said to Geom Mugeuk,

“The Young Cult Leader, who has learned the Nine Flowers Demonic Art, will hardly be affected by the Soul-Capturing Art.”

On top of that, he had also learned the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art, so there was no chance his mind would be controlled through the Soul-Capturing Art.

“As for me, the possibility of being caught by the Soul-Capturing Art is slim as well.”

Mabul’s martial arts were also inherently strong against the Soul-Capturing Art.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the Poison King.

“What about you, Poison King?”

The Poison King also had a countermeasure.

“I do not need to worry about the Soul-Capturing Art either.”

If the Poison King, who used poison, were to be controlled by someone, it would be an emergency beyond anything serious. That was why he had been prepared from the start.

“In the Thousand Poisons Forest, from the moment one possesses three or more poison sacs, one regularly consumes the fruit of the Mind-Soul Grass. The Mind-Soul Grass has the effect of clearing a person’s mind and blocking the intrusion of sorcery that attacks the mind. Naturally, the longer one takes it, the better the effect.”

Geom Mugeuk thought that even without the Mind-Soul Grass, it would not be easy for an outside force to penetrate the mental world of a person like the Poison King.

The only one left now was Geom Muyang.

“So I’m the problem.”

If it had been his brother in the past, he would have become very sensitive in such a situation, his pride wounded. But now he did not show any such discomfort.

Geom Mugeuk said to the two Demon Venerables,

“Would you please give us the room for a moment?”

At the sudden request, Mabul said to the Poison King,

“I will help you sort the poisonous herbs.”

“Thank you.”

The two of them left without asking Geom Mugeuk why he wanted them to step aside.

As he left, the Poison King waved the half-remaining Cheoneum Mandok Herb at Geom Mugeuk once before going out.

“Whew, how persistent. When it comes to poison, he truly is relentless.”

And so only Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang remained there.

“What is this about?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk walked over to the window.

He could see the Poison King and Mabul, who had left the room, walking into the courtyard. Geom Mugeuk shut the window tightly.

Geom Muyang was curious as to why his younger brother had suddenly sent the two Demon Venerables out. And the reason was something he truly had never expected.

“From now on, memorize the mnemonic formula I tell you.”

“What are you saying all of a sudden?”

“It is a martial art I learned in the Heavenly Demon Library. A mental cultivation method that counters the Soul-Capturing Art.”

Geom Muyang was bewildered. He was suddenly going to transmit a martial art that countered the Soul-Capturing Art?

“But the formula is truly difficult. You will only be able to memorize it after hearing it repeated several times.”

Soon, however, Geom Muyang’s eyes grew intense.

“Very well. Transmit it to me.”

As things stood, he could not fight at the front. One wrong move, and he might become a burden. That was something Geom Muyang absolutely did not want.

“Then I’ll begin.”

Geom Mugeuk released his qi and formed a barrier around the two of them. It was to prevent the words spoken inside from leaking outside. Since it was the first time his younger brother had ever been this cautious, Geom Muyang grew even more curious.

‘Just what kind of martial art is it?’

Just as Geom Muyang was about to ask the name of the martial art, Geom Mugeuk began reciting the mental formula. The transmission began without him even knowing the martial art’s name.

For now, Geom Muyang memorized the formula first.

“From the Mingmen acupoint, move your inner force gently through Shenshu, Zhishi, and Weishu. At this time, the true qi must be as light as snow settling on blades of grass. Up to Pishu, Ganshu, and Geshu, the speed at which you move your inner force is important…”

The formula truly was difficult. Only after hearing it repeated several times was he able to memorize it all. He could tell by instinct.

‘This is a martial art of an indescribably high realm.’

After making his brother repeat the formula several times, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Now, this time, let us perform it together.”

Geom Mugeuk was not merely transmitting the formula; he intended to teach it outright.

“The situation is urgent.”

Geom Muyang could tell. That was one reason, but the martial art itself was so difficult that Geom Mugeuk was trying to teach him directly.

Even after memorizing the formula, there were parts where he could not grasp even the slightest sense of it. Moreover, since it was an extremely profound martial art, if he moved his true qi even slightly wrong, he could suffer severe internal injuries.

At this moment, Geom Muyang did not put his pride forward. When the embodiment of vain pride has the most humble younger brother in the world, there is a truth he naturally comes to understand.

People fall because of pride, and rise because of humility.

“Very well. I ask this of you.”

Geom Muyang’s life was truly changing.

Geom Mugeuk injected a strand of inner force through his brother’s back. Like an advance party, it moved along his blood vessels and directly taught him how to move his true qi.

“This is how I interpreted ‘as light as snow.’ Now, feel it.”

Geom Mugeuk first showed the movement with his own true qi.

At every difficult section, Geom Mugeuk personally demonstrated and explained it simply. Such a process could easily ruin the other person, but the one teaching was Geom Mugeuk.

In this way, Geom Muyang felt Geom Mugeuk’s true qi and completed the formula to the end. There were several dangerous moments in the process, but he calmly trusted and followed Geom Mugeuk.

Truly, it was something that could never be done without mutual trust.

After learning the entire martial art, Geom Muyang realized something.

If Geom Mugeuk had not taught it to him this easily, it might have taken him years to learn it on his own. No, he might not have been able to learn it properly at all.

“Brother, rest for a moment before you continue.”

Geom Muyang had concentrated so intensely that he was drenched in sweat. But he did not rest.

“I will try it right away while I still have the feeling.”

Geom Muyang moved his true qi according to the formula on his own.

Geom Mugeuk watched his brother’s condition with extreme tension. He was ready to inject true qi at once if a problem arose.

But Geom Muyang safely completed the formula.

“As expected! My brother is incredible. Then again, whose son are you? How could you not manage this?”

The joy that rose within him like a shudder was not because of his younger brother’s praise. When he completed the formula, there was an overwhelming emotion he felt throughout his entire body.

Had he ever felt such a complete satisfaction upon learning a martial art?

He did not think he had felt this way even when he learned the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

With his heart brimming, Geom Muyang asked,

“Just what martial art is this?”

Then astonishing words flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“It is the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art.”

In that instant, Geom Muyang’s expression stiffened, and his eyes widened. He was so shocked that he could not say a word.

Because his younger brother was not someone who would joke about the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art. No, the feeling already coursing through his body was telling him. If this were not the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art, how could it give him such a feeling?

Geom Muyang sprang to his feet and shouted,

“You! Are you insane?”

Geom Muyang glared at Geom Mugeuk with frightening eyes.

“Do you know what you have just done?”

Geom Muyang scolded his younger brother from the heart. The Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art was a martial art transmitted only to the heir.

“I gave my brother one life.”

People often described the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art as gaining another life.

But Geom Mugeuk, who had already achieved great mastery in the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art, had experienced it many times.

It was not merely one life. He had overcome danger with the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art more than once or twice. He knew it would help him countless times throughout his life.

“Brother, this enemy is a master of the Soul-Capturing Art. Ordinary martial arts can never block it.”

What weighed most heavily on Geom Muyang’s mind was their father.

“If Father finds out, he will not let this pass.”

Beyond that, the fact that he had learned it was the problem. I learned it without knowing it was the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art. That was the truth, but he did not want to make such an excuse to their father.

“Father will not know.”

“What?”

“As long as you don’t tell him. The Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art is not a martial art that shows outwardly at all. That is also why it is such an extraordinary martial art.”

“You!”

“With this, there is now one secret between you and me.”

Geom Muyang did not know what to say.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze as he looked at his brother deepened.

“I did not teach it to you merely because of this enemy who uses the Soul-Capturing Art.”

If the opponent used the Soul-Capturing Art, his brother could simply stay back. He would regret it, but the brother he was now would accept it well enough.

“Then why?”

What came out next was even more astonishing than when he had said this martial art was the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art.

“I want you to stay safely by my side for a long time.”

“!”

It was Geom Mugeuk’s sincerity. Truly, if he followed only his heart, he would have wanted to transmit the Nine Flowers Demonic Art to him as well. But that alone was something that absolutely could not happen.

Feeling his younger brother’s sincerity, Geom Muyang could not say anything.

“Brother, you have finally gotten a proper hold on my weakness. If you tell Father about this, I will be driven out of the heir’s position.”

Of course, he would not be driven out. At least when it came to the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art, even their father was in no position to say anything. Before Geom Mugeuk became the heir, he had taught him the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art in exchange for the Wind God Four Steps.

Suddenly, Geom Muyang recalled what Mabul had said.

—The Young Cult Leader will not want the Eldest Young Master to live as his shadow.

With deepened eyes, Geom Muyang spoke bluntly.

“Fine. If you ever become truly unbearable to look at, I will tell Father then.”

“Then we will probably both be driven out of the Cult.”

And at that very moment.

His brother laughed.

It was the first time. The first time he had seen his brother smile so brightly. Had his brother ever had such an expression?

Geom Mugeuk could tell. That smile was a greater expression of gratitude than his brother telling him thank you a thousand times.

Because he knew that the moment he said something about that smile, he would never see it again.

Instead of saying anything to his brother, Geom Mugeuk walked to the window and threw open the closed shutters.

The Poison King and Mabul had spread poisonous herbs out in the courtyard and were talking.

Geom Mugeuk shouted toward them,

“If we brothers get driven out by Father, you two will come with us, won’t you?”

The Poison King and Mabul glanced at Geom Mugeuk, then, without answering, put their heads back together and resumed their conversation.

“You’ll sell medicinal herbs and support us brothers, right? You will, won’t you?”

Geom Muyang silently stared at his younger brother’s back.

Once again, he recalled what Mabul had said.

—It is not easy being the older brother of such an outstanding younger brother, is it?

Geom Muyang quietly closed his eyes and began moving his true qi according to the formula of the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art. Until now, he had only vaguely thought that since Geom Mugeuk was his younger brother, he should protect him.

‘Now I must truly play that role as his older brother properly.’

Absolute Regression, Chapter 652

Chapter 652: For the Alliance Leader
------------------------------------
Murim Alliance Leader Jin Paecheon stood in a pavilion in Cheongun Grove, his hands clasped behind his back.

As he gazed at the mist flowing through the bamboo forest, Geom Mugeuk walked up to the pavilion.

“Alliance Leader.”

“Welcome.”

Geom Mugeuk had sent word that he had something to report, and so they had met here in secret.

For a while, the two of them stood and watched the drifting mist. Jin Paecheon was the first to speak.

“That matter keeps troubling me.”

The Cheonae Geosa he had met had been no different from usual.

The problem was what he had newly come to feel. The fact that, in all the years that had passed, the man had never once been angry with him. The fact that he had never once revealed his true feelings honestly.

To say that was simply his personality, or that it was because of Jin Paecheon’s status—the years they had spent together were far too long for that.

“But that alone is not proof that he is an evil man.”

Geom Mugeuk answered calmly.

“I do not think knowing someone for a long time means you know them well. What matters is whether you made the effort to see them accurately. But friends do not make that kind of effort. They are friends because they do not have to. That is why he is the worst kind of man.”

Jin Paecheon turned to look at Geom Mugeuk. He was still convinced that Cheonae Geosa was evil.

“What happened to the experts who violated the Forbidden Demon Edict?”

At Jin Paecheon’s question, Geom Mugeuk widened his eyes and asked back.

“The Forbidden Demon Edict?”

Jin Paecheon stared at Geom Mugeuk. He was the one who had told him that news.

“There are no experts in our cult who violated the Forbidden Demon Edict.”

Then he grinned. From that mischievous smile, Jin Paecheon could tell. The old demons who had violated the Forbidden Demon Edict were already dead.

Of course, saying there had been no one who violated the edict was a joke, and Geom Mugeuk relayed what had happened as it was.

“They appeared at Myeongsim Clinic.”

In truth, Jin Paecheon had already obtained that information as well. The fact that So Jeongnak had vacated the clinic after meeting two old experts. And the fact that those old experts were demonic martial artists who had violated the Forbidden Demon Edict.

“They died in a secret chamber beneath Myeongsim Clinic.”

Geom Mugeuk deliberately emphasized the fact that there was a secret chamber underground there.

“Did you deal with them yourself?”

“Yes.”

Even after killing them, Geom Mugeuk stood perfectly fine, without so much as a scratch. This young cult leader grew stronger every time they met.

“Did Physician So die as well?”

So Jeongnak had truly been evil? He had spent so many years working for the people of Wuhan.

“Yes, he died too.”

Geom Mugeuk answered without hesitation, then explained why.

“So Jeongnak was trying to undo their restraints.”

And then he added the decisive words.

“He had been training in poison arts in a secret chamber beneath his residence. He selected patients with constitutions necessary for his experiments, conducted poison experiments on them there, and killed them.”

Cold fury flowed from Jin Paecheon’s eyes. If those words were true, then the man truly could not be forgiven.

If everything Geom Mugeuk said was true, that was.

Jin Paecheon stared at Geom Mugeuk. At this moment, if he did not believe Geom Mugeuk’s words, then all of this became a conspiracy fabricated by the Demonic Cult.

“Trust your instincts, Alliance Leader.”

“If what you say is true, then should I not distrust my instincts instead? I did not even know that an old friend was my enemy.”

“He is someone who deliberately approached you from your youth. Not you, Alliance Leader—no one could have known.”

His instincts told him to trust Geom Mugeuk. Even so, Jin Paecheon was cautious. This matter concerned not only himself but the safety of the entire orthodox murim.

“Let us suppose you are right. Was there truly any need to prepare for so many years? If it had been me, I would have overturned things several times by now.”

“That is precisely why.”

“Precisely why?”

“If it had been you, Alliance Leader, you surely would have produced a better result than this.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk spoke as though asking in return.

“But they are not you, Alliance Leader, are they?”

“!”

“Since they had to face someone like you, it could not have been easy. So difficult that even decades of preparation would not have been enough.”

Jin Paecheon could feel it. Just how highly Geom Mugeuk regarded him.

“To be honest, I had thought the person they were aiming for might not have been Ha Gun or Haryeong, but Cheonae Geosa.”

For an instant, Jin Paecheon flinched in shock. That meant Cheonae Geosa had intended to sacrifice his own life to ignite the Righteous-Demonic War.

“Why did you not tell me that then?”

“I thought it might make your judgment of your friend even more difficult. But thinking back on it now, I should have told you at the time.”

Geom Mugeuk bowed his head and apologized.

“I am sorry. Though I said I would tell you everything, I made that judgment on my own.”

Jin Paecheon did not think it was something that required an apology. The young cult leader said he would tell him everything, but he himself still did not fully trust him. He had no intention of telling him everything.

There was still a wariness toward Geom Mugeuk in his heart. He trusted him, yet remained wary. Once again, he felt just how difficult it was to trust a person completely.

“Did you find out whom they were aiming for?”

“Yes. So Jeongnak said it himself.”

Geom Mugeuk added quietly.

“Their targets were me and Ha Gun.”

The moment he heard those words, Jin Paecheon felt an unbearable killing intent for the first time in a very long while.

The rage he had felt when he had merely guessed that his grandchildren might be targets could not compare to the rage he felt now that it had been revealed as fact.

It was not simple anger. It was a dense killing intent that wanted to slaughter the one behind this in a gruesome manner, a primal killing intent that wanted to inflict the utmost agony on the other party before tearing them to pieces.

At times like this, Jin Paecheon truly found the position of Murim Alliance Leader burdensome. He wanted to seize a sword at once and rush out. While shouting one sentence.

So where are they? Who are they?

This had originally been his nature. After becoming the Murim Alliance Leader, he had endured and endured again, and his temperament had changed.

But this matter was truly bringing out his original nature.

“Thinking about it one way, their target was my father. It seems they analyzed that if I died, my father would start a war. It was a judgment that showed they did not know my father at all.”

Jin Paecheon stared intently at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“They may have seen him properly.”

“Yes?”

He had not said it simply to make Geom Mugeuk feel better. The Geom Ujin he had seen at the tripartite meeting had clearly not lost his ambition. Whether he used the grief of losing a child as a pretext, or whether it was truly because of that grief, Jin Paecheon thought Geom Ujin might indeed start a war.

Yes, perhaps the true targets this time were not the young cult leader and his grandson, but the Demonic Cult Leader and himself.

“There is something else I learned while handling this matter.”

The most important reason he had come to meet the Alliance Leader today was to convey this information.

“There is an expert in soul-bewitching arts behind this matter.”

At the mention of soul-bewitching arts, Jin Paecheon’s expression hardened. The martial arts the orthodox murim hated most were poison arts and soul-bewitching arts.

So Jeongnak had used poison, and another mastermind was someone who used soul-bewitching arts. In other words, they had deployed the most troublesome opponents imaginable.

“Please prepare countermeasures. I have sent separate word to Captain Jin regarding soul-bewitching arts.”

Surely the Murim Alliance also had preparations against soul-bewitching arts and poison arts. If only to deal with his own cult, they would have put countermeasures in place.

“Understood.”

“As you can see, the person they fear most is you, Alliance Leader.”

With that, he gave the Alliance Leader strength once more. The most important thing now was for Jin Paecheon to stand at the center and remain unshaken.

“Lastly, there is one thing I must tell you.”

“What is it?”

Geom Mugeuk spoke cautiously. With anyone else it might have been different, but with this man, he had to inform the Alliance Leader beforehand.

“I intend to meet Cheonae Geosa myself.”

For an instant, Jin Paecheon flinched. He felt that what had to come had finally come.

“If you were me, what would you do?”

After pondering for a moment, Geom Mugeuk answered calmly.

“If I were in your position, Alliance Leader, I would ask myself first. What are you most afraid of right now?”

“!”

Jin Paecheon asked himself that question.

Was he afraid that his friend would be revealed as an evil man?

Or was he afraid of learning that he had lived his entire life deceived?

“Will you kill him?”

Jin Paecheon asked heavily, and Geom Mugeuk revealed his honest feelings.

“At first, I naturally thought you should be the one to kill him, Alliance Leader. It was for your sake. But now, I think it would be all right if I killed him. That, too, is for your sake.”

Geom Mugeuk did not explain the reason.

He merely bowed respectfully, then descended from the pavilion.

“First, I will find proof that he is the one behind this.”

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk opened the door and entered, Cheonae Geosa asked with a startled expression,

“Who is it?”

Geom Mugeuk had slipped inside quietly, avoiding the eyes of the martial artists guarding the outside.

“You already know, do you not?”

Soon, Cheonae Geosa nodded.

“I heard about you from the Alliance Leader.”

Then, with an expression of genuine surprise, he added,

“I did not expect you to come looking for me.”

Geom Mugeuk could understand why the Alliance Leader was so troubled. Nowhere in this man’s appearance could one find the feel of an evil man hiding his true identity.

“If I shout, everyone outside will rush in.”

“Are you going to kill them all? Ah, that was your original goal, was it not? To have Wuhan’s martial artists slaughtered by my hand.”

As if to show him, Cheonae Geosa rose from his seat, opened the door through which Geom Mugeuk had entered, and stepped outside.

Then the martial artists standing in the corridor looked at him. No one was dead, and no one had had their numbing or sleep acupoints subdued.

Outside the window, in the courtyard, countless martial artists could be seen as well. Geom Mugeuk had entered the room while avoiding all of their eyes. What mattered was that he had done so without subduing them.

“Is there anything you need?”

“No.”

At the question from the martial artist guarding the corridor, Cheonae Geosa shook his head, closed the door, and came back inside.

In the meantime, Geom Mugeuk was looking around the room.

“You truly are frugal, just as the rumors say.”

It looked as though he was only glancing around, but Geom Mugeuk’s eyes were sharply examining every corner of the room. A secret passage to escape through in an emergency, perhaps, or a mechanism to attack an opponent.

One might think, would there be such a thing when he had lived while hiding himself this thoroughly? But there was no knowing. As he had told So Jeongnak, perhaps at first he had been perfect too. But would there still be no gaps even now?

There would certainly be one somewhere. A hole no bigger than a finger that could bring down the dam.

“Could it be that somewhere in this room, if one enters a secret passage, a truly luxurious house appears? A house with a feast laid out and beauties waiting on the bed.”

He said it like a joke, but Cheonae Geosa did not laugh.

“Does your residence have such a secret passage?”

It was a way of saying, You do not have one, so why say such a thing to me?

“Would not those of us in the Demonic Cult live in such houses without bothering to hide it?”

After looking around the interior, Geom Mugeuk sat on the chair beside the tea table and opened the teapot.

“You use the cheapest tea as well. You should at least drink good tea.”

“My tongue is not so refined.”

Geom Mugeuk behaved calmly, but all his senses were focused on Cheonae Geosa. He did not let down his guard for even a moment. This man stood at the center of all these events.

Pour.

Geom Mugeuk poured tea into a cup and drank.

“To be honest, I drink anything well too.”

Cheonae Geosa silently watched Geom Mugeuk behaving as if he were in his own home.

“Has it not been difficult, living all this time while hiding yourself? Why not speak frankly with me? In any case, neither the Alliance Leader nor the orthodox martial artists will believe what I say.”

Surely he too must have a desire to reveal himself. He would want to boast about what he had accomplished for so long. Do you know how I endured and endured to come this far?

But Cheonae Geosa did not reveal even the slightest hint of such a thing.

“What is your purpose in doing this? What do you hope to gain by slandering me?”

Cheonae Geosa’s gaze sharpened.

“Are you aiming for the Alliance Leader?”

Amazingly, Geom Mugeuk nodded and admitted it.

“Yes. The Alliance Leader is my target.”

To Cheonae Geosa, who flinched in surprise, Geom Mugeuk added,

“My purpose is to protect the Alliance Leader from you.”

Realizing he had been mocked, Cheonae Geosa’s expression hardened.

“The young cult leader of the Demonic Cult has come to protect the Murim Alliance Leader? How amusing.”

“The world is full of amusing things. The Murim Alliance Leader’s oldest friend may also be his most threatening enemy.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled, but he did not.

“Why did you come to see me?”

“There is an honest answer, and there is a dishonest answer. Which would you like to hear?”

“I will hear the honest answer.”

Geom Mugeuk frankly revealed the purpose of his visit.

“To find proof that our esteemed Geosa is hiding his true identity.”

To drag out the man named Hwa Yulcheong, who was hidden inside the name Cheonae Geosa. For the moment when he would tear away the shell called Cheonae Geosa entirely.

“You are so honest that I have become curious about the dishonest answer as well.”

“Shall I tell you?”

When Cheonae Geosa nodded, Geom Mugeuk’s demeanor changed completely. With an utterly cold gaze that peered into evil, Geom Mugeuk said quietly,

“I came to kill you.”

As if he had expected it, Cheonae Geosa showed no agitation.

“Killing you first and cleaning up afterward. It is not such a bad method for dealing with someone as dangerous as you.”

Cheonae Geosa asked calmly,

“That answer sounds more like the honest one.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk loosened his cold expression.

“You are right. In truth, that is my honest wish.”

“Then why do you not kill me?”

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat and approached him.

The two stood facing each other at a distance close enough that either could draw a sword and sever the other’s neck in a single stroke.

“Because I want to make you a proposal.”

Geom Mugeuk extended his hand to him and spoke. It was truly an offer he could never have imagined.

“Join hands with me.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 653

Chapter 653: Murim Alliance Come Out! Demonic Cult Come Out!
------------------------------------------------------------
Astonishment showed in Cheonae Geosa’s trembling eyes.

Yes, he must have been shocked.

If he were not the true mastermind, it would have been nothing more than the mad rambling of the Demonic Cult’s young cult leader—but you are different.

Because the question must have suddenly occurred to him: Is this young cult leader truly proposing that I join hands with him?

He had said they should join hands in order to shake him. If he kept shaking him, Hwa Yulcheong—the mastermind behind the conspiracy, not Cheonae Geosa, the Alliance Leader’s friend—would surely surface at least once.

The first time was the hard part. Once he revealed his true self even once, it would become even harder to hide from then on.

Cheonae Geosa silently stared at Geom Mugeuk.

From his outward appearance, Geom Mugeuk could not tell what he was thinking. At the very least, the man possessed Alliance Leader–level skill when it came to concealing himself.

Then Cheonae Geosa began to move. He passed by Geom Mugeuk and walked toward the door.

Was he going to call in the people outside?

He opened the door and went out. But instead of bringing the warriors in the corridor inside, he led them out of the building.

“Everyone, follow me out.”

Not only the warriors in the corridor, but dozens more were gathered in the courtyard and outside the building. They ate their meals there and even replaced sleep with circulating qi and regulating their breath; they truly respected and followed Cheonae Geosa.

Cheonae Geosa said to them,

“You may all return now.”

At that moment, someone shouted.

“Not yet, Master!”

It was Hyeogin, the sect leader of Jeongsomun.

Earlier, Cheonae Geosa had been injured while trying to save him. Since then, all of them had been guarding his residence like this. Hyeogin in particular had not missed a single day.

Cheonae Geosa approached Hyeogin.

“My injuries are fine now.”

“Master!”

“My body is well, so who would dare kill me? Surely you are not looking down on my abilities?”

“No, Master!”

Cheonae Geosa clasped the flustered Hyeogin’s hands. Instead of Geom Mugeuk’s hand, he took his.

“I know how you feel. But now I am uncomfortable, so I am telling you to withdraw.”

Hyeogin lowered his head, and the rest of the warriors lowered their heads with him.

“The Alliance Leader is also looking out for me. So do not worry and go.”

At the mention of the Martial Alliance Leader, their expressions eased somewhat. Yes, how could the Alliance Leader not be paying close attention?

“If anything happens, please send word at once.”

Cheonae Geosa cupped his fist respectfully to the warriors and gave them a formal salute.

“Thank you, truly, for protecting me all this time. I will never forget this until the day I die. Now return to your own lives.”

The warriors cupped their fists in unison with eyes full of respect and gave a resounding farewell.

“Master, please take care of yourself!”

After sending all the warriors back like that, Cheonae Geosa returned to the room.

Geom Mugeuk asked with a peculiar smile,

“Will I finally get to see your true face now?”

In truth, he had not thought the man would reveal his identity so easily.

As expected, Geom Mugeuk’s intuition was correct.

Cheonae Geosa said something unexpected.

“I sent them all away because I thought I might have to engage in a life-and-death blood battle with you today.”

Without another word, Cheonae Geosa immediately revealed his aura.

Shaaaaaa.

Geom Mugeuk looked around. The surroundings were a pure white snowfield.

The raging blizzard made it difficult to open his eyes. If someone with weak internal energy were here, they would be unable to breathe in this cold wind and would either collapse or be blown away.

Beyond that fierce blizzard, he stood alone and lonely. He looked like a single flower blooming alone in the snow.

This was Cheonae Geosa’s aura. A pleasant fragrance came from it.

The Cheonae Geosa he felt now was different again from the Cheonae Geosa he had sensed when he first entered this room.

He had succeeded in peeling away one layer of him, and beneath that layer was a great chivalrous hero who had protected the orthodox martial world. A warrior of righteousness who stood proudly even before the Demonic Cult’s young cult leader, one who could willingly give his life.

Geom Mugeuk could tell.

This aura was real.

He could also tell how many long years of effort it must have taken to create such an aura.

He was showing him now: what kind of person an upright old master of the orthodox faction was, one who would not yield to any provocation, threat, or persuasion.

‘Beneath this aura, what is your true aura like?’

Cheonae Geosa rebuked him in a low but frostlike voice.

“How dare you ask me to join hands?”

Moreover, the reason he had sent back the warriors outside was because he was worried they might be injured or killed if a fight broke out.

“Tell me. Whom did I ask to join hands with me? I do not know who you are.”

Cheonae Geosa spoke coldly.

“Do not bewitch people with nonsense. Instead, fulfill your true wish.”

Geom Mugeuk had said his true wish was to kill him. In other words, he was telling him to fight.

Geom Mugeuk was inwardly impressed.

The man had the magnanimity to send the warriors outside away rather than use them as shields, and he also possessed the precise judgment to know that Geom Mugeuk would not fight him.

After all, he would not truly intend to engage in a life-and-death battle alone with someone who had already dealt with Ma Yeomgun, Baekgol Honma, and even So Jeongrak.

As expected, he was no easy man. With someone like this, how could everyone not have been deceived?

Geom Mugeuk raised his own hand and spoke regretfully.

“You will not find many hands as good as this one to join with. Do not regret it later.”

Of course, this was not an atmosphere that could be loosened with a joke.

Cheonae Geosa’s expression remained cold, and taut tension flowed between the two men.

“I disliked the very fact that you people set foot in Wuhan. How dare you come here, of all places?”

Geom Mugeuk quietly gazed into Cheonae Geosa’s eyes, then spoke in a gentle tone. His tone was soft, but the meaning within it was sharp.

“Our Geosa seems to have reached the final realm of deception, where he even deceives himself.”

“You are the one deceiving.”

Cheonae Geosa snorted as he retorted.

“You said it a moment ago. That you would protect the Alliance Leader from me. And yet you ask me to join hands? Is that how you people protect others?”

Then Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“This is all because of you.”

“Because of me?”

“As you know, I suspect you. But when I saw you in person today, I found it astonishing. I could not find anything suspicious about you at all. I observed you with certainty in my heart, and yet I could not find it? Well, I suppose it is precisely because you are this kind of person that you were able to hide your identity beside the Alliance Leader for decades.”

Normally, at this point, the words “Do not speak such nonsense” should have come out. If he truly were a chivalrous hero, it really would have been absolute nonsense.

But he was waiting for Geom Mugeuk’s next words. As a human being, he wanted to hear them. He wanted the work he had done to be acknowledged for how extraordinary it was.

And if the person saying those words was the young cult leader of the Divine Cult, their greatest enemy, the satisfaction would be even greater.

“I suddenly thought I wanted to make you one of my people. That is why I extended my hand.”

Only after hearing everything did Cheonae Geosa react. He softened the sharp momentum he had shown earlier.

“I would rather die than throw myself into the Demonic Cult.”

Somehow, he had to peel away another layer here. He had to remove that thick mask of a chivalrous hero and draw out the man’s true face.

It was not merely to show evidence to the Alliance Leader. In order to discover who stood behind them, he had to bring this man onto the stage as well.

Onto this stage called Wuhan, where they had been lured in to ignite the Great War between the Orthodox and Demonic factions.

Geom Mugeuk suddenly said to him,

“So Jeongrak of Myeongsim Clinic is dead.”

What meaning does this news hold for you?

Is it sorrow at the death of someone who spent decades with you? Or is it anger that the work you were trying to carry out has been interrupted?

Cheonae Geosa revealed no emotion at all. Geom Mugeuk did not miss that reaction.

“Ah! The Alliance Leader should have seen this.”

“What do you mean by that?”

“No one knows the news that he died, so why are you not surprised? The most famous physician in Wuhan, and the physician who did the most good, has died. Should you not be surprised?”

And decisively.

“Should you not ask why I killed him? He was nothing more than a mere physician, so would it not be the normal reaction to wonder why the Demonic Cult killed him?”

At that instant, he saw Cheonae Geosa’s eyes tremble slightly. It was a minute change that only Geom Mugeuk, who had experienced such moments countless times, could notice.

“Which means you knew why So Jeongrak died. Ah! I cannot show this to the Alliance Leader! What a shame I can only tell him in words!”

Did he perhaps think that if it were the words of that young cult leader delivering it, they might be even more vivid than seeing it in person?

Cheonae Geosa did not let it pass.

“What sort of absurdity are you trying to force?”

“I am not forcing anything. I am asking why you are not curious.”

After a brief pause, Cheonae Geosa answered.

“Why? I will answer you. Because it is you people.”

He returned Geom Mugeuk’s earlier words, that it was because of him.

“Because of us?”

“Are you not the ones who kill all good people?”

His gaze was dignified, as though he truly thought so, not as mockery.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not one to let that pass.

“So Jeongrak was not a good man, was he? Just like you.”

Before he could answer, Geom Mugeuk provoked him further.

“When I think about it, So Jeongrak was truly a pitiful man. Because of you.”

The blame of “because of you” was once again turned toward Cheonae Geosa.

“For decades, he stayed by your side, playing a physician he did not want to be, struggling to protect you. Did you shed even a single tear for him? No, you did not. Even in hell, he must be feeling wronged and resenting you.”

At times like this, how nice it would have been if he had said, What do you know! Do you know how much I cherished him! But Cheonae Geosa was exceedingly calm. Truly, he looked like someone who had not uttered a single word of mourning, let alone shed tears.

“You still do not ask why I killed him.”

The peculiar smile forming at the corner of Cheonae Geosa’s mouth was so faint that only Geom Mugeuk could sense it. His emotions were being felt like this, little by little.

Yes. If he kept searching, he would surely find the hole that would bring down this embankment.

“So Jeongrak died, and the two old men who violated the Golden Demon Order died as well.”

Naturally, Cheonae Geosa would know that fact. He would also know about the warning given by the master of soul-bewitching arts.

“Now only the one who uses soul-bewitching arts remains. Can you pull it off with that subordinate? Ah, or are you perhaps receiving orders from that person?”

Geom Mugeuk lightly prodded his pride. He watched to see how the man would react, but Cheonae Geosa walked to the tea table and drank his tea.

It looked as though he were trying not to reveal his reaction, but in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes, even this was a reaction.

“Why not simply frame me and say I use soul-bewitching arts?”

At his reply, Geom Mugeuk also walked to the tea table.

“Because that is certainly not the case. There is no way the Alliance Leader would fail to notice someone beside him who had mastered soul-bewitching arts, would he?”

Pour.

Geom Mugeuk filled his own teacup.

“You were not originally this kind of person, were you? Not a frustrating old man of the orthodox faction like this, but a fiery person. That must be why you were chosen for such an important role.”

At that moment, a subtle tremor could be felt from Cheonae Geosa. Yes, how could he forget such moments? There were moments when, even if everything else could be deceived, they could not.

“I want to talk with that fiery person. I want to have a refreshing conversation, villain to villain.”

Cheonae Geosa merely stared at Geom Mugeuk impassively, without the slightest wavering.

“Deceiving people, maneuvering in the shadows, living in hiding. Are you not tired of it now? Do you not want to drag them all out and have one grand fight? Martial Alliance, come out! Demonic Cult, come out! Is that not who you are?”

As if he did not want to listen any longer, Cheonae Geosa gave the order to leave.

“If you have finished your tea, go back now.”

Their gazes tangled in midair.

Yes, he was not someone who would be shaken by a single meeting.

But today, Geom Mugeuk had discovered several minute changes in emotion from him.

Geom Mugeuk gave a respectful farewell.

“Thank you for the tea. Next time—”

Cheonae Geosa cut him off.

“There will be no next time.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him and said,

“Yes, that is what I hope as well. Next time, instead of this dull chivalrous hero Cheonae Geosa, I would like to meet the incarnation of revenge.”

“!”

Leaving behind the man who stared at him with a shocked face, Geom Mugeuk strode toward the door and said,

“Next time, let us meet Hwa Yulcheong, the incarnation of revenge.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 654

Chapter 654: I Plan to Stand on Stage with Him
----------------------------------------------
Cheonae Geosa reacted to the words “the embodiment of revenge.”

He was a man who, no matter how much Geom Mugeuk provoked him, had only ever reacted faintly, yet to these words, he reacted greatly.

“Wait!”

By the time he shouted it out, Geom Mugeuk had already opened the door and left.

Cheonae Geosa ran over and hurriedly opened the door, but Geom Mugeuk had already vanished without a trace.

Instead of Geom Mugeuk, a middle-aged man was walking toward him from the end of the corridor.

“My Lord, is something the matter?”

The one who walked over was Chief Administrator Ju, the general manager of Musojang. He was a man who had lived his whole life for Cheonae Geosa and Musojang, as if he were there yet not there.

The chief administrator’s name was Zhou Shaoming.

“Have you seen the Young Cult Leader?”

“No, I have not seen him.”

He had been walking down the corridor, yet Geom Mugeuk had disappeared, evading his very eyes. Since all the corridor windows were closed, he had vanished like a ghost.

Since it was Geom Mugeuk who had entered while evading the eyes of those countless martial artists, one might have thought it only natural for him to disappear like this.

“He even evaded your eyes and disappeared.”

It was a remark that showed, surprisingly, he trusted the chief administrator’s eyes even more than those of all the martial artists who had been guarding this place earlier.

At that, Zhou Shaoming’s gaze began to change. It was a gaze he had never once shown to visitors of Musojang.

As his usually friendly eyes grew fierce and cold, Zhou Shaoming became a completely different person. It was the moment he was no longer Chief Administrator Zhou Shaoming, but a long-trusted subordinate of Hwaryulcheong.

Zhou Shaoming surveyed the corridor again. He inspected the closed windows and the ceiling.

Soon Zhou Shaoming shook his head. Nowhere were there any traces of the Young Cult Leader having slipped out.

In other words, he had passed right by him and escaped.

“An unbelievable level of skill.”

Cheonae Geosa’s gaze turned toward the window.

“Yes, a man hard to believe.”

Just as his voice had changed to a low, deep tone, so too did his eyes change. It was a gaze and a voice he had never once revealed when he was with Geom Mugeuk.

Now even his aura changed.

Originally, his aura had been a lone flower blooming proudly in a snowfield where snow fell.

The color of the falling snow changed to a deep gray. Now what fluttered around was not snow, but ash scattering after burning something.

Screams filled with pain rang out from all directions.

On the ground where the piled snow had vanished, corpses were strewn everywhere, and blood flowed like a river. The scattering ash was the ash of burning corpses.

He stood in the center of the battlefield where only sorrowful resentment lingered.

His gaze looking over the battlefield was boiling intensely. One wondered how he had hidden this heat and lived.

This was his true aura.

“The Young Cult Leader knows about me.”

Understanding what that meant, Zhou Shaoming’s expression hardened as well.

“There is no way. He must have guessed. Is he not an exceptionally clever man?”

Cheonae Geosa nodded and revealed his true feelings.

“‘Clever’ is not enough. Had I been ten years younger, my true identity would have been revealed.”

He had been shaken by Geom Mugeuk. The Young Cult Leader possessed something that stirred people’s emotions. Because when you looked into his two eyes and listened to his words, something would surge up within you.

But he did not know.

That even without being ten years younger, he had ultimately revealed his true identity.

That Geom Mugeuk was standing right before his eyes at this very moment.

Specifically, it was Geom Mugeuk within the Spacetime Displacement Technique.

Moments ago, when Geom Mugeuk opened the door and came out, sensing someone approaching from the end of the corridor, he had entered the Spacetime Displacement Technique.

It was a momentary thought that if someone remained in this place after everyone else had left, they might share something important.

And that prediction had hit the mark.

Even though he had known that Cheonae Geosa was the mastermind, because he had put on such a perfect act, a faint, lingering doubt of “what if” had remained, but now it was definitively revealed.

Outside the Spacetime Displacement Technique, a man of Hwaryulcheong looked at him and spoke.

“Geom Mugeuk, you will become the villain of this stage. The Great Righteous-Demon War will eventually happen, and you will be its cause.”

Geom Mugeuk stood before him with cold eyes.

*Fine, I’ll play that villain.*

He had no intention of stepping onto this grand stage for free.

*Instead, the appearance fee is your life.*

* * *

Jin Pacheon stood in the garden in front of his residence, hands clasped behind his back, looking up at the sky.

The back of the man who upheld the Righteous Faction felt particularly lonely today.

He had lived a life without knowing fear.

He had feared neither villains nor experts, neither the Demon Cult nor the Sa Domeung, nor anyone in the world.

Going through this matter, he felt an inexplicable sense of helplessness. If he had lost through martial arts, he would not have felt this sense of loss. Because he could just train more. Because he could train and win again.

What tormented him now was that his judgment was no longer what it used to be.

*Have I grown old now, too?*

In the past, he felt he would have made a decision several times over by now, but he was still hesitating to decide between Geom Mugeuk and Cheonae Geosa.

He tried to excuse it as a matter requiring decisive evidence, but in the past, he would have already known instinctively—whose words were correct.

It was by that blade-sharp instinct that he had upheld the righteous martial world until now.

But he felt that instinct had dulled.

There, one person entered, guided by the guards of the Alliance Leader’s manor.

It was Geom Mugeuk, wearing a bamboo hat pulled low. Geom Mugeuk removed his hat and greeted politely.

“I greet the Alliance Leader.”

He had naturally expected to meet at the Alliance Leader’s manor, but the Alliance Leader had him come to his own residence deep within.

He had not thought he would be led here in the current situation, unless it were around the time of the Tripartite Meeting.

Then a woman entered.

“It’s been a while.”

Seeing her, he could understand why he had been made to come this far. She was none other than Jin Haryeong.

Seeing her for the first time in a while, she had matured even more. She had been beautiful to begin with, but now she looked stronger, with added experience and training. Her accomplishments could be felt.

“I sense the harsh winds of the martial world on you?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s greeting laced with a joke, Jin Haryeong asked with a smile.

“Is that a compliment?”

“Of course.”

Now, even after a long time apart, the two could treat each other comfortably like friends.

There was a clear reason for her change.

*Everyone’s changing by the day because of someone. I can’t be the only one left behind, can I?*

It was the result of efforts born from that mindset.

“Have you been well?”

“I haven’t been well, thanks to someone. I can’t leave the inner courtyard here.”

Because of Geom Mugeuk’s prediction that Jin Hagun and Jin Haryeong could be targets, she had been unable to leave the inner courtyard of the Alliance Leader’s manor. Even within the inner courtyard, martial artists of the Martial Alliance guard corps surrounded her in layers.

“At first, I thought you guys were raiding us.”

That was really how it seemed. While they were having a gathering of junior experts, the guards of the Alliance Leader’s manor rushed in and took her out. The martial artists who had been with her must have thought some major problem had occurred in the Alliance.

“If we had raided you……”

“You think everyone at that gathering would have died?”

When she looked at him with feigned suspicion, Geom Mugeuk smiled and waved his hands.

“No! I’m not someone who breaks up gatherings, so don’t worry. You know how much I like it when people gather, right? Come to think of it, it’s about time we all met up, isn’t it? I need to hear about Saini and Han Sogungju, too.”

Jin Haryeong smiled at Geom Mugeuk’s words. She knew from her grandfather how serious the recent situation was.

But Geom Mugeuk never lost his smile, even in this situation. How could she not like him?

Jin Pacheon silently watched the two converse. He suddenly recalled the moment when Jin Haryeong had brought Geom Mugeuk as the one she would marry.

*Should I have made them get married back then?*

Back then, he had truly thought it was absurd, but now he was thinking this.

*What would have happened if I really had?*

Would this current worry have been different?

Thus, as the reunion of the two ended, Jin Pacheon asked Geom Mugeuk.

“Have you met that man?”

“Yes, Alliance Leader.”

Seeing him ask in front of Jin Haryeong, it seemed she had heard everything about the situation.

“What did you find out?”

It was a question not so easily answered, yet the answer came out far too quickly and decisively.

“I confirmed that he is the mastermind.”

A heavy silence flowed.

Both of them knew well that Geom Mugeuk was not someone who would lie about such matters.

“Did the Hermit really join hands with the masterminds?”

Jin Haryeong asked Geom Mugeuk with a truly surprised expression.

Rather, compared to her, Jin Pacheon merely gazed at Geom Mugeuk without revealing any emotion.

“He did not join hands with them—he was the mastermind himself. If it were not so, he would never have betrayed the friendship with the Alliance Leader.”

These were also words for Jin Pacheon. That this was not a matter of friendship.

“This is not a betrayal. He merely entered the Alliance Leader’s life and acted from the very beginning.”

The moment he heard those words, Jin Pacheon felt as though a small hole to breathe had been pierced through his suffocatingly frustrated and complicated heart.

Geom Mugeuk began to dig that hole even wider.

“It is merely one of countless conspiracies occurring in the martial world. It only lasted long because the Alliance Leader is such an extraordinary person. So please do not attach any meaning to this matter. Even without him, how many things are there that deserve your attention?”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to Jin Haryeong. He was speaking through his actions. Was there not a precious person who truly deserved his attention and heart?

Of course, Geom Mugeuk knew well that this matter could not be healed with a few words. Therefore, he devoted himself to widening that hole further in his own way.

“It is not even worth wasting your mental energy over. Rather, you need not attach more meaning to it than to a friend of five years, ten years, or even one you made just yesterday. This is not a matter of friendship. The longer the duration, the more meaningless it becomes. Having endured for decades? That is madness, not friendship.”

Though he knew these were obvious words said for his sake, they certainly helped Jin Pacheon. Because he could feel the breathing hole growing little by little.

“This is not a matter of whether the Alliance Leader saw through him or failed to. You are a martial artist, not an actor. This is not a problem on our side, but on theirs. The problem is their ambition to devour the martial world. The problem is their persistence in hiding their identity for decades. None of them are normal.”

Geom Mugeuk added finally.

“So you can just say this: ‘You bastard, you worked so hard forcing a friendly face all this time. You worked so hard.’”

Jin Haryeong could feel it. That Geom Mugeuk was trying his hardest to ensure no wound remained in her grandfather’s heart. Yes, this was the kind of person he was.

Jin Haryeong asked Geom Mugeuk as if pressing him.

“What if you were the villain!”

Of course, she completely trusted Geom Mugeuk. These words were on behalf of her grandfather’s heart.

“Then all hell would have broken loose.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s answer, Jin Haryeong nearly burst out laughing. Because those casually spoken words hit home so hard. Yes, then it really would have been utter chaos.

Geom Mugeuk said calmly to her.

“But if I had caused chaos, it wouldn’t have been this way. I wouldn’t have plotted a scheme while being suspected until the very end.”

As if making an excuse, Geom Mugeuk said to Jin Pacheon.

“If I had plotted a scheme, it would have been a more comfortable and exciting one!”

Feeling the same way, Jin Haryeong looked at her grandfather. You know too, right, Grandfather? That this man’s words are true. That if he really plotted a scheme, he wouldn’t create a situation like this in the first place. We probably wouldn’t even know it was a scheme and would just fall victim to it. We would have already been done for!

Jin Pacheon silently looked at Geom Mugeuk. How could he not know? The sight of him trying so hard for his sake.

Through this Young Cult Leader, he was constantly reaffirmed that trust was not a matter of how long you had known each other.

“Why do you try so hard for me?”

Then Geom Mugeuk glanced at Jin Haryeong and gave an unexpected answer.

“He is my friend’s grandfather, isn’t he? Then he is no different from my own grandfather.”

Jin Pacheon looked at Jin Haryeong. If it were not for Geom Mugeuk, neither his granddaughter nor his grandson would be alive in this world now. Looking directly at Jin Haryeong, he could once again feel how great a deed saving her had been.

Though that matter was unrelated to the truth of this incident, it had a great influence on this decision.

If it were not for Geom Mugeuk, these children wouldn’t be here either, and his life would have become meaningless. Why had he been unable to trust this person?

“Tell me about that comfortable and exciting scheme you plan to plot from now on. I can’t afford to fall victim to it.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly.

Finally, Jin Pacheon believed him.

Geom Mugeuk bowed politely.

“Thank you for believing me.”

“I apologize for being late in my decision.”

“If it were me, I would have agonized for another ten years.”

Jin Pacheon smiled. This Young Cult Leader was making him smile at the very moment he had decided his friend was a traitor. Yes, he would believe him and go forward.

Jin Haryeong looked at Geom Mugeuk and smiled.

*Thank you, friend. I will definitely repay you for what you did for Grandfather today.*

Geom Mugeuk answered with a bright smile as well.

Once the decision was made, Jin Pacheon did not look back. He intended to trust Geom Mugeuk and handle this matter.

“What is the reason you asked to see me today? Did you come to ask permission to kill him?”

Having been told he wanted to meet and having met, he thought he might be seeking permission to kill him.

“No. I will show the Alliance Leader clearly. That he is truly the mastermind.”

Jin Pacheon shook his head, saying it was unnecessary.

“I believe your words.”

“That is precisely why I must show you. Because you are someone who believes in me, I must show you.”

The Alliance Leader’s breathing hole was wide open. But now, in that place, another small hole would be pierced.

A hole of doubt.

If he handled it without confirming directly, that hole of doubt would never be filled. He had no intention of continuing his relationship with the Alliance Leader in that manner.

Now Geom Mugeuk began the work of filling that hole.

“I intend to stand on the stage with him.”

And this was the request he had come to make of the Alliance Leader today.

“Please be the audience for that stage.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 655.

Chapter 655: Today I Am Not the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult
---------------------------------------------------------------------
The person who arrived at Cheongun Forest was Cheonae Geosa.

He walked through the misty bamboo grove. The one waiting in the pavilion visible in the distance was Geom Mugeuk.

Leaning against the railing, Geom Mugeuk silently watched Cheonae Geosa walk toward him. Then, when his eyes met Cheonae Geosa’s, he waved in greeting.

Geom Mugeuk did not postpone his bout with him.

‘I’ll kill him today!’

Not that any enemy he had faced until now had ever been easy, but this Cheonae Geosa was also an opponent as troublesome as any.

He could not know the potential of the man’s true form, nor did he know who might leap out to help him.

Cheongun Forest.

This was the stage Geom Mugeuk had chosen.

Since it was the place Murim Alliance Leader Jin Paecheon loved most, he thought it the most suitable stage for his final battle with Cheonae Geosa.

There was one more practical reason as well.

The mist that flowed through Cheongun Forest in every season.

Within that mist, a special peerless formation had been laid today.

The Unmu Eunhyeong Formation.

Originally, the Unmu Eunhyeong Formation was a formation for concealment, one that raised mist and allowed people to hide within it. Once the formation was activated, even a master could not find those hidden inside unless he knew how to break it.

Moreover, when deployed within mist, it was said that even a formation expert could not tell whether it was real mist or an activated formation. It was a top-grade formation, and one of the formations possessed by the Murim Alliance.

Within the Unmu Eunhyeong Formation now spread through the mist was Jin Paecheon. Thanks to this place and the formation, they had been able to create this stage without revealing the existence of the Spacetime Transposition Art.

This stage was the most important stage that would decide everyone’s fate, and Jin Paecheon was the most important spectator.

Cheonae Geosa came up into the pavilion. After greeting him, Geom Mugeuk admired the scene unfolding before his eyes.

“I think I understand why the Alliance Leader likes this place. The mist flowing between the bamboo is truly fantastical.”

Cheonae Geosa silently looked at Geom Mugeuk, who was gazing outside. Though his back was turned, there was not a single opening to be found.

Cheonae Geosa raised his hand and touched the air. It could not be seen, but he could feel it—the energy Geom Mugeuk had spread out like a spiderweb.

Though his back was turned, the Young Cult Leader had complete control over him and over this space. And he did not bother hiding that fact.

The heated fervor that flickered past Cheonae Geosa’s eyes vanished the moment Geom Mugeuk turned around.

“Our dear Geosa, welcome!”

Cheonae Geosa took a few steps forward, then stood beside Geom Mugeuk and looked out at the bamboo grove.

“The mist is thick today.”

It sounded as if he had been here often, so Geom Mugeuk asked him.

“Have you been here before?”

“I come here from time to time as well. It’s a place my friend likes.”

The two of them treated each other calmly, as if they had both forgotten all the tension of their first meeting.

“Why don’t you ask? Why the Alliance Leader isn’t here, and why I am?”

Today’s meeting had been one Jin Paecheon had arranged with Cheonae Geosa.

Cheonae Geosa merely wore an expression that said, “It’s obvious without asking,” and said nothing.

“I asked the Alliance Leader to let me meet you. Last time, you were so firm when you said there would be no next time.”

Cheonae Geosa turned his gaze toward Geom Mugeuk.

“You didn’t call me here to kill me?”

His eyes and tone seemed to say that he had come even though he knew that might be the case.

“If that were so, would you have come, Geosa? You came because you judged it wasn’t, didn’t you?”

From Cheonae Geosa’s reaction, Geom Mugeuk could tell that he had expected this situation, with Geom Mugeuk waiting in Jin Paecheon’s place. In that case, although the invitation had come from this side, this stage was also his stage.

A joint stage for him and himself.

‘What have you prepared for this stage?’

Who would be able to walk down from this stage alive would be decided by whose preparations were more thorough.

“Even if you were going to kill me, I had to come.”

“You’d come even to die? Why?”

Cheonae Geosa answered calmly.

“Because my friend called me.”

His gaze was somehow hollow and lonely.

If Geom Mugeuk had not seen his true form within the Spacetime Transposition Art, he would have been confused even at this moment. That was how sincerely the man spoke.

“If that friend told me to die, I could die.”

They were words that would pain Jin Paecheon if he heard them. In truth, he was surely watching this scene even now with a heavy heart.

“That’s impressive. If Hagin tried to kill me, I have no intention of meekly letting him.”

Cheonae Geosa’s eyes gleamed coldly.

“That is because you did not share friendship with Captain Jin, but tried to use him.”

As if for someone to hear, he brought up the rumors circulating in the world.

“When I heard the rumor that the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult had approached the leader of the Demon-Extermination Unit and was building a friendship with him, I had already seen through your intentions. Your intention to use blood ties to bewitch Alliance Leader Jin.”

Geom Mugeuk received his words with ease.

“That is an insult to your friend. The Alliance Leader is not someone who would fall so easily into a scheme.”

“That is why you tried to use his blood kin.”

Cheonae Geosa continued digging into that point.

“I understand that aside from the leader of the Demon-Extermination Unit, you also approached Haryeong. Even so, will you say you have no ulterior motive? Just what is your goal?”

If one really heard only these words, he would look like a thoroughly wicked man. That was why this fight was troublesome and difficult. He was someone who used even such points thoroughly.

“As you know, my goal is to reveal your identity, Geosa.”

“How laughable. The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult comes to Wuhan and says he will reveal the identity of someone who has lived for decades as a righteous faction man. Even a passing dog would laugh.”

“There are no passing dogs, so I’ll laugh for you.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly. A blade was hidden within that composure.

“It is only the two of us here. Is there really any need to hide your identity to this extent?”

“I will say it again. I have no identity to hide.”

Geom Mugeuk asked the thing the man would be most curious about.

“Why don’t you ask? Why I called you the Incarnation of Revenge.”

Surely those words should have drawn some kind of reaction. But he did not seem curious at all.

“Revenge? I have no idea what you are talking about.”

Geom Mugeuk perched on the railing and spoke as if to himself, like he was making a deduction.

“Yet you were the Geosa whose composure shattered at those words. Even though it is just the two of us, why do you hide your identity to this extent?”

Geom Mugeuk already knew the answer.

“You know what kind of place this is.”

In that instant, he did not miss the sharp gleam in Cheonae Geosa’s eyes.

He knew there was a spectator on this stage. He also knew that spectator was Jin Paecheon.

That was why he was choosing only words that would shake the Alliance Leader’s heart.

“You used his blood kin and bewitched him with all manner of words, but it will not work on me. I will protect my friend to the end.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. He could not move him with words. Especially if the man knew that Jin Paecheon was watching.

Now was the moment to throw out a decisive move that was not words.

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes sank coldly, revealing an atmosphere different from before.

“It doesn’t matter. I can make you reveal your identity.”

At those words, even Cheonae Geosa reacted. The gaze he directed at Geom Mugeuk contained this emotion: You? How?

“There was the fastest and surest method, but I couldn’t use it for one reason.”

That reason had to do with Jin Paecheon.

“To use that method, I needed the Alliance Leader’s permission.”

“What permission is that?”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes turned cold.

“Permission to kill you.”

The moment his words ended, an aura burst from Geom Mugeuk’s body.

In the next instant, Cheonae Geosa realized that he was standing beneath a blue sky.

Of course, it was not the sky, but above the sea. Because it was so clear, the sky was reflected upon it, making it feel as though he were standing in the heavens. Such was Geom Mugeuk’s aura.

The aura had changed from before.

The sea was even clearer. And the endless abyss beneath his feet was even deeper and darker.

Splash.

Cheonae Geosa sank into the sea.

An invisible force seized him and dragged him deep beneath the water.

His breath was stifled. It was too powerful to call the aura of a mere man.

Cheonae Geosa also released his own aura.

His aura began to push back Geom Mugeuk’s.

The area around him, which had been in the dark abyss, grew brightly lit.

Around him, he saw snow scattering across a pure white snowfield.

Then his surroundings darkened again.

Pressed down by Geom Mugeuk’s aura, he had begun to be dragged back into the abyssal sea.

Cheonae Geosa drew up his inner power and released his aura even more powerfully. His surroundings brightened again, then darkened once more.

Like a lamp going out and being lit again, darkness and brightness alternated.

Each time it grew bright, the blizzard was raging even more fiercely. The lone flower blooming there was perilously fragile.

From darkness to brightness, then back to darkness, and again to brightness.

It was at that very moment.

Shhiiik!

A sword flew at Cheonae Geosa’s eyes in a surprise attack.

Crack.

Cheonae Geosa shattered the pavilion railing and flew backward, avoiding the sword. Geom Mugeuk, who had been engaged in a clash of auras, had launched a sudden attack.

Standing at the edge of the pavilion with his sword in hand, Geom Mugeuk looked down with a chilling gaze.

“If you do not reveal your original aura, you will die by my hand.”

Despite Geom Mugeuk’s warning, Cheonae Geosa was not frightened. Rather, a smile appeared at the corners of his mouth.

“Can you truly handle what comes afterward?”

“When have the Demonic Cult bastards you know ever worried about what came afterward before doing something?”

Shhiiik.

Geom Mugeuk lightly launched himself from the pavilion and flew toward Cheonae Geosa.

Cheonae Geosa did not dodge. He drew his sword and clashed with him.

Clang-clang-clang-clang-clang-clang!

Blades struck blades, and the sounds of fierce impacts and sparks burst forth one after another.

A dozen exchanges passed in an instant.

Shhiiik!

Cheonae Geosa narrowly avoided the final strike that flew in with an irregular change.

It was an attack that truly would have severed his neck if he had failed to dodge. The killing intent carried in Geom Mugeuk’s sword was real.

“I told you before. With someone like you, killing you first and handling what comes afterward isn’t a bad method.”

Geom Mugeuk’s swordsmanship was neither the Ninefire Demonic Art nor the Flying Heaven Sword Art. They were simply attacks that thrust and cut, yet Geom Mugeuk’s martial learning was contained in those simple sword strokes.

Freed from fixed forms, Geom Mugeuk’s attacks were incomparably fast and fierce. The strength granted by freedom was being displayed without reserve in this moment today.

Paht!

Blood sprayed from Cheonae Geosa’s arm. He was as famous as the Alliance Leader, but he was not famous because he was as strong as the Alliance Leader. He was famous because he was Jin Paecheon’s friend, and also because he was a great chivalrous hero.

He could not withstand Geom Mugeuk’s momentum as he pressed him with terrifying force, as if he truly meant to kill him.

Even though he was driven into danger, he did not reveal his original form.

He had one certainty.

‘You absolutely cannot kill me!’

Just as Geom Mugeuk had thought, Cheonae Geosa understood exactly what kind of place this was today.

‘Because you have to show Jin Paecheon my identity. The more you try to kill me, a righteous faction master, the greater his suspicion of you will become.’

With every drop of blood he shed, the suspicion toward the Young Cult Leader would grow greater.

Cheonae Geosa was certain of it.

And Geom Mugeuk was also certain.

‘You will definitely show your true form.’

Because he would never want to die here in vain. After enduring for the past several decades, how could he die so meaninglessly?

Shhiiik!

Geom Mugeuk’s sword flew toward his neck.

Cheonae Geosa did not dodge.

‘In the end, he will stop.’

Because he would never kill him.

It was like two carriages charging toward each other.

Two carriages racing without stopping, each certain that the other would surely pull the reins.

Dudududududu.

And at the final moment, one of the two pulled the reins and changed direction.

Kaaang!

An ear-splitting metallic sound erupted.

Geom Mugeuk was knocked backward and retreated.

At the final moment, Cheonae Geosa had swung his sword and counterattacked. And this counterattack was incomparably faster and more powerful than the skills he had shown until now.

Like a true madman, Geom Mugeuk had not withdrawn his sword until the very last instant. It was not because he truly intended to kill him, but because he believed the man would surely evade.

No matter how strong Cheonae Geosa’s will was, there had to be something that would betray him.

That was his “survival instinct.”

He believed that the survival instinct of such a powerful master would surely make him survive, even if it meant disregarding his own will.

Just as, no matter how much Geom Mugeuk himself tried to die, the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art would not simply stand by and watch.

In the place where Cheonae Geosa had been standing stood another man.

Though he was the same person, the one exuding a completely different aura was Hwa Yulcheong. As he fully revealed himself, his presence could not be compared to before.

He was standing in the middle of a battlefield.

Instead of flying snow, ash from burned corpses drifted around him.

It was different from the aura Geom Mugeuk had seen that day at Musojang.

If the aura then had been a war that had occurred in one region, the aura now was a war that had occurred across the entire Central Plains.

Blood flowed like rivers, and corpses were piled like mountains.

In the midst of it, Hwa Yulcheong stood with eyes as hot as lava.

“You won’t step out even when he is trying to kill me?”

The person Hwa Yulcheong addressed was not Geom Mugeuk. He spoke toward the mist.

Then someone’s voice came from within the mist.

“I decided to trust that friend instead of you.”

The owner of the voice was, of course, Jin Paecheon.

Was it because he had expected it from the beginning? Even though Jin Paecheon appeared, Hwa Yulcheong’s eyes showed no fear at all.

“You trust the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, not the friend you have known for decades?”

His words contained not regret and disappointment, but mockery and anger. After all, Jin Paecheon had seen his true self.

Jin Paecheon’s voice gradually drew closer.

“That man is not the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult today.”

Soon after, Jin Paecheon fully emerged from the mist.

“Today, he is my grandson’s friend.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 656

Chapter 656: Because They Would Not Have Invited Me to a Tragedy
----------------------------------------------------------------
Had he ever seen such an expression on that friend's face?

Jin Paecheon was watching.

A person who was a friend, yet not a friend.

The unfamiliar energy emanating from that old friend. In all his time living as the Martial Alliance Lord, this was the first time he had felt such a powerful dark aura.

Just by looking at this energy, he could tell everything Geom Mugeuk had said was true. The words Hwaryulcheong had spoken just moments ago came to mind.

Are you going to believe the Demonic Cult's Young Cult Leader instead of a friend you've known for decades?

Was he really saying that to him? After deceiving him for decades on end.

Jin Paecheon forced his boiling emotions down.

"Yes, we were friends for decades."

Jin Paecheon continued, gazing at Hwaryulcheong.

"In all those years, was there even a single day when we were truly friends?"

Hwaryulcheong, instead of apologizing, rather blamed Jin Paecheon.

"If we had been friends, at least you wouldn't have hidden in the fog and spied on me."

Jin Paecheon could not adjust to the reality before his eyes.
