A great wave was surging in from afar. Jin Hyu stood atop that towering wave.

If they were swept up in that enormous wave, they would all be scattered, and then attacked by the water-men beneath the surface. His intention was to defeat them one by one.

In that hopeless situation, Jin Hagun looked at the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

The patterns on her face had all disappeared. Only the flame pattern on the back of her hand remained, faintly.

And her face was pale from the severe depletion of her internal energy.

“Demon Venerable, please escape first.”

If it was someone on the level of the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, he was certain she had some secret method to save herself.

They could not let her die here as well.

“I suppose I should.”

When she readily said she would leave, Jin Hagun felt relieved and cupped his fist respectfully.

“I will not forget the grace you have shown us by helping us until now.”

She smiled as she looked at Jin Hagun.

“Trying to save myself alone? I really am a bitch, aren’t I?”

Jin Hagun smiled.

“You were the most magnificent bitch I have ever seen. I will see you in the next life.”

The Demon Annihilation Squad warriors in the water also bowed their heads respectfully. Not a single one of them feared death.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, who had seemed as though she would leave at once, changed her mind.

“I would like to go as well, but I cannot.”

“He will understand too.”

Geom Mugeuk, do not blame the Demon Venerable too much. She did her utmost.

But then she gave a reason he could not understand.

“It is not because of the Young Cult Leader. It is because I am still in training.”

Just as Jin Hagun and the Demon Annihilation Squad warriors were wearing expressions that said they could not understand what she meant—

A pitch-black shadow fell over the place where the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable and Jin Hagun stood.

Everyone aboard the boat turned around.

They were startled. It had not been a cloud covering the sun.

Swaaaaaaaaaa.

A colossal ship was cutting through the water as it approached them.

Never in their lives had they seen a ship so large. It was so enormous that the wave Jin Hyu was driving toward them looked like nothing more than a light ripple before it.

At the prow of that ship stood the Pungcheon Cult Leader, arms folded.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 661

Chapter 661: The Reason for Siding with the Young Cult Leader
-------------------------------------------------------------
“Master.”

At the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s cry, Jin Hagun and the warriors of the Demon Extermination Squad knew.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader had arrived here. The man standing proudly at the prow of that enormous ship was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

They also knew now that the honored person the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable had said delayed her was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Even the Pungcheon Cult Leader has come?”

At Jin Hagun’s startled question, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable replied.

“Captain Jin has no idea how much care the Young Cult Leader put into this.”

Jin Hagun thought of Geom Mugeuk. Whenever he thought of him, the first image that came to mind was always of him smiling. He seemed interested only in teasing him with silly jokes every time, yet he never missed a single thing. After all, he was the sort of person who could make that huge ship arrive at a moment of crisis like this.

He had no idea how much care the Young Cult Leader had put in for him?

“No, I think I do.”

He spoke as if guessing, but his words were filled with certainty.

Just then, the water-men who had their heads above the surface saw the ship and shrieked in strange excitement. To be precise, it was a scream raised at the sight of the Pungcheon Cult Leader standing at the prow.

Because Jin Hagun was close by, he could feel it. That excitement and rage had arisen from fear. Even the water-men created within the soul-seizing art had sensed the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s presence.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader slowly unfolded his crossed arms and extended a hand forward. Then something formed in his hand.

What slid out with a whoosh was a fishing rod tinged with red energy.

“Now then, shall I show my skills for the first time in a while?”

Swaaaaaaak.

A fishing line of red energy stretched far out.

The water-men in the path of the line were caught one after another and flew into the air. Their bodies rose like dolls, and just as red energy seemed to shimmer around them—

Pop! Porok! Pororok!

The water-men vanished in midair, turning into droplets with adorable little sounds.

Swaaaaaaak.

Once more, the red-tinged fishing line cut through the air.

This time, even more water-men flew up and burst into droplets.

The water-men caught by the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s fishing and made to disappear did not merge together again as they had before.

To those watching, it looked like a child’s game, but the water-men being subjected to it screamed and hid beneath the water.

“You rascals, where do you think you’re going!”

This time, the Pungcheon Cult Leader waved both hands. Then, instead of a fishing rod, a huge net appeared in his hands. A net brimming with red energy spread out toward the sea.

Chwararararara.

This time, a net full of water-men were caught and lifted into the air. It had dragged in and captured those trying to flee as though sucking them inside.

“What a full catch!”

Pop! Pop! Pooop! Pop-pop-pop!

Jin Hagun and the Demon Extermination Squad warriors truly had never imagined it. To think those terrifying water-men would disappear like this.

No, the droplets bursting into particles in every direction as they caught the light in midair were even beautiful.

And when all the water-men had vanished like that—

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked down at Jin Hagun beneath the ship and asked cheerfully.

“How was it? Was I impressive?”

In truth, Jin Hagun did not know much about the Pungcheon Cult Leader. But through Geom Mugeuk, he had heard enough to know what sort of person he was, so he smiled and said,

“You were impressive.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader crossed his arms again in a dashing pose like a ship’s captain.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was the first to launch herself upward as she said,

“Now then, everyone, come aboard.”

Beginning with the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable, Jin Hagun and all the warriors of the Demon Extermination Squad flew up onto the ship. The ship was high, so the warriors with weaker lightness skills could not make it in a single leap and had to kick off the hull midway to rise again.

Jin Hagun flew up while bringing Gwak Ju, the hostage, with him. Gwak Ju was deeply moved that Jin Hagun had looked after him throughout this fearsome battle.

He had flown up with him, but the first person to set foot on the ship was Jin Hagun.

When they landed on the deck, Gwak Ju belatedly expressed his gratitude. During the battle earlier, there had never been a proper chance for him to give thanks. He felt he had to do it now, at least.

“Thank you, truly.”

At his thanks, Jin Hagun replied in a low voice.

“Give your thanks to your sister. She is the one who sent us here.”

Emotion passed over Gwak Ju’s face. Even without being told, he knew how hard his older sister must have struggled for his sake.

“Please, you must save me. I have to meet my sister.”

He knew that he had been kidnapped because of his sister, the iron workshop master artisan.

He did not resent his sister. On the contrary, he worried how distressed she must be, and how much she must have blamed herself because of it. That was why, when he met her, he would be sure to tell her. Before saying thank you for saving me, he would say this first.

I’m all right. Nothing happened to me.

Jin Hagun said to him,

“We will be sure to take you home.”

All the warriors of the Demon Extermination Squad landed around Jin Hagun.

Once all his subordinates had come aboard, Jin Hagun walked toward the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Cult Leader, thank you truly for saving us.”

Jin Hagun respectfully saluted with cupped fists, and behind him the warriors of the Demon Extermination Squad all bowed in unison. Gwak Ju and Grand Master Hwasong also lowered their heads.

They had truly met the Pungcheon Cult Leader at the threshold of hell.

“What is the Young Cult Leader doing, driving his friend into a corner and not even showing his nose?”

As expected, the moment they met, the Pungcheon Cult Leader began by badmouthing Geom Mugeuk.

“Well, he was always that selfish and coldhearted sort of person. He makes people suffer terribly and never once comes to check on them.”

“That friend must be in a difficult situation as well.”

When Jin Hagun took his friend’s side, the Pungcheon Cult Leader firmly shook his head.

“Impossible! Whoever is facing the Young Cult Leader is the one who must be in trouble.”

As he said that, the Pungcheon Cult Leader habitually looked behind him. Normally, whenever he badmouthed the Young Cult Leader, the man would appear from behind, but today he was not there. For the briefest moment, a trace of disappointment passed through the corners of his eyes.

Putting that disappointment behind him, the Pungcheon Cult Leader came to Jin Hagun’s side and asked casually,

“What did he take from you? A treasured sword, or some treasure? Come now, I’m like an older brother to you, so tell me everything at ease.”

Jin Hagun was flustered, having never expected him to ask such a thing in such an urgent situation. Cult Leader, do you not see the wave rushing in over there?

“No, nothing.”

“Yes, at first I was ashamed too, so I hid it. Later, you’ll end up sharing it all.”

Jin Hagun could tell that the Pungcheon Cult Leader was far more free-spirited than he had heard. Was it because he was this sort of person that he had become close to Geom Mugeuk? Or had he changed this way because he had become close to Geom Mugeuk?

After exchanging greetings with Jin Hagun like that, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s gaze turned toward his disciple.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable lowered her head as if ashamed.

“I am sorry. I should have ended it with my own hands.”

“No. You fought well enough.”

It seemed the Pungcheon Cult Leader had watched her entire battle.

He looked at the great wave approaching and extended a hand into the air.

Parat!

Blue energy rose in the air as though caught on his fingertips.

“The Blood Heaven Soul-Compelling Art spread over this place is too strong. Even I have never seen such a powerful soul-compelling art. It was probably prepared over a very long time.”

Jin Hagun nodded and said quietly,

“You have seen correctly. This conspiracy was prepared decades ago.”

The one whose heart ached most at those words was Grand Master Hwasong. He had spent all those long years with Jin Hyu as a colleague, as a friend. For that entire stretch of time, he had been deceived.

In the meantime, the wave had drawn close.

Jin Hyu, standing atop the wave, was staring at the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable.

The wave approached more slowly than its original speed. Riding atop it, he must have been observing the newly arrived Pungcheon Cult Leader.

The oncoming wave stopped. It halted high in the air like a painting, and Jin Hyu raised the wave even higher to meet the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s gaze at eye level.

He recognized that his opponent was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“So the madwoman even called her master.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable felt sorry toward her master, as if she had caused him to be insulted along with her for no reason.

“I am sorry.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader did not mind at all.

“The rougher an opponent’s mouth is, the better proof it is that you fought well.”

Comforting his disciple like that, he threw one sharp remark at Jin Hyu.

“Only I am allowed to insult my disciple. Do you understand, you cowardly old man!”

Jin Hyu’s aged eyes instantly grew fierce. He had already heard the word coward more than enough today, so the words that burst from him were not pleasant either.

“I have heard plenty of rumors about you. They say you abandoned the Pungcheon Cult and clung to the Demon Cult?”

Of course, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was not someone who lost verbal sparring anywhere.

“To be precise, I clung to the Young Cult Leader.”

For a moment, it seemed he had meekly admitted it.

“I mean the Young Cult Leader who is neatly plucking off your necks one by one. Come to think of it, it seems you clung to the wrong side, didn’t you?”

Rumble!

As if reflecting Jin Hyu’s mood, the surrounding waves rose higher and black storm clouds rolled in.

“Yes, I knew a day like this would come eventually.”

Killing intent flowed from Jin Hyu’s body. He revealed an even more powerful presence than when he had fought the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable earlier. As if the strength he had hidden was not thirty percent but a full three-tenths, the aura he displayed was different.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been quietly staring at him, said something unexpected.

“When I was young, I once heard a rumor like this. They said someone with heavenly talent in soul-seizing arts had appeared in the Central Plains.”

The soul-seizing art Jin Hyu was displaying now had reached an extreme realm. More than other martial arts, soul-seizing arts depended on talent. Unless one was born with it innately, one could never reach such a level.

Even with the help of the Blood Heaven Soul-Compelling Art, it would never be easy to defeat his disciple, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable, with soul-seizing arts. He had to be the one who had once been called a genius.

“I heard that news of him vanished at some point. It seems that talent did not go into judgment.”

Jin Hyu snorted. He did not deny that the person was him.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader confirmed that his hunch had been right. The reason that person had stayed in his memory was because of his own personality. He himself had grown up hearing that he possessed heavenly talent, and when he heard there was someone like that in the Central Plains, he had felt envious and jealous. He remembered cursing him for quite some time.

“This field of playing with ghosts is truly narrow.”

The gazes of the two men tangled in midair over the surging sea.

Then Jin Hyu also asked the Pungcheon Cult Leader,

“Then why were you won over by the Young Cult Leader?”

That was what he was most curious about regarding the Pungcheon Cult Leader. To even step down from the position of cult leader and follow the Young Cult Leader? It was utterly incomprehensible.

“I wasn’t won over.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader sighed and added,

“I was kidnapped. By the Young Cult Leader.”

Jin Hyu did not understand what he meant, but Jin Hagun, who was listening, smiled faintly.

He understood why he had described it as being kidnapped. It was the feeling of coming to his senses only to find Geom Mugeuk standing in front of him saying this:

You’ve been kidnapped by me.

Yes, he could sympathize fully.

Of course, Jin Hyu could not sympathize with those words or that feeling.

“To think you dared enter my world on your own feet. It seems you enjoy being kidnapped.”

Jin Hyu began drawing up his internal energy to the utmost limit. Blue energy burst from his body.

Saaaaaaaaaa.

The garments of those aboard the ship fluttered in the fierce wind. The wind began to blow stronger and stronger.

Soon, sea spray began to rise from the ocean, and whirlpools started to form.

Kwaaaaaaa!

There was not just one whirlpool. Whirlpools began appearing all around the ship they were on. One by one they increased, until there were more than a dozen. It was a terrifying sight just to behold.

Gradually, the whirlpools arising from within the water grew larger, and their speed became even faster.

It seemed he intended to smash the ship with the whirlpools, but that was not it.

Something rose from those whirlpools.

Puaang! Puaang! Puang!

What burst out from them were enormous tentacles. He had used tentacles that emerged from his back when fighting the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable earlier as well, but this time they were tentacles shaped like the giant legs of an octopus beneath the sea.

Kwaaaaaaa!

Tentacles flew in from every direction toward the warriors. Jin Hagun and the Demon Extermination Squad drew their swords in unison and launched themselves forward.

Kwaang!

When a huge tentacle struck down on the ship, the vessel lurched.

The warriors of the Demon Extermination Squad scattered in all directions, sending out sword energy.

The flying sword energy wounded the tentacles.

Puaaaak!

Blue blood flowed down from the tentacles.

When that blood fell onto the deck—

Chiiiiik!

Blue smoke rose as it melted the deck.

“It’s poisonous smoke!”

The warriors covered their mouths and noses as they retreated.

Jin Hagun sent out a palm force to push the smoke away and shouted,

“Don’t cut them carelessly!”

Once they became tentacles that could not be cut carelessly, the situation for the warriors of the Demon-Extermination Unit grew even more difficult.

Cult Leader Pungcheon still stood at the bow with his arms folded.

One of the tentacles came flying toward Cult Leader Pungcheon.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable, standing beside him, drew up what little inner power she had left and thrust out her hand toward the tentacle.

Fwooooosh!

The incoming tentacle went up in flames.

The tentacle howled and plunged into the water. After extinguishing the fire and rising again, it did not attack Cult Leader Pungcheon.

The deck was truly in chaos. One warrior, wrapped in a tentacle, was hurled into the air.

Jin Hagun launched himself forward and swung his sword.

Shiiiiing.

The tentacle, cut by his sword energy, released the warrior. Jin Hagun snatched him out of the spray of blood and saved him.

Watching the scene with calm eyes, Cult Leader Pungcheon asked the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable,

“What should be done at a time like this?”

“We must find the way to break it.”

“Find it.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable first looked at Jin Hyu. He still stood on the waves with his eyes closed, controlling the tentacles. Around his body, three or four tentacles were guarding him.

If they killed him, this space would naturally disappear, but surely that was not the fundamental method of breaking it that her master was asking about.

“The way to break it is underwater.”

Since the tentacles had come out of the water, the method to counter them would also be down there.

Cult Leader Pungcheon quietly looked at her and said,

“Shall I go?”

“N-no. Your disciple will go.”

Without hesitation, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable leapt straight into the water.

Seeing that, Jin Hagun followed her in. He knew that the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s inner power was nearly depleted.

“I will help.”

When Jin Hagun went in, several of the Demon-Extermination Unit warriors who saw him followed and jumped in as well.

“You’re all so young!”

Cult Leader Pungcheon’s gaze deepened as he looked at the water they had entered.

Then Jin Hyu opened the eyes he had kept closed.

When he looked at him, Cult Leader Pungcheon rubbed his belly and said,

“I’d lost quite a bit of weight lately. But after drinking with that disciple from beyond the borders, my drinking belly came right back.”

Talking about his belly in this situation? Jin Hyu’s expression seemed to say, Is this lunatic insane?

Of course, even in the midst of that, the eyes with which both men looked at each other were frighteningly cold. Eyes that would not permit even a single moment’s opening.

“You asked why I was recruited by the Young Cult Leader, didn’t you? I was no different from you. Whether others lived or died, all that mattered was satisfying my own desires. But that hateful Young Cult Leader, you see, put on an act of caring for others first. His pitch-black insides still haven’t been exposed, but someday they will be. Right?”

When Cult Leader Pungcheon laughed loudly, Jin Hyu spat out as if he found him pathetic.

“What bullshit.”

At that, Cult Leader Pungcheon’s gaze deepened.

“That’s right. That’s why. You were called a genius, and I grew up being called a genius too. But this is the result. What’s the use of being a genius in martial arts? When people with minds as narrow as soy sauce dishes gather together, all that comes of it is this kind of bullshit. That’s why. The reason I sided with the Young Cult Leader.”

Only then did Cult Leader Pungcheon answer the question he had first brought up.

“I wanted to try living a little differently.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 662

Chapter 662: Waves Are for Overcoming, Not Avoiding
---------------------------------------------------
You want to live differently?

“What kind of dogshit nonsense is this?”

To Jinhyu, these words didn't register at all.

“So? Are you satisfied living a life where you abandoned the Cult Leader’s position to run errands for the Little Cult Leader?”

He would have wanted to see a reaction saying no, but he was absolutely certain that wasn't the case.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader answered with a bright smile.

“I am truly satisfied. Heaven has gifted this to me.”

Jinhyu could tell that the Pungcheon Cult Leader truly thought this way. How could such soaring joy possibly be faked?

“Why are you saying such things to me? Are you telling me to live like a crippled bastard too?”

He had thought that this Pungcheon Cult Leader, whether senile or drunk on belated sentimentality, would somehow tell him to live like that. But cold words flowed from the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s mouth.

“What nonsense? You're going to die by my hands today.”

“Then why did you say it?”

“Why else? I was boasting.”

Curse words burst automatically from Jinhyu’s mouth.

“You crazy fat bastard, really.”

“Call me fatty one more time and you'll regret it. Ah, wait.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, standing at the bow, turned around.

The Demon Annihilation Squad warriors were fighting desperately against tentacles bursting out from everywhere, and one of them was in crisis.

A tentacle coiled around the warrior was trying to tear his leg off raw, and there was no one nearby to help him.

*Boom!*

The tentacle trying to tear off the warrior’s leg exploded from the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s palm strike. Since it vanished without a trace, no blood splattered on the warrior. After bowing his head in gratitude, the warrior staggered and swung his sword again.

Without turning back, the Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke in a low voice.

“Don't be impatient, old man. You're dying today anyway.”

A dense killing intent that seemed ready to strike at any moment flew toward the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s turned back.

“If you die today too, my disciple’s era will finally begin.”

Experts of the Soul-Devouring Art like Jinhyu were perhaps only one or two in the entire present era.

Of course, Jinhyu sneered coldly. He intended to kill the two of them today and become the supreme master of the Soul-Devouring Art.

“If you die, my era will begin.”

“If that happens, the Soul-Devouring Art will likely be lost for a very long time.”

At Jinhyu’s questioning gaze, the Pungcheon Cult Leader mentioned one person.

“Because you will die by the Little Cult Leader’s hand.”

“You couldn't kill me, but the Little Cult Leader will? Have you fallen victim to his Soul-Devouring Art or something?”

A strange smile formed at the Pungcheon Cult Leader's lips.

“It's because you haven't experienced it. Yes, our impoverished imaginations could never know. I was the same.”

“You speak such words even after sending your disciple to the jaws of death.”

Then the Pungcheon Cult Leader shook his head.

“He won't die.”

“Why are you so sure?”

“You saw it earlier. The young ones jumping into that water without hesitation, one after another. Can you do that? Neither can I.”

“That’s just how the world is—fools like that die first.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader quietly nodded.

“But this is my world, isn't it?”

To say such shameless words in a world he himself created.

“You insane fatty! This is my world!”

“I warned you clearly, yet you do it again.”

Jinhyu furrowed his brow. He was the one spewing curses, yet strangely, he felt worse. He disliked that gaze, as if the other had realized something.

It was at that moment.

*Boom! Boom! Boom boom!*

Tentacles began exploding from all directions. They were things that regenerated endlessly no matter how many were killed, but just as the water demons had burst into droplets, these too exploded, creating beautiful clusters of light.

Just as the warriors on the ship were finally catching their breaths in relief!

*Fwaaah.*

The Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable revealed herself from the water. Soon after, Lord Jinha emerged exhaling, and the Demon Annihilation Squad warriors also came out. Some were injured, but fortunately, none had suffered fatal wounds.

“We have broken it.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader smiled proudly and praised her.

“Well done, my disciple.”

The Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable smiled brightly. She felt happiest when recognized by her master.

She looked at Lord Jinha floating beside her.

“Lord Jin’s help was significant.”

When the Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at Lord Jinha, he shook his head.

“The disciple did everything. We merely played a supporting role.”

Though he said that, if it hadn't been for Lord Jinha, they would have broken through much more difficultly.

They leaped up onto the ship.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke to Jinhyu.

“See, in my world, no one dies.”

Jinhyu didn't say “my world” anymore. If they had died, he could have shut that annoying mouth.

Jinhyu raised his internal energy. Not only his body, but now even blue energy began bursting from his two eyes.

*Swoooooosh!*

The waves began to rise suddenly.

Jinhyu, standing atop them, looked small.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked up at him and shouted.

“Hey you, if you overexert yourself like that at your age, you'll collapse!”

The raging waves grew even higher.

Lord Jinha and the Demon Annihilation Squad warriors looked up at the towering waves.

If those waves crashed down, there seemed to be nothing this large ship could do. It was a situation where they didn't know what would attack them if they fell into the water.

“My disciple, what must you do at a time like this?”

“I think we must avoid it first.”

At the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable's answer, the Pungcheon Cult Leader shook his head.

“Waves are not to be avoided, but to be crossed.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader stomped his foot while standing.

*Boom!*

A shockwave spread in all directions from his foot.

*Crack! Snap!*

The ship's deck shattered and flew up from everywhere.

What fell were wooden planks that seemed just the right size for one person to stand upon.

“Now, everyone disembark!”

The moment his words ended.

*Swoosh!*

The large ship they had been riding on vanished in an instant.

On the surging sea, everyone stood atop the wooden pieces created by the Pungcheon Cult Leader. Lord Jinha took care of Gwakju, who knew no martial arts.

“Now, when a wave comes, you must ride it.”

*Whoosh!*

The wind and waves created by the Pungcheon Cult Leader pushed everyone standing on the planks into the surging water.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader took the lead and headed toward the incoming wave. His excited laughter rang out thunderously.

*Swoooooosh.*

Everyone following behind saw it. The sight of the Pungcheon Cult Leader climbing the massive wave diagonally, cutting through it.

The Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable, Lord Jinha, and the Demon Annihilation Squad warriors stood on their planks and rode the wave together. The wind raised by the Pungcheon Cult Leader pushed them vigorously from behind.

They had never imagined they would be riding waves inside the world of the Soul-Devouring Art.

Rather than feeling fear, Lord Jinha’s heart pounded. Gwakju, clinging tightly to him, was certain this would be a moment he would never forget. Though of course, no one would believe it.

*Fwaaaah.*

And everyone saw. The Pungcheon Cult Leader climbing to the crest of the great wave.

The sight of him kicking off that crest and soaring into the sky together with Jinhyu.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s red energy and Jinhyu’s blue energy clashed in mid-air. It looked as if two immortals were facing off in the heavens.

*Kwaaang!*

Two colors spreading across the clear sky like ink on paper—that red light and blue light adorned the heavens.

The moment of their first clash, this was the thought that crossed Jinhyu’s mind.

*‘He’s strong!’*

Those frivolous words of his might have made him seem weak. But with just a single exchange, he could tell. That he was astonishingly strong.

As the two clashed in mid-air, the waves subsided like a lie.

Everyone stood on their wooden planks and watched their battle. Especially the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable, who didn't miss a single movement of her master.

*Swoooosh!*

Jinhyu’s body instantly split apart and created clones. They instantly surrounded the Pungcheon Cult Leader in a circle.

True Soul Clone Technique.

They weren't just clones that looked the same. They were clones that actually attacked with one-third of Jinhyu’s power. Truly one of Jinhyu’s secret techniques.

But they couldn't attack anyone.

*Slash, slash, slash, slash!*

Jinhyu’s split bodies were cut into dozens of pieces.

Dozens of lines were already spread across the air.

Formless blades created by the Pungcheon Cult Leader were spread all around like spiderwebs.

Liquid flowed as the clones were cut by those lines.

Among it was real blood.

Jinhyu, looking at the Pungcheon Cult Leader with a shocked face while leaning his head far back, had blood pooling on his shoulder. If he had raised his protective qi barrier even a moment later, if he had leaned his head back even a moment later, it wouldn't have been his shoulder but his neck that would have been severed.

“Formless Qi?”

It was the Pungcheon Cult Leader's martial art that used invisible qi as weapons.

The Formless Qi he had demonstrated when fighting the Phantom King in the past was being displayed again.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s cold gaze looking at him.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader who had spoken below and the one now were completely different people.

The next moment.

*Whoooom.*

The surrounding space began to spin and distort.

Those standing on the sea felt dizzy and fell from their planks. It was a dizziness that couldn't be blocked by mental strength.

But they didn't fall into the water.

Before they knew it, they were in a new space.

Looking around, it was a space filled with mirrors on all sides.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s figure was reflected in those mirrors.

A mirror floated in front of the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader stared blankly at that mirror, which emitted a strange energy.

It was the manifestation of the Soul Demon Mirror, Jinhyu’s unique martial art.

The ultimate martial art that dominates the opponent.

The moment one falls into the Soul Demon Mirror, they unconditionally follow the opponent's commands.

The Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable, judging the situation to be serious, tried to find her master immediately, but all sides were filled with mirrors. The Demon Annihilation Squad warriors appeared and disappeared in the mirrors, and she couldn't tell where her master was. This place was a labyrinth of mirrors.

‘......Master!’

Now Jinhyu’s figure was visible in the mirrors.

“The deep darkness in your heart shall lead you to a new world; now, obey my command!”

Jinhyu’s incantation echoed there. Jinhyu wore a satisfied expression.

“Kill everyone here and then kill yourself!”

At that command, the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable, Lord Jinha, and the Demon Annihilation Squad warriors were all shocked. If the Pungcheon Cult Leader followed his words? No one here would survive.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, having received the command, slowly opened his mouth. Normally he should have spoken while looking at Jinhyu, but his gaze was still directed at the mirror.

“When did I grow so many white hairs? It's all because of the Little Cult Leader.”

At that moment, Jinhyu was shocked and his eyes widened. His expression contorted.

*‘It didn't work even though he looked at the Soul Demon Mirror?’*

He couldn't believe it.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader beyond the mirror glanced at Jinhyu.

At that moment, Jinhyu felt as though he were standing before a massive cliff.

He had thought it was a fight worth trying against the Pungcheon Cult Leader, but the actual Pungcheon Cult Leader was far stronger than he had imagined.

*‘I can't win like this!’*

Only one method remained.

The next moment!

*Sssssss.*

Cracks began to form on the mirrors in all directions, and the surroundings began to change.

And they were all standing in that great hall from the beginning.

Now Jinhyu’s eyes changed. Where killing intent had filled, an indescribable grim resolve took hold.

“In the end, it comes to this.”

As Jinhyu chanted a spell, the surrounding objects began to move.

The frame hanging on the wall tilted, and the flowers blooming in the vase withered. The window opened on its own, and incense rose.

“The Mad Soul Art has activated.”

The Mad Soul Art was a technique that exhibited far greater power than the Heaven-Destroying Soul Art used earlier.

But there was a fatal problem. If he used the Mad Soul Art, he himself could not survive. Moreover, it wasn't just a simple death. He would die after experiencing truly horrific pain.

“Damn it! I haven't endured until now to end like this. Fuck!”

Jinhyu shouted as if wronged. But if the Soul Demon Mirror didn't work, there was no other way.

“Damned Little Cult Leader bastard!”

He thought this was all because of Geom Mugik. He was the one who had sent them here.

Then the Pungcheon Cult Leader thunderously shouted.

“Shut up! Someone like you is not fit to speak of him!”

At the thunderous roar, both the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable and Lord Jinha were inwardly surprised. This was the Pungcheon Cult Leader who always found fault with the Little Cult Leader.

Jinhyu raised all his power with the Mad Soul Art. He had intended to detonate all his strength in one blow to drag them down with him in mutual destruction.

Jin Hyu’s expression hardened. The Mad Soul Art had not taken effect.

He was shocked and flustered. He stirred up the energy of the Mad Soul again, but the Mad Soul Art did not activate at all.

Which meant?

“You broke the Mad Soul Art? How on earth?”

He had not interfered at all when Jin Hyu used the Mad Soul Art, had he?

Cult Leader Pungcheon slowly walked toward him.

“If it had been the old me, I would have made the same choice. Even if I were to die in excruciating pain, I would never have wanted to die alone. That’s what we are. Selfish things who think only of ourselves.”

“Shut up!”

Just as Jin Hyu tried to invoke the Mad Soul Art once more.

Gush.

Blood burst from his mouth.

Jin Hyu slowly looked down at his chest.

Something invisible was lodged in his chest, which had been punched clean through.

“The Formless Spear?”

He had been too agitated by the thought that the Mad Soul Art had been broken. To let his guard down like this in front of a master who could take his life if he so much as breathed wrong?

“……What happened?”

In response to Jin Hyu’s question, Cult Leader Pungcheon flicked his finger.

Snap!

Then their surroundings began to change.

Astonishingly, it was the space where the mirror had been.

The one most shocked was Jin Hyu, who was dying. This was the place he himself had opened earlier. He had not opened it again.

“Why is this place……?”

With an incomprehending expression, he looked at Cult Leader Pungcheon, who was silently gazing at him.

At that very moment, a thought flashed through his mind.

“Don’t tell me!”

Jin Hyu realized it. The great hall they had returned to earlier had not been his original space.

“That’s right. The place earlier was one I opened.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon had used the Space-Time Transposition Art to open a place identical to the great hall outside.

It looked exactly the same on the surface, but it was an entirely different space.

That was why, no matter how he moved the objects there, the Mad Soul Art had not taken effect.

And because the Mad Soul Art had not taken effect, his shock and confusion had kept him from giving his all in the final battle. If only he had kept his wits about him, he could at least have killed the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, whose inner force had run dry, or those bastards of the Demon-Extermination Squad.

Blood gushed and gurgled from Jin Hyu’s mouth. Even after suffering it himself, he could not believe it.

“……You noticed that the Mad Soul Art had been prepared, and even calculated this situation?”

“I told you beforehand, didn’t I? This is my world.”

“!”

Had he been planning this moment since then?

Only at the moment of his death did Jin Hyu realize that this was an opponent he could never defeat.

His dying eyes filled with anger and regret.

“……Some other life, my ass. You’ve lived your whole life inside an illusion!”

He wanted to insult Cult Leader Pungcheon, but it had no effect.

“That illusion was my world. It still is.”

Then he said something unexpected.

“Do you want to know why I became this strong?”

Cult Leader Pungcheon’s gaze turned not to Jin Hyu, but to the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. He passed his realization on to his disciple.

“Because I tried to live differently, my world within the Soul-Capturing Art became stronger.”

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s eyes trembled. It was the moment his master set a milestone on the path he himself had to walk.

By contrast, Jin Hyu’s eyes shook with rage. He was even angrier because he could feel that those words were sincere.

Even at the very end, this nonsense about another life. He did not want to hear that sort of thing in his dying moments. He truly hated it.

“……Bullshit to the end.”

No, this was bullshit.

Cult Leader Pungcheon forcibly opened Jin Hyu’s eyelids as they slowly fell shut. The last thing Jin Hyu saw and heard before his breath was cut off was this.

Cult Leader Pungcheon stroked his own belly fat and asked cheerfully.

“I should lose a bit of this belly fat, shouldn’t I?”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 663

Chapter 663: I’ll Have to Crash This Stage
------------------------------------------
When Jinhyu’s breath ceased, the world he had created began to vanish as well.

Sssssss.

The place that revealed itself was the main hall where they had first arrived.

Jinhyu’s corpse, collapsed on the floor, finally made it clear that everything was over.

‘At last, we’ve returned to our world.’

Relieved, Jin Hagun first checked on his injured comrades.

For subordinates with severe wounds, he fed them medicine for internal injuries and personally applied wound salve.

There were more than a few wounded, but fortunately, none had suffered fatal injuries.

Then, among the uninjured subordinates, he sent the one with the best lightness skill to his grandfather.

“Tell him we are safe!”

He had no intention of going to help his grandfather. If he went with his exhausted, injured body, he would only get in the way. Since Geom Mugeuk was with his grandfather, it would be enough to let him know that he was safe.

Great Master Hwasong expressed his apologies to Jin Hagun.

“I have no face to show you.”

No matter that Jinhyu, their former comrade, had been a villain and had caught them off guard, they had been far too helpless.

Jin Hagun did not reproach him. What mattered was what they did from now on. If one had fallen, one had to get back up.

“We must use this incident as an opportunity to further develop the Great Orthodox Soul-Returning Art. Master Hwa, please take charge of that task.”

Hwasong nodded with a face full of resolve.

Before leaving that place, Jin Hagun expressed his gratitude to the Cult Leader Pungcheon.

“Thanks to you, we survived. Thank you, truly.”

The warriors of the Demon-Slaying Unit also cupped their fists in unison and bowed.

“Thank you.”

Truly, if not for the Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign and the Cult Leader Pungcheon, every warrior of the Demon-Slaying Unit would have died here in this main hall.

He himself would have become a hostage used to threaten his grandfather. It was a situation he did not even want to imagine.

“I will never forget this grace you have shown us today.”

At that, the Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign turned the credit to Geom Mugeuk.

“Repay that grace to the Young Cult Leader.”

Jin Hagun smiled and nodded.

The gazes with which the two looked at each other were gentle. At least for today, they were friends and comrades who had broken through the gates of death together.

In particular, Jin Hagun could not forget what she had said to him. Those words she had spoken when he had felt endless helplessness within the world of soul-bewitching.

—Once it is fully grown, what one fears is a tiger, not a serpent.

Those words had been a great comfort to him. And they had made him form this resolve.

Let us meet again when I have become a true tiger.

Just then, the Cult Leader Pungcheon’s booming voice rang out from behind.

“Why repay the grace to the Young Cult Leader? You should repay us. Repay me!”

Jin Hagun turned toward him. He had not come to know the Cult Leader Pungcheon well through this incident, but at the very least, he could tell that the man was pleasant company.

“Someday, I will come visit you with a list of things the Young Cult Leader has taken from me.”

At Jin Hagun’s joke, the Cult Leader Pungcheon burst into loud laughter. He had not forgotten how, when they first met, Pungcheon had teasingly asked him what the Young Cult Leader had taken from him.

“Let’s hold a grand gathering of victims! And naturally, I’ll be the head of that gathering!”

The Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign, who had been listening, smiled as well. There would be quite a few people who belonged in that gathering.

Through this incident, she was sincerely impressed by her master.

There was a saying: when watching someone do something, if it seemed too easy, then that person was a true master.

Others might not know, but she alone could understand. Her master had made it look as though he had dealt with Jinhyu easily, but that was absolutely not the case.

It was only because he had used the other party’s psychology and possessed the insight to see through the surrounding circumstances and the heart that he had been able to kill him.

Soul-bewitching arts were martial arts that deceived the human heart. Today, her master had shown the essence of those very arts.

In this world, he was the head of the victims’ gathering; in that world, he was an absolute being. That was her master.

“Well then, I will see you next time.”

After parting with the two of them, Jin Hagun left the main hall and said to Gwak Ju,

“Now, let us go meet your sister.”

Gwak Yeong was currently at a safe house of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. First, he intended to meet Geom Muyang and hand Gwak Ju over to him.

Gwak Ju asked in a trembling voice,

“Why are you going this far for me?”

No matter how dangerous the situation, the warriors of the Demon-Slaying Unit had taken care of him.

Who was he, after all? He was merely the younger brother of an iron forge artisan. Merely an ordinary man who knew not a scrap of martial arts. And yet they had valued him more than their own lives.

Jin Hagun turned toward him. The gazes of all the Demon-Slaying Unit warriors around them turned to him.

“Our mission today was to save you. Even if everyone here had died and only one person remained, that one person would have saved you.”

Gwak Ju looked at the members of the Demon-Slaying Unit standing around him.

Ah, so these are the warriors of the orthodox faction.

In that dazzling, chaotic world, they had silently protected him and brought him back to this reality. People who would keep their promise no matter what.

Gwak Ju bowed deeply to all of them and expressed his gratitude.

After they left, only the Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign remained there.

The Cult Leader Pungcheon was looking up at the sky with his hands clasped behind his back. The Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign also looked up at the sky from a step behind him.

She was recalling what her master had said earlier.

The effort to live differently strengthened my world.

Then, the Cult Leader Pungcheon opened his mouth. When he had been looking at the sky, his face had been incomparably serious, but before she knew it, his expression was full of mischief.

“We should go meet the man who put us through all this trouble and hasn’t even shown his nose. The man who forgets about us until he needs us, and then summons us—we should meet him and say, ‘That cult leader from back then is here.’”

His words were like that, but his face was full of anticipation at the thought of meeting Geom Mugeuk. It had truly been a long time since they had last met.

“The Young Cult Leader must be fighting a difficult battle as well.”

When they first arrived and the Cult Leader Pungcheon had badmouthed the Young Cult Leader, Jin Hagun had said the same thing. Of course, the Cult Leader Pungcheon did not think so at all.

“As if he’d be in trouble. Your Young Cult Leader is a kite, a kite.”

The Cult Leader Pungcheon began walking first and added,

“A kite that flies higher the stronger the wind blows.”

* * *

Hwa Yulcheong, the Cheonae Geosa whose identity had been revealed, demanded that Murim Alliance Leader Jin Paecheon kill Geom Mugeuk.

If he wanted to save his grandson, he had to kill him.

But Jin Paecheon refused the proposal.

He said he had come here today as an audience member. At the very least, Geom Mugeuk would not turn this stage into a tragedy.

And when Hwa Yulcheong expressed his confidence that it would be a grave miscalculation if they were counting on the Poison King and the Demon Buddha to save them, Geom Mugeuk displayed even greater confidence.

“Do you think they really went to help my friend?”

In Geom Mugeuk’s words were confidence and certainty that Jin Hagun would be safe.

“Do not bluff, Young Cult Leader.”

Hwa Yulcheong’s composure was met by Geom Mugeuk’s composure in turn.

“Bluffing is something people do when they have nothing. By now, your remaining wing is probably falling off too.”

It was said with the certainty that So Jeongnak was already dead, and that the master of soul-bewitching arts was also being dealt with.

Hwa Yulcheong stared at Jin Paecheon. His target today was, from beginning to end, Jin Paecheon.

“The judgment is yours to make. Just do not regret it later.”

He knew. To Jin Paecheon, who had already lost his own flesh and blood, the safety of his grandchildren was the greatest weakness—one that could not help but shake him.

But he had overlooked one thing. Jin Paecheon’s trust in Geom Mugeuk was far greater than he had imagined.

Jin Paecheon answered firmly.

“My judgment has already been made.”

He had already decided to trust Geom Mugeuk.

When Jin Paecheon’s gaze turned to him, Geom Mugeuk answered with confidence.

“If the person I sent cannot save Hagun, then even if you and I were there, Alliance Leader, we would not be able to save him either.”

At those words, Jin Paecheon thought of one person.

“Did you perhaps send the Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. Relief came at the same time as unease.

Hwa Yulcheong stabbed at that unease.

“The whole murim knows the new Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign is still a greenhorn. The Young Cult Leader is only trying to make it look good—as if he attempted to save Hagun.”

How could Jin Paecheon not know about the young Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign? Compared to the previous Demon Sovereign, she was bound to fall short.

And yet the reason he showed no agitation was not because he trusted that young Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign.

“It will be all right, won’t it?”

“Of course.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s resolute answer, Jin Paecheon nodded.

Hwa Yulcheong sighed mockingly.

“Foolish! Truly foolish!”

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“The foolish one is you.”

When Hwa Yulcheong looked at him as if asking what he meant, an unexpected statement came out.

“You didn’t know I suffer from anxiety, did you?”

“Anxiety?”

The Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult had anxiety? Young Cult Leader and anxiety—two things that absolutely did not go together.

“This anxiety is the suffering that torments me, and at the same time, my greatest strength. It goes like this. Ah, what if I send only one person and something goes wrong? The opponent might be stronger, right? What if another fellow comes to help him? What if an unexpected variable appears? I’m the sort of person who worries about every possible thing like that. And the result of that worry is this.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to Jin Paecheon.

“That is why I sent the Cult Leader Pungcheon as well.”

The moment he heard those words, Jin Paecheon’s expression relaxed.

If it had been the Soul-Subduing Demon Sovereign alone, then yes, he honestly would have been uneasy. But if even the Cult Leader Pungcheon had gone?

Geom Mugeuk was right. If the opponent used soul-bewitching arts, those two were better suited than himself and Geom Mugeuk.

“Then it is enough.”

The thought that it truly was enough came naturally to him.

In contrast, Hwa Yulcheong’s expression hardened.

“Bluffing again.”

Hwa Yulcheong tried to persuade Jin Paecheon.

“The Young Cult Leader is lying right now to survive.”

Jin Paecheon looked at him quietly and said,

“You are the one who fears you might die.”

Displeasure flashed across Hwa Yulcheong’s face. Since he had already revealed his true identity, he no longer concealed his emotions as thoroughly as before.

Jin Paecheon stared at Hwa Yulcheong and continued speaking.

“Until now, I never once tried to read your emotions. Thinking about it now, that was arrogance.”

Was it because he was busy as Alliance Leader?

No. It was because he had never needed to.

He had lived a life in which he did not have to read other people’s moods. Everyone else had watched his.

He had lived a life in which it had taken him decades to finally realize that the Cheonae Geosa had never once gotten angry at him and had always accommodated his feelings.

“All of this is something I brought upon myself.”

But that did not mean he had any intention of forgiving the man who had lived behind that mask.

“The moment you schemed using my grandson’s life, every bond between you and me was already severed.”

There was no need for more words, but he was curious about one thing.

“Let me ask only this at the end. The chivalrous righteousness you practiced your whole life—what sort of heart did it come from? Was that too all a pretense?”

At Jin Paecheon’s question, a sneer formed at the corner of Hwa Yulcheong’s mouth.

“Why? Does your righteousness seem real, while mine seems fake? Be honest. There must have been times when you were conscious of me. Times when you disliked me, who was revered as a more respected chivalrous hero than you, the Alliance Leader. Am I wrong?”

Jin Paecheon could not deny it. There truly had been times like that. But those had been fleeting emotions. He had soon composed himself and treated his friend with goodwill.

Hwa Yulcheong dug into Jin Paecheon even more persistently.

“If you kill me, what will the people who survived thanks to my help think? Will they truly believe that I was a villain who concealed his identity for decades? Or will they think that you killed me out of jealousy? Will they respect and follow you as they did before?”

Hwa Yulcheong shook Jin Paecheon. Having watched Jin Paecheon from his side for so long, he knew better than anyone what words would become daggers.

Jin Paecheon silently nodded.

“Yes, they may look at me with different feelings.”

Jin Paecheon somehow seemed detached. But that was not because Hwa Yulcheong had shaken him.

“Even so, it is all right. My era is now drawing to a close.”

The moment he heard those words, Geom Mugeuk understood. Jin Paecheon was finally intending to establish Jin Hagun as his successor.

Perhaps Jin Hagun, among the three of Majeongsa, would be the latest to become a successor, yet the first to become the Murim Alliance Leader.

“Yes, I acknowledge your righteousness. You lived an admirable life.”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk cut in.

“I do not acknowledge it.”

How dare you try to shake the Alliance Leader with that pathetic glib tongue of yours? If it is a glib tongue you use, then I will answer with a glib tongue!

“You seem to want to insist that your chivalry was real, but it was fake, wasn’t it? For you, the more chivalrous deeds you accumulated, the more trust you built with the Alliance Leader—it was a kind of mission. You’d be better off claiming you’re a loyal and diligent servant of the Dark Palace. I’ll acknowledge that much.”

Hwa Yulcheong’s eyes twitched at the words that struck him dead on. He had lived thinking exactly that.

“If that chivalry had truly been real, then you should have stopped So Jeongnak, who protected you, from killing innocent people with poison art experiments. You should have stopped him from harming people with Soul-Bewitching Art and forcing them to take their own lives in the end. If you wanted to say that at least that chivalry was real, then you should have targeted only the Alliance Leader by yourself, without such subordinates.”

Hwa Yulcheong could not refute Geom Mugeuk’s continuing words.

In truth, these words were not being said to Hwa Yulcheong, but to Jin Paecheon. He was telling him there was no need to suffer from being stabbed by a poisoned dagger drawn from a sheath engraved with chivalry.

“What? You say even fake chivalry accomplished something? That it was better than nothing? If not for you, someone better would have been there to perform chivalrous deeds in your place. A true knight-errant, one without subordinates who used poison and Soul-Bewitching Art. So do not speak of chivalry to the Alliance Leader with that vile mouth of yours.”

A faint smile formed at the corners of Jin Paecheon’s lips. How could he not know? Those words had been spoken for his sake.

Hwa Yulcheong, on the other hand, glared at Geom Mugeuk with terrifying eyes. So many words had been spoken, yet he had not been able to refute even a single one.

The emotion contained in his gaze was a dark energy deeper than killing intent.

“Yes, this is who you are, isn’t it?”

Hwa Yulcheong revealed himself.

The aura of Hwa Yulcheong, now exposing his true presence, was incomparable to before.

An absolute master of the Dark Palace, who had cultivated his power in darkness for the past three hundred years.

Then Jin Paecheon spoke in a calm voice.

“Though I came as a spectator, it seems I must intrude upon this stage. Would that be all right?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and replied.

“From the beginning, this stage was yours, Alliance Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk took one step back, and Jin Paecheon stepped forward.

“Ah, and I will be the one to lower the curtain on this stage.”

Then, looking down at his own sword, he said something unexpected.

“After becoming the Murim Alliance Leader, I could not display my true swordsmanship. An Alliance Leader could hardly wield such rough and violent swordsmanship without dignity.”

So it seemed the aura he revealed would be even more violent, but instead, it was still.

It was not the sea swept by fierce winds so powerful one could not even open one’s eyes, like his usual presence.

The sea within the darkness was utterly still.

And that made it all the more frightening. Because there was no telling what would happen in that sea from now on.

The dark energy flowing from Hwa Yulcheong’s body grew even stronger. Under its influence, the bamboo around them began to wither.

“I have no intention of sending the likes of you into Ha Gun’s era.”

Now calling Hwa Yulcheong “the likes of you,” Jin Paecheon properly stepped onto the stage and slowly drew his sword.

“I will have you exit together with the closing of the curtain on my era.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 664

Chapter 664: I Can Hear Everything
----------------------------------
A rough, domineering aura flowed from Jin Paecheon’s body.

From far across the dark sea came a quiet cry.

Jin Paecheon, the Murim Alliance Leader, found an old longing within that darkness. The image of himself from those days when he had fought like a madman without caring what anyone thought.

The self from those days when he had not yielded the first move for the sake of appearances, nor shown mercy to enemies he wished to kill.

Yes, he too had once had such a time. A time when he lived freely, heedless of others, if only for a very brief while.

Watching him, Geom Mugeuk felt that today, he would be able to see the man’s true face.

‘Show them clearly. Show them what kind of man you are, Alliance Leader.’

In truth, even Geom Mugeuk was seeing this side of him for the first time. In his life before regression, he had never had the chance to witness Jin Paecheon’s martial arts.

As Hwa Yulcheong drew his sword, dark energy swirled around his body. That energy coiled around him like snakes.

As if displaying the essence of the Dark Palace, the energy was sinister, giving the sensation of a frozen hand gripping one’s heart tight.

Hwa Yulcheong corrected the words Jin Paecheon had spoken earlier.

“You are not the one who will bring this era to an end.”

The dark energy flowing from him encroached upon the surroundings and spread even more thickly.

From this ominous aura alone, one could sense how strong he was.

Even in the face of that terrifying momentum, Jin Paecheon was not intimidated in the least. On the contrary, a faint smile formed at the corners of his lips.

It was not the joy of an Alliance Leader, but of a single martial artist standing before a battle against a powerful foe.

Watching, Geom Mugeuk wondered.

Just what role did Hwa Yulcheong play within the Dark Palace?

A master of that caliber would surely know of Hwa Mugi’s existence.

The person he had just said would bring this era to an end might very well refer to Hwa Mugi.

But as he had done until now, Geom Mugeuk thought of Hwa Mugi as though he did not exist. Although so much had changed after his regression, he hoped that at least the timing of Hwa Mugi’s appearance would not change.

Because he wanted him to appear only when all his preparations were complete.

And so, he sincerely hoped that while playing baduk with his father, or reading a book with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, he would be able to say, I’ll be back in a moment, and go kill Hwa Mugi.

So that if someone asked where he had gone, he could say, I went to take care of something for a moment. It’s my turn, isn’t it?

Just then, a drop of water fell with a plop onto Geom Mugeuk’s head.

A drizzle began to fall in the misty bamboo forest.

With that rain, the curtain rose on the stage.

The very instant the rain struck the ground—

Jin Paecheon vanished from where he stood.

There was no sound, nor any sign of him leaping.

The moment Hwa Yulcheong looked up—

Jin Paecheon was already above his head, bringing his sword down. It was as if the man who had been standing over there had been torn away and pasted above his head. That was how fast Jin Paecheon’s lightness art was.

A sword descended as swiftly as light.

Swaeeeeek!

Kaaang!

Light crossed with light. If Jin Paecheon was light, then Hwa Yulcheong was also light. Hwa Yulcheong struck away Jin Paecheon’s sword with his own.

“The Alliance Leader resorting to a surprise att—”

He had tried to mock him for launching a sneak attack and shake his mind, but Hwa Yulcheong was given no such chance.

Kaaang! Kaaang!

Jin Paecheon’s fierce attacks continued.

He was the Murim Alliance Leader who had not yielded the first move. Now, there was no more empty formality. He was a man prepared to throw dirt into his opponent’s eyes if it meant surviving.

In other words, his shackles had been undone.

Shwik! Shwik!

Jin Paecheon’s attacks, thrusting toward vital points, were strong and fast. As a rule, strength meant slowness, and to gain speed, one had to give up power, yet Jin Paecheon possessed both. He was fast and strong.

And Hwa Yulcheong received every one of those powerful attacks.

Had it been the sword of an ordinary master, it would have snapped long ago. But Hwa Yulcheong’s sword did not break. A powerful dark energy was protecting the blade.

Watching that sight, Geom Mugeuk realized one important fact.

‘That isn’t the sword art Hwa Mugi used.’

He had died before he could properly cross hands with Hwa Mugi, but he remembered one thing clearly.

Hwa Mugi had definitely not used a sword art that emitted this kind of dark energy.

‘Hwa Mugi used a different martial art.’

Yes. No matter how powerful this martial art appeared, it could not have defeated Father’s Guhwa Demon Art.

By watching this fight, Geom Mugeuk had discovered something extremely important.

The two men’s swords flew savagely toward each other, and in both of their blades was contained the resentment they had accumulated over decades.

Chaeeng! Chaeng! Chaeeng! Chaeng chaeng!

The striking sounds produced as the two swords clashed so swiftly they could not be seen sounded like an ensemble of musicians. They did not speed up and slow down. The instruments continued to accelerate, racing toward the climax.

Sword lines embroidered the air and vanished.

Those lines looked like a painting drawn by a master artist. Countless lines that ordinary people could not recognize appeared and disappeared, but there was someone who understood the value of this painting.

To Geom Mugeuk, they were beautiful lines. A beautiful painting containing the lifelong martial learning of two great masters.

To be able to watch such a duel between masters from this close was a stroke of fortune worthy of being called a true fortuitous encounter.

With awe, Geom Mugeuk watched Jin Paecheon’s martial arts.

The Alliance Leader could have told him to step aside so that his martial arts would not be revealed. Had he done so, Geom Mugeuk would have withdrawn far away.

But Jin Paecheon did not. He was showing all of his martial arts. To Geom Mugeuk, who might someday become his enemy.

This was the vessel of Jin Paecheon, the Murim Alliance Leader. It was an enormous vessel, large enough for even the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult to submerge his head in completely.

Thank you, Alliance Leader.

Repaying that sentiment did not mean deliberately refusing to watch. He engraved the battle between the two men into his heart more intently than any fight he had ever seen.

Before the fight began, Geom Mugeuk had considered intervening.

What if Jin Paecheon fell into danger?

He had intended to leap in without hesitation. Being resented by Jin Paecheon later was a matter for later. He had absolutely no intention of letting him die in this battle.

But once the fight began, that thought changed. It had no choice but to change.

Could he really interfere in that fight?

The battle between the two men showed no gap at all, enough for that thought to arise naturally. The moment a gap appeared, this fight would end before there was even room to step in. That was how extraordinary the skills of the two men were.

When the performance of clashing swords reached its peak—

Papapapak!

Blood spurted from Hwa Yulcheong’s arm and side.

At a moment like this, one might have enjoyed the advantage and thrown out a line of dialogue.

But like a wild beast absorbed in the hunt, Jin Paecheon pressed his opponent even more fiercely. Watching him, one could read his heart.

It did not have to be a magnificent technique. So long as it could kill the opponent.

Seeing Jin Paecheon like that, Geom Mugeuk wished he could show this fight to Ian, Seo Daeryong, and his other subordinates if only he could.

Look. Even the Murim Alliance Leader over there is fighting desperately without letting his guard down for a single instant—who do you think you are!

Of course, that “you” included himself as well.

Hwa Yulcheong desperately widened the distance.

But that was something Jin Paecheon had deliberately allowed.

Papapapapapapak!

Dozens of sword qi pierced through the ground and surged upward.

Even Geom Mugeuk had never seen it before. A martial art where sword qi came bursting up through the ground!

Jin Paecheon’s exclusive martial art, the Emperor Sword Form, had been unleashed.

Emperor Sword Form, Fourth Form: Heaven and Earth Creation.

Dozens of sword qi pierced through the earth and surged upward.

After becoming the Murim Alliance Leader, he had never used anything beyond the Third Form.

From the Fourth Form onward, the techniques were solely for killing, and so he had never had any reason to reveal this domineering martial art.

Hwa Yulcheong flew into the air to avoid the sword qi. The only place to evade was above.

At that very moment, the Fifth Form, linked to the Fourth, was unleashed.

Emperor Sword Form, Fifth Form: Flying Sword Wild Dance.

Dozens of sword qi poured toward him as he was exposed in midair. It was like dozens of arrows flying at once, but the speed was different.

As if they were pouring out from a mechanism, they were truly as fast as light.

Shwik shwik shwik shwik shwik shwik shwik shwik shwik shwik!

He could not avoid the attacks flying from below and from the front. Knowing he could not block them all, Hwa Yulcheong raised his protective astral qi to its limit and swung his sword as much as he could.

Peopeopeopeong!

The sword qi he failed to block with his sword struck his body.

Hwa Yulcheong was sent flying and crashed into the ground.

But Jin Paecheon did not stop attacking.

Shiiiiiiik.

A single strand of sword qi skimmed along the ground, shooting forward as though alive.

It was an attack meant to split the fallen man in half.

The instant the sword qi swept through the spot where Hwa Yulcheong had been, he vanished from sight.

Geom Mugeuk thought he had used the dark energy to evade somewhere, but in the blink of an eye, he crossed through space and reappeared.

Directly behind Jin Paecheon.

Paaat!

Blood spurted from Jin Paecheon’s shoulder. If he had not twisted his body on instinct to evade, the attack would have severed his arm.

Watching, Geom Mugeuk was startled.

It was not because Jin Paecheon had been cut by the sword, nor because Hwa Yulcheong’s movement had been lightning fast. Nor was it because the attack that cut Jin Paecheon’s shoulder had been a truly swift sword.

‘I didn’t hear a thing!’

At the moment Hwa Yulcheong attacked Jin Paecheon, Geom Mugeuk had heard nothing.

Not the sound of Hwa Yulcheong moving, nor the sound of the sword slicing through the air.

No matter how swift a sword was, there should have been sound for an instant.

It was as if in his world, no sound could be heard at all.

That was also why Jin Paecheon had allowed the attack to land.

A soundless sword.

Hwa Yulcheong’s exclusive martial art, the Dark Heaven Soundless Sword Art, had been unleashed.

Geom Mugeuk knew better than anyone just how difficult this attack was to block.

Because no sound could be heard, one could not rely on hearing.

The opponent was using a swift sword, yet it had to be observed only with the eyes.

Masters on the level of Jin Paecheon and Hwa Yulcheong fought not only with sight, but with every sense. Naturally, hearing was the most important sense after sight.

Especially when the opponent used a swift sword, hearing was particularly important.

But no sound could be heard?

And the most decisive reason this attack was troublesome was this.

Because there was no sound, one could not gauge how powerful the incoming attack was.

Then in the end, one had to assume that every attack flying in was the strongest possible and block accordingly. Naturally, the consumption of internal energy would be severe.

Geom Mugeuk could tell.

‘So this is the martial art he prepared for the day he would fight the Alliance Leader!’

Hwa Yulcheong laughed coldly and said,

“I will lower the curtain on your era.”

The instant his words ended—

Kaaang!

The second attack was blocked. Yes, someone on the level of the Alliance Leader could instinctively block it once.

Chaeeng!

But the third attack was blocked as well.

For an instant, shock flashed across Hwa Yulcheong’s face.

Then Jin Paecheon spoke words that were astonishing.

“I can hear it all.”

Not only Hwa Yulcheong, but Geom Mugeuk as well was shocked.

Because Geom Mugeuk’s ears could not hear it.

Hwa Yulcheong did not believe him.

“You block a few times by luck and now you bluff.”

The moment Hwa Yulcheong flashed away, he thrust from another place toward another point. It was impossible to block. It was a martial art that moved without revealing not only sound, but any energy at all. It was a martial art he had honed solely for this day.

Kaaang!

But Jin Paecheon blocked even the next attack.

At that very moment—

A gigantic sword qi silently plunged down from above Jin Paecheon’s head.

Kwaaaang!

Not only the attacks swung with the sword, but this sword qi attack as well made no sound whatsoever.

When the rising smoke settled, Jin Paecheon was standing in that place.

Beside him, having narrowly avoided the attack, a deep hole had been gouged into the ground.

Had he been struck directly, it would have been powerful enough to inflict a grave wound.

But Jin Paecheon had evaded even that attack.

“You can really hear it?”

Startled, he hid within the darkness.

In an instant, the surroundings grew dark. It was as if, in a place without light, the only lamp had been blown out.

Within the pitch-black darkness.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. Hwa Yulcheong had unleashed a martial art using the energy of darkness.

Both Jin Paecheon and Geom Mugeuk stood within the darkness.

From within the dark, Hwa Yulcheong’s voice came.

“…You truly are remarkable.”

His voice, filled with shock and emptiness, was calm. At the very least, he seemed to believe this darkness would protect him.

“Jin Paecheon.”

For the first time, he called Jin Paecheon by name.

Jin Paecheon found this moment, in which his name was spoken, truly unfamiliar. Since becoming the Alliance Leader, who would have dared call him by his name?

Come to think of it, he realized that even during all those long years they had spent as friends, Hwa Yulcheong had never once called him by name.

“I hated you.”

For the first time, he revealed his honest feelings. The emotion he showed for the first time in decades was hatred.

“I truly hated the way you acted so hypocritically, claiming it was for the righteous faction martial artists. Most of what you did were things you would never have done if you weren’t the Murim Alliance Leader, weren’t they?”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. He was trying, by any means possible, to shake Jin Paecheon’s heart. He could tell that this darkness had the effect of drawing out heart demons. The Heavenly Demon Protective Art had already begun to activate, guarding him.

Could the Alliance Leader withstand this dark energy?

Jin Paecheon did not get drawn into his provocation.

Something began to shine within the darkness.

It was Jin Paecheon’s sword.

Swaaaaaa.

The sword shone, holding a radiant light, and that light illuminated Jin Paecheon’s face. His resolute expression remained unchanged.

The moment Jin Paecheon’s sword cut through the air—

Chwaaaaaak!

The curtain of darkness was sliced away by his blade. As though a black cloth had been torn apart, the darkness split, and light entered. Walking out through the torn darkness and into the light, Jin Paecheon said,

“Now you’ll come to hate me even more.”

Absolute Regression Chapter 665

Chapter 665: Do Not Dwell on Who It Is
--------------------------------------
This was no longer a matter of whether he hated Jin Paecheon more or less.

Because the curtain of darkness being torn apart before Hwa Yulcheong’s eyes was something that absolutely must not be torn.

Amcheon Pasimsul (暗天破心術).

Once trapped within it, one would wander through a darkness without an exit, eventually falling prey to inner demons and exposing a weakness. It was a secret art.

Fine, even if he conceded a hundred times over and admitted that Jin Paecheon could break it, he should not have been able to rip his way out this easily.

Shwaaaaaaak.

Jin Paecheon emerged from the torn darkness and asked,

“Why are you standing in the light?”

It meant: rather than trapping me in darkness, shouldn’t you be the one hiding in it?

Once Jin Paecheon had made up his mind to kill his opponent, he focused only on that.

Yes, this had always been his nature. If he killed, he killed without fail; if he spared, he spared without fail. It was because he had been such a man that he had risen to the position of Alliance Leader.

At this moment, Jin Paecheon felt light. It was as if he had found the self he had forgotten, and he felt a strange pleasure from this long-awaited deviation and freedom.

Sssssssss.

The place he had torn through gradually spread with light, and the darkness that had descended vanished completely.

Then Geom Mugeuk, who had been in the darkness, also revealed himself.

Hwa Yulcheong asked in a trembling voice,

“How did you get out?”

Then one martial art name came from Jin Paecheon.

“Ilsim Gwanggeom (一心光劍).”

At those words, Hwa Yulcheong’s face filled with shock.

“You mean you achieved the great completion of Jeongsimgyeol (正心訣)?”

Jeongsimgyeol was the Murim Alliance’s protective mental art that warded off evil energy. If the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had the Cheonma Hosingong, then the Murim Alliance had Jeongsimgyeol.

Jeongsimgyeol was an oral formula passed down only to the Murim Alliance Leader, and it was known to be extremely difficult to achieve great completion in it. Even among past Alliance Leaders, those who had done so could be counted on one hand.

Ilsim Gwanggeom could only be used upon attaining the great completion of that Jeongsimgyeol. Jin Paecheon was far stronger than Hwa Yulcheong had known.

“You never once told me you had achieved the great completion of Jeongsimgyeol, did you?”

At once, Hwa Yulcheong’s gaze turned vicious.

“You hid your strength.”

Over the years, while remaining friends with Jin Paecheon, he had thought he had investigated much about him.

But he had been wrong about the most important part. Jin Paecheon had hidden far too much of his strength.

Jin Paecheon answered calmly.

“Surely not as much as you.”

Thinking about it, it was not as though he had deliberately hidden his strength. It was only natural for a martial artist not to reveal all of his martial prowess, and because he was not the type to display and boast of his martial arts, Hwa Yulcheong simply had not known of his achievements.

Perhaps the fact that they had known each other for so many years had instead hindered him from truly knowing Jin Paecheon.

“Besides, you still have more hidden away, don’t you?”

Jin Paecheon’s question was not merely a guess.

Even though the Amcheon Museong Sword Art had failed, and even the Amcheon Pasimsul had failed, Hwa Yulcheong had not attempted to flee. He clearly had one move hidden up his sleeve.

“Hoo, how frustrating.”

As if he truly was frustrated, Hwa Yulcheong lightly launched himself into the air.

He landed atop a small leaf at the tip of a tall bamboo.

Jin Paecheon also lightly launched himself and landed on a leaf some distance away from him.

Geom Mugeuk climbed onto a bamboo stalk far away from the two of them.

Above was the sky, below was the bamboo forest, and all around, the falling rain.

It was truly a magnificent stage.

When Geom Mugeuk looked up at the sky, it seemed the rain would soon stop. By the time the rain ceased, this fight would probably be over as well.

“You cannot stop our revenge. No one can.”

Hwa Yulcheong had certainty in the path he walked.

“You understand, don’t you? What this emotion is.”

When Hwa Yulcheong once again dug into Jin Paecheon’s pain, Geom Mugeuk stepped forward.

“You’re different.”

Hwa Yulcheong’s cold gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“Your revenge is a misguided revenge. Ah, I suppose it isn’t your fault. It would be the fault of the person who raised you all as incarnations of revenge.”

Hwa Yulcheong’s expression hardened. Those words were even more unpleasant. They meant that he was not the subject of his own will, but someone manipulated by another’s hand.

“Did you ask the Alliance Leader whether he understands your revenge? How could he? The Alliance Leader is not the sort of man who would place a sword in his grandson’s hand and tell him to continue revenge through the generations. From that point on, you people were wrong.”

Hwa Yulcheong shouted furiously,

“What would the likes of you know about revenge!”

What did I know about revenge? If he knew what efforts I had made to take revenge on Hwa Mugi, he would not be able to say such a thing.

But now, my mind had changed.

Though it had begun with nothing but the desire for revenge, in this life, revenge was only a part of life.

Was it said that the finest revenge against someone was to live happily and well?

Geom Mugeuk said with a smile,

“You’re right. I don’t know much.”

At that moment, Jin Paecheon saw it. Geom Mugeuk’s two eyes were shining more brightly than ever. For some reason, they were deep eyes that seemed to understand the emotion called revenge better than anyone.

Those shining eyes began to pierce through Hwa Yulcheong’s intentions.

“Why don’t you stop talking about revenge now? That isn’t real either, is it?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s questioning, which sounded like an interrogation, Hwa Yulcheong flinched in surprise.

“This affair isn’t because of revenge, is it?”

Geom Mugeuk knew well what kind of lives the forces behind them had lived before his regression.

They had become the Twelve Zodiac Kings and ruled the murim. There had never been a single announcement saying that they had killed the three leaders of the Demonic, Orthodox, and Evil factions for the sake of revenge.

“At first, perhaps it began because of revenge. But at some point, it must have changed. If that heart had not changed despite being passed down for three hundred long years, that would be the stranger thing.”

Geom Mugeuk gazed into Hwa Yulcheong’s eyes and said,

“So say it proudly. Say that you want to take this murim. Because it was that kind of dream, you were able to hide beside the Alliance Leader for so long.”

From Hwa Yulcheong’s reaction, Jin Paecheon could tell. Geom Mugeuk’s words were true. Their goal was the unification of murim.

In a grave voice, Jin Paecheon said to Hwa Yulcheong,

“Now there is nothing left for me to understand about you.”

Jin Paecheon launched himself toward him.

Chang! Chang! Chaaang!

The two clashed as they raced together across the bamboo.

Jin Paecheon’s fighting changed.

Until just a moment ago, he had been incomparably rough and domineering, but now he had returned to his usual calm, dignified sword.

“Why has your way of fighting changed?”

At Hwa Yulcheong’s question, Jin Paecheon said calmly,

“Earlier, I faced you with personal feelings.”

As an old friend who had betrayed him. As one who sought revenge by using his grandson’s life.

But now, it was different.

“If you people dream of unifying murim, then as the Murim Alliance Leader, I must naturally face you as such.”

Now, sword lines that were not rough at all, but perfectly refined, embroidered the air. The style had changed.
