He did not merely press forward; when it was time to retreat, he retreated decisively. The rhythm of the performance shifted, growing faster and slower in turn.

So had Jin Paecheon become weaker?

No. On the contrary, compared to when he had been pressing forward savagely, Jin Paecheon’s swordsmanship had become even harder to face. It was sturdier and left no openings.

Hwa Yulcheong was certain that by normal methods, he could never defeat him.

Chaaang!

Hwa Yulcheong roughly knocked aside an attack and descended to the ground as if fleeing. Jin Paecheon landed before him with footwork that was neither fast nor slow.

The trump card Hwa Yulcheong had hidden away was not martial arts.

“Do you not want to know?”

It was truly something no one could have expected him to say.

“Who killed your son.”

In that instant, Jin Paecheon’s expression stiffened. The most shocking words since they had met had flowed out.

When Geom Mugeuk had killed the Wood King last time, he had heard about that affair from the Alliance Leader.

Jin Paecheon’s son and daughter-in-law had been killed by the Mokcheon Clan.

When Jin Paecheon rushed to the Mokcheon Clan to take revenge, the place had already been exterminated. At that time, Jin Paecheon had said he had thought it truly fortunate. If not, he might have exterminated them himself.

Afterward, Baek Cheongyeong, the sole survivor of the Mokcheon Clan and the younger brother of the clan head, became Jin Hagun’s master and dreamed of revenge.

Back then, Geom Mugeuk had been suspicious. He had thought that those bastards had used Baek Cheongyeong. Perhaps the deaths of the Alliance Leader’s son and daughter-in-law were also connected to them.

And now, that suspicion had appeared as reality.

Hwa Yulcheong was certain that this truth, at least, was something Jin Paecheon could never ignore. At least when dealing with Jin Paecheon, this was the strongest weapon.

“Do you truly think it was the Mokcheon Clan’s doing?”

Embedded in this question was already the implication that the Mokcheon Clan had not been the culprit.

“I alone know who it was.”

Hwa Yulcheong savored this moment. He had imagined that such a day would come someday. How shocked would Jin Paecheon be? This moment, when he could toy with him while looking at that startled face.

He would never be able to kill him.

“If you do not let me go, that matter will be buried in secrecy forever.”

Jin Paecheon’s heart pounded violently. Other matters were one thing, but when it came to something related to his son’s death, he could not easily calm his mind.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk stepped forward.

“It does not matter if you do not know.”

Geom Mugeuk was the only person at that moment who could slow Jin Paecheon’s heartbeat.

“If you are swayed by words that may or may not be true, Alliance Leader, you will spend the rest of your life with your feet bound by shackles in the name of revenge. That is what this man wants.”

He was not merely telling him to endure for the sake of the greater cause. Geom Mugeuk had a solution.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze toward Hwa Yulcheong was colder than anything.

“It does not matter if we do not know who killed them.”

There was a clear reason he could say such a thing.

“After all, we’re going to kill all of you. If there is someone, it will be one among them.”

“!”

Hwa Yulcheong’s eyes wavered.

‘This man is speaking in earnest.’

And he felt that Jin Paecheon was being shaken by his words.

Hwa Yulcheong shook not Geom Mugeuk, but Jin Paecheon.

“That man is an outsider. How could he understand your grief? How could he understand that boiling paternal love?”

But Geom Mugeuk had an even more powerful blow than that.

“Do not become obsessed with who it was. If you attach meaning to that, the person who will inherit those shackles will be Hagun.”

“!”

If he could not find the culprit, then in the end, Hagun would live his life searching for the enemy who killed his parents.

It also meant that it would become no different from the revenge of the Dark Palace.

Those words were decisive.

Jin Paecheon nodded. It was not an easy decision, but Geom Mugeuk was right. Finding the culprit who had killed his son and daughter-in-law was important, but the grandchildren they had left behind were more important.

Once the curtain fell on his stage, there would never again be a performance with them. Hagun must never have to step onto this stage.

‘Yes, all of you will go with me.’

All of you, die on my stage.

“I will not dwell on the past.”

Because Hwa Yulcheong knew that once Jin Paecheon made a decision, he was a man who never looked back, he was truly furious.

Because of this damned Young Cult Leader, his plan to escape this place had gone awry. Even if he cut off that cursed neck, that hateful mouth would probably live on and keep talking.

Jin Paecheon spoke as if declaring it.

“I need not know!”

Looking into Jin Paecheon’s eyes, which did not waver even by an inch, Hwa Yulcheong shouted,

“If you thought the martial arts of the Dark Palace were only for darkness, you were gravely mistaken.”

The moment those words ended!

Hwaaaaak!

The entire world shone brightly with light. The light was so powerful that they could not open their eyes.

Gwangcheon Myeoran (光天滅眼).

It was the final secret art he had hidden away.

Within that blinding light, Hwa Yulcheong’s final attack flew in.

In that place where even sight was cut off, a soundless sword flew toward Jin Paecheon’s heart.

Chaaang!

His sword did not reach Jin Paecheon’s heart.

Jin Paecheon had struck aside the incoming blade.

Hwa Yulcheong was aghast. What shocked him was not that Jin Paecheon had blocked his attack.

Jin Paecheon had his eyes open within this Gwangcheon Myeoran.

“How on earth?”

The next moment!

Shiiiiing!

Along with a refreshing, crisp sound of wind that cooled the ears, sword lines flashed before his eyes.

Clack.

As Hwa Yulcheong stared blankly at Jin Paecheon retrieving his sword, he felt his chest grow hot.

He slowly lowered his head and looked down at his chest.

When he loosened his collar, he saw a character engraved on his chest in red lines.

Jeong (正).

It was the character for righteousness.

The first form of the Imperial Sword Forms, Muinjirro (武人之路).

The most basic and first sword form of the Imperial Sword Forms had been unleashed.

When Hwayulcheong slowly raised his head, Jin Paecheon spoke with a calm gaze.

“I did not win because I was strong.”

The belief that had upheld Jin Paecheon his entire life.

“We won because we were right.”

The moment those words ended—

Pshaaaaaa!

Blood spurted from the characters carved into Hwayulcheong’s chest.

His body slowly began to collapse.

The last thing he saw before his life was extinguished was Geom Mugeuk standing before Jin Paecheon, who had turned away from him, smiling brightly.

Geom Mugeuk did not look at him as he lay dying.

It was all because of that bastard that they had failed here in the Murim Alliance. And he wouldn’t even spare him a glance? You son of a bitch! Look at me! You should at least say something!

With that resentment, Hwayulcheong’s breath ceased completely.

It was the moment when the three people who had infiltrated the Murim Alliance for many long years—Hwayulcheong, So Jeongnak, and Jin Hyu—met their deaths.

Geom Mugeuk’s and Jin Paecheon’s gazes intertwined in the air.

“Thank you.”

At Jin Paecheon’s thanks, Geom Mugeuk politely cupped his fist and paid his respects.

“That is what I should be saying. Today, Alliance Leader, you showed me a new realm. It will be of great help to my advancement in martial arts.”

It was not a joke, but something said in earnest. The Alliance Leader’s fight had possessed dignity, and had properly shown the essence of the orthodox martial world.

At that, Jin Paecheon put on a deliberately troubled expression.

“It would be a problem if you kept growing stronger.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed loudly, and a smile appeared at the corners of Jin Paecheon’s mouth.

Just then, a guard from the Alliance Leader’s Hall came over and delivered a piece of news.

“We have received word that Captain Jin and the Demon Extermination Squad are all safe.”

Jin Paecheon was truly glad. He had believed Geom Mugeuk when he said that even the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable and Cult Leader Pungcheon had gone, but deep down, he had still been worried. Fortunately, they were safe.

Before long, Jin Paecheon steeled himself to a decision.

“Do you know why I did not pass the Imperial Sword Form on to Hagun?”

Jin Paecheon had not passed this powerful Imperial Sword Form on to Jin Hagun in advance.

That was why Jin Hagun had once taken the Wood King as his master and learned martial arts from him.

He had told him to become the successor fairly and by his own strength, without his grandfather’s martial arts, but in truth, there had been another reason he had not taught him the Imperial Sword Form.

“I was afraid the boy would become intoxicated by this powerful martial art and lose control of himself. I had intended to pass it on when he was older and more mature.”

Jin Paecheon himself had been that way. In his youth, trusting only in the strength of his martial arts, he had nearly caused several great incidents. Fortunately, heavenly luck had been with him, allowing him to reach this day.

“It seems the time has come to teach him now.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. Jin Hagun would now formally become the successor of the Murim Alliance.

“Hagun will be truly delighted. We friends should hold a banquet to celebrate.”

Geom Mugeuk rejoiced as though it were his own affair.

Jin Paecheon, gazing at that bright face, lifted his eyes to the sky. The falling rain had stopped before they knew it.

“Before you return to the Cult, stop by and see me.”

Chapter 666: Who Is That Person?
--------------------------------
The chief steward of Muso Manor, Ju Somyeong, was racing down the mountain road, fleeing Wuhan.

―If the Black Pearl breaks, it means I’m dead.

After Hwa Yulcheong had gone out to fight a great battle that would change his fate, the sacred Black Pearl had shattered.

He had never dreamed that the leader, Hwa Yulcheong, would be defeated. If his identity had been exposed, then it would only be a matter of time before the fact that Ju Somyeong had been his subordinate was revealed as well.

‘That damned young master of the Demon Cult!’

Everything was the fault of the young master of the Demon Cult.

When he had failed to sense the young master’s presence in the corridor of their residence, and when Hwa Yulcheong had told him that the young master knew about him—that had been his chance.

He should have urged Hwa Yulcheong to leave Wuhan first and return after the young master had departed. The problem was that he had trusted Hwa Yulcheong’s abilities too much.

‘I’ll kill him no matter what!’

Ju Somyeong vowed to avenge Hwa Yulcheong. He intended to strongly report to the organization that the young master of the Demon Cult had to be killed first.

Even as he ran along the deserted forest path, he kept watch on his surroundings.

Considering the recent circumstances, the Martial Alliance and the Heavenly Demon Divine Church must have spread their surveillance networks like spiderwebs.

If the two sides happened to exchange information, it would be impossible for anyone to escape Wuhan. He could only pray that such a situation had not come to pass.

As he ran, he suddenly stopped.

Ahead, he saw two people gathering herbs. They possessed appearances that immediately drew the eye.

The older one was extremely short, while the younger one was an exceptionally handsome boy with a pouch for herbs hanging from his waist.

Their appearances were so conspicuous that Ju Somyeong flinched, but they were definitely herb gatherers.

They paid him no attention whatsoever, gazing only at the herbs in wonder.

“To think we would find a Netherblood Herb in a place like this! How remarkable.”

“Wuhan truly seems to be a treasury of poisonous herbs.”

“It is not a treasury open to just anyone.”

“We were simply lucky. Come, dig it up.”

“Are you sure?”

Perhaps there was some pleasure in digging up herbs. When the short man yielded the task to him, the young man beamed with delight.

Watching them, Ju Somyeong veered to the side and darted into the forest. Their appearances were unusual, but they looked like genuine herb gatherers.

There was no need to confront them. If they had turned their heads and seen him, he would have killed them on the spot. They might have alerted those tracking him that he had escaped along this route.

But they were completely absorbed in gathering herbs.

‘Consider yourselves lucky that this saved your lives.’

He detoured around the path and was making his way out of the dense forest when—

He heard voices again.

“There’s another one here!”

“This time, it’s an Everlasting Flower!”

The Everlasting Flower was a poisonous herb as precious as the Netherblood Herb.

The two men were delighted, but Ju Somyeong’s expression stiffened completely.

He had clearly come from a different direction than they had. Had these skilled herb gatherers made their way here while cutting through the forest?

With a sense of foreboding, Ju Somyeong turned around and ran back the way he had come.

When he branched off onto a completely different path and continued running, a chill crept up his spine.

They were there again, ahead of him.

At this point, how could Ju Somyeong fail to understand what was happening?

‘Masters!’

They were true masters, people whose identities as martial artists could not be recognized simply by looking at them.

Of course, they were the Poison King and the Demon Buddha.

“Is this really necessary?”

In response to the Poison King’s question, the Demon Buddha looked at Ju Somyeong and answered,

“Wouldn’t it be all right to give him one chance, at least?”

“He looks rather stubborn.”

Ju Somyeong understood. Those words were referring to him.

There was no need to exchange blows to know the difference in martial arts.

‘In that case?’

Without hesitation, Ju Somyeong pulled something from inside his robe.

What he drew and pressed against his own head was a counterfeit hidden weapon called the Piercing Fang. It was the very same Piercing Fang that had been used when Hwa Yulcheong had pretended to be ambushed.

“There’s nothing you can learn from me.”

The hand holding the Piercing Fang trembled. Now that he realized he might actually die, he was afraid.

The Poison King and the Demon Buddha, on the other hand, merely stared at him blankly.

The first to speak was the Demon Buddha.

“That thing is going to hurt.”

The Demon Buddha gestured for him to aim a little lower.

“The bone there is the hardest. If it gets lodged instead of piercing through in one go, you’ll die after experiencing the pain of your skull melting down.”

The Poison King, who had been listening, added a word.

“The poison is cheap, too, so it’ll hurt even more.”

From their words, Ju Somyeong realized that his opponents were masters of the Demon Cult.

“You damned Demon Cult bastards! Do you think I’ll be frightened and obediently confess just because you threaten me with some bullshit like that? Take one step closer and I’ll pull the trigger!”

Silence descended for a moment.

The Demon Buddha and the Poison King no longer paid him any attention.

“Let’s take that ridge when we head down.”

The direction the Demon Buddha indicated was one they had yet to explore, and the Poison King’s face brightened.

“Sounds good.”

The two of them then left the area.

Left alone, Ju Somyeong was bewildered.

‘They’re letting me live? Really?’

But before long, he understood their intention.

‘These bastards! They’re planning to follow me and find out where our base is.’

It was a tactic that showed they knew far too little about him.

‘What idiots! Do they think I’ll obediently return just because I’ve fallen for such a shallow trick?’

They also knew far too little about the organization. Ju Somyeong himself did not know where it was, either. He merely left word so that he could submit reports.

But there was something he did not know.

He knew far too little about his opponents.

His feet would not move.

‘Why?’

Why wouldn’t his body move?

That was the last thought Ju Somyeong had while he was still alive.

Sluuurp.

In an instant, his body melted away.

He felt no pain, and not even a trace remained.

* * *

The carriage arrived at the residence where Geom Muyang had been staying.

Gwak Ju climbed down from the carriage with a flushed face. His heart pounded at the thought that he would finally be able to see his sister.

Jin Hagun did not enter. Instead, he sent a subordinate to announce his arrival.

A short while later, Geom Muyang came outside.

“Why don’t you come in?”

“My role ends here.”

Jin Hagun did not think that he had been the one to save Gwak Ju. Not because the Pungcheon Cult Leader and the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable had helped him.

“This man was saved by the Grand Young Master.”

Jin Hagun knew that it could not have been easy for Geom Muyang to entrust him with the task of saving Gwak Ju.

That proud man had lowered his pride and asked for his help because there was a greater chance of saving Gwak Ju if the Extermination Demon Unit took action.

That was likely the moment Gwak Ju had been saved.

It was something he would never have considered in the past. But he had changed after meeting Geom Mugeuk. As he tried to see people as they truly were, he had come to understand Geom Muyang a little more deeply as well.

He now knew that sometimes quietly enduring something could be more difficult than stepping forward to fight.

Geom Muyang examined Jin Hagun and the other martial artists of the Extermination Demon Unit. Their martial uniforms were soaked in blood, making it clear how hard they had fought.

What more needed to be said?

“Thank you. I will certainly repay this debt someday.”

The two men looked at each other and nodded once. That was enough. Their relationship would be different from before because of this incident.

Jin Hagun left immediately.

He wanted to see his grandfather as soon as possible.

When Gwak Ju entered, his sister Gwak Yeong was waiting inside.

She had originally been at the Heavenly Demon Divine Church’s safehouse in Wuhan, but after receiving Jin Hagun’s message that her younger brother had been rescued, she had come straight here to bring him back.

“Noona!”

His emotions surged to such an extent that he wondered whether he had ever been so happy to see his sister in his entire life.

Gwak Ju ran toward Gwak Yeong.

“You’re so disgusting. What’s gotten into you?”

Gwak Yeong evaded his embrace. Yet her face wore a broad smile.

Gwak Ju first said the words he had resolved to tell her when they met.

“I’m fine, Noona. Nothing happened to me.”

So you can be all right, too.

Gwak Yeong’s eyes reddened at his words. She had been pretending that everything was fine, but no one could know how worried she had been about her younger brother. To save him, she had even abandoned the honor of being the youngest master artisan of the Martial Alliance’s Forge Hall.

“Are you hurt anywhere?”

“I’m fine.”

“You’re bleeding!”

“It’s not my blood. The Extermination Demon Unit Leader saved me.”

He would never forget how the man had looked after him until the very end.

“Where is the unit leader?”

“He left ahead of us.”

Gwak Yeong looked disappointed. She had wanted to at least thank the leader of the Extermination Demon Unit.

That was also why Jin Hagun had left without coming inside.

He wanted her to offer all her thanks to Geom Muyang, so that Geom Muyang’s efforts to save her would not be overshadowed.

Gwak Yeong walked over to Geom Muyang.

She knew that all of this had begun with the meeting between herself and this Grand Young Master.

After all, it had started when he tracked down the counterfeit hidden weapons she had made.

Gwak Yeong bowed her head to Geom Muyang.

“Thank you.”

Geom Muyang attributed the credit to Jin Hagun, who was not present.

“The Extermination Demon Unit Leader saved your brother, so there is no need to thank me.”

Gwak Yeong knew that if not for Geom Muyang in the first place, her brother could never have been saved. She, too, would have been used by those people until they eventually killed her.

“Now, return to the Martial Alliance with your brother.”

At Geom Muyang’s words, Gwak Yeong revealed her decision.

“I’ll follow you to the Divine Church, too.”

Gwak Ju was startled by his sister’s unexpected declaration. Geom Muyang, however, was not surprised.

“I promised, didn’t I? I said I would make weapons for you.”

Geom Muyang spoke to her.

“There’s no need. It’s all in the past.”

“Please let me keep my promise.”

She had already made up her mind.

Gwak Yeong spoke to Gwak Ju, who was merely standing beside her and watching.

“Why aren’t you trying to stop me?”

He should at least know that this place was a den of demonic practitioners. Yet her younger brother only stood there watching.

“Noona, you’ve always made the right choice.”

“You have to come with me, too.”

Even though she was saying that he would be joining the Demon Cult, Gwak Ju was not surprised.

“If you’re going, then I have to go, too.”

He could not send his sister alone to such a dangerous place.

Gwak Yeong’s eyes trembled as she looked at her brother.

“Hey, you!”

“Yeah, you’re grateful, aren’t you? Where could you find another younger brother like me? I don’t even resent you for being kidnapped because of me, and I came out of it strong and well. Right?”

Gwak Ju had inherited his sister’s cheerful personality.

Gwak Yeong spoke to Geom Muyang again.

“Please take both of us with you. We’ll take care of our own livelihood. We won’t be a burden.”

Geom Muyang gave her one final chance.

“When war breaks out, the weapons you make will kill people you know.”

Gwak Yeong had already steeled her heart.

“Then I’ll have to go to the Demon Cult and make lots of acquaintances.”

At her words, Geom Muyang gave her an incredulous look.

“To be honest, I’m looking for a way to survive.”

She conveyed her honest feelings.

“I abandoned the honor of being a Forge Hall master and made counterfeit weapons.”

“Your brother had been kidnapped. The Martial Alliance will understand.”

“Even so, I won’t be able to become a Forge Hall master of the Martial Alliance again.”

She was probably right. No matter that she had done it for her brother, the Martial Alliance would not take someone who had made counterfeit hidden weapons back as a Forge Hall master.

“I can’t spend my life making weapons for the Martial Alliance’s elite and then go make hoes in the marketplace. I’m holding on to this for my own survival. If you’re going to curse me as a selfish bitch, I was even prepared to grab on to your trouser leg.”

Geom Muyang could tell that her resolve was firm.

“You may have to start from the beginning.”

“I don’t think so. My skills are far too exceptional for that.”

As she said this, she smiled slyly. Beside her, Gwak Ju clasped his hands together and bowed his head with an expression that seemed to apologize for his sister’s shamelessness.

“The Forge Hall of the Divine Church will be even more difficult for a woman to endure.”

“You’ve never worked there, have you? Men and women, masters and newcomers—all of them have a hard time there. No one has enough energy left to torment other people.”

Geom Muyang’s deliberation was brief. Their bond ran deep enough that he could accept her.

“If you make shoddy weapons, you’ll be punished by me.”

With those words, Geom Muyang turned around.

“Thank you! Thank you so much!”

Without looking back, Geom Muyang entered the building.

Only then did Gwak Yeong let out a sigh. It was a sigh filled with both relief at having gained permission and anxiety about her new life.

Once they were alone, Gwak Ju asked her,

“I’ll follow you because it’s your decision, but…”

Gwak Ju’s gaze turned toward Geom Muyang.

“Who is that person you’re saying you’ll follow?”

Judging by the aura he gave off, he did not seem like an ordinary person.

How should she explain this so that her little brother would not be startled?

“If you’re wondering who that person is…”

Just as she was cautiously beginning to explain, someone spoke from behind them.

“He’s my older brother.”

When Gwak Yeong and Gwak Ju turned around in surprise, Geom Mugeuk was walking toward them.

Gwak Yeong blinked for a moment.

‘The eldest young master’s younger brother?’

Her eyes widened. She already knew that the Second Young Master was the successor to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Before the stunned Gwak Yeong could say anything, Geom Mugeuk put an arm around Gwak Ju’s shoulders and acted like a friend tattling on someone.

“My brother looks respectable on the outside, right? But he’s got a foul temper and is full of wicked ambition. He’s especially harsh on his younger siblings. So when you go to the Main Cult, make sure you protect your older sister from my brother! Got it?”

After saying only what he wanted to say, Geom Mugeuk went inside. Gwak Yeong could only stand there blankly, never getting the chance to properly greet him.

Gwak Ju asked his sister again.

“What was that? He just said whatever he wanted and…”

“Shh! Be quiet! Shut up!”

His sister’s hand trembled as she desperately clapped it over his mouth. Just who was that person to make her this nervous?

“Who was he?”

Another person appeared just then.

“Who else? A good-for-nothing younger brother who badmouths his older brother behind his back.”

The person who had appeared was, at the very least, in no position to scold anyone for badmouthing others behind their backs.

He was none other than the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Is that all? If there’s anything valuable, he snatches it all away, makes his subordinates go through hell while he folds his arms and watches. He’s a vicious leader. So you make sure to protect your sister from that man! He’s the more dangerous one!”

Gwak Ju had personally seen just how formidable the Pungcheon Cult Leader was. The sight of him riding up the waves and flying to their very crest was still vivid in his mind.

Frozen stiff, Gwak Ju answered loudly.

“Yes! I’ll keep that in mind!”

Following the Pungcheon Cult Leader inside was the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable.

Gwak Ju bent so deeply that his waist nearly touched the ground. When he continued bowing until they entered the building, Gwak Yeong also bowed alongside him and asked her little brother,

“Who was that person?”

Gwak Ju answered in a trembling voice.

“That person is…”

Another person appeared just then.

“A fool.”

Someone perfectly suited to their lowered gazes passed by. It was none other than Mabul.

“The greatest fool in all the world. Because he was too good-natured, he let people take everything from him, even his liver and gallbladder, and he threw away all wealth and glory to come here.”

As the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s friend, he was in a position to say such things.

Mabul strode into the building.

Gwak Ju asked with a startled expression,

“Who’s that person now?”

Someone answered his question.

At last, the Poison King passed by them and said casually,

“They’re all crazy.”

Seeing Gwak Ju’s startled expression, the Poison King added,

“So you protect your sister.”

And with that, even the Poison King entered the building.

Gwak Ju blinked as he tried to sort out everything that had happened in the blink of an eye.

“So, to sum it up: the person my sister wants to follow has a younger brother who badmouths his older brother behind his back. Right? But then there’s a terrifying master who badmouths that younger brother again. He’s the person who saved me. Yet there’s also someone who calls that fearsome person a fool. And all of these people are crazy. Did I hear everything correctly?”

Just as Gwak Yeong was nodding, dark clouds gathered as if it were about to rain again, and thunder rumbled in the distance.

“Sis, where are we going?”

Chapter 667: I Wanted to Run Away
---------------------------------
“Brother! I made it back alive!”

When Geom Mugeuk entered the room with a cheerful greeting, Geom Muyang was standing by the window.

Without turning around, Geom Muyang said,

“I heard everything.”

“What?”

“You bad-mouthing me.”

“I forgot to say that. My brother’s ears are so sharp he even hears people talking behind his back. So if you’re going to curse him, do it from far away.”

A faint smile formed on the lips of Geom Muyang, whose back was still turned.

Geom Mugeuk collapsed limply into a chair like laundry tossed over it.

“Ah, it’s finally over! Now, let’s rest for a while! I’m going to play, so don’t stop me! I’m going to play with my friends, play with the Demon Venerables, and play with Father too. I mean it! Schemes, masterminds, plots—don’t even bring them up! Brother, play with me!”

Geom Muyang turned back and looked Geom Mugeuk over.

“For someone who just finished a tremendous fight, you don’t have a single drop of blood on your clothes.”

“Refined people like us fight in a refined manner too.”

Just then, someone opened the door and entered, saying,

“Is that really why? Or is it because you had other people do the fighting while you stood behind them with your arms crossed, just watching?”

Geom Mugeuk’s face brightened. It was a welcome voice he had not heard in far too long.

“And of course, while others were fighting, I’m sure that mouth of yours didn’t stay idle.”

Geom Mugeuk sprang to his feet and turned around to see the Pungcheon Cult Leader and the Seophon Demon Venerable entering the room.

“I missed you so, so much, Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk spread both arms and ran toward the Pungcheon Cult Leader. The Pungcheon Cult Leader used his footwork and deftly dodged aside.

“Don’t pretend you’re happy to see me. I know you’d completely forgotten all about me. Just now, when you saw my face, you thought: Who is that? Ah! It’s that cult leader from back then!”

Geom Mugeuk shouted,

“I am that Second Young Master from back then!”

As Geom Mugeuk charged at him again, the Pungcheon Cult Leader evaded once more with footwork.

“Enough! It’s disgusting!”

But contrary to his words, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face was filled with delight. Joking and playing around so comfortably after so long felt as if something that had been blocking his chest had suddenly cleared away.

While he had been in the Pungcheon Cult beyond the borders, teaching that disciple of his, had there been anyone he could treat this casually? He had missed this moment.

Yes, this was the feeling. It was for this feeling that he had abandoned everything and come here.

To meet someone to whom he could freely convey his heart, and who would accept it all and correct him even if misunderstandings and mistakes arose in the process.

It would have been tremendous luck to have even one such person, yet he had two.

At that moment, another person entered the room and clicked his tongue.

“You fell completely for that little performance.”

The one who entered was Mabul.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader and Mabul greeted each other with their eyes. Their relationship had been good, then bad, and had now recovered to some extent.

“Wouldn’t a performance like that be worth falling for?”

Mabul could not say it was not.

After he finished greeting Geom Muyang and the Seophon Demon Venerable as well, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“What about the Poison King?”

“He went to his room.”

The Poison King would be in his room, spreading out the poisonous herbs he had gathered.

“I tried to bring that chief steward bastard back alive, but things didn’t work out.”

“You did well. He probably didn’t know anything anyway.”

Geom Mugeuk formally expressed his gratitude.

“Thanks to you, we were able to handle this matter easily. Thank you.”

“I didn’t come because of you.”

As he said that, Mabul looked at Geom Muyang.

Geom Mugeuk felt he now understood what sort of feeling Mabul’s unwavering loyalty toward his brother came from.

Mabul had lived a lonelier life than anyone since childhood. That was why he knew what loneliness was to a person.

Because he himself had shut himself away in a small cave, he must also know what sort of loneliness his brother felt after being pushed aside in the succession struggle.

“That’s all right. There are two people here who came for me.”

When Geom Mugeuk looked at the Pungcheon Cult Leader, he firmly shook his head.

“Sorry, but I came to see Strategist Go.”

“That’s too much!”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the last remaining person, the Seophon Demon Venerable.

The Seophon Demon Venerable smiled. He had naturally expected her to say she had come because of him.

“I wanted to see my master after so long.”

Seophon, even you! Only when she saw that look in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes did she smile.

“I’m joking.”

There was no way the Pungcheon Cult Leader would miss such a fine opportunity.

“Joking? You didn’t come to see me?”

“No. I came to see you, Master.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader teased Geom Mugeuk.

“Life is something one must walk alone, after all.”

Geom Mugeuk walked to the window.

“Brother, let me borrow the window for a moment.”

Then he gazed outside, pretending to be lonely.

Watching that sight, the Seophon Demon Venerable felt it.

Geom Mugeuk’s back truly looked lonely.

It felt similar to when she had seen Geugak Soma standing alone on a rooftop.

Perhaps that was why the two of them got along the best, she suddenly thought.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Where is Strategist Go?”

“I don’t know. Am I not a man who walks life alone?”

“Let Strategist Go walk with you.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk turned around with a laugh.

“We agreed to meet at the main cult. He said he would take care of his work and come.”

The hidden contributor who had allowed this matter to be resolved smoothly was Go Wol. He had shared all of Eunwol’s information with Tongcheongak and relayed information to Geom Mugeuk.

“So he was still being overworked by a heartless leader.”

Geom Mugeuk did not deny it.

“That is why we need you, Cult Leader. No matter how much I tell him to slack off while working, he doesn’t listen to me.”

“Does that stubborn man listen to me?”

“Still, he listens to you better than he listens to me.”

After exchanging greetings with the Pungcheon Cult Leader like that, he looked at the Seophon Demon Venerable. He could feel that her aura had changed greatly from before. She was not the same woman she had once been.

“Thanks to you, we resolved this matter well. Thank you.”

The Seophon Demon Venerable thought Geom Mugeuk was truly meticulous. Every time he looked at a person, he did not forget to offer his thanks. Even after everything was over, he never overlooked something that could have simply been brushed aside.

“I believe it was possible because you were there, Young Cult Leader.”

She knew. The person who had overseen this entire affair and ultimately stopped every plot was Geom Mugeuk.

From the very beginning, she was someone who should not have been here in Wuhan. And was it only her? The Poison King, Mabul, the Pungcheon Cult Leader, the First Young Master, and even the successor of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult were here.

The one who had made this possible was that Young Cult Leader. The future cult leader she would serve for the rest of her life.

At her words, Geom Mugeuk was delighted.

“As expected! The only one who recognizes my worth is the Seophon Demon Venerable!”

After the reunion, which was more like an uproar, came to an end, the Pungcheon Cult Leader casually asked,

“How was things on that side?”

What he was curious about was the strength of the Murim Alliance Leader.

“The Alliance Leader’s skill is no ordinary thing, is it?”

Since everyone was curious, they focused on Geom Mugeuk’s answer.

“I’ll have to tell Father to postpone the unification of murim.”

That meant he was that strong.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader immediately shook his head.

“Your father is even more frightening.”

“You haven’t fought either Father or the Murim Alliance Leader, have you?”

“Do I have to fight them to know? If I had to face the Alliance Leader, I’d start by worrying and thinking about how I should fight him. But if I had to face your father… Ah, I don’t even want to think about it. That’s exactly the difference.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader feared the Demon Cult Leader more than the Murim Alliance Leader.

“Now, let’s head back. We should go meet that poor strategist being overworked.”

The Demon Cult masters could not remain in Wuhan for long.

Then Geom Mugeuk sent him and the Demon Venerables back first.

“You all go on ahead. Brother and I have something to do here.”

When Geom Muyang looked as if to ask what he was planning now, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Soon, good news will come from the Murim Alliance.”

“What news?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled meaningfully.

“Since we’ve come all the way here, we should give proper congratulations before leaving.”

* * *

Jin Hagun returned to the Murim Alliance.

When the warriors of the Murim Alliance saw Jin Hagun and the warriors of the Demon-Extermination Unit drenched in blood, they respectfully cupped their fists in greeting.

Because they knew very well that the blood had been shed to protect innocent lives.

Recently, Jin Hagun had been making an effort to change himself. As a result, his relationship with the warriors of the Demon-Extermination Unit had deepened, and that change had transformed the Demon-Extermination Unit into an even stronger organization.

Naturally, the way people within the Murim Alliance looked at the Demon-Extermination Unit had changed as well.

After entering the inner compound, he dismissed his subordinates.

“Go treat your wounds and rest well! You truly worked hard.”

“You worked hard as well, Unit Leader!”

Thus Jin Hagun headed to the Alliance Leader’s Hall alone.

All the way back, he had thought of his grandfather.

He did not know what sort of battle had unfolded against Hwa Yulcheong, but considering the fight on their side, his grandfather’s battle must not have been easy either.

In particular, Jin Hyu had said he would take him hostage and use him to threaten his grandfather. What if his grandfather had been injured because of that?

That was why, this time, he trusted Geom Mugeuk even more. He believed that Geom Mugeuk would have protected his grandfather so that he would not waver.

Jin Hagun entered the Alliance Leader’s Hall.

Creeeak.

The moment the door opened, he instinctively sensed that the air inside was different.

Jin Hagun’s heart began to pound.

At the sight inside the Alliance Leader’s Hall, Jin Hagun’s heart sank.

The grand chair was empty.

If that had been all, he would have thought his grandfather had stepped out for a moment.

However, beneath the grand chair stood dozens of warriors lined up on both sides.

They were no ordinary warriors. They were the heads of the various organizations of the Murim Alliance.

First were the four elite organizations that represented the Murim Alliance.

The leaders of the Blue Dragon Corps, White Dragon Corps, White Tiger Corps, and Vermilion Bird Corps were standing there.

And not only them.

The pavilion masters of the Heavenly Wind Pavilion and the Secret Pavilion, elite organizations that moved in secrecy, were also there.

In addition, all the heads of the other organizations had gathered, including the commanders of the Iron Cavalry Unit and the Secret Shadow Unit.

And before them stood the Chief Strategist of the Murim Alliance, Jegal Hyeon.

Jin Hagun’s heart began to hammer wildly. He was even more surprised to see his younger sister, Jin Haryeong, standing there too.

‘No way.’

Had something happened to his grandfather?

His steps toward them quickened.

The leaders of the Murim Alliance lined up on both sides silently watched Jin Hagun.

When Jin Hagun arrived beneath the grand chair,

the door used by the Alliance Leader opened, and Jin Paecheon walked out from within.

The moment he saw his grandfather, Jin Hagun let out a sigh of relief. The brief time it had taken him to walk from the entrance to this spot had been the most nerve-racking moment of his life.

“Alliance Leader!”

Jin Hagun looked at Jin Paecheon with trembling eyes.

Seeing him safe and sound, one person’s face came to mind.

‘Thank you, Mugeuk.’

This time, you may boast to your heart’s content. I will accept it all.

Jin Hagun bowed respectfully with proper etiquette.

“Jin Hagun, leader of the Demon-Extermination Unit, has returned after carrying out his mission.”

Jin Paecheon silently looked down at his grandson. At the gaze and atmosphere different from usual, Jin Hagun grew tense. His grandfather was safe, so why had everyone gathered like this?

At last, astonishing words flowed from Jin Paecheon’s mouth. Jin Paecheon was not a man who postponed something once he had made up his mind.

“From this moment onward, I designate Jin Hagun, leader of the Demon-Extermination Unit, as the next Murim Alliance Leader!”

Because it was something he had never expected, Jin Hagun’s eyes widened.

What his grandfather had just said meant that he would make him the official successor to the position of Alliance Leader.

As the moment he had longed for so dearly arrived without any warning, he was both shocked and flustered.

Jin Haryeong sent a voice transmission to her elder brother.

—What are you doing, Brother!

Only then did Jin Hagun come to his senses. He bowed and shouted,

“Jin Hagun, leader of the Demon-Extermination Unit, will uphold this solemn command, protect the Alliance, uphold righteousness and chivalry, and continue to defend justice.”

Then the leaders gathered there shouted in unison,

“The Way of Righteous Chivalry! The Orthodox Path Under Heaven!”

Their resounding voices rang out, filled with spirit.

Jin Hagun was happy. So happy. He could not say how much he had wished for this moment. Since childhood, it had been his dream to succeed his grandfather and become the Murim Alliance Leader.

Originally, the position of Murim Alliance Leader was not something that could be inherited, nor could the previous Murim Alliance Leader decide it arbitrarily on his own.

According to the established laws of the Alliance, the nominated candidate could become the successor only if more than eighty percent of the Murim Alliance’s leaders, including the Chief Strategist, approved. Whether they had voted in favor or against was kept strictly confidential, so the selection process was extremely rigorous.

Jin Paecheon’s decision to postpone the selection of his successor had, in the end, been a wise one. In the meantime, Jin Hagun had steadily built up his fame and reputation while serving as commander of the Demon Extermination Unit.

Moreover, in the course of carrying out acts of righteousness, even his character had come to be acknowledged.

That was why everyone had already made up their minds.

“Unit Leader Jin, please ascend.”

At Chief Strategist Jegal Hyeon’s words, Jin Hagun climbed the steps and stood beside Jin Paecheon.

The assembled leaders all offered their respects at once.

“We greet the next Alliance Leader!”

Jin Hagun, too, returned their courtesy with a bow.

“I sincerely thank you for looking so favorably upon someone as lacking as myself.”

Jin Haryeong also offered her congratulations. Under ordinary circumstances, she too should have shown formal respect, but in this moment, she wanted to congratulate him as his younger sister. She knew better than anyone how long her elder brother had looked forward to this day.

“Congratulations, Orabeoni!”

Jin Hagun looked at her and smiled brightly.

The leaders each offered their congratulations. Jin Hagun descended and greeted them one by one, expressing his gratitude.

With this, the three successors of the orthodox, unorthodox, and demonic factions had been decided. Soon, an official announcement would be made, and all of murim would come to know of it.

A grand festival would be held at the Murim Alliance.

* * *

After everyone had withdrawn, only Jin Paecheon and Jin Hagun remained in the Alliance Leader’s Hall.

“Hagun, there is something I must tell you.”

“I will engrave it deep in my heart.”

He thought his grandfather would speak of the sense of duty and attitude an Alliance Leader must possess, and of Geom Mugeuk and the peace of murim.

But his grandfather brought up something he had never expected.

“There was a part of me that hoped you would not sit in this seat. Half of my heart felt that way.”

For the first time, Jin Paecheon spoke honestly of how he felt about the position of Alliance Leader.

“This is a seat that comes with an unbearably heavy responsibility. It is a seat where one must restrain one’s desires to the utmost, and carry the burden of protecting countless lives. Even when something is not your fault, the responsibility will come flying to you, and every manner of resentment will be yours to bear.”

Jin Paecheon conveyed his feelings to his grandson with all the affection in his heart.

“It may seem exciting and wonderful, but that lasts only a day or two. You must live your whole life conscious of others’ gazes. You must mind yourself even when you eat, and you cannot even curse to your heart’s content. Because of all the tangled relationships, you cannot even show that you hate someone you hate.”

Then came a startling confession.

“There were many times I wanted to run away.”

Jin Hagun could hardly believe that his grandfather had ever thought such a thing.

“Those people who congratulated you earlier—if you offered them this seat, more of them would run away than accept it. The more one knows about this position, the more one would flee from it. It is not a seat to covet.”

Jin Hagun understood what he meant. Even the position of commander of the Demon Extermination Unit was unbearably difficult at times; how much more so must the seat of Alliance Leader be?

“Do you know how I endured it?”

“How did you endure it?”

Jin Paecheon’s gaze grew intense.

“With strength.”

Jin Paecheon did not intend merely to give his grandson this arduous and difficult position. He would also give him the power to protect it.

“I will transmit the Imperial Sword Style to you!”

Chapter 668: Our Time Starts Now
--------------------------------
The courtyard was in the midst of preparations for departure.

It had been decided that only Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang would remain, while everyone else returned to the main headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

After all, for so many masters of the Divine Cult to remain in Wuhan after the matter had been settled would not be proper courtesy toward the Murim Alliance.

Two people were watching the scene from a short distance away. They were none other than the siblings Gwak Yeong and Gwak Ju.

“We have to go back with those people?”

They were brave siblings who had thrown themselves into the Demonic Cult, but going back with those people frightened them even more.

“And without even the person who’s taking you with him, Sister?”

That was what Gwak Yeong found most disappointing. It would have been nice if Geom Muyang were going with them, but he had said he would come later because he had business to attend to.

When she thought about it, the First Young Master of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was someone even more difficult and terrifying than those Demon Venerables, and yet she was relying on him.

She was grateful to Geom Muyang. Such a frightening person had become someone they could depend on.

Gwak Yeong and Gwak Ju had found out who all those madmen were. They had also found out that the person who had called them madmen was the Poison King.

The Poison King?

The Poison King, before whom even peerless masters trembled, had passed by while joking with them.

They were all of such exalted status that it was hard to believe. In an entire lifetime, would one ever have the chance to meet the former Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Demon Venerables even once? Yet four such people were now right before their eyes.

“They wouldn’t actually eat us, would they?”

At Gwak Ju’s words, someone suddenly spoke from behind him.

“They may not eat you, but they could use you as ingredients for making poison.”

Startled, Gwak Ju turned around and saw that Geom Mugeuk had arrived at some point.

“You’ve never been put in a vat full of poison, have you? Your whole body starts turning mushy, and you can’t tell whether that thing floating over there is your finger or your ear.”

Gwak Ju stared at Geom Mugeuk, swallowing his startled breath. It was not because he was afraid of the poison vat, but because of the status of the person before him.

The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult!

He had not known yesterday, but now he did. A person of even higher status than those four terrifying people was speaking to him.

Was this a dream? Yes, this was all a dream. From the moment his sister, who had been a master artisan of the Murim Alliance’s iron workshop, said she would defect to the Demonic Cult, it had all sounded like a dream.

“Which carriage are you going to ride in?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Gwak Ju, who had been standing in a daze, came back to his senses.

“Ah, ah. We…”

Gwak Ju looked at his sister. As if Gwak Yeong had decided which one to ride in either.

At that moment, two carriages were waiting in the courtyard.

The first carriage was the one in which Demon Buddha and the Poison King would ride. The second carriage was the one for Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

“Of the people over there, who looks the most amiable?”

At that, the four people who were about to get into the carriages pricked up their ears.

Geom Mugeuk had spoken as if whispering to the siblings, but could the hearing of those four masters be ordinary?

Moreover, Geom Mugeuk was whispering while looking at them as though plotting something.

Gwak Ju looked at them, then whispered softly.

“Judging only by appearance, the handsome one over there looks the nicest.”

He thought they would not hear, but they heard everything.

Cult Leader Pungcheon frowned. He was not shameless enough to expect to be chosen when the question was who was the most handsome.

But if it was about who looked amiable! That would be me, wouldn’t it? If he could not put this generous, kindly appearance to use at a time like this, then when could he?

“If you get in the first carriage just because someone looks nice, and then you make a mistake? You know, right? The poison vat!”

Gwak Ju nodded with a pale face.

“Then we’ll get in the second carriage.”

“Yes, that sort of thing won’t happen there.”

Just when it seemed he was reassuring him.

“However, if you make a mistake there, you’ll end up stabbing your sister to death and then killing yourself. See that person over there with the nasty expression and the protruding belly? You need to be especially careful of him.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon snapped his head toward them. I can hear you, you brat! Before he could shout, someone stopped him first.

“Stop playing around.”

When they turned around, Geom Muyang was standing there.

“The two of you will return in that carriage.”

A third carriage arrived there. The people escorting it were the Jidan warriors who had come with Geom Muyang at first. He had ordered them to escort the two siblings to the main headquarters and then return.

Gwak Yeong and Gwak Ju were relieved by the fact that they could travel separately from the Demon Venerables.

“I sent word ahead, so when you arrive, go to the iron workshop and meet Master Gwak.”

“Thank you for your consideration.”

Many emotions were contained in her gaze as she looked at Geom Muyang. It had been better when she had not known his status and had spoken to him recklessly. Then she could have conveyed this boiling gratitude more clearly. It was a shame.

Thus, Gwak Yeong and Gwak Ju walked toward the third carriage.

As the two passed by, Cult Leader Pungcheon said to the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable,

“Usually, people choose the one who saved their life as number one.”

Just as Gwak Ju flinched.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable kept pace with his master’s grudge.

“I do happen to need the blood of a young man as material for a great ritual.”

With a short shriek of “Goodness!” Gwak Ju scurried over to the third carriage. He knew it was a joke, but even knowing that, it was frightening.

Gwak Yeong followed after him with an awkward smile.

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang watched the scene.

“Take good care of them from now on.”

“You’re the one bringing them in, so why should I take care of them?”

“You’re good at looking after people.”

“Seeing you this time, Brother, you seem to have a knack for it too.”

Geom Muyang gave no answer. Saving the Gwak siblings this time had been unlike his former self. It had also been something that allowed him to confirm his own change.

“I like the way you are these days, Brother. I like you more and more. So I’ll say it again—don’t even dream of going back to the way you were before.”

“…”

Just then, the carriages set off.

Geom Mugeuk ran over and bid farewell to Demon Buddha and the Poison King.

“I’ll see you at the main headquarters. And if you see the Chief Strategist, tell him not to worry about us and please get some rest!”

The two were discussing poisonous herbs and did not even look at him.

“Demon Buddha! I’m telling you, you’re just being used!”

Thus the first carriage departed.

For Cult Leader Pungcheon, he entrusted someone else to him.

“Cult Leader, please take care of our Strategist Go!”

Cult Leader Pungcheon slammed the window shut.

“As if you’re worried!”

Soon after, the door opened slightly, and the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable gave him a nod with his eyes.

Thus the second carriage also departed.

When the third carriage passed by, the playfulness had vanished from Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“Welcome to our Cult.”

An atmosphere completely different from before.

Clear, deep eyes and an overwhelming presence that could not be put into words.

Gwak Yeong and Gwak Ju bowed respectfully.

“Thank you very much for accepting us.”

Thus, the three carriages departed for the main headquarters.

Geom Mugeuk walked straight out the gate.

“I’ll be going to the Murim Alliance for a bit.”

Geom Muyang shook his head and spoke loudly.

“Is the Murim Alliance your own living room, that you can come and go so freely?”

From beyond the wall came Geom Mugeuk’s reply.

“They told me to come!”

* * *

The streets of Wuhan were abuzz with two pieces of news.

The first piece of news was the death of the Cheonae Hermit, Hwa Yulcheong.

When it was revealed that he had been a villain who had infiltrated the Murim Alliance, the warriors of Wuhan were thrown into shock.

The Murim Alliance made its position clear.

They announced that the person who had personally executed the Cheonae Hermit was the Murim Alliance Leader, and that his evil deeds had been confirmed not only by the escort warriors of the Alliance Leader Hall, but also by all the warriors of the Demon Extermination Squad.

Hwa Yulcheong had mocked Jin Paecheon, saying, “Do you really think the martial artists will believe your words?” But the repercussions were not as great as he had expected.

Those who had followed him as if worshiping him raised objections, but far more people believed in the Murim Alliance Leader and the Murim Alliance.

It was not only Jin Paecheon’s martial prowess that Hwa Yulcheong had failed to properly understand. He had also failed to understand the true strength of Jin Paecheon, who had lived his whole life as an Alliance Leader without shame.

It would take time for this incident to be settled, but there was no chaos serious enough to become a problem.

There was also a second piece of news that overshadowed the first. It was the news that Jin Hagun had become the successor.

The warriors of the orthodox faction cheered at the news. Many had believed that the next Alliance Leader should be Jin Hagun.

That was how much the Demon Extermination Squad had devoted themselves to the justice and righteousness of the murim, disregarding even their own lives.

Through the marketplace, noisy everywhere with talk of Jin Hagun becoming the successor, passed Geom Mugeuk with his bamboo hat pulled low.

The place he arrived at was the main headquarters of the Murim Alliance. At the entrance, Jin Haryeong was waiting.

She watched as Geom Mugeuk walked toward her.

‘You saved us again.’

Whenever she saw him, she kept thinking back to the first time she had met him. The greater the meaning of the man named Geom Mugeuk became, the more so.

Geom Mugeuk arrived in front of her.

“It’s all over now, isn’t it?”

At her words, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“No! Our time starts now!”

Knowing that he meant they should now have fun together, Jin Haryeong smiled happily.

“Let’s go. Grandfather is waiting.”

The two entered the Murim Alliance. The place she led him to was not toward the Alliance Leader Hall.

Perhaps orders had been given in advance, for the guards they met let Geom Mugeuk pass without asking his identity.

The two conversed as they walked together.

“Aren’t you disappointed?”

“About what?”

“You could have become the successor too.”

“That’s something only someone with ambition would covet. I don’t have that kind of ambition. I’m already overwhelmed handling the two roles of being the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter, and in the future, the Alliance Leader’s younger sister.”

Geom Mugeuk knew she had a greater ambition than that, but he was certain it was not becoming the Murim Alliance Leader.

“What about Hagun?”

“My brother is in the training ground right now.”

Having received instruction in the Sovereign Sword Form from Jin Paecheon, Jin Hagun was training alone. Since it was important to properly digest what he had learned when he first received it, he would likely shut himself in the training ground for a while. For him, it was a more important moment than anything else right now.

“That’s good. In the meantime, we can prepare the celebration banquet.”

“You’re really going to do it?”

“Of course. A friend fulfilled his dream. Let’s keep it modest and invite only our friends.”

Though they were people to whom the word modest did not quite suit.

“I already called everyone who needs to come.”

“How diligent of you.”

This gathering would truly be a meeting of the official successors of the orthodox, unorthodox, and demonic factions.

Jin Haryeong was genuinely curious.

“Isn’t doing this bothersome?”

Among the friends gathering, whether in status, martial arts, or personality, he was truly the most outstanding.

It would have been natural for him to think, Do I really have to be the one to step forward and call everyone?

“If I go through just a little trouble, I can give your brother a celebration banquet he’ll never forget for the rest of his life. And I’m supposed to not do it because it’s bothersome?”

At that moment, Jin Haryeong had this thought.

Even someone this outstanding lives so diligently, so who am I to do less? I should live even more diligently!

Of course, he also made people flustered like this.

“When should we start practicing the dance?”

“What?”

Jin Haryeong stopped walking with a startled expression.

“We need to put on a congratulatory performance.”

“No! Absolutely not! I’ll die of embarrassment! Absolutely not!”

Of course, there was no way her refusal, now that she had become his prey, would work on Geom Mugeuk.

“Then it can’t be helped.”

This person was giving up that easily?

Of course, there was no way that was true.

“Still, it’s a shame. On a day that wasn’t anything special, you danced so earnestly. But this time is the most precious day of your brother’s life. And you can’t do it because you’re embarrassed? Back then, the place was even full of masters from the demonic, orthodox, and unorthodox factions. This time, it’s only us gathering together. Your brother is such a broad-minded person that he’ll understand. Someday, when you’re older and look back on today, you’ll think, Ah, it really wasn’t anything. Why was I like that back then? Why did I wound my brother’s heart? And you’ll shed tears of regret by Hagun’s grave, won’t you? It’s all right. Hagun will still understand.”

In the end, Jin Haryeong raised both hands and feet in surrender.

“Oh, honestly! I’ll dance, I’ll dance! Fine, I’ll dance until I collapse from exhaustion!”

But there was no way she would die alone.

“I have a condition. You have to dance too, and so does Young Alliance Leader Bi!”

Geom Mugeuk would surely jump right in and dance, but she thought Bi Sain would absolutely never dance. Just as hope surged within her that if he did not dance, she would not have to dance either.

As if he already had a way, Geom Mugeuk gave a meaningful smile.

“Of course. Sain will be at the center.”

* * *

Jin Paecheon was waiting deep within the inner garden.

Before arriving there, Jin Haryeong had returned first. She had said she could go no farther. It was clearly a place only the Alliance Leader could enter.

Jin Paecheon stood with his back turned, looking up at the sky.

“This time, Alliance Leader, you have taught me another great lesson.”

Without speaking, Jin Paecheon waited for Geom Mugeuk’s next words.

“If you have made up your mind, put it into action immediately.”

He was referring to how Jin Paecheon had immediately carried out the decision to make Jin Hagun his successor.

Still gazing at the sky, Jin Paecheon said calmly,

“The right time to do what must be done is now! I have lived my whole life believing that.”

Geom Mugeuk stood side by side with Jin Paecheon.

“I will be sure to keep that in mind as I live.”

Only then did Jin Paecheon’s gaze, which had been fixed on the sky, turn toward Geom Mugeuk.

“You should live while putting things off a little.”

At Jin Paecheon’s joke telling him not to grow any stronger, Geom Mugeuk smiled.

Jin Paecheon’s expression toward Geom Mugeuk was gentle.

“If it had not been for you, not only this Alliance, but Hagun and I as well, would have fallen into great trouble.”

“No one knows what might have happened. At the decisive moment, you surely would have realized everything, Alliance Leader.”

“Would I really have?”

He said it in a negative sense, but Geom Mugeuk believed he would have.

“Yes, you surely would have.”

“Why are you so certain?”

“Because your instinct for danger, Alliance Leader, surely would not have let it pass. When a true crisis came, it would definitely have activated.”

This was why. The reason he could not simply let it go. Because the other party was this sort of person. Even if his instinct for danger could let it pass, this could not.

“I am someone who is clear about gratitude and grudges. Since I owe a debt, I must repay it.”

Jin Paecheon began to walk, and Geom Mugeuk quietly followed behind him.

After they walked a little farther, they came to a place filled with thick fog.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. A formation had been deployed.

If it was a formation protecting a precious place, then there was no way the method to break it could be revealed to him.

Just as he wondered whether he should close his eyes and follow.

Sssss.

The fog made a path on its own. He did not know what principle it worked by, but the fog twisted and wound to form a path. It went forward, then sideways, then forward again. Perhaps it was a fog that opened a path only for Jin Paecheon.

There was no need to step in one place and skip another. All they had to do was walk as the fog guided them.

After they had passed through that long path of fog, a gigantic door appeared before their eyes.

Above it, in vigorous brushstrokes, was written the name of this place.

The Murim Treasury.

Chapter 669: But You Didn't Know This, Did You?
-----------------------------------------------
Murim Treasury.

An absolute forbidden domain where all manner of treasures of the murim were stored, said to be accessible only to the Murim Alliance Leader and those the Alliance Leader permitted.

An aura as mysterious as its long history flowed out from the very doors.

As they passed through the formation, Geom Mugeuk honestly found himself filled with anticipation. It was not because he coveted martial arts manuals or divine weapons and extraordinary arms.

It was because he wondered whether, by any chance, a bead the Secret Coffer could absorb might be stored here in the Murim Alliance’s treasury as well.

But he had truly never imagined that the Murim Alliance Leader would bring him here.

“You are the first person from the Divine Cult to come this far. Perhaps the first in all history.”

“It is an honor, Alliance Leader.”

And how could it only be people of the Divine Cult? Even among the orthodox faction, those who had stood before this place could likely be counted on one hand.

“Since becoming Alliance Leader, I have never once used anything in this place for private purposes.”

Jin Paecheon looked down at his own sword and added,

“This sword, too, is one I have used since my youth.”

He had not laid a hand on the spiritual medicines here, nor on the divine weapons.

“Because I considered these things not mine, but the Murim Alliance’s—an inheritance our forebears left behind to protect the orthodox murim.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke to Jin Paecheon with respect in his voice.

“It must be because you are such a person that you have been able to protect the orthodox murim until now.”

He meant it sincerely. If the Alliance Leader had been a man full of greed, his father would not have left that weakness untouched all this time.

Though he was also a grandfather whose heart wavered because of his grandchildren, Geom Mugeuk could tell that it was precisely because he possessed such a heart that he was an even stronger martial artist.

“I thought long and hard about how to repay you.”

Geom Mugeuk had told him that even without his help, he would have resolved things well enough—but would he really have? Would his sense of danger truly have activated toward a friend he had spent several decades with? He thought not.

Geom Mugeuk had saved his life and his grandchildren’s lives, and further, the fate of the Murim Alliance. Twice now, counting the previous time.

To be honest, he had wanted to say, As your reward, I shall make you my granddaughter’s husband.

Of course, theirs was not a relationship where that could be done, and even if it had been possible, he now knew that it could not be called a gift. Rather, if Geom Mugeuk became his grandson-in-law, Jin Paecheon would be the one receiving a gift.

“If you were an ordinary expert, I would have assessed your skill and talent and bestowed upon you a martial arts manual and a precious sword as I saw fit. But with you, I cannot do that.”

Jin Paecheon’s gaze turned toward the Murim Treasury.

“So you choose for yourself.”

Then he added something astonishing.

“Take out three things you want from this place.”

Geom Mugeuk was startled by the unexpected gift.

Not one, but three?

Jin Paecheon looked at the surprised Geom Mugeuk and gave a single nod.

“Alliance Leader, I am a greedy man, so I cannot refuse such an offer. If you were thinking, Surely this fellow will decline on his own, then you have underestimated my greed. Will he reduce three to one? No. I am the sort of greedy man who will be scheming to increase three to five.”

“It is a decision I made after much consideration. Take three.”

Only after confirming it like that did Geom Mugeuk rejoice.

“Thank you so much! Thank you, Alliance Leader!”

He was not merely pleased—he was truly delighted enough to leap. He jumped up and down right in front of the Alliance Leader.

“You like it that much?”

“Of course. Isn’t this a place I will never have another chance to visit in my life? Even just looking around would be amazing, so how could I not be happy when I can take three treasures? I’m truly happy! Treasures, just you wait! The incarnation of material desire is coming! I’m coming to take five!”

“Three!”

“Yes! I’m coming to take three!”

Seeing Geom Mugeuk so delighted, a smile formed at the corners of Jin Paecheon’s mouth.

Yes, this was far better than someone needlessly refusing when offered something, only to accept while pretending to be unable to win. He was rejoicing to his heart’s content, enough to make even the giver feel good.

“But why three?”

Geom Mugeuk asked, then guessed the answer himself.

“Is it because I rooted out the three people who had infiltrated deeply into the Murim Alliance?”

Jin Paecheon had not decided on three for any particular meaning. Telling him to take only one had seemed too coldhearted, so he had simply said three.

If he had to attach a reason, it would be more accurate to say it was because he had saved him, his grandson, and his granddaughter. One for each person.

“I should tell you in advance, I’m only going to take the very best things.”

“Do so.”

Jin Paecheon took out a large key, inserted it into the hole at the entrance, and turned it.

Ruuuumble.

Then the massive door slowly began to open.

“Then I’ll be back.”

“It is fine if it takes time, so look around at your leisure before coming out.”

“Thank you very much.”

After respectfully offering a cupped-fist salute, Geom Mugeuk went inside.

Beyond the door was a corridor, and at the end of that corridor stood another door.

Night pearls were embedded in the walls along the way, so it was not dark. Usually, cracked or low-grade night pearls were set in place to replace lamps, but here, they used very bright, high-quality night pearls.

It made his heart flutter from the very beginning.

His first goal here today was to obtain a bead that the Secret Coffer could absorb.

At the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, he had obtained the Secret Coffer, and beneath the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall, he had connected to three hundred years in the past and obtained the heart method formula for governing these energies.

The energies he had absorbed so far numbered three.

The Black Crystal from the Wind Heaven Cult, the Azure Crystal from the Evil Dao Alliance, and the White Crystal from the Northern Sea Ice Palace.

Last time, beneath the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall, he had seen the Secret Coffer and the beads painted on the wall. There were six energies the Secret Coffer would absorb. Black and white, blue and red, then yellow and purple.

Would there be an energy he could obtain from the Murim Alliance as well?

Arriving at the end of the corridor, Geom Mugeuk opened the door with a pounding heart and walked inside.

The first space of the Murim Treasury was where the treasures were stored.

Usually, what one saw most in places full of treasures were all sorts of items made of gold. Gifts sent by various merchant companies or sects—golden dragons, tigers, turtles, or toads, for instance—would normally fill a treasury.

But there was no such gold or jewels in the Murim Treasury.

There was only one kind of treasure here.

Poison-warding beads.

Expensive, top-grade poison-warding beads were lined up in abundance.

When he opened the boxes piled beneath them, they too were filled with poison-warding beads. Each box was packed with poison-warding beads by grade.

Geom Mugeuk understood. The Murim Alliance had converted all its treasures into poison-warding beads and stored them that way.

‘Ah, they’re preparing for war.’

At the same time, they were preparing for the Poison King as well.

If war broke out, the poison-warding beads here would immediately be distributed to every expert in the Alliance.

‘At least for this generation, you needn’t worry so much. Even if war breaks out, Father will not put the Poison King at the forefront.’

Passing through the room of nothing but poison-warding beads, he opened the door leading to the next room.

The moment he opened it, an enormous space revealed itself.

It was dozens of times larger than the place where the poison-warding beads had been.

The aura emanating from here was filled with the energies of the orthodox faction. If an ordinary expert of the Demon Cult or the unorthodox faction stepped inside, it would be enough to make them feel burdened by the righteous energy and chivalrous spirit that filled the place.

Geom Mugeuk slowly stepped inside.

This was the place where divine weapons and extraordinary arms were stored. It was so vast he could not see the end, and weapons hung on the walls in every direction, while countless display stands bearing weapons stood throughout the inner space.

Geom Mugeuk slowly walked forward, looking at them. They were mainly weapons once used by experts of the orthodox faction. That was why the place overflowed with orthodox energy.

Beneath each display stand were written the name of the weapon and the name of the expert who had used it, as well as detailed accounts of where and against whom that expert had fought with it.

This was not simply a storeroom.

It was an exhibition hall where one could learn the history of the murim itself.

He had entered many treasuries until now, but this was the first time he had seen one as well organized as the Murim Alliance’s.

The weapons were famous ones he had heard of at least once.

The sword that immediately caught Geom Mugeuk’s eye was one such example.

The name written beneath the display stand.

Nameless Sword.

It was called the Nameless Sword because it had no name, yet it was more famous than any sword that did.

It was the personal weapon of an absolute expert who had swept through the murim over two hundred years ago, known as the Nameless Guest.

‘I can’t just pass by the Nameless Sword.’

He carefully picked up the Nameless Sword from the display stand and drew it.

Shrrring.

The sharp aura emitted by the sword chilled the surroundings.

“How fine!”

Admiration came naturally.

Any martial artist who used a sword would be unable to tear their feet away from this blade.

Beside it rested a pipa.

It was a weapon for a martial artist who used sound arts.

Heavenly Sound Pipa.

It had been the personal weapon of Fairy Huaming, an absolute master of sound arts in the past.

It was said that when the Heavenly Sound Pipa rang out, those with weak internal energy would have their eardrums burst and their blood and qi flow backward, killing them instantly.

There was a reason the Nameless Sword and the Heavenly Sound Pipa were placed so close together. Fairy Huaming had lost her life in a battle against the Nameless Guest. Beneath the display stand, there was also an explanation of the two’s final battle.

After taking a few more steps, he saw a saber engraved as though a black dragon were coiling around its blade.

Ink Dragon Divine Saber.

“Ah! I haven’t even taken ten steps since entering this room, and there’s the Nameless Sword, the Heavenly Sound Pipa, and the Ink Dragon Divine Saber? And I can only choose three things from here? I want to take everything except three!”

If Jin Paecheon had heard him, he would have burst into laughter, but truly, the place was full of divine weapons and extraordinary arms that made him want to lie down on the floor and throw a tantrum.

Each time he took a step, history that had once shaken the murim revealed itself.

Demon-Sealing Chain.

This chain weapon had also once been the personal weapon of an orthodox expert who roamed the murim unhindered, and the White Qi Fan placed beside it was likewise an absolute weapon that decided life and death with a single wave. These two martial artists had been sworn brothers.

Geom Mugeuk’s knowledge and experience of the murim were broader and richer than anyone’s, so there were many weapons he recognized. But when he read the explanations, there were also many hidden stories even he did not know.

As he looked around, he found that, unexpectedly, there were some ordinary weapons too.

If the person who had used the weapon had been a great hero, or if the weapon had been used in a truly meaningful way, it had been displayed.

If one’s martial arts reached a certain realm, a single old iron sword, merely because it had been used by a martial artist one respected, would be more meaningful than any precious blade.

In that way, Geom Mugeuk continued walking as he looked over the weapons.

How long had he walked?

Suddenly, the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art activated. It was not because some immediate danger had been detected. Rather, it had reacted because an exceptionally powerful aura could be felt ahead.

‘What on earth is there?’

Geom Mugeuk walked toward it. The closer he drew, the more intense the aura became.

When he arrived, there was a wide-open space. Until now, weapons had been densely arranged like a go board, but here, in the broad space, there was only a single display stand, and on it rested one sword.

It was because of this sword that the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art had activated. The aura emitted by a single sword had caused the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art to manifest?

Now that he thought about it, this place was the exact center of the second area. The weapons were displayed in all directions around this sword.

A single sword resting there.

Geom Mugeuk slowly approached it. Intense righteous energy and chivalrous spirit could be felt from the sword.

The moment he saw the name written beneath it, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes widened in surprise.

Gentleman Sword.

Geom Mugeuk’s cry burst out.

“The Foremost Gentleman Sword of All Ages!”

That was the name by which the Gentleman Sword was called.

The Gentleman Sword was like a symbol of the orthodox murim.

It was said that if the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had the Heavenly Demon Sword, then the orthodox murim had the Gentleman Sword.

“Ah! So this sword is the Gentleman Sword.”

It was a sword Geom Mugeuk had only heard of in words.

The scabbard and hilt of the Gentleman Sword were not ornate. But the aura felt from the sword overwhelmed that of any other weapon.

Geom Mugeuk quietly stared at the Gentleman Sword.

First, he was surprised by Jin Paecheon. As the Murim Alliance Leader, he could have taken this Gentleman Sword and used it. But he had not.

It was likely because it was a sword that was like the symbol of the orthodox murim, containing its long history and tradition.

‘He must have known I might choose the Gentleman Sword.’

Had he believed that Geom Mugeuk would never choose it? Or had he considered it acceptable even if he took the Gentleman Sword?

In any case, since he had promised, if Geom Mugeuk said he would take this sword, Jin Paecheon would let him. He was not a man who broke promises.

“No, I even said I was the incarnation of material desire! What on earth did you trust in me to tell me to take whatever I wanted?”

He thought Jin Paecheon was remarkable.

Geom Mugeuk approached the Gentleman Sword until it was within reach.

“You must be lonely too, shut up in this place all the time.”

If it was a precious sword like the Gentleman Sword, it would want to meet a good master and reveal itself to the world.

Geom Mugeuk’s deliberation did not last long.

“Fine, come out with me!”

Geom Mugeuk made up his mind to take the Gentleman Sword with him.

For such a precious sword to remain only in a treasury was not right for the murim, nor for the sword itself.

Geom Mugeuk carefully took the Gentleman Sword in his hand. Even though he had not drawn it, he could feel its aura.

When he held the Gentleman Sword, the Black Demon Sword at his waist let out a low hum.

Wooooong.
