It had sensed the existence of the Gentleman Sword. It was the moment a precious sword recognized another precious sword. Geom Mugeuk soothed the Black Demon Sword.

“Don’t be tense. It’s all right.”

Geom Mugeuk did not draw the Gentleman Sword. It was not a sword he would use.

No matter that he had been promised he could take anything, and no matter that he had saved their lives, there were still things he must not covet.

“Something worth congratulating my friend for has happened.”

He intended to give the Gentleman Sword to Jin Hagun as a gift. He possessed a character perfectly suited to the sword’s name.

Given Jin Paecheon’s personality, even if Jin Hagun became the Alliance Leader later, he would not hand this sword over to his grandson. He was a man of integrity who had never touched anything in this place his entire life, so he would not be able to give the Gentleman Sword to his own blood.

‘So I will give it to him!’

Someday, there would surely come a day when he saw Jin Hagun display the Imperial Sword Form with the Gentleman Sword.

Geom Mugeuk fastened the Gentleman Sword at the side of his waist opposite the Black Demon Sword.

In this way, Geom Mugeuk chose the first of the three things he could take. He had said it jokingly to Jin Paecheon, but he had truly chosen the very best thing.

Jin Paecheon must have believed that he would not bring this out.

But you didn’t know this, did you?

“I’m sure you’ll like that friend too.”

Chapter 670: Isn't It About Time to Eat One?
--------------------------------------------
With the Gentleman’s Sword at his waist, Geom Mugeuk continued looking around the place.

It was because he thought the Bigwe might possibly sound. The bead might have been hidden among the weapons, or inside a display cabinet drawer.

But even after he had examined every weapon there, the Bigwe did not sound.

Geom Mugeuk walked toward the door leading to the next room. Before it, he turned back and gave a greeting.

“Thank you for letting me look.”

It was a greeting to the souls of the warriors who had used those weapons.

After all, this exhibition hall valued the lives of the warriors who had wielded them more than the weapons themselves.

Geom Mugeuk opened the door and stepped inside.

This time, the room was narrower than the one with the weapons, but compared to the first room, it was still extremely spacious.

Protective armor was on display here.

As before, there were descriptions of the protective armor and records of those who had used them.

Geom Mugeuk slowly looked around.

“Oh, I’m seeing the Robe of Elegance in person!”

It was the protective armor of Yeongaek, the flirtatious swordsman once popular among female knights. This garment, with a beautiful woman painted on its back, was the long robe that symbolized him, and ordinary sword qi could not cut through that beauty.

“I’m jealous! One day, I too!”

With that half-serious, half-joking remark, he began properly looking at the protective armor.

There truly were all kinds.

“So this is what the Silver Scale Armor looks like!”

Its silver scales glittered so brightly they were almost blinding. Of course, its practicality looked rather lacking.

“Tyrant Dragon Armor! Splendid, truly splendid.”

A roaring dragon was painted across the chest, and if one wore this before enemies, it seemed like its sheer presence alone would be enough to intimidate them.

And the finest protective armor here, by far, was this.

“Heavenly Divine Armor!”

It was known as protective armor so light and elastic that wearing it felt like wearing nothing at all.

But one could not look down on it for being thin. It was protective armor that even sword qi or sword aura could not cut, truly one that vied for the very best.

“You bastards, don’t even think about fooling me from now on!”

Since the genuine article was stored here, if any weapon or protective armor said to have come from this place ever appeared on the market, it would all be fake.

But still, a martial artist would just leave this behind? The Heavenly Divine Armor?

However, he had already decided not to choose protective armor today. With precisely this thought:

“Yes, training is the best protective armor.”

Comforting himself that way, he moved on with reluctant steps.

As Geom Mugeuk continued looking over the remaining protective armor, something made him stop.

The Ice Dragon Divine Armor.

The moment he saw it, Geom Mugeuk recalled that time three hundred years ago.

This Ice Dragon Divine Armor was something the Lord of the Dark Palace had gifted to the Lord of Cheonui Palace along with the Heavenly Yin Pill. Geom Mugeuk had watched the scene that day.

At the time, the Martial Emperor Divine Sword, whom the Lord of Cheonui Palace had to fight, possessed extreme yang internal energy, so it had been given to help her prepare for him. But Geom Mugeuk had judged it to be a trap.

The Lord of the Dark Palace wanted the Lord of Cheonui Palace to lose.

He could not be certain, since he had not witnessed that day’s battle directly, but he guessed that an extreme yin attack had been unleashed against the Lord of Cheonui Palace, who had prepared for an extreme yang attack.

The Martial Emperor Divine Sword could not have been the only one in that battle. At that time, the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had definitely been involved as well.

He looked around to see if there was anything else nearby related to what had happened then. And then, another object caught his eye.

‘Ah! This is?’

It was not protective armor.

It was simply one long piece of cloth.

Dream of the Divine Maiden.

Even without seeing the name written beneath it, Geom Mugeuk knew.

‘It’s the cloth the Divine Maiden used to cover her eyes.’

The Divine Maiden he had met in the world three hundred years ago had covered her eyes with this cloth.

He recalled the prophecy she had spoken.

Darkness will lead you to Heaven’s will.

At the time, he had interpreted her prophecy as saying he would be able to find the whereabouts of the great Bigwe through the Dark Palace.

But now that he had confirmed their descendants had continued on for three hundred years to this day, the prophecy felt different.

That the battle against this hidden force was his Heaven’s will.

Geom Mugeuk slowly picked up the cloth named Dream of the Divine Maiden.

“!”

The moment he grasped the cloth in his hand, Geom Mugeuk felt a certain energy.

It was not the sort of energy one could feel from a person’s aura or martial arts. This energy was auspicious and mysterious, entirely different from those things.

Was it because he had received a prophecy from the Divine Maiden? Geom Mugeuk could clearly feel this energy that had remained even across three hundred years.

Geom Mugeuk instinctively knew. This energy was not meant for him, nor was it meant for anyone else.

‘It is meant for one person alone.’

In that moment, Geom Mugeuk thought of someone.

The face of the one person he felt he had to give this to.

‘If meeting the sacred relic left behind by the Divine Maiden here is also fate?’

Geom Mugeuk took the Dream of the Divine Maiden without hesitation. This was Geom Mugeuk’s second choice.

Both his first and second choices had been for others.

‘For the last one, let’s choose something for myself.’

Having made up his mind, Geom Mugeuk headed for the next room.

The moment he opened the third door, he could tell what kind of room this was from the scent that wafted out.

That medicinal fragrance, always pleasant no matter when he smelled it—this was the place where spirit medicines were stored.

“Wow! This is incredible.”

Countless spirit medicines were spread out before his eyes.

The first thing that caught his eye was the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng.

Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng were displayed in individual boxes.

Had he ever seen so many Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng gathered in one place? Truly, this was the first time.

With Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng here, the Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng displayed separately beside them felt like mere bellflower roots.

And that was not all. Beside them, Nine Yang Divine Pills, which bestowed extreme yang internal energy, were on display, while across from them, Nine Yin Divine Pills were arranged as if to say extreme yin internal energy was on this side.

In front of those was the Red Heart Pill. There were three or four of a spirit medicine that most people would struggle to taste even once in their lives.

And was that all?

Great Yang Pills and Lesser Yang Pills, Taiji Heart-Calming Pills and Cloud-Accumulating Pills, Bright Clarity Pills… truly, every kind of spirit medicine was here. And they were impeccably organized.

Seeing this, he could freshly understand just how extraordinary it was that Jin Paecheon had allowed him into this place.

What if he ate all these spirit medicines? What if he devoured them all like a madman and ran away?

If there was even the slightest possibility that such a thing might happen, he could never have allowed him in.

Yet the fact that he had let him in meant he truly trusted him.

‘The next room is probably where the secret manuals are. I don’t need martial arts anyway, so I should choose a spirit medicine here.’

He already possessed such vast internal energy that one might ask if he really needed more, but internal energy was also something where the more one had, the better.

There was no need to agonize over which spirit medicine to choose. As he walked along the spirit medicines, he encountered the ruler of this room at its very center.

“Gongcheong Stone Milk!”

The pinnacle of all spirit medicines!

There truly was a pure white bottle containing Gongcheong Stone Milk.

When he carefully opened the stopper and looked inside, there seemed to be several drops of milky-white Gongcheong Stone Milk. Even a single drop had effects superior to any spirit medicine in this place.

“I was planning to eat it all by myself, but you really are blessed with luck!”

He was speaking to his older brother. He had decided to split it with him. What reason was there to give him all of it? Ah, lately, he did like him somewhat. Still, he ought to be grateful to receive even half.

“Alliance Leader, I clearly told you when I came in. I said I would bring out only the best.”

Thus, Geom Mugeuk had chosen all three things he needed to select from this place: the Gentleman’s Sword, the Dream of the Divine Maiden, and the Gongcheong Stone Milk.

Now what remained was finding the bead for the Bigwe to absorb. To Geom Mugeuk, that was even more important than this Gongcheong Stone Milk.

He carefully looked through the room containing the spirit medicines, but the Bigwe showed no response.

Geom Mugeuk opened the next door and entered.

As expected, the final room was packed full of martial arts secret manuals.

Dozens of large bookshelves were filled with precious manuals the Martial Alliance had gathered over its long history.

“Ah, it smells good.”

It was a pleasant scent in a meaning different from medicinal fragrance—the scent of books.

After becoming acting cult leader, Geom Mugeuk had become immersed in reading at the Heavenly Demon Library. Now, he felt he understood a little of what the joy of reading was.

“What kind of books are here?”

Geom Mugeuk slowly examined the shelves as he moved.

They were mainly filled with martial arts of the righteous sects. As for demonic arts or unorthodox arts, they had mostly collected martial arts meant to counter them.

He could feel their will and confidence that righteous martial arts would certainly suppress demonic and unorthodox arts.

If righteous faction warriors saw them, there were peerless divine arts enough to make them faint, practically every other volume. Geom Mugeuk did not take them out to read. To him now, the only martial art was the Guhwa Demonic Art.

Thus, Geom Mugeuk slowly moved along, passing between shelf after shelf.

‘Bigwe, sound! Please, sound! You’re hungry too, aren’t you? Isn’t it about time you ate one?’

But even after he had gone around all the shelves, the Bigwe did not sound.

Geom Mugeuk forced himself to shake off his disappointment.

‘Well, that can happen.’

Geom Mugeuk was not in a hurry. From the beginning, it was not as though he had desperately struggled to gather all the power of the beads. He had simply followed the flow of fate faithfully, and had happened to gather three.

“No! That can’t happen! I have to find it!”

Geom Mugeuk returned to the first room.

He began again from the start, examining everything meticulously as he made his way inward.

From the Poison-Warding Pearl room to the weapons room, then from the protective armor room to the spirit medicine room. Finally, to the secret manual room.

He searched every place thoroughly, but the Bigwe did not respond.

‘Ah! There was no bead in the Martial Alliance report.’

He had somehow felt there would be one.

It was regrettable, but he should still say goodbye before leaving.

“Thank you for letting me look.”

He was about to simply go outside, but then Geom Mugeuk looked back around the library.

Just as he had said in his farewell, he had looked through the other places well, but here he was leaving without reading even a single book.

“I may not look it, but I’m someone who has read quite a few books lately.”

Geom Mugeuk picked out the thinnest book from a nearby shelf.

“Since coming in here is a commemoration in itself, I should read at least one before I leave.”

In a corner of the library, a small chair was set against the wall, as if for someone to sit on while reading.

It was when he approached that chair.

Wuuung.

The Bigwe sounded from his chest.

Geom Mugeuk was startled. He had not expected the Bigwe, which had not sounded no matter where he searched, to sound here.

Come to think of it, he had only gone between the shelves; he had not come all the way to this corner!

There was nothing here but one chair. The chair was also so small there was no way a bead could be hidden in it.

“There’s nothing here.”

In that instant, Geom Mugeuk flinched.

“No, there is!”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the night-shining pearl embedded in the wall behind the chair.

It was a night-shining pearl set there for reading.

‘That’s it!’

Sure enough, as he approached the night-shining pearl, the Bigwe vibrated even more strongly.

Geom Mugeuk removed the night-shining pearl with great care.

Then, from the space behind where the night-shining pearl had been, something rolled out with a clatter.

What rolled out was a single bead.

A yellow bead—it was Hwangjeong.

Geom Mugeuk cheered.

“I found it!”

Had he found it from the beginning, he would not have been this happy. But finding it after giving up because it seemed absent made him overjoyed.

There was a reason he had thought nothing in particular of the corner chair and the night-shining pearl above it.

Night-shining pearls were embedded here and there in every room. From the corridor at the entrance, high-quality night-shining pearls had been set into the walls.

Because they were so common in every room, he had treated even this night-shining pearl in the farthest corner of the final library as nothing special.

Geom Mugeuk took the Bigwe from his breast and placed Hwangjeong upon it.

Ssssss.

As always, the Bigwe absorbed Hwangjeong.

Soon after, the Bigwe vibrated greatly, and a golden energy began flowing out from it.

The energy of Hwangjeong that flowed out of the Bigwe spread from Geom Mugeuk’s hand through his entire body and began to be absorbed.

Shooooo.

The energy shimmered like light and spread like waves.

The process felt so good. It was like drinking cool water when one’s thirst had reached its peak, giving him an indescribable sense of refreshment.

Geom Mugeuk naturally began circulating the chant he had learned before.

Then the energies of Heukjeong, Cheongjeong, and Baekjeong, which had been sleeping inside his body, awakened.

These three energies began moving in a way completely different from ordinary internal cultivation methods, welcoming the newly joining energy of Hwangjeong.

When the four energies raced through the blood vessels of his entire body together, Geom Mugeuk experienced something new.

His whole body became as light as a feather, as if he might fly up into the sky.

At the final moment of the chant, the four energies merged into one.

In that instant, he felt the tremendous power contained within that energy. This power was different from the might granted by ordinary internal energy.

However, the energies could not fully become one, and scattered again, quietly falling asleep throughout his blood vessels.

Geom Mugeuk slowly opened his eyes.

“Haa.”

Now he had perfectly absorbed even the energy of Hwangjeong.

Two colors remained.

Only the red and purple beads were left.

Where could they be?

What would happen if he gathered all the energies?

As he was briefly immersed in the lingering impression of that mysterious energy—

Clack.

The Bigwe spat out the bead whose energy it had absorbed. The Hwangjeong that rolled out had now become an ordinary pebble.

“Did you eat well?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the Bigwe merely smiled as always.

Geom Mugeuk placed the energyless Hwangjeong back into its original hole and inserted the night-shining pearl again.

Then he sat there and read the book he had brought. It was writing left behind by an old master who had longed for his hometown. As he read, he missed his father, and the people at the cult headquarters.

A little while later, Geom Mugeuk returned the book he had finished to its original place.

If he had not thought to read one book before leaving, he would never have found Hwangjeong. After all, he would never have occasion to enter this place again.

Long live the Heavenly Demon Library! Long live Geom Mugeuk, the bookworm!

Geom Mugeuk gathered the three divine items and began walking toward the entrance where Jin Paecheon was waiting.

“Thank you for letting me read!”

Chapter 671: I Will Send You First
----------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk emerged from the Murim Treasury.

Jin Paecheon was waiting with his back turned to the entrance.

“Alliance Leader, I’m out.”

What had he come out with? Jin Paecheon was curious about that choice.

The moment he turned to look at Geom Mugeuk, Jin Paecheon went blank for the briefest instant. Had this been in actual combat, his life would have been in danger; for that moment, his mind was utterly empty.

‘The Gentleman Sword?’

Then came a surge of astonishment. He merely did not show it outwardly; in truth, he was shocked. He had nearly let out an involuntary gasp.

“These are the three things I chose.”

Laid across Geom Mugeuk’s outstretched arms was the Gentleman Sword.

And atop the Gentleman Sword sat a bottle containing Gongcheong Stone Milk, while beside it hung the Divine Maiden’s Dream.

Struck speechless, Jin Paecheon could not say anything for a while.

‘He came out with the Gentleman Sword?’

From the start, he had never imagined that Geom Mugeuk would bring out the Gentleman Sword. The thought had not even occurred to him.

The Gentleman Sword was practically the symbol of the orthodox faction.

It was a sword even he had only looked upon, never drawn.

‘Is what’s before me truly the Gentleman Sword?’

He looked again, and it was the Gentleman Sword.

“Do you know what kind of sword that is?”

He asked because he wondered if, by any chance, Geom Mugeuk had brought it without knowing.

“Of course.”

Geom Mugeuk looked down at the Gentleman Sword and said,

“It is the foremost Gentleman Sword of all ages.”

His gaze, which had been on the sword, returned to Jin Paecheon.

“I brought out the best sword among swords.”

No matter that he had told him to choose what he wanted, to think he had brought out the Gentleman Sword?

Jin Paecheon was dumbfounded and flustered. So much so that he did not know how to take this situation. Why had he never considered the possibility that Geom Mugeuk might choose this?

Jin Paecheon’s gaze moved back to the Gongcheong Stone Milk.

The most precious of all elixirs.

Perhaps because the Gentleman Sword had shocked him too much, that one was rather less shocking.

Yes, he could give him the Gongcheong Stone Milk. He had saved his grandchildren, and beyond that, saved the fate of the Murim Alliance.

But the Gentleman Sword was the sword that symbolized the orthodox faction. That sword entering the hands of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?

If the orthodox martial artists learned of this, there would be an uproar. Perhaps the repercussions would be even greater than the revelation that Cheonae Geosa had been a villain.

‘I must say no, even now.’

Jin Paecheon’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk. He was looking at him with a face full of mischief, as if he found something so very delightful. Yes, of course he would be delighted.

“What is that cloth?”

“It is a sacred object named the Divine Maiden’s Dream.”

Geom Mugeuk handed Jin Paecheon the Divine Maiden’s Dream that had been draped over the sword.

“It is a relic of the Divine Maiden Palace, one of the three organizations that guarded Cheonui Palace three hundred years ago.”

As Jin Paecheon examined the Divine Maiden’s Dream, he could find nothing particularly special about it.

‘It has historical meaning, but it is nothing more than a cloth with no special effect.’

From Jin Paecheon’s expression and reaction, Geom Mugeuk could tell. That sacred energy was something only he could sense.

“May I ask why you chose this?”

“There is someone I want to give it to.”

Jin Paecheon returned the Divine Maiden’s Dream to him. He did not ask who it was.

That was not what mattered. Now he had to make a decision. Once they left this place, there would be no turning back.

But in the end, Jin Paecheon did not say that only the Gentleman Sword was unacceptable.

A promise was a promise.

And it was no ordinary promise. It was a promise made by the Murim Alliance Leader to the temporary cult leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“It was an excellent choice.”

Yes, how could it be a wrong decision for a swordsman to choose the Gentleman Sword?

Jin Paecheon shook off his lingering attachment and regret, and sincerely congratulated Geom Mugeuk.

He resolved in his heart that he would not regret this choice.

Not just the Gentleman Sword—he would not have begrudged giving him every treasure and sacred object in that place. After all, he had saved his grandchildren’s lives.

If giving up the Gentleman Sword became a problem, he would gladly bear the responsibility himself. This matter was his problem, not Geom Mugeuk’s.

Now was the time to express his gratitude sincerely.

“I am grateful for what happened this time.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Jin Paecheon with an moved expression, then bowed his head respectfully.

“Please remain healthy for a long, long time and protect us.”

“Come now, man. Is that something the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult should say to the Murim Alliance Leader?”

The two looked at each other and laughed.

“Let us go back now.”

“Yes, Alliance Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk placed the Divine Maiden’s Dream and the Gongcheong Stone Milk in his robes, and wore the Gentleman Sword at his waist.

Though Jin Paecheon had firmly made up his mind to give it to him, his gaze went to the Gentleman Sword without him realizing it. Attachment still remained. As he felt his heart begin to seethe again—

‘Ah! I suppose I cannot be a gentleman after all!’

As he took a step, Jin Paecheon let out a wry laugh.

Ssssss—

Just as when they had entered, the fog within the formation cleared on its own and formed a path.

“Will you be returning now?”

“No. As I said before, I plan to call my friends, hold a celebration banquet for Hagun, and then return.”

When he first heard it, Jin Paecheon had thought it was just something he was saying. But it seemed he truly meant to hold a celebration banquet.

“If someone doesn’t raise a flag and shout, ‘Gather here,’ we’re the sort who might never see each other forever. And I’m usually the one raising that flag.”

Jin Paecheon knew how difficult that truly was. Even he was like that himself.

I am Jin Paecheon, so why should I? If you want to see me, you should contact me first.

To be honest, it would be a lie to say he did not have such thoughts.

“Why are you so intent on gathering together?”

“Because I like it. I like my friends. I like Hagun, I like Young Alliance Leader Bi of the Sado Alliance, and I like my other friends too. Seeing them is fun and enjoyable. That’s why I raise the flag. Of course, Alliance Leader, you may suspect that flag is a Charge Flag (突擊旗) for the battlefield, but it is merely a ha-ha-ho-ho Gathering Flag (集結旗) saying, ‘Everyone come here.’”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Jin Paecheon smiled faintly.

“Do you not have friends like that, Alliance Leader?”

Even knowing full well what had happened with Cheonae Geosa, Geom Mugeuk asked.

As always, it was Geom Mugeuk’s effort to bury the corpse, but not allow the wound in the heart to be buried. If he had sent off one friend, then he ought to make a new one.

“Why not raise a flag yourself, even now?”

Jin Paecheon looked at Geom Mugeuk in surprise. He was telling him to raise the flag himself?

“If you raise a flag, Alliance Leader, all of Murim will be startled, so you should write it in large letters on the flag instead: There is absolutely no other meaning. This is a flag for playing!”

Jin Paecheon laughed aloud. Truly, the only person who could make him laugh like this was this Geom Mugeuk. Even while wearing that Gentleman Sword at his waist, no less.

“If I raise a flag, who would come?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk mentioned people he had never expected.

“There’s my father, isn’t there? And the Sado Alliance Leader as well.”

“!”

As though he meant it sincerely, Geom Mugeuk added,

“They might be secretly waiting for someone to raise the flag for them.”

With an expression that said Geom Mugeuk was impossible to deal with, Jin Paecheon began walking again.

He thought it absurd, but suddenly recalled the three-party meeting.

It had been more enjoyable than he expected. Just as the thought that it might be good to gather once more crossed his mind, Jin Paecheon flinched.

‘What nonsense am I thinking!’

So the two came out of the formation and walked together through the inner court, and at the place where Jin Haryeong had parted from them earlier, someone was waiting.

“Alliance Leader.”

The person who respectfully greeted Jin Paecheon was none other than Jin Hagun.

When he had entered the training hall, he had ordered his subordinate: if Geom Mugeuk came to see the Alliance Leader, he was to be called without fail.

It was so he could see his face before Geom Mugeuk returned to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Jin Hagun asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Did you choose good gifts?”

He had heard from Jin Haryeong that his grandfather had taken Geom Mugeuk to the Murim Treasury.

“I came out with only the most incredible things.”

Jin Hagun looked at the Gentleman Sword worn on the opposite side from the Black Demon Sword. Since he had never seen it in person, he did not recognize that it was the Gentleman Sword.

One thing was certain: no matter what treasure Geom Mugeuk took, it would not be a waste.

“I was grateful this time.”

He knew that the reason he had been able to become the successor was all thanks to Geom Mugeuk.

“Are you returning right away?”

He naturally thought Geom Mugeuk would now return to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“I’m not going.”

“What?”

“I’m going to hold your celebration banquet before I leave. You became the successor, so of course we have to hold a banquet. I’ve already called all our friends.”

“Which friends?”

“You don’t even have that many friends. Why ask?”

Flustered, Jin Hagun looked at his grandfather. Jin Paecheon merely watched the two of them in silence.

“Why? You don’t like it?”

“It’s not that I don’t like it.”

To be honest, he felt good. This celebration banquet would surely be a gathering of those who congratulated him with the greatest sincerity.

“Now, and this.”

Geom Mugeuk unfastened the Gentleman Sword at his waist and held it out to him.

“My gift to commemorate you becoming the successor.”

At that moment, the one who was truly shocked was Jin Paecheon. It was beyond surprise; it was a shock. He had never dreamed that Geom Mugeuk would give the Gentleman Sword to his grandson as a gift.

Jin Hagun waved his hands and refused.

“No need. What gift?”

In that instant, Jin Paecheon made a strained expression.

Geom Mugeuk slyly teased him.

“You’ll regret it later.”

“I won’t regret it. It’s your gift, so you take it.”

Geom Mugeuk was about to wear the Gentleman Sword at his waist again. Jin Paecheon watched the sight with eyes full of lingering attachment.

Just as Geom Mugeuk was about to put it back on, he held the Gentleman Sword out to Jin Hagun again.

“Take it. I chose it to give to you. Don’t ignore a friend’s sincerity.”

Jin Hagun, who had been looking at Geom Mugeuk, turned to his grandfather. Since his grandfather had been the one to give the gift, he wondered whether it was all right to accept this.

His grandfather said nothing, only stared at the sword. Somehow, he seemed tense, unlike usual. He was not someone who would grow tense even before thousands of enemies.

Geom Mugeuk held the sword out in front of Jin Hagun.

“I received other good gifts from the Alliance Leader. So don’t feel burdened and take it. It’s nothing special, but if we drag this out for no reason, I’ll be the only one embarrassed. Hurry!”

“Thank you. Then I will accept it gratefully.”

In the end, Jin Hagun accepted the Gentleman Sword.

The moment he took the sword—

“!”

Jin Hagun’s eyes changed. The instant he held it, he knew.

‘This is no ordinary sword.’

Jin Hagun slowly drew the Gentleman Sword.

Shrrrng.

At last, the Gentleman Sword revealed itself to the world.

It was the Gentleman Sword, showing itself once more after crossing the long passage of time.

“Ah!”

An exclamation flowed naturally from Jin Hagun’s mouth.

The sharpness flowing along the blade was something he had never seen in his life. Truly, it seemed as though it might cut even the heart of the one who looked upon it.

He could feel the sword’s breath.

A deep and elegant breath of the sword.

It was the first time he had ever felt such a thing from a sword.

He had not seen every sword in the world, but still, he could tell. This sword was the finest of swords.

Jin Hagun slowly infused the Gentleman Sword with his inner force, greeting it and telling it what kind of person he was.

As if answering that greeting—

Wooooong!

The sword rang out briefly.

Thus their first communion ended, and he returned the sword to its scabbard.

Jin Hagun’s heart pounded as if it would burst.

“You said it was nothing special?”

At Jin Hagun’s question, Geom Mugeuk smiled and replied,

“It doesn’t suit me. I’m far too distant from being a gentleman.”

A gentleman?

In that instant, Jin Hagun’s eyes grew wider and wider, and he shouted,

“The Gentleman Sword!”

At his startled cry, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“Yes, it is the Gentleman Sword.”

Jin Hagun was astonished. How could he not know of the foremost Gentleman Sword of all ages?

That legendary sword was now in his hands under the name of a gift.

“The Alliance Leader gave me three items from the Murim Treasury as gifts.”

What Geom Mugeuk said from here on was meant for both Jin Paecheon and Jin Hagun.

“He has remained the Alliance Leader all his life and never laid a hand on a single item in the treasury. Considering his upright character, the Gentleman Sword would continue to be kept there in the future too, wouldn’t it?”

That was true. Jin Paecheon had never had any intention of bringing the Gentleman Sword out into the world.

“You grew up under such a grandfather. Would you be any different? The Gentleman Sword would be stored in the treasury in the next generation too. So I caused a little trouble. I thought that unless I caused trouble, that sword would never get to see the world. Well? How was it, greeting the Gentleman Sword? It’s happy to have escaped that stuffy place, isn’t it?”

Jin Hagun did not know what to say.

“Truly, you are…!”

He was right. If not for him, it was a sword he never would have been able to possess.

Moreover, it was a gift given by giving up one of the three things he could have had.

Jin Hagun looked at his grandfather. At the look in his eyes asking whether he truly could accept this sword, Jin Paecheon nodded.

Jin Paecheon did not reveal his emotions outwardly, but he was feeling an immense joy. He was far happier than he would have been if it had simply returned to the treasure vault. The Gentleman Sword would protect his grandson more reliably than anything else in the world.

Of course, the happiest person of all was Jin Hagun. What could be better for a martial artist than obtaining a fine weapon? And if that weapon was the Gentleman Sword?

“You like it, don’t you? You like it so much you could dance, don’t you?”

He liked it enough that he nearly fell into that trap. He had almost answered yes.

“I heard the Gentleman Sword is the history of the orthodox faction. From now on, my friend will be the one to carry on writing that history.”

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Mugeuk, who was rejoicing as if it were his own affair, with trembling eyes.

He could not say a word. No words could express this gratitude.

Jin Paecheon asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Why did you not say so earlier?”

Not knowing that this was how he felt, he had worried for nothing that the Gentleman Sword would pass into the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“I did want to surprise you, but more than that, it was because I trusted you, Alliance Leader.”

“You trusted me?”

“Yes. Though I had brought out something that absolutely must not be taken, you did not stop me in any way. I believed you would do so, and you kept your promise to the very end. Now, in my heart, you have become an even greater presence.”

Jin Paecheon looked at Geom Mugeuk in silence. Leaving aside the orthodox, unorthodox, and demonic factions, leaving aside status, as one martial artist—no, as one human being—he could only think that he was truly astonishing.

“Then I will be going.”

After Geom Mugeuk respectfully took his leave of the Alliance Leader, he said to Jin Hagun,

“It’ll take some time for everyone to arrive. I’ll send you the place and time later.”

Jin Paecheon and Jin Hagun stood for a long while, watching his back as he ran off in high spirits.

When Geom Mugeuk could no longer be seen, Jin Hagun spoke calmly.

“There were moments when I gave up. I thought, I can’t beat that friend. No matter how hard I try, I won’t be able to win.”

Jin Paecheon sensed that this was not the end of it, and asked quietly,

“And what about now?”

“I’m not giving up. I intend to let him go on ahead.”

At the questioning look in his eyes, Jin Hagun smiled.

“He’s such an outstanding friend, so I should let him go first. Instead, I’ll follow him in silence.”

It was by no means resignation. Nor was it a determination that he must win at all costs.

It was the will to walk together with Geom Mugeuk. Even if he was a little late, he would silently walk the same era as him.

“Someday, far in the future, I may see that friend sitting on a rock with white hair, waiting for me. Then I’ll tell him. Thanks to you, I made it this far.”

Jin Paecheon thought that the grandson who had always seemed so young had grown so much before he knew it.

He had always only worried. About the martial world Jin Hagun would lead, about the grandson who would have to face that Geom Mugeuk.

But today, a different thought came to him.

Now was not the time to worry about these children, but to cheer them on.

As Jin Paecheon began walking toward the Alliance Leader’s Hall, he said,

“When you meet him then, be sure to tell him. Tell him I was beside myself with worry that he might take the Gentleman Sword.”

Chapter 672: Why Are You So Good? Why Are You So Good!
------------------------------------------------------
Geom Muyang was in his quarters, cultivating the Heavenly Demon Guardian Art.

It was a martial art his younger brother had passed on to him, even breaking the sect rules for his sake. How could he possibly neglect it? Whenever he had time, he devoted himself solely to cultivating the Heavenly Demon Guardian Art.

Geom Muyang, who had been absorbed in training, opened his closed eyes and halted the Heavenly Demon Guardian Art.

He had sensed someone entering the house. In the past, he would have noticed it later than this, but even though it had not been long since he learned it, the effect was already showing.

When Geom Mugeuk entered the room, Geom Muyang was standing by the window.

“You’re still young. Why do you keep looking out the window?”

“Then are windows only for old people to look out of?”

“Young people don’t look. They can’t stand it and run outside.”

“That’s prejudice.”

Geom Mugeuk walked over and stood side by side with Geom Muyang.

“How did it go?”

“I met the Alliance Leader just fine.”

Had there ever been an age, since the murim came into being, when the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult could meet the Murim Alliance Leader so easily? His younger brother was probably the first.

“Are you hungry right now, by any chance?”

When Geom Muyang shook his head, Geom Mugeuk checked again.

“This is important. If you’re hungry, you might not be able to resist and gulp it down.”

What kind of nonsense is he about to say now? He looked at his younger brother with that kind of gaze.

“Now then, I bet this is the first time you’ve seen this in your life.”

Geom Mugeuk took a white bottle from his robe. The reason he had come running in such high spirits was inside that bottle.

As Geom Mugeuk slowly opened the stopper, an indescribably pleasant fragrance flowed out.

Geom Muyang, who had been quietly peering into the bottle, was startled.

“Don’t tell me that’s Gongcheong Stone Milk?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“Say hello! This is the cult leader and alliance leader of the elixir world. This is my good-for-nothing older brother, Cult Leader, Alliance Leader.”

First, it overwhelmed him with its scent. Truly, he had never in his life encountered such a sweet fragrance.

“Where did you get it?”

It was such an extraordinary elixir that even the taciturn Geom Muyang’s voice trembled.

“The Alliance Leader gave it to me, saying thanks for helping with this matter. He told me to split it with you. He also said to make sure I conveyed his thanks to you.”

Geom Muyang gave a faint laugh.

There was no way that had happened. There was even less chance he had told him to share something this precious with him.

“I’m fine. You drink it.”

“I can’t do that. He gave it to me to share.”

“Shall I confirm that with the Alliance Leader?”

“If you don’t believe me, go ahead.”

Of course, he could not confirm it. With Geom Muyang’s personality, he was not the sort of person who would go to the Alliance Leader and ask, Did you really tell him to share it with me?

“Forget it. Even if he did, you drink it all.”

“If you don’t take any, I’ll throw it away.”

“Do as you like.”

“You think I can’t?”

Geom Mugeuk made as if to fling it onto the floor.

Of course, he could not.

“Whew, I’m so afraid it’ll spill that I can’t even pretend to tilt it.”

Geom Mugeuk held the bottle up carefully with both hands.

Geom Muyang turned back toward the window.

A younger brother who wanted to share Gongcheong Stone Milk with him?

When had their relationship become like this? It felt like only yesterday that they had hated each other enough to talk of killing and sparing. In truth, the emotions from back then were still vivid. That was why moments like this sometimes felt unfamiliar.

Then Geom Mugeuk’s low voice came from behind him.

“I’m afraid.”

When Geom Muyang turned around, the playfulness had vanished from Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“I’m afraid I won’t be able to stop the bastards behind all this. As you saw this time, they’re persistent enough to endure and prepare for decades, aren’t they? What if someone even more vicious appears, and I can’t handle him?”

Geom Muyang stared at his younger brother for a moment, then abruptly said,

“Are you fighting alone?”

As if he had been waiting for those words, Geom Mugeuk held out the bottle containing the Gongcheong Stone Milk.

“That’s why I’m saying we should drink it together and fight together. I’m not giving it to you for your sake. I’m giving it to you for mine. So you can protect me. If you keep refusing, I’ll issue an order as acting Cult Leader.”

How could he stop someone who went this far?

Geom Muyang said calmly,

“Then just one drop! No more.”

“Fine. Even if I wanted to give you more, there are only a few drops.”

“Let me drink it and leave the rest.”

“I’m eating mine later.”

When Geom Muyang held out his hand, Geom Mugeuk shook his head and said,

“Open your mouth.”

“What?”

“How can I trust you with this? What if you say you’ll drink just one drop, then swallow it all in one go and call it a mistake?”

Geom Muyang looked dumbfounded.

“I’d rather not drink it.”

“Then you’re admitting my guess is right. You were trying to gulp it all down and failed.”

Geom Muyang glared at Geom Mugeuk with an expression that said, Is this bastard really insane?

Geom Mugeuk shamelessly faced him down.

“If you’re innocent, open your mouth.”

In the end, Geom Muyang parted his lips slightly.

“Don’t close your mouth carelessly! If it spills, we’re ruined.”

After emphasizing that, Geom Mugeuk tilted the bottle without hesitation.

Drip, drip, drip!

Three drops fell in succession. When the first drop entered, there was no time to close his mouth or anything of the sort.

To the flustered Geom Muyang, Geom Mugeuk shouted,

“Talk later and circulate your qi first!”

It was not a situation where he could be stubborn, so Geom Muyang immediately sat cross-legged and began dissolving the medicinal effect.

Geom Mugeuk poured all the remaining Gongcheong Stone Milk into his mouth.

Drip! Drip!

There were two drops of Gongcheong Stone Milk left.

Thus the two brothers sat side by side and dissolved the power of the Gongcheong Stone Milk within their bodies.

No protector was needed. Geom Mugeuk’s realm had now reached the point where, even if a problem occurred, he could briefly stop dissolving the medicinal effect, respond to it, then resume.

Geom Muyang was experiencing something new.

Even one drop contained tremendous energy, yet this was three whole drops! At the vast power he felt for the first time in his life, all his blood vessels opened their paths at once.

Following the flow of his true qi, the energy of the Gongcheong Stone Milk began to melt into his blood vessels.

Saaaaak, saaaaak!

Each time it dissolved, powerful energy was transmitted into his blood vessels. The thrill of that moment could not be expressed in words.

Geom Muyang dissolved the energy carefully, yet boldly.

When all three drops of Gongcheong Stone Milk had been absorbed, that energy rode through his blood vessels and raced fiercely.

It was a charge. A tremendous surge of power unlike anything he had ever experienced.

The energy that had been rampaging wildly gathered into his dantian and merged with his existing internal energy.

The next instant!

As if exploding, the grand internal energy filled his dantian to the brim, and rapture spread across Geom Muyang’s face. Of all the pleasures he had ever felt in relation to martial arts, the joy he felt now was greater than any of them.

After calmly gathering and settling his internal energy, Geom Muyang opened his eyes.

Grand internal energy was surging in his dantian, and his two eyes had become even clearer and deeper. His body felt light, and it was as if his skin gave off a glow. He could vividly feel that he had grown stronger.

The effects of the Gongcheong Stone Milk were not limited to increasing internal energy. It had thoroughly cleansed his blood vessels and made his internal energy even purer.

Geom Mugeuk, who had finished circulating his qi first, was looking at him.

He had probably waited without even breathing heavily until Geom Muyang finished.

“Isn’t it amazing?”

This, at least, he could not deny. It truly was amazing.

Geom Muyang silently nodded and asked,

“Why did you give me three drops?”

“Damn it! You think I wanted to? My hand slipped.”

Geom Mugeuk looked indignant.

“I’d rather drop my sword while fighting an enemy.”

That was nonsense. There was no way Geom Mugeuk could have failed to control that, so he had intended to give him three drops from the start. He had been had by this bastard again.

“At least I was lucky two drops were left. If I’d poured it all in, I would’ve cut your belly open.”

Instead of claiming credit, Geom Mugeuk chose to play the villain.

“You think I spared you for nothing in the succession fight? You’ll spend your whole life suffering while looking after your younger brother. Prepare yourself.”

Geom Muyang did not say thank you. If it meant looking after this bastard, three drops of Gongcheong Stone Milk were not enough.

Geom Mugeuk sprang to his feet.

“Let’s go out.”

“Where?”

“We came to Wuhan. We should look around the marketplace.”

Just as Geom Muyang was about to say he did not want to, Geom Mugeuk was one step faster.

“We came all the way to Wuhan, so we should buy Father a gift. Are you not going to buy one? Ah, our poor father, who only loves his eldest son. When will he ever receive a gift from that eldest son……”

Before his words grew any longer, Geom Muyang rose from his seat.

* * *

The marketplace in front of the Murim Alliance headquarters was crowded with people.

Shops lined both sides of the street, and peddlers with street stalls called for customers in hoarse voices.

Countless passersby came and went.

Into that bustling street appeared Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang.

Geom Mugeuk strode about with his face exposed, but Geom Muyang had pulled a bamboo hat low over his head.

“Brother, no one’s looking. No, even if they do, so what?”

But Geom Muyang did not take off the bamboo hat. For a demon cultist to walk around Wuhan with his face exposed was something only his younger brother could do.

“Have we ever walked through a marketplace together like this? This is the first time, right?”

When he had traveled with their father, he had once walked through a marketplace with him. Today, he was walking with his older brother. Someday, surely, there would come a day when the three of them walked together.

He had thought his brother would simply walk while looking straight ahead, but unexpectedly, he looked around here and there as he walked.

It seemed he really was choosing a gift for their father.

Just then, from a cart selling dumplings, a merchant shouted loudly,

“Come taste dumplings so good that if two people eat them and one dies, the other won’t notice!”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk turned to his brother and said,

“Killing either of us would be hard to begin with, so I don’t think we’ll be able to feel that kind of flavor. Still, shall we try some?”

Geom Mugeuk dragged his brother over there.

They ordered a plate of dumplings and tasted them, and for street food, they were quite good.

“Have you ever bought food and eaten it standing on the street?”

At that, Geom Muyang shook his head.

“How does it feel?”

“I want to kill one person and eat quietly?”

Geom Mugeuk burst into loud laughter. It was a precious joke from his brother, so he had to laugh loudly.

As they ate the dumplings, the two of them looked around.

People arguing while haggling, a woman running with food in a bowl, children chasing after a taffy seller, passersby stepping aside to avoid men carrying loads on their backs.

That was when Geom Muyang saw it. He saw Geom Mugeuk smiling as he watched them.

Watching lives he himself had never once paid attention to in his entire life, what was his younger brother so happy about that he smiled like that?

Geom Mugeuk put the last remaining dumpling into his mouth.

“Since my Gongcheong Stone Milk was stolen, I should at least get to eat this.”

Geom Muyang let out a hollow laugh.

Then the two of them began walking again.

As they walked through the marketplace, Geom Muyang looked at one shop.

Geom Mugeuk sensed that the look in his brother’s eyes as he gazed there was different from when he looked elsewhere. It was a shop that sold musical instruments.

“Let’s go in and have a look.”

Geom Mugeuk went in first, and Geom Muyang followed after him.

“Brother, is there any instrument you know how to play?”

“No.”

For someone who said that, the look in his eyes as he had gazed at this place had been different.

“Well, of course. What instrument would you know how to play? Seeing as I have no sense of pitch, you must be the same. I’ve never seen Father play an instrument either, so it’s all in the blood, the blood! All we know how to do is kill people!”

Just then, a sound came from behind him.

Startled, he turned around and saw that Geom Muyang had begun playing a danso.

When he blew breath into the danso, a beautiful sound flowed out.

The melody spread, piercing through the surrounding noise.

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes widened.

He was not just trying to play it. His brother was properly performing a song.

Because he had never dreamed his brother knew how to handle an instrument, he was truly surprised.

It was another moment that made him realize how difficult it was to truly know someone, no matter how hard one tried to see them well.

Was it because he felt his brother’s heart was contained in this music? Listening to it felt so good.

When the performance ended, applause burst out from behind them. He turned to find that people who had been passing by had stopped and gathered at the entrance of the shop.

Geom Mugeuk shouted at his brother,

“What is this? Why are you good? Why are you good at it!”

The owner of the instrument shop also expressed his surprise.

“Your performance was truly excellent.”

Perhaps burdened by the praise from the people gathered outside and from the owner, Geom Muyang went out.

Geom Mugeuk quickly bought the danso and chased after his brother.

“Why did you buy that?”

“Because you played it, this danso’s price has already gone up a thousandfold. And perhaps we might have use for it again soon.”

When Geom Muyang looked at him as if asking what he meant, Geom Mugeuk changed the subject.

“You seem like a different person. If you laid your enemies’ corpses all around, let your hair flutter in a bloody wind, and played the danso once! Magnificent, truly magnificent! All the women would collapse!”

“They’d collapse from fear.”

It was right then.

Splash.

A cargo cart passed by, and muddy water splattered onto his brother’s pants.

But Geom Muyang paid it no mind at all and continued walking.

“I thought a true bloody wind would blow and the Wuhan marketplace would be massacred.”

At those nonsensical words, Geom Muyang, who was walking ahead, laughed silently.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. His brother was enjoying this moment too.

Yes, smile like that and go around a little. How nice it is to see.

Geom Mugeuk found this time with his brother deeply enjoyable.

The two of them looked around at this and that. Geom Mugeuk had been the one to bring up a gift for their father, but the one now searching more seriously was Geom Muyang.

However, there was no gift that particularly pleased them.

Geom Mugeuk held up a pair of pajamas and asked,

“How about Wuhan-brand floral pajamas?”

At Geom Muyang’s look, which asked whether he was in his right mind, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“Ah, you haven’t seen it, have you?”

Geom Mugeuk quietly put the pajamas down.

“No, never mind. The shock would be too great for you.”

As they walked through the marketplace again, Geom Muyang’s gaze stopped in one place.

An elderly painter was sitting in place, drawing portraits of people who passed by.

For some reason, Geom Muyang walked over and sat down in front of the painter.

Geom Mugeuk followed after him with a startled expression and asked,

“What are you doing? You’re scaring me.”

He was a brother who would never in his life sit in a place like this.

“You sit too.”

“Me too?”

Even more surprised, Geom Mugeuk sat beside his brother.

Don’t tell me he wants to leave behind a painting of us together?

As if that was truly the case, Geom Muyang took off his bamboo hat for the first time since coming to the marketplace.

After revealing his face, he said to the painter,

“Draw us together. I’ll pay you more, so please draw us as well as you can.”

The painter began moving his brush with care.

Geom Mugeuk looked at his brother with a surprised face.

It was surprising enough that they were having a picture drawn together, but he even asked the man to draw it well?

“Who are you? Where did our brother go, and why are you pretending to be him!”

Even at Geom Mugeuk’s jest, Geom Muyang’s expression did not change in the slightest. On top of that, he went one step further.

“I’m going to have this painting made into a hanging scroll.”

“What?”

Geom Muyang said something he had never expected.

“I’ll have it made and give it to Father.”

“!”

Only then did Geom Mugeuk understand his brother’s intention. This was the gift for their father.

Because there could be no greater gift for their father than a picture of the two brothers standing side by side.

“They say there’s no younger brother quite like the elder brother. Were you always this smart?”

“Look forward.”

Geom Muyang added bluntly,

“Or that ugly face of yours will come out strange.”

Chapter 673: The Younger Sibling's Affection Is Invincible
----------------------------------------------------------
The painting was splendid.

A famous painter, for one reason or another, had drifted all the way to the marketplace and made his living painting portraits. His skill was good enough that one might have called him the protagonist of this tale.

The two people in the painting were unmistakably Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the painting straight on, then from the side.

“No matter how I look at it, he drew you more handsome, Brother.”

Looking at the completed painting, Geom Mugeuk began spinning a conspiracy theory.

“You threatened this painter with sound transmission, didn’t you? ‘If you don’t draw me better, I’ll make sure you never hold a brush again!’”

The aged painter merely smiled. He had spent many years here painting portraits and had seen countless people.

Perhaps because he had looked upon so many faces, now, with just a glance, he could sense a person’s life.

And today, he had a special experience.

It had been when he was painting the younger brother’s face. The man was someone who tossed out frivolous words so lightly they seemed almost weightless.

Strangely, however, while painting him, the painter felt not as though he were painting a portrait, but a landscape.

When he painted the hair, it felt like painting a reed field swaying in the wind; when he painted the eyes, it felt like painting a vast sea. He painted a snow mountain swept by a blizzard, and he painted a wasteland without a single blade of grass. He felt the four seasons, and he felt the passage of time.

He had painted human faces all his life, but this was the first time he had ever felt such a thing while painting someone.

Geom Muyang silently looked down at the result.

Unlike his expressionless self, his younger brother in the painting was smiling brightly. Even while chattering on about all sorts of things, it seemed his expression had continued to smile. He liked this painting.

“Here. The money.”

Geom Muyang paid enough for ten paintings, but the old painter returned the money.

“It has been a long time since I enjoyed painting so much. I would like to give this painting to you as a gift.”

He did not want to put a price on today’s painting. If he had to accept money, he felt he would need to receive hundreds, even thousands of times this amount.

When the painter bowed his head once more, as if making a sincere request, Geom Muyang nodded.

“Do you know a place that can make it into a hanging scroll?”

The painter told him the location of a mounting shop in the marketplace.

After parting with the painter, the two of them headed straight to the mounting shop.

The proprietor’s face was full of pride, as befitted someone who had spent his entire life mounting paintings. Geom Muyang handed the painting to him.

“Make it so it can be hung on a wall. No matter how much it costs, make it the finest.”

“The finest, is it. Then it will take some time. Come back in seven days.”

Geom Muyang looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“We’ll still be in Wuhan until then.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s answer, Geom Muyang spoke briefly to the proprietor and turned away.

“I’ll come in seven days.”

Thus, the two left the mounting shop and set off toward their lodgings.

“When did you learn the danso?”

“A long time ago.”

“Does Father know too?”

Geom Muyang shook his head, but Geom Mugeuk thought differently.

“He probably does.”

Geom Muyang looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression asking what he meant.

“I mean, he probably knows you can play an instrument.”

His brother did not seem to believe it, but Geom Mugeuk thought their father knew. When he had first returned to the past, their father had known that he had been afflicted by Energy-Scattering Poison as well.

Geom Mugeuk knew that their father merely pretended not to know, and that he actually knew many things about his children.

In any case, he was not the type to say, Play something for me. Nor was Geom Muyang the type to say, I will play something for you.

He thought he would have to set up an occasion someday. He wanted their father to hear his brother’s performance at least once.

They were walking along together when—

Pat patter.

Heavy raindrops began to fall, and then—

Shwaaaaaa.

A sudden shower began to pour down.

The two of them stood for a while beneath the eaves of a shop, waiting for the rain to stop.

With their martial prowess, they could have walked through the rain without getting wet, but they simply waited for it to pass.

Geom Muyang, who had been watching the rain, glanced at his younger brother.

The falling rain had sealed his talkative brother’s mouth. Geom Muyang knew that this image at this moment was his brother’s true self.

That deep gaze, silently watching the rain, was his brother’s real gaze.

Geom Muyang asked quietly.

“What do you like?”

Startled by the unexpected question, Geom Mugeuk looked at his brother.

Geom Muyang turned his head toward the falling rain and asked again.

“I asked what you like.”

Brother, do you know? This is the first time you’ve ever asked me about something personal. And you asked first.

“As for me, there are lots of things I like. I like drinking and having fun with people.”

“Too much…”

Geom Muyang had been about to warn him that if he was too swayed by affection, it could get him killed.

But when he thought about it, that very affection was causing all their enemies to die.

His younger brother’s abundance of affection was invincible.

“You like being alone more, don’t you?”

His brother kept surprising him.

It was surprising that he knew this about him, and even more surprising that he expressed it.

“Our brother is a ghost, a ghost. What’s gotten into you today?”

“What is it you really like? Stop going on about people and alcohol.”

Knowing that his brother was asking seriously, he answered honestly.

“I like lying down comfortably and looking at the sea. I like soaking in a hot spring amid a snowy landscape.”

With the sea and hot springs coming out of nowhere, it might have sounded like a lie, but they truly were things he liked.

“I like this right now too. Standing under the eaves like this and watching the rain.”

Watching the world alone.

In his life before his return, he had experienced that moment so many times.

In a snowstorm, atop a drifting raft, while hanging from a cliff…

Even in those unbearably lonely moments, when he looked at the world like this, he felt better. Even just watching people pass by made him feel better.

Perhaps that was why. Why he still liked moments like this.

Geom Muyang, who had been quietly looking at his younger brother, turned his gaze back to the rain.

“You said staring outside was something only old people did.”

“At least I’m watching from outside.”

As if he himself found what he had said childish, Geom Mugeuk laughed aloud. A faint smile formed at the corners of Geom Muyang’s mouth.

Shwaaaaaa.

“Brother, when Father finishes his closed-door training, let’s the three of us go hunting together.”

Geom Muyang gazed wordlessly at the falling rain for a moment, then nodded.

“Sure.”

Then he added one more thing.

“…Always take care of your own things first.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his brother with trembling eyes.

Today, his brother had asked for the first time what he liked, and for the first time, had shown concern for him.

“I knew it! You’re not my brother! I suspected it from the moment you suggested we get a portrait done. Give me back my brother! When did you switch places with him?”

“…”

When the joke ended, Geom Mugeuk spoke seriously.

“Brother, I’m more selfish and cold-hearted than I look. You don’t have to worry too much. If it seems like I’m doing something wrong, don’t hesitate to curse me out.”

Geom Muyang also answered seriously.

“I held back several times today too.”

Shwaaaaaa.

* * *

Until the hanging scroll was completed, the two decided to wait at their lodgings while training in martial arts.

Geom Muyang devoted himself to practicing the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art. The power of the three drops of Gongcheong Stone Milk had a great effect on this training as well.

Perhaps because his internal energy had become purer, his martial arts training proceeded more smoothly than before, and the achievements he gained came faster too.

Geom Mugeuk volunteered to take charge of meals so that his brother could focus on training.

At every mealtime, he bought dishes from an inn and set them out. Since he knew his brother did not like going out to eat, he went out to buy food under the pretext of wanting some fresh air.

With his lightness skill, he went back and forth in a flash, so it seemed like nothing much, but each meal had different dishes. When asked what he wanted to eat, Geom Muyang answered, “Anything,” but it could not be “anything” every single time.

Thanks to that, Geom Mugeuk came to learn his brother’s tastes as well. What dishes he liked, and what he disliked.

Even while eating, he tried hard to exchange even one more word with him.

When else would he have the chance to spend seven whole days under the same roof as his brother?

For Geom Mugeuk knew better than anyone that if one missed the right time, one was not even given the chance to try.

And on the final, seventh day, Geom Muyang went out personally and bought the food.

Surprisingly, although he had not asked, Geom Muyang bought only dishes that Geom Mugeuk liked.

“How on earth did you know I liked these dishes?”

“A picky eater’s tastes are obvious.”

He answered bluntly, but over the past seven days, whenever they ate, Geom Muyang had been carefully watching which dishes his younger brother’s chopsticks went to more often.

Thus, seven days passed.

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang appeared in the marketplace to retrieve the hanging scroll.

The streets of Wuhan were abuzz with a single piece of news.

Today, the Murim Alliance was holding a grand banquet to celebrate Jin Hagun becoming the successor. Not only the leaders within the alliance, but also many renowned martial elders from across the Central Plains were all in attendance.

The marketplace was in a festive mood as well. Martial artists had gathered from every region, and the streets were truly packed shoulder to shoulder.

“You bastards, if you get carried away and even bump my brother’s shoulder, there’ll be hell to pay! I’ll wipe you all out!”

He only said it; Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang were the ones who avoided people first.

“Young Alliance Leader Bi said he would arrive tomorrow, didn’t he?”

“Yeah.”

Bi Sain had sent that news through Tongcheongak.

“Ah, I’m curious. I want to hurry up and meet him so I can ask what happened with Miss Han from the Ice Palace.”

Back when they had all gathered and then parted, Bi Sain had suddenly suggested to Han Seol that she stop by the Sado Alliance before returning.

Even he had thought, Oops, after saying it, but unexpectedly, Han Seol had answered that she would.

At the time, Geom Mugeuk had sent him off without teasing him by sound transmission, telling him to savor his happiness.

“When we meet this time, I’ll wring out everything that happened. Would that bear of a friend even have managed to speak properly? This time, I should thoroughly teach him how to seduce a woman.”

Thus cutting through the crowd, the two arrived at the mounting shop.

The hanging scroll had been completed as magnificently as the proprietor’s confidence had promised.

“It’s wonderful!”

Geom Mugeuk exclaimed in admiration. The painting had been made into a very elegant and splendid hanging scroll. It gave a different impression from when it had simply been a painting on paper.

Geom Muyang also smiled in satisfaction. It was good enough to present as a gift to their father.

Geom Muyang carefully rolled up the hanging scroll and kept it safely in his robe. To anyone watching, it would have looked as though he were storing away a treasured sword.

“You’ve worked hard.”

After paying the price for the scroll, the two left the mounting shop.

They were about to return to their lodgings when—

“Get out of sight!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s shout, the two of them leapt together into an alley.

With a tense expression, Geom Muyang asked quietly.

“Is it them?”

At Geom Muyang’s question, Geom Mugeuk slightly poked his head out of the alley. His sharp gaze pierced through the crowd.

“Yeah. A very fierce and frightening-looking bastard. And that frightening bastard is doing something adorable.”

Adorable?

Wondering what he meant, Geom Muyang also slightly poked his head out and looked where his younger brother was looking.

He was certainly there.

Far off at the end of the marketplace, a fierce and frightening-looking man. Even the people passing by opened a path when their eyes met his.

Surprisingly, it was Bi Sain. They had understood that he would arrive tomorrow.

“It seems his schedule was moved up. But why are we hiding?”

At Geom Muyang’s words, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes narrowed.

“Not at all! Look beside that liar.”

Beside Bi Sain, a woman in pure white martial attire was looking around and taking in the sights.

She was Han Seol of the Ice Palace. Astonishingly, she was with him.

“Why are the two of them together?”

“Look behind her.”

Behind Han Seol, the Ice Twin Swords who guarded her could also be seen.

“The Ice Twin Swords aren’t paying any attention to guarding her and are busy looking around the marketplace. That means they trust Sain. In other words, the four of them walking around together like this is something they’re used to. Which means Miss Han, after going to the Sado Alliance, stayed there sightseeing instead of returning to the North Sea.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. Bi Sain had probably shown her around all sorts of places. Then, after receiving his message, he had come here with her.

“He deceived me!”

“He probably just happened to arrive a day early.”

But he could not escape Geom Mugeuk’s keen eyes.

“Look at those clothes.”

Now that he saw it, Bi Sain was wearing white.

“He matched his outfit with hers. And he deliberately chose clothes with patterns so it wouldn’t look like they matched exactly.”

Hearing it, it did seem that way.

Was Geom Muyang the sort of person who would enjoy spying like this?

But since he was with Geom Mugeuk, even this was amusing.

Geom Mugeuk’s deductions continued.

“Do you know why he isn’t wearing a bamboo hat?”

“Why?”

“Because he’s afraid it’ll mess up his hair.”

“Surely not.”

That Bi Sain, of all people? Judging by his face, he looked capable of conquering murim at any moment.

Geom Muyang could not believe it, but Geom Mugeuk was certain.

“Otherwise, there’s no reason for him to walk around showing that face in the front yard of the Murim Alliance.”

Just then, Bi Sain, who had been far away, whipped his head toward them.

But the two had already hidden their heads back inside the alley.

“What is it?”

At Han Seol’s question, Bi Sain turned back to her. His terrifying, sharp expression had softened gently.

“It’s nothing.”

Bi Sain looked at the peddler’s cart beside them and asked her,

“Would you like some sweets?”

Chapter 674: I Could Tolerate the Sweets, But
---------------------------------------------
“Heavens! Sain is buying candied fruit!”

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang were peeking out from an alley, secretly watching the two.

“The shopkeeper saw Sain’s face and is so scared his hands are trembling as he hands over the candied fruit.”

Geom Muyang laughed silently. Of course, it was an exaggerated remark meant to be funny, but one thing was certain. At the very least, Bi Sain and candied fruit did not go together.

“Could those two be….”

Geom Muyang didn’t finish his sentence, but Geom Mugeuk knew exactly what his older brother was curious about.

“They’re not dating yet.”

“How do you know?”

“If they were, he would’ve bought one and they would’ve shared it.”

The candied fruit Bi Sain had bought had over ten pieces skewered on a stick. It was enough for two people to share, yet Bi Sain had bought two and was handing one of them to her. Even the manner in which they gave and received it was extremely polite.

“Here you are.”

“Thank you.”

Han Seol took the skewer of candied fruit.

“This is my first time eating this since coming to the Central Plains.”

In truth, it had been a very long time since Bi Sain had eaten candied fruit as well. He felt like he had eaten it when he was little, and then never again. He couldn’t even remember when he had last had it.

Han Seol took a bite of the candied fruit.

“It’s delicious.”

The two started walking again as they ate.

A smile formed on Geom Mugeuk’s face as he watched them. Watching the two walk beneath the sunlight, they looked so fresh and wholesome.

Of course, it was not an easy relationship.

The North Sea Ice Palace was a sect closer to the orthodox faction.

It might not be to the extent of the successors of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Martial Alliance, but the relationship between the successor of the North Sea Ice Palace and the successor of the Demonic Path Alliance was likewise not one that could easily be joined.

*Good luck, friend.*

Of course, the words that came out of his mouth were the exact opposite.

“So while I was fighting with my life on the line and bleeding, you were enjoying romance in the spring breeze?”

“You didn’t put your life on the line, though? You didn’t spill a single drop of blood.”

Geom Mugeuk pretended not to hear and moved quickly.

“Hyung, this way.”

Geom Mugeuk moved as swiftly as if infiltrating the Alliance Lord’s residence of the Demonic Path Alliance.

“I need to hear what they’re saying!”

Geom Muyang quickly followed Geom Mugeuk. There was truly never a dull moment with his younger brother.

The two found another place to hide and concealed themselves.

In the past, he had spied on the two walking with Jin Hagun, but today he was spying on them with his older brother.

“Look there. He’s subtly blocking people with his body so they won’t bump into Lady Han. Hey, friend! You can’t do that! You have to act indifferent! Women like bad men!”

Geom Muyang’s reaction, on the other hand, was different.

“What’s wrong with you? It just looks nice.”

Geom Mugeuk glanced at his brother.

Geom Muyang was watching the two with gentle eyes. Judging by his expression alone, he seemed to be recalling an old love.

Come to think of it, had his older brother had a woman he liked?

Given his personality, he wasn’t someone who would talk about such things. He would have to be several times closer than they were now to hear that story.

Indeed, the same was true of Bi Sain over there. He had only realized it clearly because he had been caught red-handed like this today—Bi Sain wasn’t someone who easily revealed his feelings.

“It might just be that he’s treating her well because she’s the Young Palace Lady of the Ice Palace, not because it’s romance.”

“Look at their gazes and tell me. That bear-like bastard over there is head over heels right now.”

At his younger brother’s words, Geom Muyang observed the two’s gazes. While Han Seol was looking around here and there out of curiosity, Bi Sain was looking only at her. And whenever Han Seol glanced at him, he instantly turned his head elsewhere.

“The speed he turns his head is like lightning. He could probably dodge my sword.”

Unaware the two were spying on them, Bi Sain was dedicating his entire attention to Han Seol. Until she returned, he considered her safety his own responsibility.

He had been truly delighted when Han Seol came to find him at the main stronghold of the Demonic Path Alliance. She had said she would come, but he had thought she might not.

Would she really come all the way to the Demonic Path Alliance to find him? But she had come to find him just as promised.

Bi Sain treated her as an honored guest.

He showed her around not only the main stronghold of the Demonic Path Alliance but also various areas within its territory.

Then a situation arose where Bi Sain had to return to the Demonic Path Alliance due to urgent matters.

At that time, it was a situation where they naturally should have parted.

*“If you would wait for me, I shall finish my business and return. There is a place I very much wish to show you.”*

He had been saving the most beautiful scenic spot to show her last, so he regretted having to send her away without seeing it. He didn’t know when she would come to the main stronghold again. No, it would likely be the last time.

Honestly, he didn’t have high expectations. She had already seen many places, and more than that, he would need over twenty days to finish his work and return.

It was an unreasonable request, but she readily agreed.

*“Yes, let us do that.”*

Han Seol waited for Bi Sain to finish his work and return.

Bi Sain was more deeply moved by her waiting than by her coming to find him. Later, if the chance arose, he wanted to ask her.

Why, and with what heart, she had waited for him.

Thus the two were able to stand side by side before that beautiful waterfall.

Just as they had seen the last scenic spot and were truly about to part, he received contact from Geom Mugeuk. Thanks to that, his connection with her continued here in Wuhan.

“If the crowds are uncomfortable, we may enter an inn or tavern.”

If there were too many people there as well, he intended to simply rent out the entire place.

At Bi Sain’s words, Han Seol shook her head.

“No, looking around is more fun.”

The streets of Wuhan were filled with all sorts of merchants who had gathered from across the Central Plains for Jin Hagun’s celebration banquet. Thus there were many sights one wouldn’t normally see.

“I truly am seeing the Central Plains to my heart’s content this time.”

She hadn’t known her trip to the Central Plains would become such a journey.

She had visited the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and even toured the Demonic Path Alliance. That alone had been an incredible journey, yet now she had come before the main stronghold of the Martial Alliance.

If she had the chance to tour the main stronghold of the Martial Alliance this time, she would become the only martial artist in the history of the Ice Palace to have visited the main strongholds of the Orthodox, Unorthodox, and Demonic factions.

“If you come to the North Sea next time, I shall guide you.”

Bi Sain was surprised by the unexpected proposal.

“May I truly go?”

“Of course.”

Bi Sain wanted to climb the snow-covered mountain with her.

At first, he had been drawn to her because her appearance was his ideal type.

But after spending time with her this time, he had grown fond of her personality as well.

She didn’t act overly familiar, yet she didn’t keep her distance either. He liked that appropriate sense of distance she maintained in dealing with people.

*Come to think of it, meeting Lady Han was also thanks to that friend.*

Had it not been for Geom Mugeuk, she would never have come to the Central Plains in the first place. Geom Mugeuk was the one who had created the beginning of this relationship.

At first, he had thought she interacted with him because Geom Mugeuk was there.

But Han Seol moved independently of Geom Mugeuk.

She came to find him at the Demonic Path Alliance, waited for him there, and came to Wuhan with him like this.

That made him more grateful, and his heart leaned toward her more.

But he didn’t reveal such feelings of his. He didn’t want to burden her.

Bi Sain spoke to Han Seol.

“If you are hungry, there is a place called Wuhan First Restaurant at the end of that road. The cuisine there is quite special, so let us dine there.”

“You must come to Wuhan often.”

In truth, Bi Sain had only been to Wuhan once before. And that was when he was very young.

What business would the successor of the Demonic Path Alliance have in Wuhan, where the main stronghold of the Martial Alliance was located? Even the Thirteen Wolves of the Demonic Path were currently waiting on the outskirts of Wuhan.

He had come because he was invited to a gathering of friends; Wuhan was not a place one could enter carelessly.

“No. Instead, I studied a bit.”

At his words that he had studied, a slight smile formed on Han Seol’s lips.

“It just so happens that I have a subordinate from this place.”

There was someone among the Thirteen Wolves whose hometown was Wuhan. However, he had left so long ago that he was of no help at all.

In the end, Bi Sain requested information from the Evil Shadow Pavilion, the Demonic Path Alliance’s intelligence organization.

He asked them to tell him the best restaurants and worthwhile sights in Wuhan.

This was likely the first such information request since the Evil Shadow Pavilion’s establishment.

In any case, he couldn’t possibly say that he had gone that far to find out, so he said he had heard it from a subordinate.

Han Seol looked behind her and spoke.

“I am fine, but the two of you must be hungry.”

She walked over to the Hanbing Twin Swords who had been following behind. The two of them were taking in the streets of Wuhan after a long time. This place had changed, that place had changed—they were sharing old stories about how much had truly changed.

“Please go to Wuhan First Restaurant down there and eat first. We shall look around a bit more and then go there.”

Sa Han, the husband of the Hanbing Twin Swords, said they couldn’t do that, but he was dragged by his wife, So Bing, toward the guesthouse. He received a scolding to read the room.

At that moment, Bi Sain sharply turned his head.

He had felt someone watching him. But there was no one where the feeling had come from.

“Is something wrong?”

“It is nothing. Come, let us go.”

From behind the cart where Bi Sain’s gaze had rested a moment before, Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang’s heads popped out.

“Sly bastard! You even took care of the obstacles so you could be alone together.”
