“I think Lady Han was the one who sent them away just now.”

How could Geom Mugeuk not know? Han Seol also had some feelings for him, which was why she was walking with him like this. If he were someone she disliked, she wouldn’t have stayed with him for a day, no, not even an hour.

“Sain is taking out his money pouch again. He never buys me a single meal, but he spends money like crazy.”

This time he was buying something else for her. He was buying a tassel to decorate her sword scabbard.

“If you’re going to buy it, buy a pair—two of them! Sigh, but he doesn’t have that kind of sense.”

Then Geom Mugeuk let out another sigh.

“Ah! I can’t stand it.”

“Why?”

“He just tried to tie the thread onto her scabbard and their hands brushed.”

“That can happen.”

“Yes, and at that thing that ‘can happen,’ Sain’s face turned red.”

Geom Mugeuk was reading every single expression of the two using his Divine Eye Technique.

“These days, acting innocent like that doesn’t work!”

“Either treat him like a clueless simp or a playboy. Pick one.”

Meanwhile, Bi Sain and Han Seol went to where people were gathered.

“Step right up! Hit the center with the throwing knife and win a prize!”

The prizes stacked beside it were dolls. There were martial artist dolls holding swords, and animal dolls like dragons and tigers. There were cloth dolls, and also ones carved from wood.

Seeing her gaze linger on the dolls.

Bi Sain stepped forward without a word.

“How much is the entry fee?”

Bi Sain paid the fee and stepped forward. Doing this kind of business in front of the Martial Alliance? And in front of the Martial Alliance where martial artists swarmed no less.

Bi Sain took the throwing knife as if to say what else was there but to try, and lightly threw it.

Thwack.

The moment he threw the first knife, Bi Sain tasted the bitterness of the world.

The knife had not hit the center.

*Impossible!*

Bi Sain paid the entry fee again and received another knife.

This time, when he calmly examined the knife, it was a defective product with poor balance.

As if a defective product could stop him from hitting the target? He was Bi Sain.

Swoosh.

He missed.

“No, this….”

Flustered, Bi Sain paid the entry fee again.

He didn’t know what principle was at work, but these knives seemed designed so that throwing them properly would absolutely miss. The higher one’s martial arts, the harder they were to hit with.

Watching him attempt once more, Geom Mugeuk sighed.

“Ah! He’s going to lose all the points he’s saved up until now.”

Continuing was a problem, beating up the shopkeeper for fraud was a problem, and giving up and leaving was also a problem.

Geom Muyang agreed with Geom Mugeuk’s sigh.

But he also understood Bi Sain’s heart. In front of the woman he liked, how could he stop even if his pride was wounded?

It was when Bi Sain failed for the fifth time.

Han Seol pointed at one of the dolls. It was a small bear doll in the corner.

“I want that one.”

Flames ignited in Bi Sain’s eyes.

Throwing, and throwing again.

Finally, on the tenth try!

Thwack!

The throwing knife accurately hit the target. Among the knives, some that flew properly had been mixed in. The shopkeeper wasn’t a complete fraud after all.

The shopkeeper brought the bear doll with a grin.

“Congratulations, martial warrior!”

Bi Sain did not get angry. He spoke to the shopkeeper with a tone that was half-joking, half-serious.

“Don’t do this business in front of the Demonic Path Alliance. You’ll be stabbed to death before you even get to ten tries.”

The shopkeeper answered with a bright smile.

“That’s why I only do it in front of the Martial Alliance!”

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang had already come close enough to hear their voices and were hiding.

Geom Mugeuk whispered to Geom Muyang.

“Seeing him say that even after looking at that face, the shopkeeper’s got more guts than the masterminds we fought.”

Geom Muyang nodded as if in agreement.

Bi Sain dusted the bear doll clean and presented it to Han Seol.

“It’s so cute. Thank you.”

Han Seol smiled as she received the doll.

Thus, with a doll in one hand and candied fruit in the other, Bi Sain and Han Seol passed by in front of where the Geom brothers were hiding.

Watching them let the pair pass while crouching behind the cart to hide, Geom Muyang asked with a curious expression.

“Why are you just letting them go? I thought you were going to startle them.”

He had thought they had approached so closely precisely for this moment.

“I was going to.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to Han Seol.

There had been various reactions she could have had a short while ago. She could have told him to stop and leave, or waited patiently, or gotten angry at the shopkeeper for cheating.

But instead, she had said she wanted the doll.

Geom Mugeuk thought that choice had been the best one for both Bi Sain and her. She hadn’t poured cold water on the moment; rather, she lifted the mood.

A wise choice that ended that moment without making anyone feel bad.

“I’m letting it go today for Lady Han’s sake.”

Of course, there was a condition attached.

“Only for today!”

“Then what about tomorrow?”

This time, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes gleamed toward Bi Sain.

“Coming here in secret! I put up with the candied fruit, but a bear doll? This I cannot stand.”

There was no doubt it was the gaze of someone plotting something.

Geom Muyang inwardly offered condolences to Bi Sain.

*Since I probably won’t be able to protect you, I’ll apologize in advance.*

Absolute Regression, Episode 675

Chapter 675: What Do the Successors Discuss When They Gather?
-------------------------------------------------------------
That evening, Bisain and Hanseol arrived at Geom Mugeuk's residence.

Hanseol gave Hanbing Twin Swords a rare moment alone together. Now that they were meeting Geom Mugeuk as well, they no longer needed a bodyguard.

"You've finally come."

Geom Mugeuk welcomed them warmly.

"Weren't you arriving tomorrow?"

He had originally intended to say, "It just happened to work out that way." But seeing Geom Mugeuk's face in person, the lie would not come out.

"We arrived early today and went sightseeing with Lady Han in Muhan."

They had eaten delicious food at the Muhanjeil Restaurant and had tea at the Daru, famous for its fine incense. They even enjoyed a stroll before arriving here. Honestly, Bisain felt guilty toward Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Muyang also came out to greet them.

"Welcome."

"I'm sorry for visiting so late."

Rather, it was Geom Muyang who could not meet their eyes. The memory of having spied on the two moments ago flashed through his mind, leaving him embarrassed.

"Come, let's go inside. We have matters to discuss and much to prepare."

Geom Mugeuk led them to the main hall.

"I plan to hold our own private celebration the day after tomorrow."

Since Jin Hagun would be exhausted from attending the Martial Alliance banquet today, he would let him rest a day and hold it the day after.

"And a congratulatory performance is in order, don't you think?"

At the words "congratulatory performance," everyone flinched and tensed. The emotions in their gazes were all the same.

What is he scheming now?

Before Geom Mugeuk could even speak, Bisain said firmly:

"Absolutely not."

"What?"

"Whatever you're thinking right now, absolutely not!"

Then Geom Mugeuk asked with a meaningful smile:

"What am I thinking?"

Bisain refused to speak those words aloud. If he did, they would undoubtedly become reality immediately.

But Bisain was not Geom Mugeuk's target.

Geom Mugeuk subtly asked Hanseol:

"Would you dance? Surely a celebration banquet cannot be without dance and music, can it?"

Such was the extent to which Hanseol regarded Geom Mugeuk as an eccentric that these mad words did not sound strange at all.

"Must we go that far? Isn't it enough to congratulate him from the heart?"

"Of course, that would suffice. Just like the countless guests who met Hagun today to offer their congratulations. If the banquet hasn't ended even now, he's probably still receiving them. But in ten years, Hagun will think, 'What did that person say to congratulate me? Did they even come?'"

No one could argue against those words.

"So you want to do something memorable?"

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

"It is not for him."

"Then for whom?"

"It is for us. For the memory of us preparing such a performance for a friend. Think about it. Do you not remember what you have given more than what you have received?"

"!"

Bisain, watching, shook his head. He could feel that Hanseol was being slightly swayed. No! She must not fall for it!

"I understand what you mean, but I cannot dance."

Relief washed over Bisain at her words.

'Well done. Truly well done.'

But like a bolt from the blue, Hanseol added:

"...But what about singing?"

Bisain looked at her in shock. Not only him, but Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang were surprised as well.

"Though I cannot sing well."

"That does not matter! We have only gathered tone-deaf people here, so this is perfect."

Geom Mugeuk cheered, but Bisain's heart grew complicated.

If she stepped up, he could not stay out of it.

But no matter what, he could not dance again.

This time, Geom Mugeuk dragged his older brother into it.

"Besides, among us is someone who can give a wonderful performance to match the lady's song."

Just as Geom Mugeuk was stealthily reaching for the bamboo flute in his bosom, his brother's voice transmission flew in:

—Pull that out and you're dead!

—You cannot have her sing without accompaniment, can you?

Using her as a shield, Geom Mugeuk pulled out the bamboo flute.

"Is it not surprising? That my brother knows how to play an instrument."

At the same time, he subtly praised his brother.

"He is probably the strongest person in the world among those who play instruments."

Geom Muyang looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression that said he was beyond stopping.

His younger brother's eyes seemed to say exactly this:

—Brother, wouldn't it be fun to live a life you've never lived before?

Yes, if he was going to refuse now, he should not have played at the instrument shop that day.

"I will play."

Yes, if he was going to do it anyway, it was better to decide cleanly and be done with it.

More honestly speaking,

playing the bamboo flute was a hundred times better than dancing or singing.

He thought it a relief. If he had to dance? He would sooner apply gold powder to his face and live in seclusion.

Only one person remained now: Bisain.

He was driven to the edge of a cliff. If he fell, he would have to dance! No! Absolutely not!

Now Geom Muyang knew for certain what Geom Mugeuk had been scheming for Bisain.

He really could not help with this one. Sorry.

Because if he slipped up, his flute could be snatched away and he could be dragged onto the dance floor.

Geom Mugeuk began to persuade Bisain.

"Fortunately, we have a long day ahead of us."

"All of one day."

"So what if we sing poorly, dance poorly, or play poorly? We are the only ones who will hear and see it."

Geom Mugeuk emphasized the words "just us."

"It is something the five of us pull off together. We take it to our graves."

"Why five?"

"Lady Jin has already agreed to join."

Even Lady Jin!

Thud!

A stone fell from behind his feet down the cliff.

"Were you a dancer in a past life? Why are you so obsessed with dancing?"

Geom Mugeuk's reason lay in an unexpected place.

"When we reached a life-or-death crisis, you said it. That if you survived, you would dance."

"!"

Because of that promise, he had ended up dancing at the Three-Party Meeting.

"It was a choice made by your subconscious. The first thing you wanted to do if you survived. Perhaps it was because deep down, you wanted to live a different life rather than your ordinary one?"

He sure knew how to turn a passing remark into something grand!

But at least thanks to Geom Mugeuk, he was getting the chance to live a different life. His current relationship with Hanseol was like that too.

Whoosh.

Just as he could hold on no longer and was falling from the cliff—

Grab.

Someone caught his hand.

It was Hanseol.

"Let's do our best."

At her words, Bisain's mind changed. What he had thought he could not do in front of her, he would now bravely attempt precisely because she was watching.

Bisain declared to Geom Mugeuk:

"This is the last time I dance."

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and shouted:

"Right, let's dance to our heart's content, and then look for something else we haven't tried before!"

* * *

Two days later.

Jin Hagun entered the Blue Cloud Forest.

Today's celebration banquet was to be held here. The place of the final battle.

The pavilion was destroyed and gone, but the bamboo forest remained as it was.

A banquet table had been set up in the middle of that bamboo forest.

The table was laden with fine liquor and excellent dishes. These were foods that Jin Haryeong had requested from the alliance's master chefs.

"Welcome, my friend."

Geom Mugeuk and Bisain, Hanseol and Jin Haryeong, and Geom Muyang were waiting for him.

The moment he saw them, Jin Hagun's mood lifted. He felt happy just looking at them, before a single word was spoken.

"Come, have a drink first."

As Jin Hagun strode over, Geom Mugeuk poured him a drink first.

Clear.

The pure sound of a cup being filled.

Jin Hagun gazed at Geom Mugeuk. That he had ascended to the position of successor—

'No matter what anyone says, it is thanks to you.'

Once the cup was full, this time Jin Hagun filled Geom Mugeuk's cup.

'I will follow slowly, my friend.'

After filling Geom Mugeuk's cup, he filled everyone else's cups as well.

Geom Mugeuk raised his cup high.

"To the future Lord of the Martial Alliance!"

Everyone there shouted in agreement and emptied their cups.

A smile filled Jin Hagun's face as he drank.

He had achieved his dream, and there were people who sincerely congratulated him for it. Among those here, there was no one trying to look good to him simply because he was the Martial Alliance Lord.

"Now, let me pour one."

This time, Bisain poured liquor into Jin Hagun's cup.

"Thank you for coming all this way."

"I would have come no matter where."

As the two drank, Geom Mugeuk quipped:

"You might have wished the road was longer."

At Jin Hagun's questioning expression, Geom Mugeuk subtly glanced at Bisain.

Flustered, Bisain looked away. Jin Hagun understood immediately.

'He noticed.'

How quick-witted he was.

He thought it rather for the best. Being teased by Geom Mugeuk was more comfortable than the awkwardness of deceiving him.

Next, Jin Haryeong stepped forward.

"Congratulations, Brother."

"Thank you."

At their warm gazes toward one another—

"Brother, that is the look siblings give each other."

Geom Muyang pretended not to hear and poured liquor for Jin Hagun.

"Please take my cup as well."

"Thank you so much for coming."

Jin Hagun had not separately asked Geom Muyang to look after the Gwakyeong siblings. The Young Lord of the Demonic Cult was now someone who gave Jin Hagun the faith that he would do well even without being asked.

Finally, Hanseol poured him a drink.

"Congratulations."

"Thank you so much for coming."

And so Jin Hagun took turns drinking with everyone gathered there.

Now the real drinking began.

When everyone had grown tipsy from passing drinks back and forth—

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat and walked outside.

"Now, we have a congratulatory performance to celebrate our friend Hagun becoming the successor!"

Because it was so unexpected, Jin Hagun was startled.

He was even more surprised to see everyone stand up and walk out.

'All five of them?'

He could understand the others, but even Geom Muyang? That was the most surprising.

The five people took their positions.

Geom Muyang and Hanseol stood at the far left and right ends, and between them stood Geom Mugeuk, Bisain, and Jin Haryeong.

The five stood in a line and looked at Jin Hagun. Because he had not expected such an atmosphere, Jin Hagun was rather bewildered.

'What on earth are they trying to do?'

Could they be planning to demonstrate swordsmanship since there were no musicians? Just as he was wondering—

Geom Muyang took out a bamboo flute. That was the first shock. It was the moment the Young Lord of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult pulled out a flute instead of concealed weapons.

As Geom Muyang took his stance and breathed into it, the bamboo flute resonated beautifully. It was truly masterful skill.

Soon, a clear, elegant singing voice followed the bamboo flute's melody.

Jin Hagun was surprised for the second time at the sight of Hanseol singing.

The Young Palace Mistress of the Northern Sea Ice Palace singing to congratulate him?

Finally, the three in the middle began to dance to the melody and song.

Jin Hagun's gaze turned to one person. It was a person with the appearance least suited to dancing in this world.

'Sain, I'm sorry.'

How much he must have suffered at Geom Mugeuk's hands to perform this dance? He could tell without looking.

But separate from those feelings, Bisain was dancing the hardest among the three. He had said he would rather die than dance, but he was not one to do a half-hearted job once he decided to do it.

It was evident that he had practiced a great deal. The dance of the three matched the bamboo flute and singing perfectly.

Bisain's trance-like dance.

Had he practiced dancing normally? It was an unbelievably splendid dance for just one day of practice.

Bisain deserved to stand in the center.

Geom Mugeuk and Jin Haryeong on either side danced diligently as well, but they minimized their own movements to make Bisain stand out even more.

The music grew faster and more exciting, and Hanseol's song grew more cheerful. The mist flowing around them made the stage even more spectacular.

A sight he would never forget for the rest of his life was unfolding before Jin Hagun's eyes.

If someday he grew old and told someone about today, what would happen?

"Long ago, a congratulatory performance was held to celebrate my becoming the successor. The Young Lord of the Divine Cult played the bamboo flute, and the Young Palace Mistress of the Northern Sea Ice Palace sang. And the successors of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Sadomaeng, and my own sibling, danced to that song."

After hearing it all, that person might go to meet the Divine Physician of the Martial Alliance.

"...I believe the Alliance Lord is unwell."

The danso and the singing stopped, and the dance ended as well. Even the finishing move was perfectly in sync. For something they had practiced for only a day, it was far too splendid a performance.

A brief silence flowed.

Jin Hagun rose from his seat and bowed his head.

“I will never forget this moment for as long as I live.”

He was sincerely grateful to them. Were these people the sort to play the danso, sing, and dance? The fact that they had practiced for his sake moved him beyond words.

Bisain, who had burned himself completely out, staggered back to his seat and drained his cup.

“How is it? Now that you’ve done it, don’t you feel relieved?”

He could not deny Geom Mugeuk’s words. The wine he had just drunk was the most delicious in the world.

At that moment, Bisain’s eyes met Han Seol’s.

“You were wonderful.”

That one word from her was enough. Bisain smiled in satisfaction. Still!

‘There will be no more dancing in my life!’

* * *

That night.

Beneath the moonlight, three men stood atop the tips of the bamboo.

They were the three successors who would lead the Murim in the future: Geom Mugeuk, Jin Hagun, and Bisain.

“I’ll be leaving tomorrow.”

Geom Mugeuk intended to return straight to the Sect as soon as this gathering ended. There was somewhere he needed to go, but he had been away from the Sect for too long, so for now he planned to return to the main headquarters with his older brother.

Jin Hagun felt regretful. If they parted like this, he did not know when they would be able to meet again.

And in this moment, Bisain was regretting another parting even more.

When this gathering ended, Han Seol too was scheduled to return to the North Sea together with the Hanbing Twin Swords.

Bisain’s gaze turned toward Han Seol below.

Down there, Geom Muyang, Jin Haryeong, and Han Seol were drunk and talking among themselves.

Let’s have a drink as fellow people who were pushed out of the successor position!

With the cup Jin Haryeong had offered, Geom Muyang and Jin Haryeong had become one, and the bear doll Han Seol had brought all the way here had also become a topic of conversation.

They were three people who seemed as though they would be awkward together, surprisingly enough, yet the conversation did not stop. Even Geom Muyang, who seemed unlikely to say a single word, was adding a few remarks now and then.

Bisain’s gaze went to Han Seol, then returned to Geom Mugeuk. He knew he had already been found out.

“Why aren’t you teasing me?”

He had expected Geom Mugeuk to pick on him, tease him, and fluster him over the fact that he liked her.

“The road ahead is ninety thousand li long. I figured I could tease you slowly.”

Those words held the worry that it would not be easy for the two of them to end up together.

Jin Hagun nodded as if he thought the same.

Bisain asked Jin Hagun,

“When did you notice?”

“From the moment I first saw you.”

“How?”

“You came wearing white together and even brought a bear doll. Did you expect me not to know?”

Bisain’s face flushed slightly. With Geom Mugeuk, he had been put through so much that there was nothing left to be embarrassed about. But somehow, in front of Jin Hagun, he was still a little embarrassed.

He was the sort of man who would rather die than talk to anyone about matters involving women.

But thinking that the two of them knew made his heart feel at ease.

“If the Ice Palace Lord opposes it, invade the North Sea! The Sect will pretend not to know.”

Jin Hagun picked up Geom Mugeuk’s joke.

“Don’t do that. We’ll have to go help the Ice Palace.”

“You should help your friend! Sain, tell them to give back your dance!”

At those words, Bisain laughed. It had been a long time since he had smiled so brightly.

“Oh! What a charming smile! Show that smile to Lady Han!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Bisain let out a light sigh. Would such a day ever come?

Just then, Jin Haryeong called up from below.

“What are you talking about that’s so funny?”

Three young men had gathered, so of course it was talk about women.

Geom Mugeuk shouted back to her.

“What else would successors talk about when they gather? Of course we’re talking about peace in the Murim!”

Absolute Regression Chapter 676

Chapter 676: Curse When They’re Not Around
------------------------------------------
The celebratory banquet continued until dawn.

Everyone drank and talked as though, once they parted today, they would never meet again.

But just as time seems to pass faster when one is with good people, merciless time employed its swiftest footwork. In the blink of an eye, the night had passed, and now the hour of farewell had come.

Han Seol was the first to bid goodbye.

“I’ll pay my respects to the Alliance Leader, stay a few more days, and then return to the North Sea.”

Over the course of the night, she had grown close to Jin Haryeong through their conversations.

She had decided to visit the Alliance Leader’s Hall with Jin Haryeong, pay her respects to the Murim Alliance Leader, stay a few more days, and then depart.

Truly, on this journey, she had experienced meeting all three leaders of the orthodox, unorthodox, and demonic factions.

Bi Sain promised to meet her again.

“I will see you next time.”

A farewell as calm and composed as possible. And because of that, his reluctance felt all the greater.

Han Seol bowed her head and expressed her gratitude.

“Thanks to you, I was able to enjoy seeing the Central Plains.”

Blunt, taciturn, and reserved. She knew very well that such a Bi Sain had tried extremely hard for her sake.

“Next time, I’ll be sure to show you around the North Sea.”

When she mentioned seeing the North Sea once more, as if it had not been an idle remark, Bi Sain’s mood brightened.

“I will look forward to that day.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been listening, cut in.

“What about us? We want to see the North Sea too! Drink ice wine on the snowy mountains and all!”

Naturally, he expected Han Seol to say, I’ll invite all of you as well.

“Young Cult Leader, you’ve already been there before, haven’t you? And you drank ice wine too.”

Then, after giving a slight bow, she turned away. It was a firm declaration that she intended to invite only Bi Sain.

A smile that Bi Sain simply could not hide slipped onto his lips.

With an expression that said, I can’t let this stand, Geom Mugeuk dragged Jin Hagun into it.

“And you still say you’ll go help the Ice Palace?”

Jin Hagun nodded firmly and said,

“I have to help. She said she’d invite me separately.”

“What? When?”

“Together with my sister.”

Feigning a shudder, Geom Mugeuk said in a low voice,

“Our Cult will strike the Ice Palace!”

“We’re saying we’ll go to help them?”

The Sado Alliance also joined the will of the Murim Alliance.

“We’ll go help too.”

“You’re all so cruel!”

Jin Hagun and Bi Sain laughed together.

Watching them, Geom Muyang suddenly had this thought.

‘If these three become Cult Leader and Alliance Leaders, then perhaps a new era will open in murim, one the previous generation could never have imagined.’

A murim that had never existed before, and would never be seen again.

And surprisingly, Geom Muyang realized that he, too, was secretly looking forward to that murim. He was curious about that gangho.

Jin Haryeong said goodbye to Geom Mugeuk.

“Get back safely.”

She turned away more cleanly than ever.

It was because she felt too regretful. Was it because she had sensed the subtle current flowing between Bi Sain and Han Seol? More than usual, parting with Geom Mugeuk left her feeling reluctant.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk did not let her leave just like that.

“Don’t reveal your hidden ambition to your brother! If he catches you by any chance, run away to us!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words from behind her, a faint smile formed on Jin Haryeong’s lips.

“So you can catch me and hand me over to my brother?”

“Huh? How did you know?”

“The three of you are always scheming like that. How could I not know?”

And so Jin Haryeong and Han Seol left the place first. Han Seol, walking some distance away, turned back toward Bi Sain and lightly lifted the bear doll.

A bright smile spread across Bi Sain’s lips. For a while, he would probably be hearing, “Did something good happen?” quite often from Ilrang, the head of the Sado Thirteen Wolves.

Now the three successors bid farewell to one another.

At the end, they did not forget the peace of murim either.

“Their leadership is still intact. So don’t forget that something similar could happen at any time.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Jin Hagun and Bi Sain nodded. They had already experienced enough of what their enemies were like.

“If anything strange happens, be sure to contact us. You know, right? You must never try to handle it alone!”

In the past, they would have let those words pass in one ear and out the other, but Jin Hagun and Bi Sain nodded to this as well.

“Then I’ll see you next time!”

After the three exchanged glances, each set off in a different direction.

Geom Mugeuk, who had walked some distance away, turned back and shouted,

“I already want to see you again! When will we meet next?”

Jin Hagun and Bi Sain smiled soundlessly and continued on their way without looking back.

Geom Muyang saw it.

He saw Geom Mugeuk joking until the very end, then turning back again. On his face, the playfulness had vanished, leaving it calm and serious. This was the kind of person he truly was, and yet he was trying so hard.

‘I could never live like you, even if I died trying.’

The two walked in silence for a while.

“Brother.”

When no words followed even after quite some time, Geom Muyang asked,

“Why call me if you have nothing to say?”

“I just felt like calling you.”

They walked for a long while like that before Geom Mugeuk spoke again.

“Brother, promise me one thing. Later, you’ll grant me one request.”

“No.”

“You won’t even hear what the request is?”

“It’s bound to be some absurd request.”

“That’s why I’m asking you. Who else would grant an absurd request like that?”

“……”

Far in the distance, the sun was rising over the horizon.

Naturally, the two stopped walking and watched the sight.

Through this departure from the Cult, Geom Muyang had seen a new side of life.

He had only left the Cult to catch the person who had made the fake hidden weapons. Because he had been furious at the fact that his younger brother had nearly been killed.

Originally, his life had been fixed.

Catch him and kill him.

He had lived this predetermined life along a single path, and he had believed that to be his life.

But this time, he had encountered another side of life.

He had saved the younger sister of the man who made the counterfeit hidden weapons, watched the rain beneath dripping eaves, spied on someone, and played the danso in front of people.

Geom Muyang looked at Geom Mugeuk standing beside him.

The light that flew in to awaken the world brightly illuminated his younger brother’s face.

Come to think of it, his brother had been saying it all along.

Let us live a different life.

Let us not engage in a successor struggle where we kill each other. Let us have a different kind of successor struggle.

When Geom Mugeuk sensed his gaze and turned, Geom Muyang said,

“Shall we run?”

A smile brighter than the sunlight spreading across the world formed on Geom Mugeuk’s lips. It was the first time his brother had ever asked him to run together.

“Sounds good.”

The two unfolded their lightness skills and began to run forward.

Running alongside his brother, Geom Mugeuk shouted,

“Let’s go. Home!”

* * *

At the main Cult, the news they had been waiting for so eagerly was waiting for them.

“The Cult Leader has completed his closed-door training.”

At that news, Geom Mugeuk headed straight to the Cult Leader’s Hall with his brother.

In truth, he had thought the closed-door training would last longer than this. After all, it was training for the next stage of the Guhwa Demonic Art, which had already reached great completion.

Yet he had emerged sooner than expected. Then again, for someone like his father, the length of time probably had no meaning.

Had he truly made progress in the Guhwa Demonic Art?

“At last, my days as temporary Cult Leader are over!”

“Are you sad about it?”

“Sad? Whew, you only say that because you don’t know, Brother. Do you know how much work there is in that seat? Maybe Father ran away under the pretext of closed-door training because he didn’t want to work either.”

“Do you think Father is you?”

“How did you know? I left the Cult because I didn’t want to work.”

Geom Muyang shook his head with an expression that said there was no helping him.

“Ah, after we see Father, there are so many people I need to meet! All right, everyone, form a line!”

Geom Muyang thought he should first go meet Gwak Yeong and his sister. Since he had brought them all the way here, he ought to look after them.

And so the two entered the Cult Leader’s Hall.

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang slowly walked side by side along the Path of Blood.

Their father sat in the taishi chair, unchanged as ever.

‘Father, thank you.’

Now he knew. How precious and valuable it was to have someone remain steadfastly by one’s side.

Someday, that day would come.

The day he would have to walk this Path of Blood alone and sit in the empty taishi chair.

After walking to the foot of the taishi chair, Geom Mugeuk greeted him in a loud voice.

“Father, I have completed the task and returned to the Cult!”

Geom Muyang also greeted him with proper courtesy.

“I have returned, Cult Leader.”

Geom Ujin looked from one to the other, then spoke calmly.

“Did you have a safe journey?”

“Yes!”

The two men’s resounding answer echoed through the Cult Leader’s Hall.

“Congratulations on completing your closed-door training.”

As he greeted him, Geom Mugeuk stared intently at his father.

“Why are you looking at me like that?”

At his father’s question, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“No matter how much I look, I can’t tell. How did the closed-door training turn out?”

“If I trained, that is enough. What does the result matter?”

“It matters. This wasn’t anyone else, but you entering closed-door training after so long, Father.”

Geom Mugeuk felt a certain confidence in his father’s eyes.

It was a change he never would have noticed before. He had never even looked at his father’s face like this.

But now he could tell. Because he had kept looking, again and again, he could now see that subtle difference. He could see what that confidence meant.

‘Father has gained results!’

The eleventh stage? No. Even before entering closed-door training, his father’s martial arts had risen through continuous training. Then?

Great completion of the twelfth stage!

As his father’s life had changed recently, his martial arts must have changed as well.

Geom Mugeuk’s heart began to pound fiercely.

The twelfth-stage Guhwa Demonic Art! Just what kind of power would it have? Had the evil spirits changed too? And what about the Heavenly Demon Soul?

There had been many changes after his regression, but this was the moment that brought the most powerful change of all.

‘Congratulations, Father!’

It seemed those emotions were contained in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes and expression.

Geom Ujin sent him a look that seemed to say, ‘Surely you noticed?’

Geom Mugeuk answered with a look that seemed to say, ‘Do you not wish to boast?’

‘Please boast to your sons as much as you like.’

Of course, Geom Ujin was not the sort of person to boast like that. Instead, a reprimand came down.

“You caused trouble while I was away! You should have known that the two of you must not leave the Cult at the same time.”

“We knew. That’s why we went with the Demon Venerables.”

His father must have received all the reports from the Chief Strategist, who had surely been anxious the entire time they were out together.

Reports that the Poison King and the Demon Buddha had accompanied them, and that Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable had joined them as well.

But reports were reports.

Geom Mugeuk reported to his father what had happened outside.

It was something he always did whenever he returned after leaving the Cult. He told him in detail even the parts that could not be known from reports alone. Another kind of conversation between his father and himself. He showed this sight in front of his brother.

Brother, perhaps you might be even better at conversations like this. So watch carefully.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk would never miss an opportunity to tease his brother in this situation.

At the end of his report, he dragged his brother into it.

“Brother saved Master Gwak’s younger sister. In the past, he was the sort of person who would have given an order like, ‘It’s annoying, so get rid of them all!’”

Geom Muyang looked dumbfounded, but their father did not stop the report.

“I believe bringing those siblings back alive was meaningful in many ways, just as much as killing the mastermind this time. We gained a skilled young artisan as one of our own, and what is more important than that is…”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his brother standing beside him and continued.

“It was something that showed Brother has changed.”

A slight look of fluster passed through Geom Muyang’s eyes, but because they were before their father, he did not say anything.

“Lately, Brother is becoming cooler and cooler.”

“What is this? You are praising your brother?”

“He’s right beside me, isn’t he? Insults should be said when he’s not here.”

A sneer formed on Geom Ujin’s lips. At that unchanged sneer, Geom Mugeuk’s heart grew at ease.

‘You have no idea how much I wanted to see that smile.’

When the atmosphere was this good, one had to give a gift.

“Ah, and we have a gift we prepared. It’s a gift Brother chose.”

Geom Ujin’s gaze turned to Geom Muyang.

Geom Muyang took a scroll from his bosom and held it out with both hands.

When Geom Ujin extended his hand.

Srrrk.

The scroll flew through the air and entered his grasp.

Geom Ujin slowly opened the scroll and looked down at it in silence.

Their father stared at the scroll for a long time.

This was the first time their father had ever looked at something for so long.

Because the scroll hid his face, they could not see his expression. But somehow, Geom Mugeuk felt he knew what that expression would be like.

After Geom Ujin finished looking at the painting, he rolled up the scroll and set it down beside him.

“No, please don’t just put it down like that. There was something extraordinary about that painter. If you keep it well, you might be able to sell it for a high price later.”

Geom Ujin silently looked down at Geom Muyang.

The eyes of a father gazing at his eldest son.

From that gaze, Geom Mugeuk felt his father’s long-held regret. At this moment, his father was regretting.

If only he had become successor without killing his brother.

Father, we will be sure to fulfill that unfulfilled wish. We will live our whole lives preserving our brotherly affection.

Geom Mugeuk took something thickly wrapped from inside his robes.

“There is one more gift.”

When he heard that their father had emerged from seclusion, Geom Muyang had seen his brother grab that from the carriage. He had wondered what on earth he was taking so carefully.

Srrrk.

That, too, flew into Geom Ujin’s hand.

“They’re pajamas. Wuhan-brand floral pajamas. If I hadn’t seen them, that would be one thing, but since I did, I simply couldn’t pass them by.”

Geom Muyang was dumbfounded. It seemed he had gone back after all and bought those floral pajamas.

‘You fool, do you think Father would wear those?’

He thought their father’s thunderous rebuke would fall.

But their father wordlessly placed that gift down beside him as well.

Whether he was pleased with the two gifts, his father’s expression was gentle.

“Go and rest.”

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang bowed respectfully, then turned around.

After taking a few steps, Geom Mugeuk turned back as if he had just remembered something.

“Oh, Father. Sometime, the three of us should go hunting together with Brother.”

He could feel his brother standing beside him tense up. He must have been worried that Father might not be inclined to agree. It was truly a needless worry. Their father’s decision was far too quick.

“We depart tomorrow at dawn.”

Of course. Our father was a man who would never take his children’s schedules into consideration.

Chapter 677: This Alcohol Is Friends with My Brother
----------------------------------------------------
At the break of dawn, Geom Muyang stood anxiously at the entrance to Daecheonsan, where they had agreed to meet.

His heart had been trembling, and he had spent the entire night tossing and turning.

A hunt with Father.

Back when Geom Mugeuk had gone hunting with Father, he had wondered about it.

What had they talked about in the mountains?

At the time, he had hated his younger brother, so he had worried about what sort of wedge Mugeuk might have driven between him and Father.

Now, he was going on that hunt himself.

At that moment, Geom Muyang’s eyes gradually widened.

He was startled to see Geom Mugeuk walking toward him.

More precisely, he was startled by the pack Mugeuk was carrying on his back.

“Are you moving house?”

He really was carrying a mountain of luggage on his back.

“You only think that because you don’t know, Brother. Everything is necessary.”

He had tied one bundle after another on top of a large leather bag. Cooking utensils, a gourd flask, a dagger for dressing animals, even an axe dangled from it.

With three people going, there was even more luggage.

By comparison, Geom Muyang carried only a single small leather bag. Even that was less than half full. He had wondered what he ought to bring, but there was not much to pack.

“Don’t mind me. Just think of me as a porter!”

“Let’s split the load.”

“It’s fine. You have to attend to Father.”

That was what Mugeuk had said when the two of them met, but the moment Geom Ujin appeared, he began to play the victim.

“Oh, it’s so heavy. If your younger brother is carrying this much, surely an older brother could offer to take some of it.”

Of course, that did not work on Geom Ujin. The eldest son had obviously offered to share the load. That was precisely why Mugeuk was playing this little prank. By now, Muyang could see right through him.

“Muyang, let’s go.”

“Yes.”

Geom Ujin took only his eldest son into consideration and led the way up the mountain.

“Please give me your leather bag.”

“It’s fine.”

Father had also brought a small leather bag today. The first time he had gone hunting, he had come empty-handed.

What had he brought?

Geom Ujin walked at the front, Geom Muyang two or three steps behind him, and Geom Mugeuk at the very back.

Mugeuk deliberately lagged far behind.

So that Father and his older brother could speak comfortably.

Father and his older brother climbed the mountain path in silence, occasionally exchanging a few words. Muyang could have listened if he tried, but he did not.

Instead, he listened to the calls of mountain birds, the sound of water flowing along the valley, and the wind. He listened to insects chirping and leaves rustling.

The path changed from dirt to stone, and then, beneath the sunlight filtering through the leaves, became a jeweled road.

How long had they been walking like that?

“Shh.”

At Father’s signal, everyone held their breath.

Father pointed in one direction and asked his eldest son,

“Can you see it?”

Geom Muyang opened his eyes wide and searched ahead, but he could see nothing.

“Close your eyes and feel.”

“Yes.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. Father intended to teach his older brother how to release qi, just as he had taught Mugeuk during their previous hunt.

“Send out only a single thread of energy. Slowly, without breaking it. Slowly, like a silkworm spinning silk.”

All experts manipulated qi and used it to sense their surroundings.

But they did not think of drawing it out like thread and sending it ten or twenty zhang away. Without knowing the essential formula of carrying qi upon the wind, it was impossible.

Just as he had, his older brother must be experiencing something new now.

“It broke. Concentrate and try again!”

“Yes!”

Geom Muyang faithfully followed Father’s instructions.

When had he last spoken with Father so closely about martial arts? It had truly been a long time.

“Release your qi as if you were slowly unwinding a wound thread.”

It was a difficult and tense situation, yet it was not as uncomfortable as he had expected. Father was different when teaching martial arts from the way he felt while seated on the grand chair.

‘Was Father always this talkative?’

He had seemed like the sort of father who would say, “Figure it out yourself,” and turn away. But when it came to teaching martial arts, he was meticulous.

‘So this is what my younger brother felt from Father.’

Perhaps this was why Father had suggested the hunt—to let him experience this feeling as well.

That spending time with Father was not as uncomfortable as he had thought.

“Now, carry your qi upon the wind.”

At first, there was only a single thread. But later, dozens of strands of qi would stretch in every direction and spread out like a spiderweb. Geom Mugeuk had already reached that level.

“Again!”

Unlike Mugeuk, who had managed it with ease, his older brother was unable to master it quickly. Even so, he had to realize it for himself. Especially when it came to manipulating qi, one had to understand and accept it in one’s own way for it to truly be effective.

Father did not grow impatient. He waited for his older brother.

Yes, this was probably what was normal. He had simply learned too quickly.

Geom Mugeuk silently watched Father, seated on a rock as he guided his older brother.

Seeing that sight, his heart inexplicably swelled, and he turned his head away.

Had he sensed the emotion of that moment?

As Geom Mugeuk gazed at the mountains layered one behind another until they met the sky, he felt Father’s gaze upon him.

Mugeuk slowly looked toward Father. Their eyes met in the air. In Father’s gaze was the meaning, You arranged this well.

“Teaching me was fine, but teaching Brother instead must be driving you crazy, right?”

At those hateful words, Geom Ujin strode toward Geom Mugeuk.

“There is always someone else who drives me crazy.”

Father stopped right in front of him and asked coldly,

“Do you have nothing to say to me?”

His eyes said, I know everything. Geom Mugeuk flinched and avoided his gaze.

“You knew?”

“Of course!”

Mugeuk understood. Father had realized that he had passed the Heavenly Demon Bodyguard Technique on to his older brother.

“Weren’t you supposed to be unaware?”

Unless his older brother activated the Heavenly Demon Bodyguard Technique, there had been no reason for Father to notice it. The technique was impossible to recognize by sight alone.

‘Ah! He knew because he had achieved the twelfth-star perfection!’

Geom Mugeuk cried out,

“Brother forced me to teach him! He said he would kill me if I didn’t!”

“……”

“Ah, if he’d really gone that far, then he would have asked me to teach him the Nine Hells Demonic Art.”

Since he was still in the wrong, Geom Mugeuk dropped to his knees and begged.

“Father, please let it go just this once! Brother, come over here and beg, too!”

“Why are you dragging your brother into this?”

And just like that, he tried to let his brother escape.

“The situation was urgent. A powerful enemy who used Soul-Subduing Arts might have threatened Brother. Just imagine what would have happened if someone with an unpleasant personality like his had also fallen victim to Soul-Subduing Arts. Good grief, I wouldn’t have been able to stand by and watch.”

Geom Muyang should have reacted with disbelief, but he quietly walked over and knelt beside Geom Mugeuk.

He thought that remaining still and not drawing attention to himself might help the situation.

But this was about the Heavenly Demon Bodyguard Technique. He could not leave everything to his younger brother.

“I’m sorry.”

Geom Ujin asked Geom Muyang,

“Did you know it was the Heavenly Demon Bodyguard Technique when you learned it?”

“No.”

Geom Ujin wore an expression that said he had expected as much, but Geom Muyang defended his younger brother.

“My younger brother did it for my sake.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his older brother with deep emotion, but at the sight of Father’s sharp gaze, he hurriedly lowered his head and shouted,

“Please forgive me! Please let it go just this once!”

Geom Ujin looked down at his two sons and said quietly,

“I cannot let it go for nothing.”

He would forgive them, but not without extracting a price.

Geom Mugeuk raised his head abruptly.

“Fine. I’ll do anything you order. Anything except unifying the martial world!”

Beside him, Geom Muyang lifted his head and said,

“I’ll accept even unifying the martial world.”

Geom Mugeuk turned to him.

“Joking at a time like this? Brother, you’ve really changed.”

“I’m not joking.”

Yes, Mugeuk knew he was not joking. If Father wished it, his older brother would willingly stand at the vanguard of the Great Demonic War.

Whether it was because of his older brother’s answer, or because of what he intended to order them to do later, Father turned away with an inscrutable smile.

“Resume your training.”

“Yes!”

Geom Muyang rose and returned to his original position.

The qi-release training resumed.

They repeated it again and again.

How much time had passed like that?

At last, Geom Muyang released his qi and found the animal hiding in the distance.

“It seems to be a wild boar.”

Geom Ujin nodded with a satisfied expression.

Geom Mugeuk handed his older brother the bow and arrows hanging from his leather bag.

“Father must be hungry.”

* * *

“Brother, just start the campfire.”

Mugeuk had said he would take care of the rest himself, and he truly did.

The way Geom Mugeuk dressed the wild boar put hunters and chefs alike to shame.

He worked quickly and skillfully, then cut out the tastiest portions and skewered them on wooden spits.

Next, he took the seasonings needed for cooking from his leather bag.

He salted the meat and coated it with layer upon layer of his special sauce, deepening its flavor. The meat began to roast, filling the air with a delicious aroma.

Watching him, Geom Muyang was secretly astonished. They had camped in the forest several times on the way back to the Cult, but he had not known Mugeuk was this skilled. It was truly a dexterity entirely separate from his martial ability.

After he began roasting the meat, he started unpacking the rest of the luggage in earnest.

First, he opened the bundle he had carefully wrapped separately.

Whoosh!

A tiger hide spread out for Father. It was large enough for him to sit on and even lie down.

“Father, please sit here comfortably.”

Geom Ujin sat on it.

“Just as I thought! It suits you perfectly! You look magnificent!”

When Geom Ujin extended a hand, the wild boar over the campfire turned by itself.

“Of course! Right now, making sure the meat doesn’t burn is more important than looking magnificent!”

Geom Mugeuk opened the next bundle.

“This is for Brother and me.”

He laid out two ordinary animal pelts, clearly distinguishing them from Father’s. He placed his brother’s seat a little closer to Father.

“Now, I prepared this as well.”

From his leather bag came a small wooden tea table that could be folded and unfolded. He placed it in front of Father. Next, he took out chopsticks and tableware that he had washed clean and set them before him.

Geom Ujin seemed to be enjoying roasting the meat. He kept turning it with psychokinesis so that it would not burn.

When the meat had browned to perfection, Geom Mugeuk cut off the softest and most delicious portion and brought it to Father.

“Please eat, Father.”

He also cut meat for his older brother and himself.

“How is it?”

“It’s acceptable.”

Father’s “acceptable” was the highest praise, equivalent to saying it was superb.

His older brother proved it.

His eyes widened, and his expression said it all.

It’s delicious?

Geom Mugeuk laughed.

“The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Grand Duke caught it, the heir dressed it, and the Cult Leader roasted it! How could it possibly taste bad? If you say it’s bad, the entire family will be wiped out!”

At that, Geom Muyang laughed.

When he had tossed and turned all night from nervousness, he had never imagined the hunt would have such a pleasant atmosphere.

It was enjoyable. Meat eaten beside a campfire in the mountains had a flavor all its own.

“I’ll make it even tastier!”

Geom Mugeuk rummaged through his leather bag again. Geom Muyang watched to see what else would emerge.

“You can’t have meat without alcohol.”

This time, alcohol came out.

It was Father’s favorite liquor. Mugeuk had even prepared cups that had been washed clean, along with something chewy to eat as a side dish. He had been thorough in his preparations.

Just then, Geom Ujin extended a hand. The leather bag he had set beside him flew through the air and landed in his hand.

What was he planning to take out?

The brothers watched curiously.

“I brought a bottle as well.”

What Father took out was also alcohol.

Geom Mugeuk was puzzled. Since he had brought some the last time, Father must have assumed he would bring it again.

“What kind of liquor is it?”

Then words Mugeuk could never have imagined came from Father’s mouth.

“I brewed it myself the year Yang-i was born.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang were both shocked.

But Geom Muyang was especially shaken by the revelation that Father had brewed it himself in the year he was born. At that moment, one word from Father filled his mind.

Yang-i.

When had Father ever called him Yang-i? He had heard it once when he was very young, and today was the first time since.

That was not all.

‘He brewed the liquor himself?’

Emotion surged through Geom Muyang’s heart. And not just any liquor—he had made it to commemorate Muyang’s birth?

And he had brought that liquor today.

“……Cult Leader.”

Geom Muyang’s voice trembled. It was the first time Geom Mugeuk had seen his older brother tremble like this.

Father lifted the bottle and said,

“Today, I came as your father.”

“!”

“Come here and accept a cup of my liquor.”

“……Yes, Father.”

Geom Muyang approached Father and respectfully accepted the drink.

“You come here as well.”

Geom Mugeuk also went over and received a cup. His older brother was trembling so badly that Mugeuk could not even make a joke.

Geom Muyang respectfully filled Father’s cup, and the three of them drank together.

“It’s truly superb!”

Geom Mugeuk exclaimed. It was not an empty compliment; the liquor really was delicious.

“This liquor is Brother’s friend. His drinking companion.”

The three of them passed the bottle back and forth until they emptied it. They drank it all freely, without sparing a drop.

Of all the liquor Geom Muyang had ever tasted since birth, this was the finest. He felt that he might never drink anything so delicious again for the rest of his life.

“When should we drink the liquor brewed in the year I was born? At our next hunt?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s excited question, Geom Ujin flinched.

“You brewed one for me too, of course, right? No matter how much you favor your eldest son, your second son is still your child. Right?”

At the Cult Leader’s ambiguous expression, Geom Mugeuk asked with an expression of dawning horror,

“Don’t tell me you didn’t brew it yourself and had Uncle Hui do it? Hui, go brew a bottle of liquor. He’s still your child, so we ought to commemorate it.”

As Father continued to wear that strange smile, Geom Mugeuk raised his voice.

“Don’t tell me you forgot even that, did you? Is that why you’re smiling so awkwardly? Please tell me that isn’t true! Tell me you at least bought some and had it brewed!”

And so, the first day of the hunt was drawing to a close.

Chapter 678: Eating, Drinking, and Lazing Around
------------------------------------------------
“You’ve changed lately.”

At the words that came from behind him, Gowol turned around to find Cult Leader Pungcheon standing there.

“What do you mean, Cult Leader?”

At Gowol’s courteous attitude, Cult Leader Pungcheon radiated displeasure.

“You’ve changed starting with that cold, formal way you speak to me.”

“Aren’t there many eyes watching here?”

The place where the two stood was the inner courtyard of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“Many eyes, my foot! No one is paying attention to us!”

Of course, everyone was paying attention. Who would dare ignore him, the former Cult Leader Pungcheon and master of the current Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable?

Cult Leader Pungcheon, who had returned to the cult before Geom Mugeuk, had sought out Gowol first.

But even after returning to the cult, Gowol was so busy with something or other that it was hard to even see his face. Cult Leader Pungcheon’s dissatisfaction had begun there.

How could Gowol not know how he felt? At least for today, he would stop using busyness as an excuse.

“Pungcheon.”

At Gowol’s informal address, Cult Leader Pungcheon’s expression eased instead.

“I truly enjoy my work.”

At that, Cult Leader Pungcheon asked as if pressing him.

“Do you really enjoy it?”

With a determined look in his eyes, Cult Leader Pungcheon said to Gowol,

“I’m telling you, you’ve changed. In the past, I could hear your heart pounding. Now all I hear is the sound of your head turning. You used to be burning hot, but now you’ve gone cold.”

And as always, he laid the blame on one person.

“This is all because of the Young Cult Leader. He didn’t even know that if he entrusted work to a perfectionist like you, the result would obviously turn out like this. No, that’s not right. He must have known. There’s no way someone that clever wouldn’t know.”

Of course, Cult Leader Pungcheon liked Geom Mugeuk too.

But seeing Gowol suffer like this made him angry.

Because after finally setting him free, Gowol had put the shackles back on himself. No matter what anyone said, to him, Gowol came before the Young Cult Leader.

“Did you turn me into a cold-blooded man just so you could curse the Young Cult Leader?”

Even at Gowol’s joke, Cult Leader Pungcheon did not let go of his mood.

“What did the Young Cult Leader say? It’s obvious. I’m sorry for making you suffer. Let’s endure just a little longer. I’ll be counting on you from now on! Am I right?”

“I haven’t met him yet.”

“What?”

“He went hunting with the Cult Leader and the First Young Master.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon looked dumbfounded.

“So there was a real cold-blooded one elsewhere. See? Even if I try not to curse the Young Cult Leader, I can’t help it. No matter how loyal you are, the Young Cult Leader puts family first. Family always comes first.”

“Isn’t that only natural?”

Then Gowol stared straight at Cult Leader Pungcheon and added,

“Aren’t I first to you, Pungcheon? To me, you come before anything else.”

“!”

Those words meant that he was family.

They were also the method that undid Cult Leader Pungcheon’s anger in a single blow.

“Because we know we’re that kind of people to each other, the Young Cult Leader uses me, and I am loyal to the Young Cult Leader. There’s a trust that comes from someone who has things he must protect.”

After a brief pause, Cult Leader Pungcheon said,

“It’s because you say things like that so lightly that you always end up suffering like this.”

Gowol’s gaze turned toward the Tongcheon Pavilion building visible in the distance.

“I have to suffer. I’m on the path to becoming the master of that place.”

It was the first moment he had revealed his ambition to become the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s chief strategist.

“Pungcheon, I’m not cold. I’m burning hotter than ever.”

“Good for you, being hot.”

At his needless petulance, Gowol smiled.

“Pungcheon, the Young Cult Leader is a man great enough for us to stake our entire lives on. We’re the ones using the Young Cult Leader.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon lifted his head and looked toward distant Daecheon Mountain, then said loudly,

“The Young Cult Leader must have it nice. He has a passionate strategist who understands everything, a father, and an older brother too. Damn it! How is someone without family supposed to live, feeling this wronged?”

At that, Gowol moved and stood side by side with Cult Leader Pungcheon.

“Why don’t you have family? You’re offending the family member listening.”

* * *

The hunt continued on the second day as well.

To be precise, it was the discovery of prey. My older brother continued training in projecting his qi and found all kinds of animals. Blood could not be denied; his skill in projecting qi improved rapidly.

It was nearly as fast as mine, with my Heavenly Martial Body, and I could understand why.

It was because Father’s martial realm as a teacher had grown even higher.

“You’re teaching Brother better than you taught me! You never explained things like that when you were teaching me!”

Father did not even pretend to hear me and immersed himself in teaching his eldest son. He was pouring out, to his heart’s content, the affection he had not been able to give all this time.

While training like that in qi projection, at last, something big appeared.

“I think it’s a tiger!”

At Geom Muyang’s words, Geom Wujin nodded.

“We came hunting, so we ought to catch at least one tiger before we leave.”

Geom Muyang launched himself first, and Geom Wujin and Geom Mugeuk followed after him.

Landing lightly on a rock, Geom Muyang silently drew his bowstring.

Geom Wujin and Geom Mugeuk landed soundlessly behind him. Even if it had not been a tiger but a massive spirit creature carrying a neidan, it would not have been able to escape this hunt.

In the distance, the tiger came into view. It truly was a great tiger of enormous size.

The bowstring was pulled taut.

The arrow that seemed ready to fly at any moment and pierce the tiger did not leave the string.

Geom Muyang spoke respectfully to Father.

“It is so large that it seems like a sacred tiger guarding Daecheon Mountain, so I wished to spare it.”

Father nodded.

Just as the two were about to turn away without regret.

“Not because of those little ones over there?”

Geom Mugeuk was pointing at one place. A little distance away from the tiger were two tigers that appeared to be cubs. Since I had seen them, Father and Brother must have seen them too.

“Father, you should say it to Brother as well. That it’s cheap sentimentality perfect for getting yourself killed. If I had done this, that’s what you would have said! You would have told me to catch it at once and replace the hide I sleep on with that one!”

Geom Mugeuk saw it. One corner of Father’s mouth lifting ever so slightly.

Yes, if it had been you, I would have.

Geom Wujin spoke firmly and turned to walk away.

“One does not kill spirit beasts carelessly.”

Brother strode after him.

Watching the tiger cubs follow their mother in the distance, Geom Mugeuk shouted,

“Second-born, let’s hang in there!”

A smile formed on the lips of Geom Wujin, who was walking in front.

It was a rare and precious smile, but unfortunately, his two sons could not see it.

* * *

That night as well, a campfire burned.

Geom Mugeuk liked this time better than the day.

Roasting meat together with Father and Brother, drinking together. Borrowing the strength of the liquor to talk about this and that.

And if, like tonight, the stars in the night sky seemed to be pouring down, adding their own elegance, then his mood grew even better.

“Father, may I loosen up my posture a bit?”

“When have you ever found that difficult?”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and relaxed his posture. He stretched his legs out, propped himself up with his arms, and leaned his body back at an angle.

Then he looked up at the stars in the night sky. A cool breeze blew in and tossed his hair. Ah, this is nice! The words came out on their own.

“Brother, you should loosen up too. The true pleasure of camping lies in eating, drinking, and rolling around.”

Given Brother’s personality, it was absolutely impossible in front of Father.

Still, compared to that dawn when they had first set out hunting, Brother had relaxed quite a bit.

“Father, I will read a book.”

This time, a book came out of the leather pouch that seemed to contain all manner of things.

Since he had never expected him to read a book while out hunting, Geom Muyang asked curiously,

“What kind of book is it?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk lifted the cover of the book to show him. It was a poetry collection.

“You read poetry too?”

“Goodness, Brother. You only know how to kill people. What would you know about poetry? About literature?”

From the moment he took out the book and brought this up, he had a purpose.

“Ah, though you do know a little about music.”

At the mention of music, Geom Muyang flinched.

Don’t tell me, you?

Sorry, Brother.

Before Geom Muyang could send a voice transmission to stop him, Geom Mugeuk spoke to Father first.

“Father, did you know Brother can play the danso?”

His flustered brother looked at Father. Almost no one knew, so how could Father know? Yet surprisingly, Father slowly nodded.

“See? I told you. Father knew.”

Father truly knew many things about his two sons.

It was only astonishing that he had never let it show.

“Brother, if only in consideration of Father’s hidden affection, we should have another drink.”

Geom Mugeuk reached into his leather pouch to take out liquor.

But instead of a liquor bottle, something else came out.

The moment Geom Muyang saw it, it drew a sigh from him. It was a danso.

“Huh? Why is this in here? I must have packed it by mistake while gathering my things in a hurry.”

As if! Geom Muyang shook his head.

Father did not stop his younger brother’s prank. Which meant?

If his younger brother had made up his mind anyway, he would eventually end up playing.

Geom Muyang stepped forward first.

“My skill is lacking, but may I play one piece?”

Geom Wujin nodded.

Geom Mugeuk felt he understood the feeling contained in that blunt nod. Father had been waiting for this moment for a long time.

Meanwhile, Geom Muyang, who had composed himself, brought trembling lips to the danso. He was probably more nervous now than he would be in front of thousands or tens of thousands of people.

When a clear, deep sound flowed out, Geom Mugeuk saw it.

The sight of Father gently closing his eyes.

Words that had never been spoken became a beautiful melody and rang out. The tune was like the sound of water flowing through a valley, and also like the flickering campfire. The pouring stars, intoxicated by the music, shone even brighter.

Thus, the clear and deep sound that burrowed into the heart rang across Daecheon Mountain.

At last, when the performance ended, Geom Wujin opened his eyes.

The gaze with which the father looked at his son was gentle.

“It is truly good.”

Even “good” alone would have been high praise, yet the word “truly” had been added before it.

“Thank you, Father.”

Geom Mugeuk had never seen his brother’s face look so unburdened.

Perhaps something that had settled heavily in his brother’s heart had vanished through this hunt.

When Geom Muyang’s gaze turned toward him, Geom Mugeuk shouted,

“Believe me! I didn’t mean to pack the danso. I mistook it for a skewer for roasting meat and brought it!”

He did not know if he had ever said these words to his younger brother before.

“Thank you.”

The mischief disappeared from Geom Mugeuk’s playful face.

“Thank you, Brother.”

Their father watched in silence as his sons exchanged words of gratitude.

“Who did we take after to become this wonderful? As you saw, Father, your sons are the kind who read poetry and play instruments.”

As he said it almost like a boast, Geom Mugeuk saw it.

A faint longing passing through Father’s eyes. It was an emotion he had sensed from Father for the first time.

But the emotion vanished in an instant.

Father lay down with his back to the tiger hide.

“Let us sleep now.”

* * *

The next day, the three of them climbed to the summit of Daecheon Mountain.

Here, where the boundary between sky and earth had collapsed.

The peaks rising all around looked like martial arts masters standing tall.

The three stood at the summit and gazed upon the magnificent scenery.

The last time he had come up here with Father, Father had told him. When martial arts training was difficult, he would come up here.

Over Father’s back, the back of Father in his youth seemed to overlap.

‘What were you thinking back then, and what are you thinking now?’

At that moment, Father asked with his back turned.

“Your Guhwa Demonic Art has reached the ninth stage, hasn’t it?”

What Father had seen through was not only the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art that Brother had learned.

“Yes, I have reached the ninth stage.”

What about Father?

“How long did it take you to reach great completion from the ninth stage?”

The time Father had taken was.

“Seven years.”

That, too, must have been possible only because Father possessed innate talent.

“You could accomplish it within five years.”

Geom Wujin acknowledged his son’s martial talent to that extent.

After all, he himself had not reached the ninth stage at Geom Mugeuk’s age.

If he achieved great completion within five years, he would become the cult leader who reached the great completion of the Guhwa Demonic Art the fastest in history—no, he would even create the legend of reaching great completion while still the Young Cult Leader, before ascending to the position of Cult Leader.

“If, during training, your chest feels stifled or you sense a wall, climb Daecheon Mountain. The energy of Daecheon Mountain will certainly help you.”

“Yes, I will.”

Geom Mugeuk said proudly to Geom Muyang,

“This is the kind of person I am. I’m the heir Father lends this entire great Daecheon Mountain to!”

If he said that, Brother ought to argue back: How is that lending? Is that something to brag about to the brother who was pushed out of the heir’s position?

“I’m envious.”

For a moment, Geom Mugeuk flinched. He had no way to respond to that powerful blow.

“You just looked down on me, didn’t you? Ah, Brother, you’re saying that because you don’t know, but Father brewed liquor in the year I was born too. He’s just saying that to tease me. Now, Father. It’s time to confess! My son, on the day you were born!”

Geom Wujin strode away as he said,

“The sun is setting. Let us descend now.”

Geom Muyang approached Geom Mugeuk.

“I said it’s a misunderstanding!”

“Give me the pack.”

He was asking for the pack Geom Mugeuk carried on his back.

“It’s fine. Escort Father to the end today.”

After a brief silence, Brother nodded and turned away.

Then, after walking a little distance, he looked back and asked,

“Aren’t you tired?”

Geom Mugeuk could tell that he did not simply mean the pack on his back.

“How could I not be? I’m dying from exhaustion.”

At those words, Brother grinned and then resumed walking.

Geom Mugeuk stood there and watched the backs of the two as they walked away. Compared to when they had climbed up, Brother was walking one step closer to Father.

Now that they had grown one step closer, it had become a relationship that would require twice the effort. Disappointment and hurt feelings were fellows that waited only for closeness.

From somewhere far away came the roar of a tiger.

Thinking that perhaps it might be the tiger Brother had spared, he moved to follow the two.

“Of course I thought you’d carry the pack on the way down, Father! Brother only knows how to kill people and play the danso—he’s this cold-hearted. Can you not hear me? Father?”

Chapter 679: Only the Beginning of a Long Journey
-------------------------------------------------
The first person Geom Mugeuk met upon returning from the hunt was Go Wol.

“Young Cult Leader!”

Go Wol felt humbled that Geom Mugeuk had come to see him at his quarters.

“If you had sent word, I would have come to see you.”

“I rushed over because I wanted to see you as soon as possible.”

“I’m relieved you returned safely.”

Geom Mugeuk expressed his sincere gratitude to Go Wol.

“If it weren’t for you, I wouldn’t have been able to roam the streets of Muhan so freely. Thank you.”

Because Eunwol’s help had been immense in this Muhan operation.

“You seem to have lost some weight since I last saw you. Are you ill anywhere?”

“I’m fine.”

Then, a gruff voice came from behind.

When Geom Mugeuk turned, the Pungcheon Sect Leader was standing there.

“And when the leader asks if you’re hurt, you say no? I’m hurt! Where is a healer? If it were you, you would have listed every ailment, real or imagined.”

“Of course, I would have made a fuss. I don’t even know what’s hurting on my own body; how could anyone else know? If you don’t speak up, no one will ever know. But Sect Leader, how did you know I was coming and arrive at just the right time?”

At that, the Pungcheon Sect Leader approached Geom Mugeuk, narrowed his eyes, and glared at him.

“I was watching to see whether you would come to meet Commander Go first after returning from the hunt.”

“No wonder. My survival instincts led my steps this way.”

The Pungcheon Sect Leader wanted to nag more, but Go Wol spoke with his eyes. That’s enough!

Go Wol asked Geom Mugeuk.

“How was the hunt?”

Geom Mugeuk answered with a pleasant smile.

“It was the most splendid hunt of my life.”

Go Wol knew full well how difficult this hunt had been to accomplish.

He had to win a bloodless succession struggle, open the heart of the Eldest Young Master who had been pushed aside in that conflict, and move the Cult Leader’s heart as well.

If he had been told to accomplish it himself?

He felt it would be easier to strike and defeat the Murim Alliance.

Yet Geom Mugeuk had accomplished that difficult task.

“Young Cult Leader, you must be busy, so please attend to your affairs.”

Was he the only one who wished to see him? All Eight Demon Lords would be eager to see Geom Mugeuk. Not to mention Ian, Seo Daeryong, and Jang Ho.

The mere fact that Geom Mugeuk had come to see him first was already deeply moving and appreciated.

That was why there was something Go Wol wished to say.

“Young Cult Leader. From now on, you need not concern yourself with me.”

The Pungcheon Sect Leader tried to step forward with an expression calling it nonsense, but Go Wol suppressed him with a look. Sect Leader, stay out of this!

“I am not someone the Young Cult Leader needs to worry about. Rather, my role is to resolve the matters that concern you. That is what a strategist does.”

In the end, the Pungcheon Sect Leader couldn’t hold back and stepped forward.

“Are you a slave? Saying you’ll work hard even if he doesn’t look after you.”

Since Geom Mugeuk was right there, Go Wol spoke politely to the Pungcheon Sect Leader.

“I am receiving sufficient compensation.”

“What compensation?”

“My monthly salary, is it not?”

At those words, the Pungcheon Sect Leader snorted.

“What are a few measly coins worth?”

“I receive a great deal. As far as I know, it is second only to the Chief Strategist.”

For a moment, the Pungcheon Sect Leader flinched in surprise.

“Truly?”

“Of course. I am not one to work for free. I am someone who secures the best possible treatment, by whatever means.”

When the Pungcheon Sect Leader asked with eyes seeking confirmation, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“I would like to pay you the most, but I cannot do so while the Chief Strategist serves.”

After blinking for a moment, the Pungcheon Sect Leader demanded of Geom Mugeuk.

“Why don’t I receive one? Haven’t I suffered greatly by Commander Go’s side?”

“I know how hard our Sect Leader has worked.”

“Then why won’t you give me one?”

“If you receive a salary, it means you are officially coming under my command. How could I dare do such a thing?”

The Pungcheon Sect Leader smacked his lips and pondered briefly.

“I’ll join, so give me one too.”

Of course, he said it half in jest. No matter how much the salary was, it was merely pocket change to someone of his vast wealth.

“That would be difficult.”

“Why? I’m swallowing my pride to join!”

“Since you are the master of the Seophyeon Demon Lord, you cannot receive a salary under me. Rather, it is I who should receive an allowance. Young Cult Leader, my thanks. This isn’t much, but please use it. Speaking of which, please give me some spending money.”

“Enough! That’s it!”

The Pungcheon Sect Leader, who had tried to shake it off only to nearly get further entangled, said to Go Wol with a dumbfounded expression.

“Did you see? Did you hear? He was trying to squeeze even a single penny from me. The Young Cult Leader is such a ruthless man.”

Geom Mugeuk and Go Wol looked at each other and laughed.

“Come, Commander Go. There is somewhere we need to go together. Sect Leader, you should come too.”

Geom Mugeuk led the two inside the inner courtyard.

A short distance from the Young Cult Leader’s quarters, a building had been erected.

It had been completed only recently, but the plaque above the main gate was covered with a black cloth.

“What is this place?”

At Go Wol’s question, Geom Mugeuk raised his hand and flicked it slightly.

Swish.

The black cloth covering the plaque was pulled away.

Two characters were engraved on the plaque.

Eunwol.

The moment he saw them, Go Wol’s eyes went wide.

“It’s the Eunwol Main Hall.”

At those words, the Pungcheon Sect Leader was also startled, for he had never expected a plaque bearing the name Eunwol to be hung within the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Geom Mugeuk had already resolved the most important issue as well.

“Father has already granted permission, so Eunwol has become an official organization of the main cult. My direct organization.”

Ever since Go Wol and the Pungcheon Sect Leader had traveled the Central Plains building the Eunwol organization, this was what Geom Mugeuk had been preparing.

Go Wol was deeply moved. He had never dreamed that Geom Mugeuk had been making such preparations for him.

“Come, shall we take a look inside?”

Following Geom Mugeuk, the two entered.

“It cannot compare to the Tongcheon Hall in scale, but it has everything it needs.”

As they opened the door and entered the hallway, the Pungcheon Sect Leader observed the walls on both sides and spoke.

“These walls are no ordinary walls.”

“Yes, you have a keen eye. Traps have been installed. They are not yet active, but once operational, no one will be able to enter recklessly. Since this is a place that handles information, security must be thorough.”

They were no ordinary traps. They were mechanisms for the highest level of security.

Just by looking at this alone, one could see how much effort Geom Mugeuk had put in.

After passing through the long hallway lined with mechanisms, a large space opened up.

“This is the operations room.”

It was not merely a newly erected building. The interior facilities were perfect as well.

One wall was designed to process information flying in from all over the Central Plains through mechanical devices.

“This is your office.”

There was a fine desk, a comfortable chair, and bookshelves, and it was structured so that the entire operations room situation could be observed from there.

Geom Mugeuk opened the door leading inside the office.

“This is your room.”

In the fairly spacious room, there was only one thing: a bed.

“Here, do not read books, do not sit and think—just sleep.”

It was a room that contained Geom Mugeuk’s heart, thinking of Go Wol’s health.

Go Wol was truly grateful for such meticulous consideration.

“Thank you for your consideration.”

Go Wol’s voice trembled.

The facilities were not the only things.

“Rest spaces have also been prepared for the martial artists working here. Dedicated chefs will be on hand to cook.”

Not only workspaces, but places to eat, sleep, rest, and wash were all perfectly prepared.

“As a precaution, secret spaces have been created where you and your strategists can hide. Of course, there is also an emergency escape route to exit the building.”

If Eunwol in the inner courtyard were attacked, it would already mean the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had been breached, but Geom Mugeuk had considered even such a worst-case scenario. Though Go Wol knew martial arts, the strategists were likely people who knew none.

“If there is ever need for force in Eunwol going forward, contact the Ghost Shadow Corps led by Ian. They will assist actively.”

“Understood, Young Cult Leader.”

Go Wol was truly moved. Now, his own place had been established within the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. It was the moment he saw the fruits of the hardships endured to build the Eunwol organization in the Central Plains.

Without a word, Go Wol expressed his gratitude with a respectful martial salute.

Geom Mugeuk also received the greeting with cupped hands, as if to say he was counting on him.

‘Commander Go, this is only the beginning of a long road.’

Geom Mugeuk intended to make Go Wol the Chief Strategist when he became Cult Leader.

He intended to let Sama Myeong, who had devoted his life to the main cult, step down honorably from the position of Chief Strategist and spend his remaining years in comfort.
