Of course, having worked his entire life, Sama Myeong would not easily adapt to such a comfortable life, but surely he would find the joys of leisure in time.

The Pungcheon Sect Leader, who had been speaking ill of Geom Mugeuk until moments ago, now asked with an awkward expression.

“Is there no room for me?”

* * *

After meeting Go Wol and the Pungcheon Sect Leader, Geom Mugeuk headed to the Valley of Villains.

Upon entering the Valley of Villains, the Faceless Guests passing by greeted him respectfully.

It was not a greeting given merely because he was the successor. The eyes behind their masks were smiling brightly. They were truly delighted to see him.

It was thanks to having grown close to the Faceless Guests during the last battle with the Dark King. Geom Mugeuk was now glad to see them as well.

Receiving such a warm welcome from the Faceless Guests, Geom Mugeuk entered Geukak Soma’s quarters.

The moment he entered the room, Geom Mugeuk said nothing and watched Geukak Soma.

Geukak Soma stood in the center of the room.

Before him floated a dagger. It was the ten-thousand-year cold iron dagger that Geom Mugeuk had previously given him.

The moment Geukak Soma snatched the dagger.

Chak! Chakchakchakchakchak! Chakchak! Chaaaaak!

Geukak Soma swung the dagger in continuous strikes. Thrusting, slashing, blocking, then thrusting again.

Short blade lines created by the dagger in the air appeared in succession.

The form created by those continuous lines was truly unique.

‘A trajectory I have never seen before.’

If the dagger were to fly along such a trajectory, could one truly evade it easily?

After unleashing that barrage with the dagger, Geukak Soma slowly caught his breath and turned to look at Geom Mugeuk.

“You have come?”

At the unchanged, ever-polite greeting, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and said.

“Yes, I have returned.”

Geom Mugeuk walked toward Geukak Soma and asked.

“Was that martial art from just now, perhaps…?”

“Yes, that is correct. It is the Sohyang Bisul.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. That Geukak Soma had deliberately shown him the Sohyang Bisul upon hearing of his visit.

“Truly fantastic!”

“It is still lacking, but I am gradually growing accustomed to it.”

If the incomplete Sohyang Bisul was already this impressive, the completed Sohyang Bisul would display unimaginable power.

“It is thanks to the Young Cult Leader granting me the secret manual of the Sohyang Bisul.”

As he spoke, Geukak Soma lifted his mask onto the top of his head.

In the past, when he wore a mask, he had often worn it raised to the top of his head like that, facing the heavens.

Now, Geukak Soma had revealed his face with his mask raised like that.

“I think hiding this handsome face behind a mask is the same as storing the Heavenly Demon Sword in a vault.”

At such an excessive comparison, Geukak Soma was slightly embarrassed.

Until now, such expressions had surely all been hidden behind his mask.

“I sometimes take it off like this even when alone. I try to be as comfortable as possible.”

Geukak Soma was gradually changing. Having taken off his mask only before Geom Mugeuk, he had now begun to remove it even when alone.

Especially, completely removing the mask and removing it by lifting it atop his head felt distinctly different. It felt freer.

“Lord Soma. You seem somewhat different from when I last saw you.”

It was not merely a change in how he removed his mask.

The aura emanating from Geukak Soma was different.

Geukak Soma had risen in martial prowess through two days of sparring with him and real combat against enemies.

Yet the aura visible now felt different from that time as well.

Then Geukak Soma smiled.

“As expected! The Young Cult Leader sees right through me.”

Even after Geom Mugeuk had left the cult, Geukak Soma devoted himself to martial arts training.

He faithfully upheld Geom Mugeuk’s request to grow stronger.

As the Sohyang Bisul newly harmonized with Magik Gwangpokjang and Hyeolangji, his martial realm began to move toward a new level once again.

If these three martial arts were achieved in harmony, he would truly be able to display formidable divine might.

“All of this is thanks to the Young Cult Leader.”

“Of course, it’s all thanks to me, whom Lord Soma saves every time!”

“I believe you have saved me far more often?”

The two looked at each other and smiled brightly.

“By the way, how is my sister-in-law?”

She was doing well. In fact, he had been thinking of meeting her soon.

“Ah, that’s good.”

Geom Mugeuk took something from his bosom.

“If you go, please deliver this to my sister-in-law.”

What Geom Mugeuk took out was one of the three items he had selected from the Murim Treasury: the Sinnyeo Jimong.

The moment he saw the Sinnyeo Jimong, he had felt he should give it to Cheonhwa Pavilion Master Yeojeong. He was simply following that instinct.

He had intended to meet the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master directly to deliver it, but he had been away from the cult for too long and had been unable to stop by the Cheonhwa Pavilion. He could not entrust it to just anyone, but if Geukak Soma went himself, he could trust him with it.

Geukak Soma’s gaze turned toward the Sinnyeo Jimong.

An ordinary cloth from which nothing could be felt. Because of that, it seemed even more important. If it were not something significant to her, Geom Mugeuk would not have asked him to deliver it.

“Is simply delivering it enough?”

“Yes. If my intuition is correct.”

Geom Mugeuk handed over the Sinnyeo Jimong and said.

“You will surely know what kind of item it is.”

Chapter 680: Even If I Lay Down the Sword in My Hand
----------------------------------------------------
When Geom Muyang entered the ironworks, he saw Gwak Yeong in the distance.

She was loading chunks of iron onto a handcart and moving them.

Geom Muyang did not go to her, but walked straight to where Artisan Gwak was working.

Artisan Gwak, who had been inspecting finished weapons, saw Geom Muyang and greeted him.

“I am glad you returned safely.”

Geom Muyang also returned the courtesy respectfully.

“Thanks to your concern, I made it back well. Have you been well?”

At that, Artisan Gwak gave a strange smile and shook his head.

“No. Thanks to you, Eldest Young Master, I had a bit to ponder.”

Geom Muyang could guess what he meant. He was surely speaking of Gwak Yeong.

In the letter he had sent to Artisan Gwak, Geom Muyang had conveyed the facts exactly as they were.

That Gwak Yeong had been an artisan of the Murim Alliance’s ironworks, and that she had made counterfeit hidden weapons in order to save her younger sibling.

He had told him to see for himself and decide whether to accept her into the cult’s ironworks. He had also said that he was sending her only because her skill would be a waste, and that she had no connection to him, so Artisan Gwak should judge as he saw fit.

He intended only to introduce her to the ironworks, and had no thought of exerting any further influence. All authority here belonged to Artisan Gwak, and Geom Muyang respected that.

“I already knew the rumors about that woman.”

How could he not? The person who had become an artisan of the Murim Alliance’s ironworks at the youngest age ever. And as a woman, at that.

Artisan Gwak brought up something unexpected.

“The Gwak bloodline is well versed in handling iron.”

“Could it be?”

“Yes, that is right. I checked, and she too is a descendant of the Gwak Family Iron Gate.”

The Gwak Family Iron Gate.

It was a family once known to possess the greatest skill in all the Central Plains when it came to making weapons.

But at some point, the brothers of the Iron Gate quarreled, drew blades against one another, and scattered in every direction. The time it took for a great family to fall was no more than half a day.

The reason Gwak Yeong had been able to become an artisan of the Murim Alliance’s ironworks at such a young age was also because she had inherited the exceptional bloodline of the Gwak Family Iron Gate.

Artisan Gwak’s name was Gwak In.

He too was a descendant of the Gwak Family Iron Gate, and strictly speaking, he was related to Gwak Yeong.

“That was why I agonized over it all the more. Ordinarily, I would have accepted her at once, but I worried it might look as though I was taking her in because she was of the same house.”

Artisan Gwak was, by nature, a man of upright character.

“But you accepted her.”

When Artisan Gwak had first seen the counterfeit Jin-Gyeok, he had truly been impressed. The workmanship was so fine he could have believed they had made it themselves. Moreover, when he learned that she had made them not in a proper ironworks, but in a converted storehouse, with people who were not true ironworks technicians, he could not refuse her. Of course, that did not mean he could immediately entrust her with an important post.

“I told her to start from the bottom, like anyone entering the ironworks for the first time. In any case, if she wants to adapt here, she will have to prove herself with skill.”

How long that would take was impossible to know.

Artisan Gwak’s gaze turned toward Gwak Yeong, who was moving iron in the distance.

“That child must have lived thinking of herself as a sword already complete. But when she melts herself down again and begins from molten iron, I too am curious what kind of sword will emerge.”

Geom Muyang looked at her, her face flushed red, sweat pouring down as she did menial labor.

After gazing at her for a moment, Geom Muyang bade Artisan Gwak farewell.

“Then I will take my leave.”

After exchanging parting greetings with Artisan Gwak, Geom Muyang walked out of the ironworks.

He deliberately did not go to see her. He thought that acknowledging her in front of everyone would not help her.

Gwak Yeong also knew Geom Muyang had come, but she did not look at him. In her heart, she wanted to run to him and thank him. To thank him for arranging for her to enter the ironworks like this. But she did not.

The one she had to befriend now was not Geom Muyang, not Artisan Gwak, not her seniors, and not her colleagues.

The sole partner before her now was this.

Iron.

Depending on how close she became to this, her relationships with everyone else would also be decided.

‘I’ll keep the promise I made you after I marry this iron. Ah, I suppose it’ll be a remarriage.’

* * *

“Elder!”

After leaving the Valley of Villains, Geom Mugeuk ran straight to the residence of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

When he opened the door and went in, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was in the courtyard practicing martial arts.

The training of the Demon Venerables, which had spread like a trend.

In truth, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had been at the forefront of that trend. He had resumed martial training quite some time ago.

And recently, with Geom Ujin entering closed-door cultivation as the occasion, each of the Demon Venerables had felt a sense of mission. They had gained a goal: they had to overcome the wall that blocked their path.

The martial art the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was currently displaying was the Annihilating Heaven Demonic Blade Form.

The third form, Annihilating Blade Blood Gale.

Whoooooosh!

A fierce blade energy extending from his blade whirled forth like a storm. Since he had unleashed such a perilous martial art in the courtyard, it seemed certain the surroundings would be devastated.

Just at the moment when the blade energies were about to blow away the garden wall—

Ssssss.

As if it were a lie, the blade energies vanished right before the wall.

Seeing that, Geom Mugeuk could tell. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was deploying the Annihilating Heaven Demonic Blade Form only within a precisely fixed area.

Just as walking slowly up into the air would be harder than soaring upward swiftly, performing martial arts under such restrictions was a far higher realm than grinding the wall to powder and destroying the buildings behind it as well.

‘The elder has reached a new realm too!’

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon swung the Heaven-Annihilating Great Blade again. This time, the target was Geom Mugeuk.

Shwaaaaaak!

An immense wave of blade energy surged toward Geom Mugeuk, who stood before the door.

The second form of the Annihilating Heaven Demonic Blade Form, Annihilating Blade Wave.

Geom Mugeuk did not dodge that terrifying attack.

At the moment of absolute crisis—

Ssssss.

Just as before, the incoming wave of blade energy vanished like a lie right before Geom Mugeuk’s face.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shouted at Geom Mugeuk.

“Do you want to die? You did not even raise your protective qi!”

Only belatedly had he realized that Geom Mugeuk had been standing there defenseless.

“I trusted you, Elder.”

“What if I had made a mistake?”

“Then every day I’d appear in your dreams as a vengeful spirit, saying, ‘Give me back my handsome face!’”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shook his head with an expression that said there was no stopping him. He could endure that, perhaps, but he would not be able to endure feeling sorry toward the Cult Leader.

“I missed you!”

When Geom Mugeuk tried to run over and embrace him, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon blocked his path with the Heaven-Annihilating Great Blade.

“I smell of sweat. Get away!”

“Your sweat smells better than spiritual medicine, Elder.”

“Say that after you smooth out that frown.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk burst into laughter. Seeing the Blood Heaven Blade Demon put his heart at ease. There was a comfort that only he could give.

“I will wash and return.”

“I said it’s fine.”

“It is not fine with me.”

If asked who among the Eight Demon Venerables was the cleanest, Geom Mugeuk would answer firmly that it was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. One could tell just by looking at his bed, which was always clean. Even though he seemed like someone who would be far from fastidious.

Just then, something caught Geom Mugeuk’s eye. Unfamiliar flowers and plants had been planted in the garden at the corner of the courtyard; proof that he was getting along well with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable was growing there.

How long had he been crouching there, looking at the grass and flowers?

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s voice came from behind him.

“The Cult Leader was pleased, was he not?”

Geom Mugeuk knew he was asking about the hunting trip with his father and older brother.

Yes, perhaps the person who understood his father’s heart best was this very Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat, turned toward the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and said,

“Phew, you would not believe how much he favored his eldest son. I cried myself to sleep every night.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon wore an expression that said, as if you would ever do that.

“That mouth of yours must not have rested even there.”

“How could it rest? It was two against one.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon smiled faintly and said,

“Well done.”

It was a brief remark, but it carried the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s sincerity. It was not said for Geom Mugeuk’s sake, nor for his brother’s. It was said with his father in mind. That he had gone and returned well for the Cult Leader’s sake.

“Next time, you should come with us, Elder.”

“Why would I go there?”

“To take care of me against my father, who favors only his eldest son.”

“If that is the purpose, you should take the drunkard or the masked one.”

“The drunkard would flatter Father and take my brother’s side. And Father wouldn’t want to take the masked one.”

“Then take your master, that fist-brute.”

“You know, don’t you? Master will only take Father’s side. The only one who will wipe away my tears is you, Elder!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon laughed as if he found it amusing.

Geom Mugeuk carried the pleasant mood into an even better topic.

“Do you know this? This time in Wuhan as well, you saved me, Elder.”

“I did? I never even went near Wuhan.”

When the Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at him as if asking what trick he was trying to play now, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Because of you, I came to like books, didn’t I?”

When he had given up on finding the bead for the secret coffer to absorb in the Murim Repository and was about to simply leave—

If, at the very last moment, he had not felt like reading just one book before going, he would never have obtained the Huangjing.

“The thought that I should read a book changed my fate. Thank you.”

Geom Mugeuk bowed his head and conveyed his gratitude.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon watched him silently for a time, then spoke calmly.

“If you want to defeat others, you must never let go of that sword. But if you want to defeat yourself…”

What followed was advice that no one would believe was being given to the next Heavenly Demon by the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Even if you put down the sword in your hand, do not put down the book.”

* * *

The Drunken Demon’s gaze turned toward the lake as he ascended the Drunken Dream Pavilion.

Where his eyes came to rest, a corpse was floating away.

A corpse in his residence?

It would have been natural to be startled, yet the Drunken Demon was not startled in the slightest.

He walked over the surface of the lake toward the corpse.

Looking down at the corpse floating facedown on the water, the Drunken Demon asked indifferently,

“Is this a new way of expressing apology?”

As if it had been waiting, the corpse rolled over.

The person floating on the lake was Geom Mugeuk.

“It’s been a while, hyung.”

“What are you doing here?”

“I was trying to surprise you. I didn’t know you’d lost your childlike innocence to this extent.”

“It is not a matter of childlike innocence, but of conscience.”

The Drunken Demon had every right to say such a thing.

The last time Geom Mugeuk had left the cult, returned, and become acting Cult Leader, he had not seen the Drunken Demon then either. So it truly had been a long time. Even in ordinary times, the Drunken Demon felt slighted because Geom Mugeuk visited him later than the other Demon Venerables.

“Hyung, how long has it been for us?”

The Drunken Demon looked down at Geom Mugeuk quietly, then offered a courteous bow and said,

“Even when my father turned into a waiting stone and passed away, he spoke of the Young Cult Leader. He said that although the Young Cult Leader had treated him the worst among the Demon Venerables, Father still liked the Young Cult Leader the most.”

Geom Mugeuk was not one to let the Drunken Demon’s joke pass unanswered.

“You truly are handsome with atmosphere, just like your father. Aside from his personality and his drunken habits, you may take after him in everything else!”

The Drunken Demon shook his head once, then leaped back toward the Drunken Dream Pavilion. His movement technique as he walked over the water was exceedingly smooth and elegant. Geom Mugeuk shouted after him,

“Even so, it hasn’t been that long since we last saw each other!”

Geom Mugeuk lay back on the lake again and looked up at the sky.

“Ah, this is nice. As expected, the sky looks best from here.”

Floating in the lake of the Drunken Dream Pavilion after so long felt good.

After fully savoring the emotion of returning after so long, Geom Mugeuk climbed up to the Drunken Dream Pavilion.

The Drunken Demon poured liquor for him into the Ice Palace Holy Cup.

“How did the matter you went for turn out?”

“It went well.”

He explained to the Drunken Demon everything that had happened in Wuhan. It was the same story he had told the Extreme Evil Young Demon and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon before parting from them.

It had always been this way. When it came to the force behind the scenes, he told all the Demon Venerables everything. He was not fighting alone; he was fighting together with them.

After listening to the whole story, the Drunken Demon filled Geom Mugeuk’s cup again.

“You worked hard.”

At the Drunken Demon’s calm response, Geom Mugeuk tilted his head.

“You seem different today, hyung.”

“I do?”

“Should I say you somehow seem calmer?”

He seemed like he might have played more pranks of one sort or another. Yet he was not the sort of person who would truly sulk and act this way. Though Geom Mugeuk teased him for being petty, he knew better than anyone that the Drunken Demon was a person of great breadth.

The problem was that today’s change was a calmness tinged with worry.

“Is it perhaps because of Miss Yeo?”

He already knew that the Drunken Demon and Yeo Bin, one of the Three Great Drinkers, treated each other with affection. He wondered if something had happened between the two while he had been away.

But he had sensed nothing wrong from her when she had guided him earlier.

The Drunken Demon emptied the cup in his hand and stood.

“Follow me.”

The Drunken Demon took Geom Mugeuk and headed somewhere.

The two flew with lightness skill and landed on the lakeshore opposite the entrance.

The Drunken Demon led Geom Mugeuk into the Great Drunken Forest.

Deep within the forest, alcohol energy spread like mist.

The absolute forbidden zone of the Great Drunken Forest.

Because it was alcohol energy that could not be expelled with inner force, even an expert could not easily pass through it.

When the Drunken Demon walked forward, the alcohol energy formed a path as if it were alive. In that place where only the Drunken Demon could go, there was a brewery. It was the very place Geom Mugeuk had once visited while following the Drunken Demon.

After entering, the Drunken Demon spoke with an expression that said he could not understand it.

“The matter you went for went well, the Cult Leader has emerged from seclusion, and the Demon Venerables are growing stronger by the day. Everything is going well.”

The Drunken Demon’s steps stopped. His gaze turned toward the large cup set in the center of the place. There lay the reason the Drunken Demon’s mood had sunk.

“Then why has the newly brewed liquor essence spoiled again?”

Chapter 681: The Heavenly Demon Destroys the World
--------------------------------------------------
“You might consider it superstition, though.”

When the brew had first spoiled, Geom Mugeuk had teased the anxious Chwima, asking what kind of demon lord would believe in superstition.

Chwima’s gaze deepened as he looked down at the spoiled brew.

“To me, it is not superstition.”

Then came Geom Mugeuk’s voice from behind.

“If it is not superstition to Hyung-nim…….”

When Chwima turned around, Geom Mugeuk finished the thought.

“It is not superstition to me either.”

Geom Mugeuk walked up beside Chwima and looked down at the spoiled brew.

“It seems we are still lacking.”

Chwima stared back at him with an expression asking what he meant.

“It seems we need to grow stronger, and matters need to proceed more smoothly.”

Just as our fates have changed, so too must theirs.

That was right. He could not simply assume that because they had grown stronger, their opponents had grown weaker. Since they were walking a different path from before, it could lead to nonsensical results. He must not let his guard down until the very end.

“This spoiled brew is telling us to live more diligently.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Chwima shook his head.

“How could you possibly live more diligently than you do now?”

“Hyung-nim, you don’t seem to know, but I slack off quite a lot. I went hunting with Father and you, and we drank earlier too, didn’t we?”

“The drink we had earlier was the first in a hundred years. And the three of us going hunting was the first time in our lives. You’re all talk. You’ve never actually played properly even once.”

As if to say seeing someone often did not mean knowing them well, Chwima understood Geom Mugeuk better than anyone.

“It’s because you haven’t seen the wild drinking gatherings with my friends.”

“A gathering of successors—hardly what I’d call wild.”

“We even danced. Why are you like this?”

Geom Mugeuk opened the main gate and windows wide to ventilate the room, then brought a wooden bucket from the corner and began cleaning up the spoiled brew.

“Just leave it. I’ll clean it up later.”

“So you can do all the worrying while cleaning it up alone?”

The two cleaned up the spoiled brew together and washed the cups clean as well. Since they were cleaning anyway, Geom Mugeuk rolled up his sleeves to clear everything, and eventually the two of them thoroughly cleaned the inside of the brewery.

“You’re not going to brew for a while, right?”

He thought the answer would obviously be yes.

“I am going to brew.”

And the reason was Geom Mugeuk himself.

“You said you’d believe this superstition, didn’t you? Then you have to keep brewing. Whether it spoils worse or less. The brew keeps sending us signals.”

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. Chwima’s heart, telling him to be more careful in battle based on those signals. His older brother’s heart—anxious and frustrated, yet still wanting to let him know.

“When I return after leaving the sect next time, I’ll definitely come to you first. Elders! I apologize in advance!”

“Come last. If you’re busy, you don’t have to come. I’ll be fine.”

Chwima went outside first. As Geom Mugeuk followed after him, he said.

“Now that you have a woman to protect, it seems Hyung-nim is maturing too.”

“As if.”

Chwima took a long drink from the blood gourd at his waist and walked into the thick, fog-like demonic energy.

“Where in the world is there a mature drunkard?”

* * *

After parting with Chwima, Geom Mugeuk went to his father’s residence.

In front of the residence, Hwi revealed himself by dispelling his stealth.

“Young Lord, have you arrived?”

Hwi greeted Geom Mugeuk with his usual unchanging demeanor.

“Have you been well, Uncle?”

“Yes, I have been well.”

Hwi’s gaze toward Geom Mugeuk was gentle. It was the only moment when Hwi, who normally never revealed his emotions, became soft.

“The Lord is not yet asleep. Please wait a moment.”

Hwi disappeared and then reappeared.

“He says you may enter.”

Geom Mugeuk opened the door and went inside. It was his father’s bedchamber, which he had entered before.

“You are not asleep yet. Ah!”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been about to greet him, looked at his father in surprise.

His father was wearing floral pajamas—exactly the ones Geom Mugeuk had bought as a gift in Muhan. Geom Mugeuk smiled broadly, delighted.

“It really suits you! My gift is much more practical and better than Hyung-nim’s, right? Where did you stash that scroll painting? You already threw it away, didn’t you? I’ll keep it a secret from Hyung-nim.”

Where Geom Mugeuk’s gaze stopped while looking around the room, there they were—his brother and himself.

A scroll painting hanging on the wall.

Geom Mugeuk was moved not simply because it had been hung on the wall. It was because of where it was hung.

The scroll was hung in the position most visible when his father lay on the bed. He could picture his father lying there, looking at his sons before falling asleep.

Geom Mugeuk silently gazed at the scroll for a moment. Seeing it hung in his father’s room like this, it looked new to him.

“Hyung-nim would really love to see this.”

“Muyang already saw it.”

“What? When?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father in surprise.

“It was Muyang who hung it there.”

Had his brother come first? Or had his father called him? Either way, it showed how much both of them had changed.

“Father, next time let’s have one painted with the three of us. Shall I send the Demon Army to Muhan right now to capture an artist?”

In the past, refusal would have come first. But the refusal did not come.

“What brings you at this late hour?”

“Well, for most people it is still early evening.”

As his father’s eyes narrowed, Geom Mugeuk quickly spoke.

“I have a request.”

“What is it?”

“I’ve come to learn Guhwamagong.”

Despite the sudden request, his father was not very surprised.

“Did you know I would come?”

His father nodded.

“Did you not notice that my closed-door training this time was effective? I knew you would come at least once.”

“Of course I had to come. If I do not learn now, when would I?”

And he had a specific goal in his martial training.

So far, he had mastered up to the fifth form of Guhwamagong.

“I think it is time to learn the sixth form.”

The seventh, eighth, and ninth forms were martial arts performed by the Heavenly Demon Spirit, so practically speaking, the final form of Guhwamagong was the sixth form.

The sixth form: Heavenly Demon World Destruction.

The Heavenly Demon annihilates the world.

The final move, known to be the most powerful in Guhwamagong.

But the final sixth form was extremely difficult to execute, and since it was a move that poured out almost all internal energy at once, a slight mistake could result in severe internal injuries.

“I am ready, Father.”

Geom Ujin, who had been quietly gazing at his son, rose from his seat.

“Follow me.”

Geom Ujin went outside wearing only his pajamas and carrying just the Heavenly Demon Sword.

Geom Mugeuk followed after him.

He felt a certain freedom in his father’s appearance, clad in pajamas with only a single sword dangling at his side.

It was the first time he had felt that emotion from his father.

The place they arrived at was a training ground deep within the inner courtyard.

It was different from the training ground where Geom Mugeuk had gone to meet his father before.

This place was surrounded by iron walls on all sides.

Geom Mugeuk knocked on the wall.

“These walls are incredibly thick?”

“There are ten more such walls erected behind. At intervals of ten paces.”

“Is this a place where they lock up criminals to prevent escape?”

Then one wall caught Geom Mugeuk’s eye.

A wall large enough for an adult to hide behind was erected further back.

It was no ordinary iron wall.

“Could it be… Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron?”

Surprisingly, his father nodded.

“Why is such precious Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron here?”

Moreover, it was incredibly thick.

“What exactly is this place used for?”

His father did not answer, as if implying he would find out later.

“Now, try executing the sixth form.”

He already knew the mantra, so he could attempt it.

He had never executed it before, nor had he ever seen his father execute it.

Just as Geom Mugeuk was about to perform the sixth form, his father went behind the Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron wall.

“What are you doing?”

“How could I let you commit the unfilial act of injuring your father?”

The moment he heard those words, he understood.

That the Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron wall in front was erected to block the power of Heavenly Demon World Destruction.

It meant Heavenly Demon World Destruction was powerful enough that it could even injure his father.

Then what about this room made of iron walls and the walls behind it? They were to verify the power of Heavenly Demon World Destruction, and also to prevent that power from escaping outside. Just how powerful was it?

“Originally, to unleash the sixth form, one must properly train the form for months, or at the very least ten days even at the fastest.”

“Then why are you already going behind the wall?”

Then an unexpected answer came.

“Because it is you.”

It meant he might be able to unleash it in a single attempt. It also meant his father valued his son’s talent that highly.

“How long did it take you, Father?”

“Ten days.”

“Then I can do it in a day. Because it is you who is teaching me.”

A scoff formed at his father’s lips.

“Well then, let me begin.”

Geom Mugeuk took a deep breath and then moved his true energy according to the mantra to execute the sixth form.

But the move was not unleashed.

“Your true energy is moving too slowly.”

His father came out from behind the wall and explained the mantra in detail. It was an explanation of a realm beyond the Ten Stars.

“Try again.”

This time too, his father went behind the Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron.

One would think his father would have no need for such a wall. Yet his father was not ashamed at all to hide behind it.

Geom Mugeuk could understand the reason.

Because it was Guhwamagong. Because it was his father’s martial art.

Had it been any other martial art, he would not have done so.

This time too, it was not unleashed. But he felt the flow of his true energy was different from before.

“It feels different somehow.”

How could teaching and learning ever be the same? Each time there was instruction, it changed.

“This time, internal energy gathered in the sword! Just a sliver, but it did.”

Unleashing and failing, unleashing again and failing.

Geom Mugeuk asked, and his father explained.

Several hours passed like that.

The night deepened and time headed toward dawn, but his father did not stop teaching. As always, when teaching martial arts, his father was a completely different person.

And finally!

The moment to see the fruit of his efforts came.

An enormous force seemed to swarm into Geom Mugeuk’s Black Demon Sword.

Hwaaaaaaaang!

Light burst forth from the Black Demon Sword pointed forward.

Flash!

The world turned white. It was as if the sun was exploding before his eyes. There was a massive explosion, but no sound could be heard.

The iron walls disappeared. They simply vanished as if melting. The wall standing behind them disappeared, and again, far beyond that, the next wall disappeared as well. Everything around was vanishing.

When the light faded, nothing remained in the surroundings.

Everything visible had been erased.

This was no longer a room made of iron walls.

Sunlight shone through the vanished ceiling, and the blue sky was visible.

Only one thing remained there—the Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron wall standing in front.

“Father!”

As Geom Mugeuk shouted in surprise, his father walked out from behind the wall.

“Haa.”

Geom Mugeuk let out a sigh of relief.

He understood why the move was called Heavenly Demon World Destruction. It was a martial art that annihilated everything.

‘This is an insane martial art.’

Geom Mugeuk asked his father in a trembling voice.

“Did I succeed?”

Geom Ujin nodded. As if he had expected his son to unleash it in a single day, his father was not surprised.

Geom Mugeuk jumped for joy. Finally, he could execute all the forms of Guhwamagong up to the final move.

“I did it!”

Then he slumped down on the spot. All the internal energy in his dantian had vanished. It was a move that could only be executed by expending all one’s internal energy.

Geom Ujin looked around. He should evaluate the power by counting how many iron walls remained, but nothing remained.

Power beyond measurement.

It was because Geom Mugeuk’s vast and pure internal energy was so tremendous.

Even the wall made of Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron had been deeply gouged.

Geom Ujin looked at his son with surprised and admiring eyes, then spoke calmly.

“When you unleash the sixth form, anyone passing beyond the wall will die, and people in buildings far away will die too. Every living creature in sight will die. Those lying face down will die, and those flying away will all die. Do you understand what I am saying?”

Geom Ujin warned him of the consequences of using this martial art carelessly.

He said it because he now knew his son well. Because he knew his son would be scarred for a long time if an innocent victim were to die.

Geom Mugeuk made a request to his father.

“I want to see Father’s Heavenly Demon World Destruction.”

The Heavenly Demon World Destruction that had achieved Twelve Stars Great Perfection.

He could have told him to achieve Ten Stars Great Perfection first before asking.

For some reason, Geom Ujin immediately drew the Heavenly Demon Sword.

“Wait! I need to go behind the Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron wall too! Father, your son will die!”

But light was already bursting forth from the Heavenly Demon Sword.

Flash!

At the moment he thought he was dead, Geom Mugeuk saw.

That the light, which seemed like it would extend throughout the entire world, was shining only around his father.

‘Father is controlling the powerful energy of Heavenly Demon World Destruction!’

The light that should have flown in all directions gradually gathered into one place, forming the shape of a sword.

The moment the snow-white light sword was completed!

Shaaaaaaang!

With a refreshing wind sound he had never heard before, the light sword flew toward the Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron.

The place his father aimed at was the corner.

Puaaaaaaang!

The light sword pierced straight through the Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron. That thick Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron was pierced like tofu.

“Ah!”

An exclamation burst from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

He had never seen such thick Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron pierced through in a single move.

Geom Ujin asked calmly.

“Did you see?”

His father could probably execute a Heavenly Demon World Destruction that annihilated everything, just as he had done.

And he had achieved Twelve Stars Great Perfection, succeeding in gathering that power into one.

Father controlled destruction. In Father’s destruction, there was order.

“Yes, I saw clearly.”

His father had casually planted a new milestone for his son once again.

And then he walked out with a large yawn.

“I am going to bed.”

Chapter 682: In Youth One Does Not Believe in Fate
--------------------------------------------------
Heavenly Demon World-Extinction.

The strongest technique of the Nine Transformations Demon Art, annihilating everything in sight.

But it was a martial art that was not easy even to train in. One had to find a place where no one was around, and at the final moment, someone might still enter the area affected by Heavenly Demon World-Extinction.

Fortunately, that could not be a restriction for Geom Mugeuk.

After returning to his residence, Geom Mugeuk immediately performed the Space-Time Transposition Art.

Snap!

The moment Geom Mugeuk flicked his fingers, he was standing on a wide-open plain.

Whooooosh.

The wind that blew in sent Geom Mugeuk’s robe and hair fluttering.

‘Here, I can use Heavenly Demon World-Extinction to my heart’s content.’

Intending to determine precisely how far the power of this martial art reached, he created rocks at set intervals throughout the field.

He even made rocks so far away that he wondered whether the technique could truly affect them.

They were, quite literally, so distant they could scarcely be seen. And not just in one direction, but in every direction.

After completing all his preparations, Geom Mugeuk slowly drew the Black Demon Sword.

After taking a deep breath, he unleashed the Heavenly Demon World-Extinction he had learned from his father.

Flash!

Saaaaaaang!

A blinding light spread out in every direction.

Grass, trees, rocks. Everything created in this place began to vanish.

Just as the light that had illuminated all sides disappeared.

At that final instant, Geom Mugeuk used the Divine Eye Art and looked at the last rock he had set up far in the distance.

The rock he had created there was gone as well. Even a rock placed at a distance that could only be confirmed by using the Divine Eye Art had been annihilated.

Geom Mugeuk knew. Because the internal energy he possessed was so vast and pure, it had produced such tremendous power.

Ssssst.

The next moment, the Space-Time Transposition Art, having consumed all its internal energy, collapsed.

Geom Mugeuk circulated his qi and replenished his internal energy, then performed the Space-Time Transposition Art again.

Since he had been able to use it even while the Space-Time Transposition Art was active, it was not something he could only perform when his dantian was completely full.

Then just how much internal energy had to be in his dantian for him to use it?

After testing it again and again.

Ninety percent.

Only when more than ninety percent of his internal energy was filled could he unleash it.

And once the martial art was used, all of that ninety percent was consumed. Considering the terrifying power of the technique, it would be stranger if any internal energy remained.

‘Ninety percent. Can I reduce that?’

What if he could use it with eighty percent of his internal energy? If he could lower the requirement to seventy, sixty, fifty percent—if he could make it possible to unleash the technique that way?

Then he would be able to fight as usual and use Heavenly Demon World-Extinction as his final choice at the last moment.

‘First, I’ll have to achieve great completion in the Nine Transformations Demon Art to see whether this requirement can be reduced.’

And ultimately, Heavenly Demon World-Extinction was a martial art that could be used freely only upon achieving great completion of the twelfth level.

There would be no situation where killing everyone in sight would solve the problem. He had to achieve great completion of the twelfth level, just like his father.

For several days, Geom Mugeuk repeatedly trained in Heavenly Demon World-Extinction.

His goal was to make Heavenly Demon World-Extinction flow out as naturally as any other martial technique.

This training had another meaning as well. In the process of pouring out all his internal energy and then refilling it, he was naturally training his cultivation method too.

How many days had he been immersed in training like that?

Now, he could unleash Heavenly Demon World-Extinction as naturally as any other technique.

He had also determined exactly how far the power of Heavenly Demon World-Extinction reached, and exactly how much its power diminished as the distance increased.

Really, when had he ever trained in martial arts like this, as if he were studying?

Only when he was able to use Heavenly Demon World-Extinction freely did Geom Mugeuk finally leave his residence.

Standing before the towering wall called great completion, after focusing solely on martial arts training, there was someone he wanted to see.

* * *

The Fist Demon’s fist split the air.

Booooom!

The swiftly flying fist struck a woman directly in the stomach.

Thud!

The person who was shoved backward by the Fist Demon’s palm strike was none other than Cheon Sohui, the Fist Demon’s disciple and successor.

If one were to look only at this scene, every chivalrous hero in the world would rush in to save Cheon Sohui. After all, a terrifyingly large man was trying to beat a woman to death with his fists.

Thud!

This time, she was struck mercilessly in the side, and she clenched her teeth with a pained expression.

Thwack! Thud!

Fists struck her arm and shoulder in succession once more. Cheon Sohui staggered, but she did not fall.

Ordinarily, this was a situation in which she would have suffered serious internal injuries, but surprisingly, she endured it well.

But it hurt. It felt like being hit by a carriage, crushed under a giant pillar, and struck by a flying lump of iron.

Even so, she did not collapse. She endured, and endured again.

At last, the fists that had been flying at her without pause stopped.

“Haa, haa, haa.”

Now, only her rough breathing echoed there. If more fists had come, she would not have been able to endure and would have collapsed with internal injuries. The Fist Demon had driven her precisely to her limit.

“Well done.”

When she raised her head, the Fist Demon was smiling as though he had never beaten her at all.

“Thank you, Master.”

The protective astral energy that had guarded her body even as she was being battered was none other than the Heavenly Steel Body. Today’s training was training in the Heavenly Steel Body.

Heavenly Steel Body.

A protective art Geom Mugeuk had brought back from the underground chamber he had descended into with the Sword King. At the time, Geom Mugeuk had taken only one martial art from that place: this Heavenly Steel Body.

The reason Geom Mugeuk had chosen the Heavenly Steel Body was that it could be used in conjunction with other protective arts.

It was a martial art that could be used simultaneously with the Heavenly Demon Protective Art and the Vajra Asura of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. In conclusion, it meant three protective arts could be manifested at the same time.

Geom Mugeuk had first modified the Heavenly Steel Body, which consumed an enormous amount of internal energy, and then the Fist Demon and the One Blossom Sword Venerable discussed it further and improved its completeness even more.

At that time, Geom Mugeuk had passed the martial art on to the Fist Demon, the One Blossom Sword Venerable, Ian, and Seojin, and the Fist Demon had taught the Heavenly Steel Body to Cheon Sohui, the next Fist Demon, as well.

In truth, the people who needed the Heavenly Steel Body most were the Fist Demon and Cheon Sohui, who fought barehanded.

“It truly is an outstanding protective art.”

At Cheon Sohui’s admiration, the Fist Demon credited Geom Mugeuk.

“It is a martial art your senior brother passed down, so give your thanks to him.”

Just then, someone walked in from behind the Fist Demon and spoke.

“That’s too much, Master! How could you hit our delicate junior sister like that?”

The one who appeared was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

A slight smile formed at the corners of the Fist Demon’s mouth as he stood with his back turned, and Cheon Sohui’s face blossomed with delight.

“Senior Brother! Thank you for saying so, but I’m not exactly delicate.”

Her body had become even firmer and more muscular than before. Just by looking at her body, one could tell how diligently she had trained in fist arts.

“You look good.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s praise, Cheon Sohui answered humbly.

“I still have a long way to go.”

Geom Mugeuk began by picking a quarrel with the broad back of the Fist Demon, whom he had not seen in a long while.

“Surely you’re not aiming for Master’s body, are you? Absolutely not, Junior Sister. Aim for this sleek senior brother’s body instead!”

Only then did the Fist Demon turn toward Geom Mugeuk.

“Those baby hands can’t do anything.”

Hearing “baby hands” after so long made him so glad that Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and greeted him.

“Master, your disciple left the cult and has now returned.”

As if he had arrived at just the right time, the Fist Demon turned to Cheon Sohui instead of returning the greeting and said,

“Seeing it directly will be the fastest way to learn.”

Geom Mugeuk immediately understood what the Fist Demon meant. He was telling him to give a direct demonstration of the Heavenly Steel Body.

“I was wrong. Junior Sister, if you’re going to become the Fist Demon, of course you should build your body like Master’s, not like my frail one! Your back needs to get at least twice as broad as it is now!”

The Fist Demon, looking as though it was already too late, clenched his fist and walked forward. Geom Mugeuk lowered his voice.

“I have come to see the Fist Demon in my capacity as Young Cult Leader.”

Of course, that did not work on the Fist Demon.

The Fist Demon leaped lightly and sent his fist flying as fast as lightning.

Whoooong!

Geom Mugeuk did not dodge the incoming fist. This was a demonstration of the Heavenly Steel Body, after all.

The fist, packed with tremendous power, struck Geom Mugeuk in the chest.

Thud!

He activated the Heavenly Steel Body and blocked the impact. But while he did not suffer internal injuries, the pain was transmitted fully through his body.

Whoosh! Whoosh! Whooom!

The Fist Demon’s fists struck Geom Mugeuk’s torso in succession once more. They were fists incomparably faster and stronger than the ones he had used to attack Cheon Sohui earlier.

Boom! Bang! Boom!

Even as fists of tremendous power flew at him one after another, Geom Mugeuk did not fall. As if taking hits was also a skill, Geom Mugeuk truly took them well.

In truth, though he had acted as if he did not want to do it, this was extremely useful training for Geom Mugeuk. When else would he be able to test the Heavenly Steel Body against the Fist Demon’s fists? Each and every blow was priceless.

After he had thrown fists for a while like that, the Fist Demon asked Cheon Sohui,

“What was different between you and him?”

Cheon Sohui, who had been standing blankly with a startled expression, suddenly came to her senses.

“At first glance, it looked as though he was simply enduring with the Heavenly Steel Body, but Senior Brother seemed to be letting the incoming fists flow past him. By subtly twisting his body.”

The Fist Demon looked satisfied. Cheon Sohui had seen correctly.

“You saw well. Not only that, he adjusted the strength of the protective astral energy differently for each part of his body. It wasn’t that he strengthened the area being hit; he controlled it so the impact could be diverted more easily.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk asked in surprise,

“I did that?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s surprise, the Fist Demon nodded.

“You manifested it that way instinctively. It isn’t something you can do intentionally.”

The Fist Demon looked back at Cheon Sohui. Though he said nothing, the look in his eyes seemed to say this:

Now do you know the path you must take?

Cheon Sohui bowed respectfully.

“Master. Thank you truly for bestowing such a great lesson.”

Cheon Sohui also expressed her gratitude to Geom Mugeuk.

“Thank you, Senior Brother.”

“I’m glad I could help. Ugh, it feels like my whole body is about to fall apart.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s exaggerated whining, Cheon Sohui whispered her own complaint before leaving.

“Do you think it hurts as much as it does for me? I’m really in pain!”

Cheon Sohui left the place, groaning.

As Geom Mugeuk watched her back, the playfulness vanished from his face.

“I look forward to that day. The day that fist lands a blow upon the martial world.”

She was a Demon Venerable who would carry on the next generation together with the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. He would surely be able to witness her achievements.

“Let’s walk for a bit.”

“Yes, Master.”

Geom Mugeuk and the Fist Demon walked together through Donggwonmun, just the two of them.

The passing Iron Fists bowed respectfully at Geom Mugeuk’s visit. Because he had deep ties with them as well, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze as he looked at the Iron Fists was gentle.

Naturally, the place the two headed toward was the cliff the Fist Demon wanted to bring down.

As they walked there, Geom Mugeuk told him everything that had happened during this recent outing.

After hearing the whole story, the Fist Demon recalled events from long ago.

“In the past, when I roamed the Central Plains with the Cult Leader, we encountered unknown people several times. Back then, we simply assumed they were some mysterious organization coveting money and power and let it pass. Perhaps among them were descendants of the Dark Palace as well.”

“That was probably the case. No, they were definitely there.”

They must have been secretly expanding their organization while earning money and building strength all throughout the Central Plains.

The Fist Demon asked, puzzled,

“But why now?”

Surely there had been many opportunities over the course of three hundred years.

“It seems they believed in the prophecy of the Divine Maiden Palace.”

“That the prophecy would be fulfilled in this generation?”

“Yes, that’s right.”

The Fist Demon’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

In an era that just so happens to have a Young Cult Leader like this? If they waited three hundred years, they should have waited another hundred.

Because such a thought naturally occurred to him, he could declare this with certainty.

“It must be a mistaken prophecy.”

He said it with Geom Mugeuk in mind, but Geom Mugeuk attributed the reason to the Fist Demon instead.

“Not a chance when Master is here.”

Of course, the Fist Demon knew very well that he was not the one who would change the prophecy.

He also knew that the person standing before the greatest destiny, the one who would change everyone’s fate, was Geom Mugeuk.

“Do you believe in fate?”

How could he, who had returned to the past, not believe in fate?

However, he did not simply say that he believed in it.

“While I’m young, I intend not to.”

When the Fist Demon looked at him as if asking what he meant,

“If I believe there’s a fixed fate, I feel like I’ll only end up with more excuses.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been walking while looking ahead, turned his gaze to the Fist Demon.

“Even if we can’t decide fate, we can decide the mindset and attitude with which we face it. While I’m young, I’ll live as though nothing in the world is fixed. I’ll move my body relentlessly and refine my heart, and later, I’ll say this to fate: I worked this hard, so what are you going to do about it?”

It was also the conviction Geom Mugeuk had held consistently since his regression.

Though he said, What are you going to do about it? in truth, it was also something he said to himself.

If even after working this hard I can’t change fate, then at that time, there’s nothing I can do.

“When I reach your age, Master, I think I’ll look back on my life and believe in fate then.”

The Fist Demon was curious. When Geom Mugeuk reached his age and looked back on his life, what would he feel? What would he say then?

As they spoke like that, the two arrived before the cliff.

After looking up at the cliff for a moment, Geom Mugeuk abruptly said,

“You won’t bring down this cliff anymore, will you?”

“Why do you think that?”

“Because now you must be certain that you can bring down this cliff.”

Geom Mugeuk could feel that the Fist Demon had grown stronger.

In truth, even in the previous battle, the Fist Demon had not insisted on his pride and had fought flexibly.

The fact that the Fist Demon, who had never bent, had become flexible meant that he had grown that much stronger.

“I am still not confident.”

Knowing that those were words only someone who had become stronger could say, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and looked up at the cliff.

“Congratulations. Looks like you survived.”

A faint smile also formed at the corners of the Fist Demon’s mouth.

At that moment, one of the Iron Fists ran to where the two stood and delivered a written message.

“A message has arrived for the Young Cult Leader.”

It was a message sent by the Extreme Evil Little Demon, and the contents inside were brief.

—Please come to Cheonhwaru. Soma.

Chapter 683: If Heaven Delivers a Prophecy
------------------------------------------
“Who is the message from?”

At Kwonma’s question, Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“It is a message for Soma-nim. He left the sect to grant my request.”

Kwonma was well aware of the special relationship between Geom Mugeuk and Geukak Soma.

At first, he had found their relationship quite unexpected, because he believed their personalities were completely incompatible.

But now, his thinking had changed.

The more he learned about Geom Mugeuk, the more he often sensed an indescribable loneliness from him, and he felt that loneliness closely resembled that of Geukak Soma.

“Must you go out again?”

“I believe I must.”

“Go and return safely.”

Though the greeting was brusque, it held Kwonma’s worry.

“May I ask one favor?”

Kwonma nodded.

“Please tell my father. Tell him I quietly left the sect because I have business.”

He had to let his father know, even if he told no one else.

“Why not tell him yourself?”

“I have someone I must meet.”

Kwonma spoke as though scolding him.

“Should you not meet the Cult Lord first, and then tell that person?”

“The person I am meeting is my Master’s daughter.”

“…….”

“Very well. Tell Ian I left without seeing her……”

Before he finished speaking, Kwonma turned and began to walk away.

“I will deliver your words to the Cult Lord properly.”

* * *

Ian was in her quarters.

Worry filled her face as she stood in the courtyard looking up at the sky. Just as she let out a sigh she had been holding back!

Whoosh!

A sneak attack came from behind.

*Too late!*

Realizing she could not dodge in time, she raised her Cheongangsin and threw herself forward to minimize the impact.

Thwack!

She allowed the swiftly flying palm strike to strike her back, but fortunately, her Cheongangsin protected her body.

She bounced forward, then turned around as fast as lightning and drew her Ilwol Sword.

Shiiiiiik!

Spewing a frigid aura, the Ilwol Sword flew and stopped just short of cutting the opponent’s throat.

“Young Master!”

Standing there smiling without dodging was Geom Mugeuk.

“Pass!”

That she had used her Cheongangsin to protect her body, and that she had stopped the sword without recklessly cutting her opponent, was excellent.

“What if I had accidentally cut you, Young Master!”

Her sword, gripped with immense relief, trembled.

“If you’ve reached that level, you shouldn’t pull pranks like sneak attacks.”

It meant that with his current strength, she could not kill him even by accident.

Joy filled Ian’s face as she sheathed her sword. The person she had missed so terribly was standing right before her.

“You heard I had returned, right?”

“Yes.”

“But you didn’t come see me. You’re cruel, Ian. Have you cast me aside now?”

“I went three times to see the face of the one who cast me aside. I went when you were out hunting, and two more times when you were away. What in the world was keeping you so busy!”

In the past, she would have waited until Geom Mugeuk came to see her, worried she might bother him while he was busy.

But this time, Ian did not.

“You could have at least left a letter saying you had come.”

“What for? I can just come see you again. I would meet you again like this anyway.”

“What? Did you achieve enlightenment or something while I was gone?”

Then Ian let out a sigh.

“If I had, I wouldn’t be worrying.”

“Is something wrong?”

She had been worrying greatly over Gwiyeongdae matters lately.

Once she had begun seriously running the organization, all sorts of unexpected things occurred.

Especially recently, a major problem had arisen. A conflict had broken out between Iljo led by Cheongmyeon and Ijo led by Seojin.

At first, it was a conflict stemming from a trivial quarrel between two ordinary members, but gradually it had grown until it became a conflict between squad and squad.

“I thought about intervening directly, but for now I’m pretending not to know. Yet I feel I cannot just leave it be. How dare they do this to me, their Daeju? It irritates me.”

Ian revealed her honest feelings. She would not have said this to anyone but Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk fanned the flames of that burning house.

“They’re looking down on you. Imagine if Jang Gunju were the leader. Would they dare split into factions and fight? They think you’re kind and easy.”

“Right? Ugh, I should just beat them all up.”

“Then beat them up. Why haven’t you?”

“Well…… there are the squad leaders too. I don’t think this is something I should step in for. And it’s not something that can be solved by beating them.”

Geom Mugeuk said with a smile.

“You already know the answer, so what are you worrying about?”

Ian smiled along.

*‘Why would I worry? Because I want to do well. It is an organization directly under the Young Cult Lord, an organization where I was entrusted as Daeju—how could I not care?’*

Cheongmyeon and Seojin must have their own thoughts, so they would resolve it themselves. She should think this way, but she worried the matter would spread and cause those two to fall out as well.

The habit of always imagining the worst first was an occupational illness from her days as a bodyguard.

Ian made her decision.

“Yes, I will simply pretend not to know about this matter. There, end of worries!”

“Then they’ll think you truly know nothing. You’ll become an indifferent and incompetent leader.”

“Then what should I do?”

“You must let them know you are aware of everything but pretending not to be. Only then do you become a leader who gives subordinates the chance to resolve things themselves. A leader who is not frivolous and does not act rashly despite knowing.”

Ian shook her head.

“It is difficult, so difficult. I thought I would do brilliantly once I became Daeju. I liked it better when I followed Young Master around without a care.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been quietly watching her, made a surprising suggestion.

“Then let us do what you liked.”

“Huh?”

“I am leaving the sect right now. Come with me.”

Ian’s eyes went wide. She did not ask where.

“How much time do I have to prepare?”

“Half a ke.”

The moment he said that, Ian dashed into her house and began packing her things.

“You only ever see me swinging swords in the training ground, but I am a woman too! How could you tell me half a ke before we depart!”

Even as she said that, she moved like lightning, stuffing clothes and travel necessities into her leather bag.

“If we leave like this, what about the members?”

“What members? Did I have members?”

She even finished preparing to leave in less than half a ke.

“A frivolous leader who acts rashly—departure preparations complete!”

She was so overjoyed that he wondered what would have happened if he had not asked her to come.

“Are you that happy?”

“Of course.”

“You hate dangerous things, don’t you? What if a terrifying danger awaits us?”

“Tell that terrifying thing that a neurotic woman, exhausted by work and people, is coming along. Tell it that this time, it is the one that must avoid us—make sure you do.”

The two smiled at each other.

“But where are we going?”

“To Cheonhwaru.”

Ian's expression brightened even more at the fact that their destination was Cheonhwaru.

Was it because the Lord of Cheonhwaru always said kind words to her? Or was it because of that mysterious atmosphere of hers? Meeting her always put Ian in a good mood for no reason.

And so, the two who had left the sect began to run swiftly toward Cheonhwaru.

* * *

The two ran and ran.

When it was time to rest from running, they made a campfire and camped.

While Ian recovered her internal energy, Geom Mugeuk hunted animals and cooked for her, and they sat side by side by the campfire sharing stories.

These days, he was doing his best for his father, elder brother, and the Demon Venerables. But the person he ought to treat best was Ian.

“Thank you.”

“Suddenly?”

“Yes, I am suddenly thankful.”

“You need not say such things; I was already planning to wash the dishes and clean up.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled at her. Being with her was so comfortable and pleasant.

“Nothing happened at Cheonhwaru, right?”

“Soma-nim has gone there.”

That one sentence was enough. This business was surely related to the Sinnyeo Jimong.

Would she indeed be a descendant of Sinnyeongung as expected?

Having recovered her internal energy and finished resting, the two began to run again.

It was a busy journey where they could not properly sightsee even once, but Ian was happy nonetheless. For if she was busy with Geom Mugeuk, she was happy being busy.

* * *

Finally, Geom Mugeuk and Ian arrived at Cheonhwaru.

In the inner courtyard of Cheonhwaru, Geukak Soma and Yeojeong, the Lord of Cheonhwaru, stood waiting, having received word.

“Welcome, Young Cult Lord.”

At Geukak Soma’s greeting, Yeojeong also gave her greeting.

“I should have come to see you. I am sorry to have made you come here.”

As expected, the reason she had asked him to come was certainly because of the Lord of Cheonhwaru.

“If Sister-in-law asks me to come, I must run over at once.”

Geom Mugeuk treated her as his sister-in-law, as always. He could not know how greatly this small title moved Yeojeong.

“This Daeju came with me.”

After greeting Geukak Soma first, Ian greeted Yeojeong, just in case bringing her along might cause trouble for Geom Mugeuk.

“I begged him to bring me because I wanted to see the Lord. It has been a while, Lord.”

The gazes of the two women looking at each other were soft.

“This warrior has become even more beautiful.”

Then Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk and spoke.

“You see? When you meet someone after a long time, they say things like this. Not immediately beating you up.”

Having roughly guessed the situation, Geukak Soma and Yeojeong smiled.

“Come, let us go inside.”

And so, the four sat facing each other in the Lord of Cheonhwaru’s quarters.

“As you may have guessed, the reason I asked my husband to invite the Young Cult Lord here was because of this.”

The Lord of Cheonhwaru brought out a jade box.

Carefully opening the lid, inside was the Sinnyeo Jimong. She had kept it very preciously.

“I received what you sent and have kept it stored away as it was until now.”

From the Lord of Cheonhwaru’s gaze as she looked down at the Sinnyeo Jimong, Geom Mugeuk could feel it—that she knew what this object was.

*My intuition was right.*

She was undoubtedly a descendant of Sinnyeongung.

“Young Cult Lord, do you know what this is?”

“It is the Sinnyeo Jimong.”

“So you sent it knowing what it was.”

When Geom Mugeuk sent the Sinnyeo Jimong through Geukak Soma, she could not describe how surprised she had been.

Geom Mugeuk added an explanation regarding the Sinnyeo Jimong.

“I only know that it is the divine object of Sinnyeongung, said to have been destroyed along with Cheonoigung three hundred years ago. I do not know exactly how it is used.”

The Lord of Cheonhwaru asked Geom Mugeuk again.

“Then why did you send this to me?”

Geukak Soma sat by and silently watched the two. He felt that the deep bond between himself and Geom Mugeuk now extended even to Yeojeong.

“I discovered this divine object by chance in a report from the Murim Alliance. And the moment I saw it, I thought it belonged to Sister-in-law.”

“May I ask the reason?”

“I suddenly remembered Sister-in-law seeing people’s fates.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to Ian beside him.

“At that time, you saw this Daeju’s fate. That is why I thought perhaps Sister-in-law might be a descendant of Sinnyeongung.”

He had already heard the answer from the Lord of Cheonhwaru’s expression. If it were not so, she would have denied it at once. Yeojeong said nothing.

Soon, a surprising secret flowed out.

“You saw correctly. I am a descendant of Sinnyeongung.”

After speaking to Geom Mugeuk, Yeojeong looked at Geukak Soma.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. That she was revealing this fact to Geukak Soma for the first time as well.

“I did not deliberately try to deceive you. I had resolved to forget forever that I was a descendant of Sinnyeongung. I only learned this time as well that the force behind this matter was Amheukgung.”

Geukak Soma was not shaken in the slightest, his gaze showing that her identity did not matter to him at all.

The Lord of Cheonhwaru’s gaze turned toward the Sinnyeo Jimong.

“This Sinnyeo Jimong is the divine object passed down to a Sinnyeo capable of prophecy. I understood it was lost long ago, yet it was in the Murim Alliance.”

And then a surprising remark followed.

“With this, one can make prophecies.”

The Lord of Cheonhwaru raised her head and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“That is, if I am a Sinnyeo capable of receiving prophecy. Even among those of the Sinnyeo bloodline, I understand there are far more who cannot receive prophecies.”

Geom Mugeuk recalled the appearance of the Sinnyeo from three hundred years ago. Her appearance with her eyes covered by the Sinnyeo Jimong.

Knowing the future, being one who prophesied—she was by no means a happy figure.

“I did not give this so that Sister-in-law would receive a prophecy. I simply returned it because it is yours. You may keep it stored in that box forever, or burn it right here and now and forget about Sinnyeongung forever—it does not matter to me.”

The Lord of Cheonhwaru looked at Geukak Soma. Geukak Soma nodded once as if to tell her to decide for herself.

The Lord of Cheonhwaru slowly took out the Sinnyeo Jimong.

“Honestly, I was curious whether I was someone who could receive prophecies or not.”

That was not actually the reason. If it was fate that Geom Mugeuk had sent her the Sinnyeo Jimong? She felt she must not avoid this fate. Even for Geukak Soma’s sake.

“And if the heavens were to grant me a prophecy, what kind of prophecy it would be.”

The moment the Sinnyeo Jimong entered her hands, the mysterious aura she felt grew even stronger.

The Lord of Cheonhwaru carefully covered both eyes with the Sinnyeo Jimong.

Geom Mugeuk felt the aura rising all around her body.

A suffocating silence. Geom Mugeuk, Ian, and Geukak Soma held their breath and watched her.

How much time passed that way?

The Lord of Cheonhwaru removed the Sinnyeo Jimong.

Surprise dwelt in her pupils as she slowly opened her eyes.

“I saw a certain scene.”

It meant a prophecy had been granted. Surprisingly, she was a Sinnyeo who could deliver prophecies.

The Lord of Cheonhwaru’s trembling gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk.

“There, I saw the Young Cult Lord.”

Chapter 684: I'm the One Who Kills
----------------------------------
A mysterious light flowed from the Mistress of Cheonhwaru’s eyes.

In the way she looked at him, Geom Mugeuk sensed many emotions.

Surprise, dread, terror.

And the final emotion born from that mingling of negatives was hesitation.

“What did you see of me?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru did not answer. Instead, she even avoided his gaze.

At her reaction, Ian felt a sense of foreboding. In this situation, what could there be that she could not say?

‘What on earth did she see?’

Geugak Soma was silently staring at her. He could easily have said a word, telling her to speak to the Young Cult Leader.

But he did not force her to speak. He merely waited for her choice.

Geom Mugeuk lifted the sunken atmosphere.

“I’ll try to guess the prophecy. Did you perhaps see me standing at the vanguard of the Demonic-Righteous War? Striking the Murim Alliance, striking the Sado Alliance. Striking here, striking there. Did I perhaps end up unifying Murim? Were thousands, tens of thousands of heroes prostrating themselves and worshiping me?”

At that, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru slowly shook her head, as if to say that was not it. Still, she could not meet Geom Mugeuk’s eyes.

“That’s a relief. To be honest, I was worried you might have seen that.”

Geom Mugeuk guessed again.

“Then you must have seen something like this. You saw me slowly climbing a flight of stairs. At the top, there would have been a grand preceptor’s chair. The moment I sat upon that seat of power, the surrounding scenery finally came into view. Corpses piled up on all sides, blood flowing like rivers. And at the end, I must have looked over those corpses and laughed coldly. Right? That’s the sort of scene you saw, isn’t it? That’s why you can’t tell me?”

But as if that was not it either, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru shook her head.

“I almost wish I had seen that instead.”

In that instant, the smile vanished from Geom Mugeuk’s face. Ian and Geugak Soma also grew tense. Her words meant that what she had seen was something even more terrible.

With everyone’s gaze fixed on her, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru spoke in a low voice.

“The Young Cult Leader was killing someone.”

“!”

There was no room for even a single joke to intrude upon those words. Just who had he killed, for her to say that a scene strewn with countless corpses would have been preferable?

“Whom was I killing?”

If he had been killing some passing villain, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru would not have been this grave.

Her gaze slowly turned toward one place. The person she looked at with trembling eyes was Geugak Soma.

“You were killing Brother.”

“!”

At that moment, everyone was startled. A heavy silence descended, born of shock and disbelief.

‘I kill Soma? Impossible!’

Geom Mugeuk was certain that would never happen. It was an utterly absurd prophecy.

Then one thought suddenly occurred to him.

‘Could it be? Did she see that moment?’

The moment in his life before his regression, when he had killed Soma!

It occurred to him that perhaps that might be the case.

If not, there was no way he would ever kill Geugak Soma. Nor would Geugak Soma ever do anything to deserve such a thing.

But the prophecies of the Divine Maiden Palace were surely meant to show the future. They had shown the past, and not just any past, but the past before his regression? That, too, was impossible to understand.

Geom Mugeuk broke the silence with a smile.

“What on earth did you do? No, what are you planning to do?”

Geugak Soma lightly accepted Geom Mugeuk’s joke.

“When you kill me, please send me off without pain.”

Looking at each other, the two men laughed.

They were composed, but Ian alone could not be.

She knew that what she was about to say was terribly rude, but since no one else had brought it up, she had no choice.

“Can a prophecy be wrong?”

At that, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru answered with a sunken gaze.

“A prophecy may fail to descend, but it is never wrong.”

Words that she did not want to say, and no one wanted to hear, followed.

“What I saw will definitely happen.”

If that was truly the case? Ian’s heart began to pound violently.

At that moment, though she felt sorry to Geugak Soma, she was more worried about Geom Mugeuk than about him.

If such a thing happened?

Could Geom Mugeuk simply let it pass that he had killed Geugak Soma? It would remain as a wound for the rest of his life. No, it might even change Geom Mugeuk. Into something very dark and gloomy.

“But fate can be changed, can’t it?”

At her words, Geom Mugeuk nodded vigorously.

“Yes, it can be changed.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke confidently to the Mistress of Cheonhwaru as well.

“That will not happen. So please do not worry.”

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru lowered her head and showed no response. Perhaps her heart was complicated, for she still did not look at Geom Mugeuk.

“You must be tired from receiving the prophecy, so rest. We’ll go get some air.”

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat and left the room, and Ian followed him out.

After the two of them left, Geugak Soma asked the Mistress of Cheonhwaru,

“Will you have a drink?”

“Yes.”

Geugak Soma rose and brought over the liquor from the wall cabinet. There was liquor he liked, and liquor the Mistress of Cheonhwaru liked, and he brought the one she liked.

Ttororong.

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru emptied her cup in one breath.

Was it because she was worried that Geugak Soma might die? She emptied cup after cup.

Geugak Soma silently filled her empty cup.

Then, after she had been sunk in thought for a long while, a certain resolve settled in the Mistress of Cheonhwaru’s eyes.

“Brother.”

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru opened her mouth with difficulty, her voice trembling. Never in her life had she felt such fear as she did now.

“Brother, I lied. The prophecy… I told it differently.”

Even at that shocking confession, Geugak Soma’s eyes behind the white mask did not show the slightest surprise.

“You knew?”

Amazingly, Geugak Soma nodded.

“How did you know?”

“I know you. How could I not?”

In truth, there had been a decisive reason. She had supposedly seen him die, yet Yeojeong had been far too calm. The Yeojeong he knew was not that sort of person.

Especially since she was someone who believed prophecies were bound to come true, she would have thought that his death, too, could not be avoided.

On top of that, she had been unable to meet the Young Cult Leader’s gaze. It was not that she could not look because he was the man who killed someone she loved; he had clearly sensed guilt in her.

“Perhaps the Young Cult Leader noticed as well. He is exceptionally clever and quick-witted.”

“I’m sorry, Brother.”

“It’s all right. You must have had a reason.”

Geugak Soma filled the Mistress of Cheonhwaru’s empty cup again and asked,

“What did you see in the prophecy?”

Now was the moment when the true prophecy she had received would be revealed.

“It wasn’t the Young Cult Leader.”

After a brief pause, shocking words flowed from her mouth.

“Brother, you killed the Young Cult Leader.”

“!”

Through the eyeholes of the white mask, Geugak Soma’s startled eyes were visible.

He had laughed when she said he would die, and he had laughed when she said she had lied. But now, he did not laugh.

“Brother stabbed the Young Cult Leader in the heart with a dagger.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and Ian walked through the inner garden of Cheonhwaru.

It was a beautifully arranged place, but none of it entered Ian’s eyes.

“If I’d known this would happen, I wouldn’t have come. I ran away to escape one worry, so how could I have known this kind of worry would be waiting for me?”

She had been worrying over the conflict between Squad One and Squad Two, and now she had come to worry over Geom Mugeuk killing Geugak Soma.

“That’s why you have to look out at the world. Now that you’ve come out, that worry seems like nothing, doesn’t it?”

“What do you mean, nothing! It’s a conflict between the direct units under the heir of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult! It’s just that this problem is too enormous!”

Geom Mugeuk laughed as though amused, but Ian did not laugh.

“Why? Are you afraid such a thing will really happen?”

“Then you’re not scared?”

“Don’t worry. That won’t happen.”

“Of course it won’t, but still.”

What weighed on her heart was the fact that the one who had made the prophecy was the Mistress of Cheonhwaru.

If it had been anyone else, she would have dismissed it as nonsense and refused to believe it. But the Mistress of Cheonhwaru was not someone who would make a false prophecy.

“Ah! Let’s go back in and ask.”

“Ask what?”

“Where you kill Lord Soma. Then we just never go to that place. That would change fate, wouldn’t it?”

“Oh! That’s clever.”

“If I were clever, I would have asked earlier. We’re always the sort to realize what we should have done only after it’s passed. Come on, let’s hurry and ask.”

But Geom Mugeuk did not move from where he stood.

“But what if you defy fate like that, and fate changes?”

Ian flinched, and Geom Mugeuk spoke with deep eyes.

“What if Lord Soma ends up killing me? What then?”

For a moment, Ian could not say anything. Geom Mugeuk dying? That would surely be the moment her world ended as well.

“What do you mean, what then? I’d shout, ‘I’m finally free!’ and cheer.”

“Then that’s settled.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and began walking, and Ian followed behind him.

‘What do you mean, settled? If you die, Young Master, I die too! So even for my sake, you absolutely must not die!’

When the two of them had walked once around the inner garden and returned, Geugak Soma and the Mistress of Cheonhwaru were waiting in front of the residence.

When they had left the room, it had not seemed like they would meet again so soon.

Geugak Soma said to Geom Mugeuk,

“She says she has something to tell the Young Cult Leader.”

At that, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru knelt on the spot.

At the sudden act, Geom Mugeuk and Ian were startled.

“I lied to the Young Cult Leader. The prophecy I saw was a different scene.”

This time, she did not avoid Geom Mugeuk’s gaze as she told the truth.

“The prophecy I saw was a scene of Brother killing the Young Cult Leader.”

The moment Ian heard those words, she froze in shock.

“I was so shocked and flustered that I lied in the moment. I am sorry.”

Geom Mugeuk neither grew startled nor angry.

“Please stand.”

Geom Mugeuk hurriedly helped her to her feet.

“I understand. I would have done the same.”

From Geom Mugeuk’s calm reaction, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru could tell.

‘Just as Brother said, the Young Cult Leader knew as well.’

This lie was so great that she would have had nothing to say even if the Young Cult Leader killed her for it.

It was a lie made over the life of the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Yet he had already noticed, and had pretended not to know. If she had never spoken the truth to the end, Geom Mugeuk would have pretended not to know to the end. Geugak Soma would have done the same. Really, these men.
