“Whether I kill Lord Soma, or Lord Soma kills me, it is the same thing in the end. There is no need to distinguish between the two.”

Geugak Soma’s eyes were smiling. Because he felt the same.

Thinking about it, this was also a matter upon which all four of their fates depended. If one person died, the aftermath would shake all three who remained.

“Thank you for telling me the truth.”

At Geom Mugeuk, who was thanking her instead, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru bowed her head.

Geugak Soma walked over and lightly patted the Mistress of Cheonhwaru on the shoulder. If there had been no one watching, he would have embraced her.

A short while ago, in the room, when she had asked whether she should tell the Young Cult Leader, Geugak Soma had said this to her.

—What I learned from the Young Cult Leader was how to create a relationship without misunderstandings.

That was why Geom Mugeuk told him everything. This time as well, when he returned after leaving the cult, he had told him everything that had happened at the Murim Alliance.

We win by knowing. Problems always arise when we do not know.

Geugak Soma had told her of that conviction of Geom Mugeuk’s.

—The moment we decide to handle it ourselves, things will become tangled.

At those words, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru had immediately risen from her seat, come out here, and waited for Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk guessed that Geugak Soma was behind this swift confession.

“Thank you, Lord Soma.”

“I am the one doing the killing. There is no need to thank me.”

At Geugak Soma’s joke, Geom Mugeuk smiled.

The two men were composed, but the women were not in the least.

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru looked at Ian. Ian stood there wordlessly, her face pale. The Mistress of Cheonhwaru knew better than anyone what she must be feeling now. She herself had lied for Geugak Soma’s sake.

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru approached Ian and said,

“I’m sorry.”

“No, I’m the one who’s sorry.”

Ian felt truly sorry to the two of them. When Geugak Soma had said he would die, she had not been this shocked. She should not have shown such selfishness in front of them, but it had not gone as she wished.

The mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion silently held her hand tightly.

Just then, Geugak Soma took a dagger from his bosom. It was the ten-thousand-year cold iron dagger he had received from Geom Mugeuk.

“Was this the dagger that killed the Young Cult Leader?”

The mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion slowly nodded.

Geugak Soma looked down at his dagger.

Of all times, at the very moment he had received the ten-thousand-year cold iron dagger as a gift, and even the martial arts manual of the Sohyang Dagger Art, a prophecy had shown him killing Geom Mugeuk with a dagger.

Geugak Soma could not simply let it pass, so he handed the dagger to Geom Mugeuk.

“Please hold on to it for a while.”

When Geom Mugeuk accepted the dagger, Ian’s face brightened.

“Ah! If the dagger is with you, young master, then that will absolutely never happen.”

But Geom Mugeuk thought differently.

“Wouldn’t that lead to an even worse result?”

For he knew better than anyone that the thing called fate was not so easy to deal with.

Geom Mugeuk returned the dagger to Geugak Soma and said,

“If you and I are entwined in a single fate, let us face it head-on and unravel it. Even if I am stabbed by that dagger, I will survive.”

Geugak Soma looked at the dagger. He could see the character so (笑), “laughter,” that Geom Mugeuk had personally engraved on it.

Yes, just as they had done until now.

Geugak Soma accepted the dagger with a smile.

“I am ready.”

Ian knew she could not stop the two of them. They were not people who avoided fate. So she said it in her heart. I’m ready too.

Geom Mugeuk asked the mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion,

“Do you perhaps know where the place you saw in the prophecy was?”

The mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion closed her eyes for a moment and recalled the scene.

“It was a place with countless people watching from around a high platform.”

“Was it a sparring stage?”

“I think so, but I’m not certain. What I saw was that there were truly many people there.”

“Fortunately, it would not have been a lonely death.”

Just as Ian was glaring at Geom Mugeuk for his pointless joke, someone entered.

It was the chief steward of Cheonhwa Pavilion.

He approached and handed a letter to the mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion.

“What is this?”

“It is an invitation asking you, Pavilion Mistress, to serve as a judge.”

“Me? As a judge?”

If judging was required, then it meant there was a competition.

The mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion unfolded the invitation. Her eyes widened as she checked its contents.

“Where did this invitation come from?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion held out the invitation so everyone could see.

Only then did they all understand why the mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion had been invited as a judge. The name of the competition was written at the very top of the letter.

The World’s Greatest Beauty Selection Competition.

The moment they chose to face it head-on, fate had sent them an invitation.

Chapter 685: When There Are So Many Pretty Women
------------------------------------------------
It was truly an unexpected invitation.

“This is the first time I’ve been invited as a judge.”

When the Cheonhwaru Master spoke as if she could not understand, Ian thought it was only natural.

“We’ve called the finest judge. Who better understands beauty than you?”

Cheonhwaru had dozens of branches throughout the Central Plains, not counting the main pavilion here. Having met and experienced countless beautiful women in that process, it was only right that she receive such an invitation. Whoever it was, they had chosen well.

“Has there ever been a World’s Greatest Beauty Contest before?”

At Geommugeuk’s question, the Cheonhwaru Master shook her head.

“No, as far as I know, this is the first time it is being held.”

Whether this contest had been held in her previous life before the regression was impossible to know. Since it was something she had no interest in, it might have taken place, or it might have come about due to the various changes after her regression.

“Who is hosting the contest?”

Then, an unexpected name came up.

“They say it is the Shenhua Bang.”

The Shenhua Bang was a famous major sect in the martial world, belonging to the unorthodox faction. Ian also knew of the Shenhua Bang. It was not even an orthodox faction sect—why would an unorthodox faction host such a contest?

“Why would the Shenhua Bang suddenly host such a contest?”

To Ian’s question, Geommugeuk pointed at her.

“Because of you.”

“Huh? Because of me?”

To her, now extremely tense, Geommugeuk spoke with a smile.

“They are trying to officially announce your name to the martial world.”

“Ah, that’s ridiculous….”

In that instant, Ian’s expression stiffened.

“Don’t tell me… you mean for me to participate in this contest?”

“Why not? If it were a contest for the World’s Greatest Handsome Man, I would have gone. No, I would have sent our Lady Soma.”

Geugaksoma sighed in relief behind her mask. She knew that if it were Geommugeuk, he would have somehow truly sent her.

“Why do I have to attend?”

“There may be enemies among the participants. Find them while you are among them.”

In the past, she would have thought, *What enemies could there be among the participants?* But now she knew that it was possible.

Normally, Ian would have done this.

*I hate it! Absolutely not!*

Waving her hands, stepping back, even a look that said she’d show no mercy, not even to the Young Sect Leader, if he tried to make her do it. She would have refused everything at once. Geommugeuk would have been waiting for this reaction, too—trying not to take her to that dangerous place. Because it was a place where the Demon Lord would plunge a dagger into the Young Sect Leader’s chest.

“I will participate.”

“Really?”

When Geommugeuk asked in surprise, Ian nodded.

“Why on earth?”

Then Ian casually asked the Cheonhwaru Master.

“How much is the prize money?”

At the unexpected question, the Cheonhwaru Master looked surprised. She knew full well that Ian was not someone who would attend such a contest for money.

And soon, she could guess what was in Ian’s heart as she asked this.

“It is a contest to select the World’s Greatest Beauty, after all. Surely they would give at least fifty thousand nyang?”

Ian’s eyes widened and she shouted.

“They give fifty thousand nyang?”

“There may be separate prizes as well.”

“Fifty thousand nyang! That is enough to retire and live off of for a lifetime! Living idly without working! The dream of every salaried worker!”

Ian said to Geommugeuk with an excited face.

“I am entering too! Fate has given me the chance of a lifetime!”

How could Geommugeuk not know? That she was acting this way because she was afraid he would send her back to the main sect, saying it was dangerous.

The Cheonhwaru Master added another word to help her.

“As if the prize money is all there is. The great merchant guilds of the Central Plains will invite you to their events, and each time they will give several hundred nyang.”

“Hundreds of nyang just for showing my face!”

Geommugeuk grabbed her and shook her.

“Get a hold of yourself! You are bewitched by money!”

“How many merchant guilds are there in the Central Plains?”

“What about the Ghost Shadow Squad?”

“Just keep telling them to divide into factions and fight!”

After the two played around like that, Geommugeuk finally spoke seriously.

“Ian, I will take you with me. And you do not have to participate in the contest.”

To flaunt her beauty before countless heroes? He knew all too well that Ian would not be willing. He had naturally expected her to readily agree.

“No, I will participate in the contest.”

No matter how much she hated it or felt embarrassed, there was a clear reason to participate.

“The stage you saw in the prophecy—it is this contest stage, isn’t it?”

The Cheonhwaru Master nodded.

“It seems so.”

That was where Geommugeuk would be stabbed in the heart.

“I will stand on that stage with him.”

Geommugeuk could feel that she had made a firm resolve. Once again, Ian showed that she was someone who would gladly step forward, even for things she hated to death, if it was for his sake.

*Even so, you try to protect me, but the one who protects you is me.*

“I will go with you as your escort warrior.”

Ian was inwardly relieved. Yes, with Geommugeuk by her side, what was there to fear?

Geommugeuk also assigned a role to Geugaksoma.

“Lady Soma, please take charge of the Pavilion Master’s escort.”

“Understood.”

Geommugeuk asked the Cheonhwaru Master.

“Where will the contest be held?”

“In Qingyang, Anhui Province.”

Qingyang was the region where the Shenhua Bang was located; they were hosting the contest in their own front yard.

“When can you depart?”

“We can leave anytime.”

“Then let us leave early tomorrow.”

The Cheonhwaru Master nodded and looked down at the invitation she held.

“I am going to judge, yet somehow I feel as if I am the one going to be judged.”

It was a fitting thing to say. Whether it was fate or a force behind the scenes, it was pulling them in.

Geommugeuk said to her with a smile.

“Please cast one vote for Ian in the finals!”

Ian shouted in surprise.

“That is biased judging! Please do not say such things!”

“Oh? You must have confidence that you will make it to the finals. Weren’t you just joining for the sake of participating?”

“That is not what I meant. Ugh!”

Ian, her face slightly flushed, ran into the building, and Geommugeuk followed after her.

The Cheonhwaru Master and Geugaksoma watched the sight, smiling.

Then the Cheonhwaru Master suddenly spoke.

“Thank you.”

After hearing Geugaksoma’s words, she had told the Young Sect Leader everything honestly. She did not even want to imagine how her heart would feel now if she had not told Geugaksoma as well.

Geugaksoma smiled at her.

A smile that needed no further words.

The white mask reflected in the sunlight was shining brilliantly.

* * *

A young man was looking up at the stage, where final construction was in full swing. The stage was being built large and splendidly; this was the very place where the World’s Greatest Beauty Contest would be held.

Experts radiating chilling auras stood all around the man. Passersby did not dare approach and circled far around him. Just by looking at his escorts, one could tell that this man’s status was no ordinary thing.

This young man was none other than An Houping, the heir of the Shenhua Bang who was hosting this World’s Greatest Beauty Contest.

“Young Master, have you come out?”

A middle-aged martial artist walked up to his side and greeted him respectfully. The middle-aged man was Cao Wan, the external chief steward of the Shenhua Bang, who was in charge of the preparations for the World’s Greatest Beauty Contest.

“The streets are already swarming with people.”

People were beginning to gather from all over the Central Plains to watch the contest. While martial arts tournaments were held all over the Central Plains, this was the first time such an official World’s Greatest Beauty Contest was being held, so the attention of the Central Plains people was focused here.

An Houping kept his eyes on the stage and asked.

“Steward Cao.”

“Yes, Young Master.”

“Do you know what my dream is?”

“To marry the World’s Greatest Beauty.”

He had heard it countless times since childhood. Since that dream still had not changed, it was sincere. If it had been anyone else, he would have mocked them, saying, *Dreaming big with that ordinary face!* but…

“You are about to make your dream come true. Congratulations, Young Master.”

“The celebration is premature. The woman who becomes the World’s Greatest Beauty must accept my choice.”

“What woman would refuse the Young Master?”

A confident smile formed at An Houping’s lips.

Steward Cao knew. Even if that woman refused the choice, it would be useless. He knew well how tenacious and terrifying this An Houping was, having watched him since childhood. Even after watching him for so long, he was still nervous about misspeaking in front of An Houping.

Moreover, he was a lecherous womanizer. Since childhood, he had been mad about women. He did not know which woman would become the World’s Greatest Beauty, but he thought that perhaps she would become the most unfortunate winner in her life.

“When does it begin?”

“The preliminaries start in three days. Beauties from all over the Central Plains are arriving one after another for the contest.”

An Houping looked at the three chairs placed before the stage. They were the seats for the judges. VIP seats were prepared on either side of them.

“What of the judges?”

“Two of the three have already arrived; we only need the last one, the Cheonhwaru Master, to arrive.”

In that instant, An Houping’s eyes gleamed sharply.

“The Cheonhwaru Master? Isn’t Cheonhwaru a pleasure house?”

“Yes. We have invited the Cheonhwaru Master as a judge this time.”

Then An Houping’s expression hardened at once.

“They appointed a mere pleasure house owner as a judge?”

“Cheonhwaru is no ordinary pleasure house. It is the largest and most famous, with dozens of branches in the Central Plains.”

*You’ve been there often, haven’t you?* The words rose to his throat, but he could not spit them out. Perhaps he hated it even more because it was a place he had frequented.

“At the end of the day, a pleasure house is a pleasure house.”

Thinking of marrying a World’s Greatest Beauty judged by that pleasure house owner made him feel thoroughly disgusted.

“Who on earth decided to make this woman a judge?”

Steward Cao made an expression that it was difficult to say, then finally spoke carefully.

“It is the Sect Leader.”

In that instant, An Houping was startled.

“It was originally someone else, but he changed it at the last minute.”

At those words, doubt sprouted on An Houping’s face. It was his father, who had spent his whole life wielding a blade.

“Inform me when the Cheonhwaru Master arrives.”

* * *

The carriage carrying Geommugeuk’s party entered the streets of Qingyang.

Geommugeuk poked Ian in the side.

“Stop staring!”

Ian had been staring at Geugaksoma sitting in front and was startled. She could not help but look. That morning, upon arriving at their destination, Geugaksoma had removed her mask and donned a veil.

It was the first time seeing Geugaksoma without a mask. Though the area below her eyes was covered by the veil.

“You are so handsome!”

Ian blurted out as if mesmerized. Realizing she had said it to her face, she hurriedly lowered her head.

Geommugeuk looked at Geugaksoma and said.

“It pains me, but I cannot deny it.”

With eyes and forehead revealed, Geugaksoma looked like a completely different person. Moreover, the special veil also played its part. It was a stylish and splendid veil adorned with a flower, and below it the characters *Cheonhwa* were embroidered.

“I embroidered it myself.”

It was the work of the Cheonhwaru Master sitting beside her.

“But why did you ask me to make ten more of the same thing?”

Geugaksoma had asked her to make ten more, but had never given the reason, neither then nor now.

“The moment I take off this veil, chaos will erupt. The beauties of the Central Plains will all follow me, so ten is not enough!”

At Geommugeuk’s joke, the Cheonhwaru Master laughed pleasantly.

“I suppose I shall have to make more veils!”

Then the coachman’s voice came from the driver’s seat.

“We have arrived at the destination.”

Geugaksoma opened the carriage door and stepped out first.

The moment they saw outside, everyone knew. Why ten more veils had been needed.

Ten martial artists were waiting before the carriage. Surprisingly, they were the Faceless Ones who had come ahead and were waiting. The ten strongest among the Faceless Ones. They had somehow received them and, like Geugaksoma, removed their masks and wore veils.

Wearing the same martial robes and same veils, they looked exactly like escort warriors from Cheonhwaru.

Geugaksoma had concluded that the only reason she would have to stab a dagger into Geommugeuk’s chest was the Cheonhwaru Master. If the Cheonhwaru Master were taken hostage and threatened with her life. Since Geugaksoma had recognized that possibility, such a thing would never happen.

She had limited it to ten to avoid drawing attention; if possible, she would have brought all the Faceless Ones.

Standing before the ten Faceless Ones, Geugaksoma spoke with a smile. She was now the chief of Cheonhwaru’s escorts.

“Now, let us go, Pavilion Master.”

The Cheonhwaru Master was moved. She was ineffably grateful to Geugaksoma for going to such lengths for her.

Before getting out of the carriage, the Cheonhwaru Master turned back to Ian and spoke.

“Then I will see you at the finals stage.”

“The finals! I will not make it there.”

The Cheonhwaru Master smiled and got out of the carriage.

Clack, clack, clack, clack, clack.

The ten Faceless Ones surrounded her in all directions. Leading them was Geugaksoma.

Thus they disappeared into the crowd.

Geommugeuk and Ian did not get out together and remained in the carriage for a moment. Because of the attention, they thought it better to get out separately rather than together.

Ian's eyes gazing out the carriage window trembled slightly.

“There are so many people.”

It seemed she was nervous about standing before so many people.

“A martial arts contest would be better, wouldn’t it?”

Even on the way here, Ian had trained in martial arts whenever she had a moment. She had always been obsessed with training, but this time she trained even harder. Because her dream was to become the World’s Greatest Expert rather than the World’s Greatest Beauty.

He had naturally expected her to say a martial arts contest was better.

“No, I can enter a martial arts contest even when I am older, but I can only challenge for World’s Greatest Beauty now, right?”

Ian donned the veil she had brought and got out of the carriage first.

Since she was going to do it, she was determined to do it well. Like someone who had truly come to become the World’s Greatest Beauty. She had to focus on the contest and immerse herself completely in order to discover any suspicious persons.

Geommugeuk did not get out and watched the people passing by through the window for a moment.

*We’ve finally arrived.*

What events awaited them here? He thought it was all fate that had led him here. From discovering the Divine Maiden’s Dream in the Martial Alliance report, to the Cheonhwaru Master receiving a prophecy, and even being invited here. And as he had done until now, he would fight with all his might.

“Aren’t you getting out? What are you thinking so hard about?”

When he turned his head, Ian was standing outside the carriage looking at him.

“With so many pretty women around, I am worried what will become of our Ian?”

“In my eyes, I do not see any woman prettier than me.”

Ian’s beautiful eyes smiled brightly above the white veil.

“And let us worry about that after we register for the contest.”

Chapter 686: Don’t Kill Without Permission
------------------------------------------
The Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion arrived at the Shinhwa Gang.

Chief Steward Jo, who had come out upon hearing the news, greeted her.

“Welcome. You must have had a hard journey coming all this way.”

“Thank you for inviting me.”

Chief Steward Jo’s gaze shifted to Geugak Soma and the Faceless Guests standing behind her. The veil with a single flower painted on it drew his eye.

‘Since this is a competition hosted by the Evil Faction, she’s brought plenty of guards.’

But what of it? How strong could warriors who guarded a pleasure house possibly be? If anything, having so many of them would only grate on An Hupyeong’s nerves.

Chief Steward Jo guided her to the main hall.

When the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion took her seat, Geugak Soma stood directly behind her, and the Faceless Guests lined up to his left and right.

Normally, they would have scattered into every corner of the room, crouching here and there, sitting on the window frames, or perching on tree branches outside the window to peer in. Today, however, they behaved like well-trained warriors.

The Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion glanced behind her. She saw the veil of Geugak Soma standing right at her back.

She was grateful to him not only because he was protecting her. What she was even more grateful for was that he was standing there for her, when he was not the sort of person who should ever have to stand in a place like this.

Her gaze turned forward again. Yes, with him behind her, whom could she possibly fear facing?

As Chief Steward Jo served her tea, he spoke cautiously.

“I should be showing you directly to your lodgings, but there is someone who wishes to meet you, Pavilion Mistress.”

“May I ask who that person is?”

“He will be here shortly. Please wait while having a cup of tea.”

Chief Steward Jo bowed politely and went outside.

“I don’t have a good feeling about this.”

Making a guest who had come from far away wait like this, before she had even unpacked her luggage, was clearly discourteous.

It meant either the matter was extremely urgent, or the other party was a rude person.

In this case, it was both.

A short while later, An Hupyeong, a man who would never lose to anyone when it came to rudeness, entered the room.

He was in a hurry. The fact that his father had changed the judge to the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion had grated on his nerves.

Because he was worried that, in his later years, his father might have fallen for some sly temptress from a pleasure house.

An Hupyeong knew better than anyone, having frequented pleasure houses himself, just how well courtesans handled men.

One might think that his father, who had established his own household within the Evil Faction and possessed such profound martial arts, would never fall for something like that. But the stronger a person was, the easier they were to topple.

Because those women knew, like ghosts, how to slip between the vanity and loneliness of those who had reached the summit.

The moment An Hupyeong saw the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion, he was certain.

‘My intuition was right.’

She was beautiful, and she had elegance. There was a dignity about her that made it hard to see her as the owner of a pleasure house. It seemed possible that his father had fallen for this woman.

The warriors guarding him followed in behind An Hupyeong. They lined up behind him with threatening gazes.

Without even greeting the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion, An Hupyeong first flicked his hand at Geugak Soma and the Faceless Guests, ordering them out.

With this very first action, one could tell what sort of person he was.

An Hupyeong was a man who used the power he possessed to his heart’s content.

He was not someone who had grown up minding other people’s moods, and he had lived a life in which, if he told someone to leave, they left. Until now.

But the opposing warriors did not budge.

An Hupyeong’s gaze naturally shifted to the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion. Before he could say anything, she asked first.

“Who might you be?”

He silently looked down at her.

“I am An Hupyeong, the Young Gang Leader of the Shinhwa Gang.”

At that, the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion rose from her seat and cupped her fist politely.

“So you were an esteemed person. I am Yeo Jeong, who leads Cheonhwa Pavilion. Thank you for inviting me.”

“I wasn’t the one who invited you, so there’s no need to greet me.”

Then he looked again at Geugak Soma and the Faceless Guests standing behind her.

Now that you know who I am, get out! He looked at them with eyes that said as much, but they still did not move.

Before the atmosphere could turn vicious, the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion quickly stepped in.

“I am very timid, so I ordered them not to leave my side under any circumstances. Please understand, Young Gang Leader.”

“If a situation were to unfold where you had reason to be afraid, do you think you would be safe just because they were there?”

“At the very least, I would gain some comfort in my heart.”

“So you will not send them out to the end?”

“Please understand the feelings of a timid woman experiencing the Evil Dao for the first time.”

After An Hupyeong snorted, he looked back at his subordinates behind him.

“Throw them all out.”

There was a reason An Hupyeong was coming on so forcefully.

A woman who ran a large pleasure house like Cheonhwa Pavilion would surely not have ordinary nerve, so he intended to crush her spirit thoroughly before beginning. He wanted to see that composed face tremble with unease once the blades protecting her disappeared.

The man who walked out from behind was Xie Qiu, the head of the warriors An Hupyeong kept with him.

As the one responsible for the safety of the Shinhwa Gang’s heir, he was naturally a person with outstanding martial skill.

As he walked toward Geugak Soma, he stopped. Unable to approach any closer, he halted about ten paces away.

Xie Qiu stared back and forth between Geugak Soma and the Faceless Guests.

‘What are these bastards?’

From the moment he had first entered and glanced at them, they had felt strange.

He had met all manner of rough and vicious men from the Evil Faction until now, but this was the first time he had encountered people who gave off this sort of feeling.

They were not rough, yet they were not easy to look down on either. To call it madness, they seemed too sane; to call them sane, they were too bizarre.

How could he know? That he was facing the most peculiar ones among the demons led by the Eight Demon Venerables.

Especially that fellow who drew his gaze!

Seeing him standing in the middle, he was clearly their leader. And yet he was merely staring at him in silence.

He was unpleasant.

Yes, that was exactly the right word. He felt like someone who should not be treated recklessly, but at the same time, he did not give off the feeling of being a true master. Xie Qiu clearly thought that if they fought, he would not amount to much, and yet he did not want to fight him.

Had he read his mind? A faint smile appeared in the other man’s eyes.

‘He’s laughing?’

He was laughing in this situation? Normally, Xie Qiu would already have pierced his heart and lopped off his head. And yet he did not feel like doing so.

The one who broke the taut tension was An Hupyeong.

“What is this? Is he someone you need to be nervous around?”

An Hupyeong looked at Geugak Soma with renewed eyes.

Of course, even if he were a master who could make Xie Qiu nervous, there was no reason for An Hupyeong himself to be afraid. No one would dare harm him.

Because from the moment he was born, he had been wearing the powerful protective armor called the Young Gang Leader of the Shinhwa Gang.

“So, despite my position as the Young Gang Leader of the Shinhwa Gang, I’ve been walking around all this time with a man weaker than some blade-for-hire a pleasure house owner keeps around?”

The corner of Xie Qiu’s eye twitched, but even so, he did not draw his sword.

At that moment, the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion gently soothed An Hupyeong.

“I have been so distracted selling liquor that I am ignorant of the laws of the martial world. So please forgive me generously, Young Gang Leader.”

At that, An Hupyeong asked in a cold voice.

“What is your relationship with my father?”

The Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion already knew why he was acting this way. During her journey here, she had learned a certain amount about the Shinhwa Gang through Cheonhwa Pavilion’s intelligence network in this region. It was information that would be difficult even for Tongcheon Pavilion or Eunwol to learn. After all, what this An Hupyeong had said while drunk at Cheonhwa Pavilion was something only she could know.

“I have never met the Gang Leader.”

An Hupyeong snorted.

And yet he invited her to such an important event? When they had never even met?

Naturally, he thought the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion was lying.

This was why he had tried to crush her spirit. In any case, these lowly things would only spew lies if dealt with through words.

At that moment, Chief Steward Jo’s voice was heard from outside.

“The Gang Leader is looking for you, Young Gang Leader.”

His father was stopping him? An Hupyeong thought his prediction had been exactly right after all.

An Hupyeong turned and walked toward the door.

“You can’t even kill him, so why glare like an idiot? Let’s go.”

Xie Qiu turned away from Geugak Soma. In that brief time, countless thoughts had flashed through his head, but the conclusion he reached at the end was this.

‘I’m glad we didn’t fight.’

Those who knew him would never believe it, but that was truly what he thought.

Whether he knew that feeling or not, An Hupyeong spoke to Geugak Soma from in front of the door.

“I’ve marked you. If you’re still laughing the next time I see you, you’ll die by my hand.”

After issuing that cold warning, An Hupyeong left the room.

Trying to ease the stiffened atmosphere, the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion said with a smile,

“What will you do now if you can’t smile anymore? It seems you’ll have to change your title.”

At her joke, not only Geugak Soma but also the Faceless Guests standing beside him laughed silently.

The laughter An Hupyeong truly needed to worry about was precisely the laughter of these Faceless Guests.

The moment he insulted Geugak Soma, the protective armor called the Young Gang Leader of the Shinhwa Gang had turned into a scrap of cloth.

Because the ones who would ignore such things and start hacking him to pieces first were none other than these Faceless Guests.

Knowing them better than anyone, Geugak Soma said just one thing to them.

“Don’t laugh.”

For them, laughter meant precisely!

And so those words also meant this:

Do not kill him without permission.

* * *

After leaving the main hall, An Hupyeong glared coldly at Xie Qiu.

“What was that?”

“He was no ordinary man.”

“Yes, I’m sure he wasn’t. Then I suppose you are an ordinary man. Have you been deceiving me all this time, pretending to be extraordinary?”

Xie Qiu made no excuses and lowered his head deeply.

An Hupyeong, too, did not insult him any further. He knew very well how strong Xie Qiu was. The Pavilion Mistress must have brought an extraordinary master with her.

Fine. If she wanted to occupy a place inside the Shinhwa Gang, she would have to make at least that much of an investment.

“Call Mister Yong.”

At the mention of the man who appeared like an all-purpose troubleshooter, Xie Qiu flinched in surprise.

“The Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion has come to this competition as a guest.”

“Who said I was going to kill that woman?”

“If Mister Yong steps in, the matter will grow larger. And…”

“What? What are you trying to say? That even Mister Yong can’t win?”

“No.”

Xie Qiu shut his mouth.

For a moment, he thought perhaps that might be true, but then another thought came to him. What if Mister Yong actually came and fought that man, only for it to be revealed that the atmosphere had merely been strange and his skill was nothing special? No matter the result, it would be a problem.

That was why it was right not to call him.

An Hupyeong strode forward as he spoke.

“If it’s something that won’t be solved even if Mister Yong comes, then that means something is happening right now without my knowing, doesn’t it? Call him immediately.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and Ian also arrived at the Shinhwa Gang.

Applications for the competition were being accepted at the grand training ground, and there were truly many people present.

There were many women, and many people who had come with those women.

When Ian entered, people naturally looked at her. A beauty that could not be blocked by a veil drew in their gazes.

“They’re lining up over there.”

Because so many people had gathered, applications were being accepted at more than a dozen lines.

“Look here.”

Ian, who had been heading that way to stand in line, discovered a notice about the competition posted on the wall.

“Ah, if you become the greatest beauty under heaven, the prize money isn’t fifty thousand nyang but thirty thousand nyang. As an additional prize, they give you the opportunity to attend a dinner with the Leader of the Evil Dao Alliance.”

Geom Mugeuk thought of the Leader of the Evil Dao Alliance. Come to think of it, it had been quite some time since he had seen him. To think he would miss those little eyes.

“You’re disappointed it isn’t fifty thousand nyang, aren’t you?”

Ian flinched and feigned innocence.

“There’s no way!”

“It feels like you lost twenty thousand nyang, doesn’t it? Be honest.”

“…It is a little disappointing. This is a competition aimed at all of the Central Plains, after all.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed aloud, and Ian laughed along with him.

Ian was happy in this time she was spending with Geom Mugeuk.

Even now, she could feel the gazes around her looking at her, but she focused only on Geom Mugeuk.

Because she did not know when they would be able to laugh and chatter together like this again.

“Now, take a look around, and if there’s anyone prettier than me, please kill her.”

At Ian’s whisper, Geom Mugeuk looked around.

“Then a public enemy of the martial world would be born, wouldn’t one?”

“Your friend is the captain of the Demon-Extermination Unit, so what are you worried about?”

Geom Mugeuk also enjoyed this time with her. There was a comfort that only Ian gave him. And Geom Mugeuk knew very well that such consideration came from her intelligence.

“Let’s go stand in line over there too.”

The two of them stood in the shortest of the dozen or so lines.

It was just then.

From far away, exclamations of “Oh!” erupted one after another.

Naturally, everyone there turned their gaze in that direction.

The crowd parted wide, and through the opening, a woman was walking in.

The woman, wearing palace robes that revealed the shape of her body, drew everyone’s attention.

She was beautiful. Even the splendid clothes she wore were overshadowed by her appearance, making it hard to care what she had on.

There were many beauties here, but hers was a beauty that overwhelmed them all. The only person who could stand against that beauty was Ian.

If Ian’s beauty was pure white, then this woman’s beauty was magnificent. If Ian was intellectual and pure in her beauty, this woman was sensual.

While everyone held their breath, the woman walked toward the line where Geom Mugeuk and Ian were standing.

Standing before the two of them, the woman smiled enchantingly and said,

“This line is the shortest, at least.”

Chapter 687: My Sect Is the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult
------------------------------------------------------
“As they say, life is easier when you get in the right line.”

From her looks alone, she seemed like someone who would not exchange a single word with others, but the woman spoke to them with ease.

Ian silently gazed at her.

Seeing someone this close should have revealed at least one flaw, yet this woman was perfect.

Even her clothes were impeccable, and her makeup was done so well. Truly, Ian almost wanted to learn how to dress and apply makeup from her.

Ian wondered whether this meeting was really a coincidence.

Because the woman gave off far too special an impression to be dismissed as someone they had simply met while standing in line.

Ian looked around at the other lines nearby. The line they were standing in was still the shortest.

“We don’t know yet whether we chose the right line.”

Ian looked toward the front of the line she was in and continued.

“You never know what problem might arise up ahead, do you? If you’re unlucky, they might cut it off right before your turn.”

At Ian’s words, the woman gave a subtle smile.

The gazes of the two women tangled in midair.

Like two absolute masters facing each other, their opposing beauties stood tautly against one another.

Everyone around them held their breath and watched the two.

Geom Mugeuk was watching them from the closest distance of all.

The woman’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

Her bewitching eyes were different when she looked at a woman and when she looked at a man; she was a woman who instinctively knew how to seduce men.

Ian poked Geom Mugeuk in the side.

“What are you staring at so blankly?”

Only then did Geom Mugeuk smile awkwardly and say,

“You were so beautiful. I was rude.”

As though she did not dislike those words, the woman smiled, and Ian repeated exactly what Geom Mugeuk had said in the carriage.

“It’s vexing, but I have no choice but to admit it.”

Then she sent a voice transmission to Geom Mugeuk.

—Please kill this woman.

It was an extension of the joke that he should kill any woman prettier than her. It meant this woman was more beautiful than she was.

—Isn’t killing her going a bit too far?

—You should say that while looking at me, not at that woman.

—Someone in the carriage once looked at Lord Soma like this.

—Now you understand how I felt then, don’t you?

She said that, but she thought the case of her staring at the Extreme Evil Soma and Geom Mugeuk’s case were different.

The Extreme Evil Soma was someone she knew, and someone who definitely had a person he liked.

But this woman? She was someone they did not even know.

‘Did she really catch his fancy?’

This was the first time Ian had seen Geom Mugeuk stare so intently at another woman. So she was inwardly surprised.

‘Well, the young master is a man too.’

How could he not look when she was that beautiful? Even Ian, a woman herself, could not help admiring her. Yes, it was understandable enough.

If Ian could feel it, how could the woman herself not have felt it? At Geom Mugeuk’s gaze, confidence flashed through the woman’s eyes.

Ian changed the subject and asked,

“Did you come here alone?”

“Yes.”

She was clearly a woman who had learned martial arts, but Ian could not accurately discern her skill. Whether it was because her ability was outstanding or because the martial arts she had learned were special, Ian did not know, but the other woman was extremely adept at hiding herself.

“You’re so beautiful, yet you travel alone. That’s surprising.”

“I’m not easily frightened.”

The woman introduced herself first.

“My name is Cha Iran (車利蘭).”

“I’m Ian.”

Geom Mugeuk introduced himself last.

“I am Geom Yeon.”

Cha Iran smiled with her eyes at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“What a fine name.”

“Thank you. Your name is beautiful as well, Miss Cha.”

Cha Iran asked Geom Mugeuk,

“What is the relationship between you two?”

At Cha Iran’s question, Geom Mugeuk answered,

“She is the person I serve.”

Cha Iran, who had been staring fixedly at Geom Mugeuk as if to bore through him, tilted her head.

“You don’t look like someone who would serve anyone.”

As she said that, she glanced at Ian.

“Where can one find such a splendid martial artist?”

Would you look at this woman?

Her gaze toward Geom Mugeuk, and now these words too. She was openly seducing Geom Mugeuk.

Ian glanced at Geom Mugeuk. Don’t you dare stand there grinning like an idiot!

Ian spoke to her crisply.

“You won’t be able to find one.”

“You never know.”

Cha Iran looked at Geom Mugeuk and added,

“There’s no guarantee a person has to protect only one person for life, is there?”

Protect me instead of this woman.

Only after saying everything she wanted did she smile brightly at Ian.

“I’m joking.”

Ian did not get angry. In this fight, getting angry meant losing.

“You’re right. If he finds someone new he wants to protect, I should let him go at any time.”

Only when someone had begun to truly wedge herself between the Young Cult Leader and her did Ian realize how she had thought of Geom Mugeuk.

Until now, she had thought this way.

If the Young Cult Leader found someone he liked, she would sincerely bless them and be happy as if it were her own affair. Of course she should. That was how she had felt.

No matter how much the Young Cult Leader called her his heart.

Even if the Cult Leader had permitted her to learn the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

Even if she had become the Fist Demon’s foster daughter.

Even if she had become captain of the Ghost Shadow Unit.

Her heart had always remained in the days when she had served as Geom Mugeuk’s guard.

And today, through this woman, she realized.

She might not be able to sincerely bless him after all.

“If he goes, will you truly let him go?”

At Cha Iran’s question, Ian shook her head.

“No. How could I let someone like him go? If he says he’s leaving…”

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“I’ll kill him.”

Geom Mugeuk stared at Ian with startled eyes.

Soon after, Ian looked at Cha Iran and said with a smile,

“I’m joking too.”

They were both saying it was a joke, but between the two women, an unknowable, taut energy was swirling.

In the meantime, the line gradually shortened, and at last, it was Ian’s turn to apply.

Geom Mugeuk handled the application on her behalf.

The martial artist receiving applications asked without raising his head.

“Please tell me the name of the participant first.”

“Ian.”

The martial artist wrote the name on the document.

“Ian, and you are a martial artist?”

“That is correct.”

“A martial artist, then your affiliated sect or family?”

Then words no one could have imagined flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

The martial artist wrote it down on the document as he heard it.

“The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, and next…”

After he had written it all down, the martial artist flinched in shock.

“What did you just say?”

Then four syllables came through clearly.

“The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

The martial artist’s expression hardened as he raised his head.

If you make such a joke, you will be punished!

He was about to scold them sharply.

But he could not. First, Ian, who wore a veil, was too beautiful.

And he instinctively knew that Geom Mugeuk, standing beside her, was no ordinary person either.

“I heard one could apply regardless of origin. Was that not the case?”

“That is correct. Are you truly from the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?”

“That is correct.”

He had not expected the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult to participate in this contest, and he had expected even less that they would reveal their identities so boldly.

The martial artist asked in a trembling voice,

“May I ask your position within the Divine Cult?”

“I cannot tell you that much.”

“Yes, understood.”

In truth, Ian was just as surprised. No, Ian was even more surprised than the martial artist receiving the application.

She had naturally thought he would hide their identities and give a false one.

And that woman was even listening from behind them.

‘Surely he isn’t doing this to look good in front of that woman, is he?’

She thought Geom Mugeuk would never do such a thing, but the woman was so beautiful that he might have been moved to it.

The martial artist at the reception desk handed over the participant token.

“The preliminary round will be held three days from now.”

Then he said to the people standing in that line,

“Please wait a moment.”

He left his seat and ran off somewhere.

Ian turned around. Cha Iran must certainly have heard the words Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, yet she did not look very surprised.

“You don’t seem surprised.”

“I told you I’m not easily frightened.”

The gazes of the two women tangled in midair.

“Still, you chose the wrong line today.”

After saying that, Ian strode away.

As though wanting Ian to hear, Cha Iran spoke to Geom Mugeuk as he followed after her.

“Let’s have a meal together sometime.”

Geom Mugeuk gave a faint smile, bowed his head slightly in greeting, and followed Ian out.

Only after the two left the grand training ground and came completely out of Sinhwabang did Ian finally speak.

“An unparalleled beauty asked you to eat with her. Why didn’t you make an appointment?”

“We’ll end up eating together later anyway.”

At Ian’s gaze, asking what he meant, he said,

“That woman will approach us again.”

At those words, Ian’s steps stopped. Her stiff expression completely loosened.

“Right? That’s it, isn’t it? That woman knew our identities and approached us deliberately, didn’t she? You knew, didn’t you? That’s why you revealed our identities.”

When Geom Mugeuk nodded, relief washed over Ian’s heart.

“Ah, I was nervous. I thought you had fallen for that suspicious woman, young master.”

It was an untruthful statement. She should have said beautiful woman, not suspicious woman.

“But there’s another reason I revealed our identities.”

“What reason?”

“Because of you.”

“Pardon?”

Just as Ian blinked with an expression that said she did not understand,

a reason she truly had not expected flowed out.

“Even if you become the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven, it means nothing if it is under a false identity, does it?”

“!”

“Ian of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult is the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven. That is how it should be known to the world.”

Ian could tell Geom Mugeuk was sincere. And there was one more reason.

“That way, you won’t be bothered by troublesome matters as you live.”

If people knew that the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven was from the Demonic Cult, who would dare trouble her? Even if some lecher of the murim felt tempted to go see the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven just once, would he dare go if she were from the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?

‘Ah! You revealed it because of me.’

Ian was moved. He was a Young Cult Leader who thought of her this much, and yet she had misunderstood him.

“But how did you know that woman approached us deliberately?”

The reason was simple.

Because she was someone he knew.

She was one of the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

The Seventh King of the Twelve Zodiac Kings, the Flower King.

She had been the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven during the time when the Twelve Zodiac Kings ruled the world.

With her overwhelmingly beautiful appearance, she drew the attention of the martial world, but no one knew her true identity.

All sorts of rumors spread. Some even said she must have risen to the position of one of the Twelve Zodiac Kings because of her beauty. There was also a rumor that one of the Twelve Zodiac Kings had loved her and seated her there.

But that was nonsense born of ignorance about her.

“She is an assassin.”

“!”

Ian’s eyes widened in surprise. Not for a single moment had she imagined that such a beautiful woman could be an assassin. If Geom Mugeuk had not told her, she would not have believed it easily.

A true assassin unknown to the world.

During the time when the Twelve Zodiac Kings ruled the murim, the most famous assassin known to the world was the Fourth King, the Assassin King.

But she had operated in secret until the very moment of her death.

She had lived under the dishonor of being called the flower of the Twelve Zodiac Kings, and only after she died did the martial artists of the world come to know that she had truly been an exceptional assassin. Why she had lived such a life was never revealed to the world.

She had been the most beautiful assassin in the history of the murim, and the assassin with the greatest skill.

Though the Assassin King possessed all the fame as an assassin, she had not wanted that fame.

In that sense, she was the true blade in the darkness.

The reason Geom Mugeuk had stared intently at her earlier was because he had been surprised at first, and later because he had thought she had approached in order to use a beauty trap on him.

“So she approached with the Young Cult Leader as her target.”

Ian grew tense at once.

An assassin and a bodyguard.

An opponent who, by fate, stood at the opposite point from her.

Fortunately, the target was not Geom Mugeuk.

“Her target isn’t me.”

What if he had not chosen the Dream of the Divine Maiden from the Martial World’s Treasury? What if he had not given it to the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master? And so, what if he had not left the cult to meet the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master? He would not be here. Coming here had been entirely his own choice and will.

But this contest had been decided on far earlier than that.

In other words, separate from her approaching him now, the purpose behind holding this contest and her participating in it lay elsewhere.

Geom Mugeuk stopped walking.

He looked at the poster for the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven contest pasted on the wall.

Only then did Ian understand.

“The target was the Alliance Leader of the Evil Path.”

If one became the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven, one could attend a banquet where the Alliance Leader of the Evil Path would be present as the prize. If it was her, she could certainly become the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven.

Geom Mugeuk nodded. If it was an assassination she had to carry out personally, then the target was likely the Alliance Leader of the Evil Path. Of course, it might not be, and even if it had been, the situation might change now that he had intervened.

Geom Mugeuk intended to respond with every possibility in mind.

“The way to stop it is simple.”

“You can kill that woman, Young Cult Leader.”

“Nothing has been confirmed yet, has it?”

“Ah, right. Then how do we stop it?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk smiled meaningfully and said,

“You just have to become the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven. Then that woman won’t be able to attend the banquet. Ian, the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven, saves the Alliance Leader of the Evil Path!”

“Stop teasing me!”

Could she really defeat that woman and be chosen as the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven?

Ian recalled the sight of her walking toward her earlier. Even thinking back on it, she still could not believe that woman was an assassin.

“It seems being entangled with us was fate too. Of all times, she came in when our line happened to be the shortest.”

It might seem fated that they ended up standing in the same line, but Geom Mugeuk knew that was not the case.

The secret assassin organization led by the Flower King, Gain Bridge.

The assassins of Gain Bridge were all women. And it was said they were made up only of beautiful women.

“I don’t think it was a coincidence that our line was the shortest when she came in.”

“What do you mean? Surely…”

“Yes. The assassins she leads stood in the other lines and manipulated their length. So that she could naturally stand behind us.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk could now understand clearly why they had held a contest to select the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven instead of a martial arts tournament.

Because in this place, crowded with countless women gathered from the Central Plains, the assassins of Gain Bridge could naturally blend in without drawing any suspicion.

He could not know how many of them were here, but of one thing he was certain.

“This street is now filled with beautiful assassins.”

Chapter 688: I Lose My Wits Whenever I Enter This Room
------------------------------------------------------
“Father, did you call for me?”

An Hupyeong entered the main hall where the leader of the Shinhwa Gang conducted his affairs.

The leader of the Shinhwa Gang, An Cheonggwang.

He was a truly self-made figure, a man who had raised the Shinhwa Gang from an ordinary local sect into a great power known to everyone on the Evil Path.

His martial arts were profound, and his temperament was anything but ordinary. If anyone caused him harm, he would take revenge without fail, and he had the nerve not to blink even with a blade at his throat. On top of that, he possessed a desire for success stronger than anyone else’s.

There were two guests in the main hall, and when An Hupyeong realized who they were, he flinched in surprise.

Whenever his father’s legend was spoken of, two masters were always mentioned. The two leading figures who, together with his father, had made the Shinhwa Gang what it was today.

The Ran-Snake Sword and the Iron Cold Saber.

Had they not aided his father as if his affairs were their own, the Shinhwa Gang could never have grown this large. For that reason, his father treated them more preciously than family.

And as long as his father’s ambitions were still in progress, there would be no casting them aside after they had served their purpose.

They were two men whose faces were difficult to see under normal circumstances, yet now they were gathered in one place.

“I pay my respects to the elders.”

An Hupyeong greeted them politely.

The two masters nodded once, accepting his greeting with only their eyes. Of course, An Hupyeong was dissatisfied with that attitude.

‘No matter how young I am, I am still the Young Gang Leader in name.’

Since it had been so long, could they not at least ask whether he had been well?

It was not that he hated them; he hated his father. Those two masters had always been cold-hearted and wicked by nature.

But if his father cherished and trusted him, then for his father’s sake at least, they would not treat him so indifferently.

Just how displeased with him must his father usually be for them to disregard him like this?

An Cheonggwang asked abruptly.

“I hear you met the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress?”

“Yes, I am just returning from meeting her.”

“Why did you meet someone I summoned as a judge?”

“Is the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress not the owner of the most famous brothel in the Central Plains? She must surely be an extraordinary person, so I went to see her thinking there might be something I could learn.”

An Hupyeong thoroughly concealed his true feelings.

To be honest, he wanted to ask this.

Have you fallen for that woman? Is that why you summoned the owner of a mere brothel as a judge?

But if he did, given his father’s temperament, a thunderous rebuke would fall upon him. Moreover, if he said such a thing in the presence of the Ran-Snake Sword and the Iron Cold Saber, he might even be beaten to death.

“I heard that you changed the final judge to the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress at the last moment, Father.”

It would have been nice if he explained that he had changed it for such and such a reason.

“This is not something for you to concern yourself with.”

With answers like that, how could he not be suspicious and worried?

‘Not something for me to concern myself with? The wealth of our gang is at stake here. How is that yours alone, Father?’

At that moment, a subordinate entered and reported.

“They say one of the contestants in this tournament is from the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

An Cheonggwang’s expression immediately hardened. The Ran-Snake Sword and the Iron Cold Saber, who were with him, also looked at each other and exchanged glances.

“Is that certain?”

“We are currently verifying it through the Evil Path Alliance.”

“Report to me as soon as you find out.”

“Yes.”

The subordinate ran outside.

An Cheonggwang, who had been about to turn toward the Ran-Snake Sword and the Iron Cold Saber, also dismissed his son.

“You may go.”

An Hupyeong bowed politely and left, then turned back to look at his father from the doorway. His father was speaking with the two masters with grave expressions on their faces.

‘What is so serious?’

If it were a martial arts tournament, perhaps, but was this not a beauty contest?

What did it matter whether the contestant was from the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult or the daughter of a righteous faction hero? It was simply a tournament held for the entire martial world to enjoy.

When the decision to hold the tournament was first made, his father had said this.

—Through this tournament, there will be no one in the Central Plains who does not know the name of our gang.

If it became a tournament in which even the Demon Cult participated, would that not suit the purpose even better?

And yet the atmosphere surrounding it was not that of a festival.

‘Something I don’t know about is definitely happening.’

When he came out of the main hall, Sachu, who had been waiting there, relayed the news.

“Mr. Yong has arrived and gone to the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress.”

At the unexpected news, An Hupyeong was startled.

“Already? How?”

“The Gang Leader summoned him, so Mr. Yong was already here at the gang.”

Normally, Mr. Yong stayed outside. But his father had even called Mr. Yong to the gang? Now he was even more curious about what was going on.

“And yet he went without even seeing me?”

“You know Mr. Yong’s temperament, don’t you?”

Of course he knew it well. He was short-tempered and willful. He was truly uncontrollable, but at the very least, his skills were acknowledged by all.

The reason such a man was called “Mr.” was because he himself wanted to be addressed that way.

His father believed that Mr. Yong would one day cause a major incident and did not use him in important matters, and thanks to that, An Hupyeong had been able to join hands with him.

“You knew Mr. Yong was a difficult man to control.”

That was why he had said so many times that they should not call him.

An Hupyeong scowled and glared at Sachu.

“If you had done your job properly, there would have been no need to call him.”

Sachu did not make excuses and lowered his head. He knew well what kind of person An Hupyeong was. He was someone who took another’s excuses as a challenge. At times like this, it was best simply to keep one’s mouth shut.

An Hupyeong moved quickly. This was not the time to be harrying Sachu.

‘I have to see that hateful bastard get beaten down with my own eyes.’

Especially that fellow who was as handsome as a male courtesan! He was today’s target.

An Hupyeong strode toward the guest hall where the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress was staying.

* * *

The words Mr. Yong had heard most often were these:

Even for someone of the Evil Faction, isn’t this too much?

Those words contained his entire life.

He had committed deeds without hesitation that made even the average villain of the Evil Faction click his tongue.

And today, that Mr. Yong was angry.

Because the Gang Leader had summoned him, he had rushed to the Shinhwa Gang as if flying. He had come full of anticipation, wondering why he had been called, but two days had passed since his arrival, and he had still not seen the Gang Leader’s face even once.

Yet when he heard that the Gang Leader was meeting first with the Ran-Snake Sword and the Iron Cold Saber, who had arrived today, his anger burst forth.

“Leeches living off their old reputations!”

Once An Hupyeong ascended to the position of Gang Leader, he intended to deal with those two first. For that to happen, he had to carry out the task entrusted to him by the future Gang Leader.

Mr. Yong arrived at the guest hall where the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress was staying.

Sachu had told him to appropriately teach the best-looking one among the guards a lesson.

‘Consider yourself unlucky today.’

Because today, the standard for “appropriately” was likely to be somewhat extreme.

Mr. Yong slowly pushed open the door to the guest hall.

Then the scene inside unfolded before him.

The Extreme Evil Lesser Demon and the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress were sitting side by side drinking tea, and the Faceless Guests were scattered throughout the guest hall, resting.

The gazes of those inside all turned toward Mr. Yong at once.

Why was that? Truly, why was that?

Srrk, tak.

Mr. Yong closed the door again. He shut it quietly, like someone who had come to the wrong room.

After closing the door, Mr. Yong asked himself in front of it.

‘Why did I close the door?’

No one had drawn a weapon, and no one had even revealed killing intent. They had simply looked at him in an utterly peaceful atmosphere.

Unable to withstand those gazes, his hand had closed the door of its own accord.

‘Don’t tell me I was frightened? Impossible!’

It was the first time something like this had ever happened, so he was bewildered.

It was right then.

Puak!

A dagger pierced through the door and shot out.

The protruding dagger stopped right in front of his left eye, where he stood before the door. If it had come out even a little farther, it would have stabbed his eye.

Even as he looked at the blade of the dagger jutting out before his eyes, Mr. Yong did not step back. He had instinctively realized that it had not been an attack meant to kill him.

Puak!

Another dagger pierced through beside it. This time as well, it stopped precisely in front of his other eye.

Still, Mr. Yong did not step back.

Puak!

The dagger that pierced through next stopped in front of his throat, but Mr. Yong did not retreat.

Puak!

The next dagger that shot out stopped before his heart.

All four daggers were aimed at his vital points.

From the start, they had not intended to kill him. They had simply shown him, with perfect precision, that they were aiming at his vitals.

One might think it was nothing special for a martial artist to pierce a door with a dagger, but the technique displayed just now was no ordinary skill.

The daggers protruded without the slightest tilt, and they had accurately calculated the positions of his eyes and throat outside the door. What was astonishing was that the distance had been controlled exactly, close enough to touch but not quite.

Moreover, the problem was that it was not one person who had thrust in several daggers.

Each dagger was different.

There was no way one person would carry so many different daggers, which meant several people had each displayed a move in turn.

Could that be possible?

The fact that it looked as if one person had stabbed them in meant that their skills were both remarkably high and evenly matched.

Sssrk.

The daggers that had been jutting out withdrew inside all at once.

The slowly vanishing daggers seemed to be saying this:

If you want to live, leave.

Ordinarily, he would have smashed the door down and gone in at once.

How dare they do something like this to him? He would have twisted and broken the wrists of the ones who had stabbed out those daggers. Though he had been told merely to teach them a lesson, he would have wrecked them all.

That was the kind of man he was.

And yet his body would not move. He did not feel like it. But he could not simply turn away either.

Enduring humiliation? He had never done such a thing in his life.

If he just left, what would he say to the Young Gang Leader? What if rumors spread through the gang that he had grown frightened and turned away?

He should have left when his instincts first warned him.

His survival instinct could not overcome the life he had continued by habit.

He opened the door again and walked inside, shouting with great vigor.

“What kind of men wear face veils like girls?”

But at the scene inside, Mr. Yong flinched in surprise and his eyes widened.

Huh?

The people inside were not wearing face veils. Instead of veils, they were wearing pure white masks.

Creeeak.

The white mask closing the door behind him was smiling.

* * *

When An Hupyeong arrived at the guest hall, he expected to hear screams of agony, but it was unexpectedly quiet.

“Surely not? He didn’t kill him, did he?”

At An Hupyeong’s worried words, Sachu answered.

“In case something like that happened, I told him they were the Gang Leader’s guests.”

At that, An Hupyeong was relieved. Mr. Yong was willful, but he was afraid of his father.

An Hupyeong looked forward to it.

The sight of that guard sprawled face-first on the floor, his face swollen and his front teeth all broken.

‘You smiled so arrogantly, didn’t you? Let’s see if you can smile today.’

And he intended to personally tear off and rip apart that damned face veil of his. Unless Mr. Yong had already torn it off, that was.

Thus An Hupyeong opened the guest hall door and stepped inside.

“If you had arrived, you should have come to see me first……”

At that moment, An Hupyeong fell silent. Mr. Yong, whom he had naturally expected to be there, was not in the room.

The Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress was drinking tea, and the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon, who had been looking out the window, turned to look this way. The rest of the Faceless Guests were scattered throughout the guest hall. Some were maintaining their weapons, some were sitting in chairs and resting, and some were folding clothes.

At the sight of them spending an utterly peaceful moment, An Hupyeong turned to Sachu and asked with his eyes.

‘What happened? You said Mr. Yong went here first.’

Sachu wore an expression that said he did not know either.

An Hupyeong turned his head again and asked the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress.

“Did anyone happen to come here?”

The Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress shook her head.

“No, no one came.”

Right. If he had come, there was no way these bastards would be so perfectly fine.

An Hupyeong’s gaze turned toward the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon.

The man who had been standing by the window was now standing behind the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress, looking at him.

The other Faceless Guests were also looking at him. No one was smiling.

‘Mere guards, and yet!’

It displeased him greatly that they were staring so intently at him, the Young Gang Leader of the Shinhwa Gang.

Moreover, what lay in their eyes was neither respect nor fear. They were unpleasant gazes, as if they were looking at an animal trapped in a cage.

‘Where the hell did he go without smashing these things apart?’

Meanwhile, Sachu was examining the door.

When he had just entered, he had seen marks left on the door. So he examined the inside as well, and there were marks in the exact same places.

‘Traces of blades piercing through!’

When he realized they had not been there when he first came earlier, the downy hairs on his arms all stood on end.

‘No way. He came?’

He looked around. Not a single piece of furniture was broken. A fight had broken out with him, and yet the room was this intact?

Most decisively, not one person in this room was injured. There was not even a scratch.

And Mr. Yong was such a master.

But the reason his heart grew uneasy was the same.

There was no way that foul-tempered, impatient man would say he was going and then not come.

‘If, by any chance, these people killed Mr. Yong? Then the body?’

This was the Shinhwa Gang. It was hard to believe they had killed him without anyone getting hurt, but how had they disposed of the body?

At that moment, Sachu looked at the Faceless Guest standing near him.

The Faceless Guest was staring straight at him. The Faceless Guest standing behind him was looking at him with a similar gaze.

The instant he saw those strange eyes that could not be described, a chill ran through him, and he instinctively knew.

‘These crazy bastards killed Mr. Yong!’

At that moment, An Hupyeong walked toward him and said,

“What are you doing? Whenever you enter this room, you lose your senses.”

An Hupyeong left the room without even greeting the Cheonhwa Pavilion Mistress, and just as Sachu was about to follow him out—

The Extreme Evil Lesser Demon slowly raised his hand and made a shushing gesture.

At that sight, Sachu’s heart sank.

‘He realized that I realized.’

The next moment, the rest of the Faceless Guests all raised their fingers in one movement.

Shh!

All of them were looking at him and making shushing gestures. It was a terrifying and bizarre sight unlike anything he had ever seen in his life.

Just then, An Hupyeong shouted from behind him.

“If you can’t even win, then why aren’t you coming? What are you staring at?”

Sachu reflexively looked back at An Hupyeong, then turned his eyes back into the guest hall.

As if nothing had ever happened, those inside were drinking tea, folding clothes, yawning, and each attending to their own business.

Chapter 689: No Returns Accepted
--------------------------------
“What are you thinking so hard about! What’s with you today?”

Even after leaving the guest hall, Sachu was half-dazed.

At An Hupyeong’s displeased gaze, Sachu lowered his head.

“It’s nothing.”

“Find Teacher Yong immediately. Just what is he doing and where has he gone—bring him here at once!”

You have to exist to be found. Whether he was melted away and washed down Mount Hwagol, or buried somewhere in this rear garden.

Sachu hesitated. Ordinarily, he would have spoken honestly. That Teacher Yong had likely been killed by them. That they had even warned him to keep his mouth shut.

What would happen if he spoke honestly?

Wouldn’t An Hupyeong run straight to the Pavilion Lord? And if so, what would the Pavilion Lord do?

Teacher Yong was someone kept alive solely because of his martial arts, despite acting arrogant and willful. If he had died because of a pointless task, a furious order would come down.

And then? Would he mobilize every martial artist to go kill them?

Wouldn’t the conclusion be to leave them be until the tournament ended?

It was entirely possible. This tournament was far too important, being already widely known throughout the martial arts world.

Besides, they still didn’t know why the Cheonhwaru Lord had been invited as a judge.

So if the matter of holding someone responsible for Teacher Yong’s death was ultimately postponed?

Sachu thought of Geukak Soma and the Faceless Guests. If they were people who could kill Teacher Yong without leaving a trace, killing him would be child’s play. In the end, he would pay the price for ignoring the warning to keep his mouth shut.

Even if an order was given to capture them immediately, could they really catch them all?

A few experts going in wouldn’t stand a chance. But they wouldn’t wage war right before this great tournament either.

No matter what result came from speaking up.

‘I die.’

Had An Hupyeong been his caring superior as usual, it would not have driven him to the conclusion that he simply needed to look out for his own survival.

“I don’t think we’ll be able to find Teacher Yong.”

“What do you mean?”

It was not because the man was dead, but because he himself wanted to survive.

“I believe the Pavilion Lord must have intervened and sent him back.”

“Father?”

“You know Teacher Yong’s temperament, do you not? He said he would come here, so he couldn’t have failed to come. Yet since he is nowhere to be seen… who else could possibly stop him midway?”

It was a plausible enough guess that An Hupyeong nodded.

From the start, it had been odd that his father had summoned Teacher Yong in advance. He must have done so to prevent him from being used for such matters.

“Right, forget Teacher Yong. Find out who those masked fellows are. They are definitely not ordinary men.”

An Hupyeong started walking first, then added as if he had just remembered.

“Oh, and there’s a woman attending this tournament from the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult—find out where she’s staying.”

A beauty from the Demonic Cult? He had never enjoyed himself with a woman from the Demonic Cult before, so this was an opportunity.

Even amid all this, his lust and desire for women remained as ever.

“Yes, I understand.”

Sachu let out a sigh, then looked back at the guest hall far behind him.

In that moment, Sachu was startled. A gasp involuntarily escaped his lips.

The Faceless Guests were clinging to the guest hall windows, staring at him. There were so many faces pressed to the glass that he wondered if so many could even be visible there.

The sight was as bizarre and terrifying as the shushing gesture they had made earlier.

Sachu whipped around and quickened his steps to escape that place.

Find out who they are?

Ah! He truly did not want to know.

* * *

Geom Muguk and Ian took a room at an inn on the main street.

“One large room with two beds.”

Ian was surprised. She had naturally assumed they would take two rooms, but Geom Muguk had taken only one.

“We sleep in the same room until this tournament ends.”

Since he had confirmed that the opponent was an assassin, he had no intention of letting her sleep alone.

Feeling embarrassed for no reason, Ian spoke.

“You still don’t believe in my skills.”

“It’s not that. You’re supposed to protect me.”

The innkeeper smiled in satisfaction with an expression that said, ‘I know exactly what’s on your mind.’

“Let’s eat first. You’ve never tried Anhwe cuisine, have you?”

“No. Have you, Young Master?”

“Of course I have.”

Geom Muguk called the waiter and skillfully ordered dishes. He knew this region’s famous cuisine well, and he promptly ordered dishes that would suit Ian’s palate.

“Wait, when did you come here and try it?”

I was able to try it thanks to you. Because you saved me.

The inn was full of people who had come to attend the tournament. Ian’s gaze deepened as she looked at them.

Earlier, Geom Muguk had said that this street was full of beautiful assassins.

If so, among those eating beside them, there might very well be an assassin led by her.

“Young Master, you told me to protect you, but I failed to realize that woman was an assassin.”

Bodyguards were originally adept at detecting assassins. They had to be.

But she hadn’t been able to feel that the woman was an assassin at all. She had even felt jealous of her. Thinking back now, it was utterly humiliating. She would be ashamed of it for a long time to come.

“It’s because they are special assassins.”

“But you, Young Cult Lord, recognized her, didn’t you?”

That’s only because I knew in advance.

“Because I’m a special person.”

Ian laughed at Geom Muguk’s rare display of cockiness.

“They likely haven’t undergone the standard training that assassins usually undertake. That’s why you can’t sense the peculiar habits or unique ki flow inherent to assassins.”

Because she and the Gain Sect she leads are not ordinary assassins.

Ian nodded and said.

“They wouldn’t need assassin training. Because they possess weapons more powerful than anything else.”

The price of that captivating smile must be one’s life.

* * *

Three days later.

Ian’s expression was not good as she walked out of the building where the preliminary round had been held.

She walked out with her head lowered, trudging along.

“I knew this would happen. Me, the greatest beauty under heaven? I could tell as soon as you made a fuss before it even started! That’s why I said I didn’t want to do it!”

She waited for words of comfort, but Geom Muguk did not react.

When she raised her head, she saw that Geom Muguk was already crossing the training ground and walking out ahead.

“Huh?”

Ian urgently ran after Geom Muguk.

“Where are you going?”

“Where do you think? I need to prepare for the finals.”

“But I got eliminated!”

“Monkeys may fall from trees, and a Sword King may drop his sword, but you couldn’t possibly fall from a beauty pageant. And in the preliminaries, no less.”

A hint of joy crossed Ian’s face. She felt happier at those words from Geom Muguk than at passing the preliminaries.

“Don’t praise me so much! I’m really getting my hopes up.”

“That way I can tease you even more later.”

The preliminary round ended the moment she removed her veil.

She had hoped to see the Cheonhwaru Lord’s face, but the Lord was not present at the preliminary judging. Perhaps she would attend the finals judging.

“But where are we going now?”

“Didn’t I say we’re going to prepare for the finals?”

“Where to?”

The place Geom Muguk took her was a building located a short distance from the main street.

From the outside, it was a building where one couldn’t tell what was sold, but inside, it was a clothing shop.

It was different from the other shops on the main street. It dealt in very precious garments. They were clothes made by renowned artisans from various regions, and naturally, the prices were steep.

“How did you know about this place?”

“The Cheonhwaru Lord told me. She said to bring you here later and buy you clothes.”

“Ah, I see.”

Since the Cheonhwaru Lord couldn’t look after her directly, she seemed to have spoken to Geom Muguk about it in advance.

And she hadn’t told only Geom Muguk.

“Welcome.”

A middle-aged woman who appeared to be the shop owner came out to greet the two. Perhaps she had received word from the Cheonhwaru Lord as well, for her attitude toward them was very respectful.

“Please choose clothes to your liking.”

Ian looked around the interior. There were so many splendid and beautiful clothes that she was rather flustered.

“I’ve never been to a place like this before.”

As if Ian would be the only one? It was Geom Muguk’s first time in such a place as well.

“Please take your time.”

The middle-aged woman did not step away for the moment and watched to see what clothes she would choose.

Ian looked at the clothes. Since they were made by artisans, how could the quality be anything but excellent?

But having always worn only martial attire, she couldn’t easily judge which clothes were good or suited her.

Then Ian stopped before an eye-catching white palace dress. It didn’t have ornate patterns, but it was a garment that accentuated the figure more than what she was currently wearing.

“Could I really wear something like that?”

She was already blushing, perhaps embarrassed merely by imagining it.

“If it’s just once in a lifetime, wouldn’t it be fine to try it on at least once?”

Ian, who had hesitated briefly, made her decision. Geom Muguk’s words had helped her decide.

“I’ll take that one.”

Then the middle-aged woman smiled.

“You’ve chosen well. It’s a garment the artisan made stitch by stitch with her own hands.”

It was Ian’s first time with such expensive clothing. She wouldn’t even be able to draw her sword while wearing it for fear of tearing it somewhere!

Just then, someone spoke from behind.

“I’ll buy that dress.”

She turned to see a woman entering the place.

To her surprise, it was Cha Iran.

Now she knew her opponent was an assassin, but Ian treated her naturally.
