An Cheonggwang’s heart pounded wildly. If the leader of the Sado Alliance had come knowing something, he was a dead man. Never in his life had he been this nervous.

Baek Jagang lifted him up from his bow.

Then he quietly gazed at An Cheonggwang before suddenly asking,

“Do you intend to kill me with this contest?”

“!”

So shocked by the direct question, An Cheonggwang’s eyes widened.

“No. What do you mean by that?”

His voice trembled. An Cheonggwang protested again as though wronged.

“Absolutely not!”

In that instant, goosebumps rose along Baek Jagang’s ears.

He realized An Cheonggwang was lying, but instead Baek Jagang laughed loudly and said,

“I mean there are so many peerless beauties that my heart feels as if it might burst.”

An Cheonggwang, whose heart had dropped, finally realized Baek Jagang had been joking.

“My heart nearly burst first.”

An Cheonggwang smiled in relief. He was still afraid, unsure whether it had been a joke or whether Baek Jagang had asked because he knew something, but he could not discern his true intent from Baek Jagang’s expression.

“As it happens, the finals are being held today, so please enjoy them at your leisure. Now, please come inside first.”

They walked together toward the main hall, conversing as they went.

“Rather than me, that boy Sain will be the one who enjoys it.”

“Has the Young Alliance Leader come with you as well?”

When Baek Jagang nodded, An Cheonggwang looked around.

“Where is the Young Alliance Leader?”

“He said he would just take a quick look and went to watch the contest first. The boy did not even offer his greetings.”

In truth, if one examined it closely, it was not rude. Not only Bisain, but also Geom Mugeuk, whom he had gone to meet, held a far higher position than the Gang Leader.

“Is he not still at the height of youth? My son is the same.”

An Cheonggwang ordered the subordinate following him to go find his son.

“Go at once, inform him that the Alliance Leader has arrived, and bring the Young Gang Leader here.”

Then he said to Baek Jagang,

“My son has always admired the Young Alliance Leader. Whenever he opened his mouth, he would say he would become like the Young Alliance Leader.”

At that, Baek Jagang also said to a guard,

“Go find Sain. We should let the youngsters greet each other.”

An Cheonggwang smiled pleasantly and said,

“My son will truly be delighted.”

Chapter 695: You Said This Was Where I’d Die?
---------------------------------------------
An Hupyeong arrived at the main hall with a pounding heart.

The unfamiliar martial artists surrounding the building from the entrance were the guards of the Demon Alliance Lord. Their oppressive presence made the Shinhwabang martial artists look like children—he couldn’t dare meet their eyes.

‘Should I have changed into new clothes?’

Even though he went around looking down on everyone in the world, he had to make a good impression on the Demon Alliance Lord and the Young Alliance Lord. If he made a bad impression on them while belonging to the Demonic faction, the extermination of his clan would happen in an instant.

An Hupyeong took a deep breath and entered.

The Demon Alliance Lord was sitting in the honorary seat where his father always sat.

His father was standing a short distance away, and it was the first time he had seen his father standing respectfully before someone with both hands clasped together.

‘So that’s the Demon Alliance Lord.’

Unable to dare meet his gaze, An Hupyeong slightly lowered his head and walked forward. He practiced smiling while keeping his head lowered. Having always gone around scowling, trying to smile felt extremely awkward.

‘I have to make a good impression.’

An Hupyeong walked over with a trembling heart and greeted respectfully.

“It is an honor to meet the Alliance Lord whom I have always respected.”

“Nice to meet you.”

Baek Jagang roughly knew what kind of person An Hupyeong was.

Since he was visiting Shinhwabang, he had naturally received basic information about the Hall Lord’s family.

A wild successor who liked alcohol and women. Just looking at this An Hupyeong, the future of Shinhwabang was dark.

Of course, the moment An Cheonggwang joined hands with the forces behind him, it had become darkness as black as pitch, rather than merely dark.

An Cheonggwang sent a voice transmission to his son.

—Don’t make a mistake and do well!

—Don’t worry, Father!

Just then, the door opened and someone entered.

An Hupyeong turned his head to look at the entrance and was startled.

‘Why is that guy here?’

The person who entered was none other than Bisain.

An Hupyeong quickly spoke to Baek Jagang and his father.

“Excuse me for a moment.”

An Hupyeong ran toward Bisain, who was walking this way.

An Hupyeong stopped Bisain in his tracks, then whispered with a scowl.

“Do you even know where this is, following me here?”

He had probably entered by saying at the entrance that the Young Hall Lord had called for him.

That An Hupyeong would think the Alliance Lord’s guards would just let anyone into a place where the Alliance Lord was present simply by saying he was here to see him—this alone showed how self-centered and thoughtless he was.

“Hurry up and leave. Get out.”

It was an extremely nervous whisper. An Hupyeong’s nerves were on edge. If he ruined this occasion, it would truly be the end.

“Do you want to die?”

With his sole determination to make a good impression on the Demon Alliance Lord, even Bisain’s frightening face wasn’t scary right now.

Just as Sachu had done earlier, the possibility that Bisain might be the Young Alliance Lord didn’t exist at all in An Hupyeong’s head.

Could the Demon Alliance’s Young Alliance Lord have asked Ian’s guard if many beauties had arrived? How could that be?

An Hupyeong understood this frightening-looking fellow. Right, how could he refuse when the Shinhwabang’s Young Hall Lord told him to come down? And he was being offered double the money.

Then Bisain brought up what An Hupyeong had said to him earlier.

“Didn’t I say I would tell the person I serve directly?”

It was the first time he had heard Bisain’s voice. It was a voice as unpleasant to hear as his face was to look at, but now was not the time to point that out.

“The person I serve is right over there, so speak to him directly.”

An Hupyeong’s gaze followed Bisain’s. Baek Jagang was there.

This guy is insulting the Demon Alliance Lord? This is an opportunity. The perfect opportunity to make a good impression on the Demon Alliance Lord.

‘I couldn’t stand by and endure it as this guy who doesn’t know heaven and earth was trying to send him away nicely, because he insulted the Alliance Lord.’

In that brief moment, he racked his brains.

“You madman!”

An Hupyeong shouted toward the door.

“Sachu! Drag this bastard out right now!”

An Cheonggwang, who had been watching, came running in alarm.

Naturally, An Cheonggwang had met Bisain before. Seeing Bisain enter and his son run toward him, he thought they had already exchanged greetings at the martial arts tournament. Anyway, his son was a fellow who looked out for his own interests faster than anyone.

Right, he had been thinking that it was fortunate his son had at least that much sense.

Did he just tell him to drag this bastard out in front of the Young Alliance Lord?

An Hupyeong thought his father had turned deathly pale and rushed out because of that merciless face. The Alliance Lord is watching; why aren’t you kicking him out quickly?

He had to take care of this himself before Father came and resolved it.

The moment An Hupyeong raised his hand high to strike Bisain’s cheek—

Thwack!

An Cheonggwang, who had arrived just in the nick of time, grabbed his wrist. Of course, even if he hadn’t caught it, An Hupyeong probably wouldn’t have been able to land the hit.

Too shocked, An Cheonggwang looked at his son, his face drained of color.

An Hupyeong said in a loud voice.

“It seems this guy who doesn’t know his place chased after me saying he wanted to join under me. Yet he insulted the Alliance Lord.”

Then Baek Jagang, who was far away, asked.

“What insult?”

“He said that if I wanted to hire him, I needed to get the Alliance Lord’s permission.”

Baek Jagang said with a smile.

“That’s not incorrect.”

Just as An Hupyeong blinked his eyes, not knowing what it meant—

Smack!

An Cheonggwang ruthlessly struck his son’s cheek. Setting aside the pain, An Hupyeong was too surprised.

“Father?”

“What kind of rudeness is this to the Young Alliance Lord? Why aren’t you kneeling and apologizing immediately?”

An Hupyeong’s eyes blinked.

‘Young Alliance Lord? What Young Alliance Lord?’

As he stood there still blankly, another slap flew.

Smack! Smack! Smack!

Emotion filled An Cheonggwang’s hand as he ruthlessly struck his son’s cheek. A son who did all sorts of pathetic things, and now was committing such insane acts.

He should have beaten him up like this from the start.

If this matter caused problems and the Demon Alliance Lord left? He might really beat his son to death.

At that time, Baek Jagang’s voice transmission flew to Bisain.

—So this is the Young Cult Leader’s handiwork.

Because his disciple wasn’t the type to hide his identity and create such a situation. If there was someone who needed a beating, he would have already half-killed them directly.

—So it’s a warning to properly educate his child?

Of course, Geom Mugeuk probably hadn’t anticipated this situation, but this was the result nonetheless.

—If I had been alone, that guy would have come here as a corpse.

At Bisain’s voice transmission, a faint smile formed on Baek Jagang’s lips. Even without seeing it, he could roughly imagine the situation.

All the emotions that had built up over the years poured out now.

Smack! Smack! Crack! Smack!

Thinking he had been hit enough, Bisain stepped forward.

“Please stop now.”

Then An Cheonggwang stopped the beating.

An Hupyeong, his face swollen red, spoke with a sob.

“……Father.”

“You dared to curse at the Young Alliance Lord and raised your hand to strike him? You deserve to die. I should cut off that hand first.”

“Please spare me! Father!”

“Won’t you apologize immediately?”

An Hupyeong knelt and begged.

“I failed to recognize a noble person and committed a crime worthy of death! I was wrong! Please spare me!”

Bisain did not rebuke him.

He could have completely destroyed An Hupyeong further here, but he didn’t.

He already knew An Hupyeong was a wild, arrogant wretch, and scolding and reprimanding him further here wouldn’t change his nature. Rather, he would only harbor more resentment inside.

Moreover, since he had come to stop a conspiracy to assassinate his master, there was no need to make a grudge against An Hupyeong.

Bisain lifted An Hupyeong up and spoke in a kind tone.

“I’m sorry. I failed to reveal my identity in time.”

“No, Young Alliance Lord! Thank you for forgiving me.”

But as Bisain had expected, people don’t change in an instant. Far from reflecting, An Hupyeong was resenting someone.

The target of resentment should have been his father or Bisain, but he resented an absurd person.

‘This is all because of that bastard!’

Ian's guard bastard! He had misunderstood because he saw the Young Alliance Lord talking with that guy.

‘I’ll somehow make it up to the Young Alliance Lord, and as for that bastard, I’ll get him!’

How would this moment be any different from his past life of always taking revenge on those weaker than himself?

An Cheonggwang went before the Alliance Lord, bowed his head, and apologized.

“I am sorry for showing such a disgraceful sight.”

Baek Jagang said in a kind tone.

“It was something that happened due to a misunderstanding, so do not mind it.”

Then a cheer was heard from outside. It seemed the main tournament was about to begin.

An Cheonggwang stepped forward to dispel the awkward atmosphere.

“It seems the main round will begin soon. I shall escort you to the VIP seats.”

Baek Jagang rose from his seat and concluded the situation.

“Keeping the ladies waiting is not polite.”

* * *

The waiting room was filled with women.

Ian was quietly surveying her surroundings from a corner of the waiting room.

She was finding the assassins of the Beauty Sect among the participants. She was not trying to find the assassins.

To find an assassin, one must not try to find an assassin. That was the core principle.

There were a few women who gave her a feeling. Literally just a feeling.

Women found by the martial artist Ian, not the bodyguard Ian.

Then Chairan entered the waiting room.

After glancing around the interior once, she walked to where Ian was.

Ian didn’t look at her; she looked at the women she had found. Who reacted to her appearance. Or rather, who didn’t react.

Chairan walked right up to Ian.

“What to do? This young lady’s guard seems to have already fallen for me.”

“What do you mean?”

“We’ve arranged a drinking party to celebrate advancing to the finals. Just the two of us, intimately.”

She had meant to include Ian in the drinking, but Chairan said “the two of us.”

Ian did not rise to her provocation. Geom Mugeuk would not have done such a thing, and even if he had, there would be a reason.

“He won’t be able to drink without my permission.”

Chairan asked provocatively to Ian, who tilted her head.

“Is that confidence? Or arrogance?”

“I know him well. That person.”

“Bodyguards who know their master well are common, but a master who knows their bodyguard well? That’s a precious relationship.”

Chairan sat down beside Ian.

Even then, Ian observed how the women around them were reacting.

That was originally Ian’s purpose for participating in the tournament. She might be able to save someone’s life on their side by identifying someone this way.

Chairan suddenly spoke.

“You’ve finally taken up the treasured sword.”

In reality, Ian was wearing a treasured sword, but this remark now meant that her makeup was pretty.

Of course, Chairan would be able to recognize the skill of the woman who did the makeup much better than Ian herself.

“It’s because my opponent is who she is. Be careful, I become a different person when I wear makeup.”

“This young lady is scarier without makeup. Pure beauty.”

“You’re trying to take me down somehow.”

The two faced each other and smiled.

Right, you should be afraid. Of all those training hours spent sweating without even a single application of makeup.

Then the waiting room door opened and a martial artist spoke loudly.

“The main round begins now!”

Cheers of the gathered heroes were heard from outside.

* * *

Finally, it was Ian’s turn.

Wearing a snowy white palace gown with beautiful makeup, she slowly climbed the stairs.

When she stepped onto the stage, she could see the countless gathered heroes.

Cheers that seemed ready to burst.

She had thought her heart would beat as if it would burst from embarrassment when she came up here. That’s why she had gone up the mountain from dawn and swung her sword.

But once she actually stepped onto the stage, she was less nervous than she thought.

‘Maybe I’m unexpectedly the type who does well in actual combat.’

Ian slowly moved her steps along the designated path.

And she saw.

The figure of Geom Mugeuk looking at her among the countless people. He was visible precisely, as if by a miracle.

Geom Mugeuk revealed no presence at all, yet she found him.

Among these countless people, only Geom Mugeuk’s figure was visible. Everyone around turned black and white, and only Geom Mugeuk remained in color.

Geom Mugeuk was smiling at her.

Ian suddenly thought.

So this journey was a time to confirm my feelings for the Young Cult Leader! I had even felt jealousy for the first time. I had liked the Young Cult Leader since he was little, but the emotion I learned of this time was deeper than before.

Ian moved her steps confidently.

Standing in the center of the stage, she could see the specially prepared judges’ seats straight ahead.

Among the three judges, she saw the figure of the Lord of Cheonhwaru. Their gazes met.

I am here, and the Lord of Cheonhwaru is there, each carrying out our tasks.

‘From this place where I stand, I won’t let the Young Cult Leader be stabbed by Lady Soma’s dagger.’

The two women who sought to protect their man communed through their eyes.

Nothing happened from the time she went up on stage until she came down.

Before coming down from the stage, she looked at Geom Mugeuk one last time.

It seemed as if Geom Mugeuk’s mouth was moving, saying something.

Not bad for an ugly duckling?

Wasn’t that what he said? She smiled to herself and descended the stage.

Someone approached Geom Mugeuk as he watched Ian descend the stage.

“It’s been a while.”

Surprisingly, it was the Demon Alliance Lord, Baek Jagang. He was wearing a bamboo hat, so no one could tell he was the Demon Alliance Lord.

He had told An Cheonggwang that he wanted to watch quietly today, and had mixed in among the gathered heroes.

The two conversed while looking at the stage straight ahead. Geom Mugeuk released his energy to create an invisible barrier around them so his voice wouldn’t be heard by the person next to him.

“I told you not to come. Why did you come?”

“I came to see you.”

“Not to see the beauties?”

“Why not? I came to see you and the beauties.”

Geom Mugeuk turned to look at Baek Jagang and spoke.

“Honestly, I would have been disappointed if you hadn’t come.”

“Isn’t this the place where I die?”

The two faced each other and laughed. In truth, Geom Mugeuk had wanted to see Baek Jagang. He would never know how charming those small eyes looked in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes right now.

“And there she comes now. That woman over there is the assassin who intends to kill the Alliance Lord.”

The woman who stepped on stage was none other than Chairan.

At her appearance, the gathered heroes blazed with hot passion, but conversely, Baek Jagang grew cold and calm.

“Tell me. The reason I mustn’t jump onto that stage right now and kill that woman. I am not someone who waits and watches someone who tries to kill me.”

He was not someone who toyed with his enemies. He was someone who killed his enemies with all his might. Because he was an Alliance Lord who always wore martial robes.

“If there is no reason I can accept……”

Baek Jagang’s small eyes became even smaller. That point shone with intensity, as if it were the source of all killing intent in the world.

“I will kill her right here and now.”

Chapter 696: Who Is First?
--------------------------
Geom Mugeuk could tell that the Sado Alliance Leader was serious.

He wanted to fly up to that stage this instant and kill Cha Iran.

She was striding across the stage, drawing the eyes of the assembled heroes. A person who ought to have lived the most secretive of lives was smiling brightly at the world.

All for the sake of killing this Sado Alliance Leader, for the sake of becoming the greatest beauty under heaven.

An astonishing sentence slipped from Geom Mugeuk’s lips.

“Kill her.”

“!”

When Geom Mugeuk told him to kill her, Baek Jagang’s eyes widened slightly. It was an unexpected answer.

“Kill her?”

“Yes. Kill her.”

Baek Jagang could tell as well. Those words were not a lie. The hairs beside his ears did not stand on end.

Baek Jagang’s gaze turned toward Cha Iran on the stage. She stood at the center, smiling radiantly toward the judges’ seats. Soon, she would be coming down from the stage.

The intense killing intent he could feel beside him was drawn taut, ready to shoot toward the stage at any moment.

How many moves could an assassin exposed to the world endure against the Sado Alliance Leader?

Baek Jagang would step on her corpse and say this.

I am the Sado Alliance Leader. Congratulations on holding the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven Competition.

At the Sado Alliance Leader’s surprise appearance, all the unorthodox martial artists would go wild. They would cheer without asking why he had killed her. Because he was a man who held the absolute faith and support of the unorthodox factions.

Sssut.

The pinpoints in his eyes where the killing intent had gathered widened, and that fierce killing intent vanished.

In a calm voice, Baek Jagang revealed why he had given up.

“If she were a woman who had to be killed, you would have killed her before I arrived.”

At that deep trust in him, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly.

Now it was Geom Mugeuk’s turn to answer that trust.

“Yes. There was a reason I did not kill her.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the stage. Cha Iran was descending from it amid cheers.

“Would they truly have entrusted such an enormous task to her alone? There is probably someone else behind her.”

Baek Jagang nodded. He understood that Geom Mugeuk intended to draw out whoever was behind her and dispose of them together. There might be another someone somewhere among these gathered heroes.

“There is an even greater reason than that. Why did they target you, Alliance Leader? Even if the surprise attack succeeded, Sain would be in a position to inherit the Alliance Leader’s seat.”

In truth, that was also why Baek Jagang had not killed her. He wanted to find out the reason even if he killed her.

“It would not be so bad to die at the hands of the greatest beauty under heaven.”

It was a joke that carried the meaning that he would wait instead of killing her first.

As Baek Jagang was about to leave, he asked one final question.

“What were you planning to do if I had truly killed that woman?”

Geom Mugeuk thought for a moment, then spoke calmly.

“If you killed her, then I would have found another way accordingly.”

Looking at Baek Jagang, he added,

“What would there be to worry about when you are here, Alliance Leader?”

Even at those words, the hairs beside his ears did not stand on end. Baek Jagang could tell that Geom Mugeuk truly believed in him. He was a man who spoke so much, and yet the times he made the hairs by Baek Jagang’s ears rise could be counted on one hand.

Baek Jagang pulled his bamboo hat low and vanished among the people.

No incident occurred until the main stage ended.

After the competition ended, the eight beauties who would advance to the finals to be held three days later were announced.

Along with seven beauties, including Cha Iran.

Ian advanced to the finals.

There was no upset.

* * *

With his face swollen like a drum, An Hupyeong sat near the front of the stage.

Even in this state, he had come out to watch the competition. He had to make a show of telling Ian he had seen it, and he was also curious to see what other pretty beauties there were.

He paid no attention whatsoever to the gazes around him.

I am the young master of Shinhwa Gang. Whatever state I’m in, I’m better than you, aren’t I? Can you look down on me? You can’t, can you? He had lived with that mindset.

Of course, right now his mood was at its worst.

Earlier, he had been too shocked and flustered to think straight, but as time passed, it hurt terribly and felt unbearably unfair.

He thought that if he looked at beauties, his mood might improve, but hearing the heated excitement and cheers only made him sink lower.

Yes, when one felt miserable, one should not look at happy things, but at things more miserable than oneself.

Just then, someone held something out to him. When he turned his head, Sachu was offering him ice wrapped in cloth.

“Press this to your face.”

“Forget it!”

He reacted irritably, but Sachu kept holding out the pouch.

“I said forget it! Are you mocking me?”

An Hupyeong threw the pouch to the floor.

Because he had shouted, his face hurt.

“Damn it, that hurts.”

Sachu looked at An Hupyeong without a word.

He thought it served him right, and yet seeing him in such a state still did not feel good.

“How much of my money is at the money house?”

“About twelve hundred nyang.”

“That’s all?”

Considering how extravagantly he usually spent, it was fortunate even that much remained.

“What kind of gift can I buy with that?”

“What gift do you mean?”

“What do you think? I’m buying something for the Young Alliance Leader. I have to put him in a better mood, don’t I?”

He had to do something. He had cursed at the Young Alliance Leader and tried to hit him, so it was safe to say he had been marked properly.

“It will be difficult to buy something the Young Alliance Leader would like with that money.”

“Damn it!”

Just then, cheers erupted.

When he raised his head and looked at the stage, Cha Iran was stepping onto it.

“That woman?”

She was the woman who had been talking with Ian’s guard. Strangely, she had been someone he found difficult to say even a single word to.

The moment he saw her overwhelming beauty, a thought flashed through An Hupyeong’s mind.

“Yes, that’s it! I have to find that guy!”

An Hupyeong sprang to his feet and ran into the crowd of heroes.

Wondering what else he was going to cause, Sachu sighed and followed after him.

* * *

After wandering and wandering, the person An Hupyeong finally found was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

“What happened to your face for it to end up like that?”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk could guess the reason. If only he had come to his senses after being beaten.

“That’s none of your business! That person who spoke to you earlier.”

“You mean the frightening-looking man?”

“Did he really ask whether many beauties had come?”

“Yes. He said it had been hard to run here before it was too late.”

An Hupyeong’s eyes gleamed.

“You said you were drinking tonight, didn’t you? Where are you going with the young ladies?”

“I haven’t decided yet. If we go, I was thinking of renting a room at a nearby tavern.”

“Come to Hwamyeonggwan! I’ll rent out the whole place.”

Hwamyeonggwan was the largest and most expensive tavern in the marketplace.

An Hupyeong had decided to make up for this mistake by introducing Cha Iran to Bi Sain.

Yes, if he was fond of women, this arrangement would surely work.

When Geom Mugeuk looked troubled, An Hupyeong frowned.

“An important guest will be coming as well.”

“Who do you mean?”

Since he was a guard after all, if An Hupyeong did not tell him who it was, he would never bring Ian and Cha Iran.

An Hupyeong held up two fingers.

“The person ranked second among the rising talents of the later generation in the murim.”

“Who is that?”

“The Young Alliance Leader of the Sado Alliance.”

It was also a chance to show Ian just how remarkable a person he associated with. He would make a good impression on the Young Alliance Leader and on Ian as well.

He was worried that Bi Sain might take a liking to Ian.

Well, if that happened, there was nothing he could do. At that point, he would switch to Cha Iran. Right now, making a good impression on the Young Alliance Leader was more important.

“All you have to do is bring them. I’ll let you eat your fill of expensive liquor and dishes you’ve never tasted in your life.”

An Hupyeong threatened in a low voice.

“If you don’t come, I’ll really tear that mouth of yours open!”

At the words “tear your mouth open,” Sachu, who was behind An Hupyeong, flinched.

His eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s.

Sachu covered his own mouth with his palm. It was a sign that he had absolutely said nothing.

As An Hupyeong turned away, Geom Mugeuk asked him,

“Then who is ranked first among the later-generation talents?”

As if asking how he could not know that, An Hupyeong strode away and answered,

“You, a demonic martial artist, should know better than anyone.”

* * *

A place was prepared in the center of Hwamyeonggwan.

All manner of delicacies from mountain and sea were laid out, and fine liquor was prepared.

“If you make a mistake, know that you’ll all die today!”

At An Hupyeong’s shout, the cooks and servers working at Hwamyeonggwan grew tense.

Then An Hupyeong caught sight of Sachu standing there blankly.

An Hupyeong grabbed Sachu’s shoulders from behind and shook him.

“What is it now? What’s wrong?”

“N-nothing.”

“Are you scared because the Young Alliance Leader is coming? You just need to stand there quietly.”

In truth, Sachu’s heart was trembling not because of the Young Alliance Leader, but because he was going to meet the Sado Thirteen Wolves up close. Why had there been so many shocking things happening lately?

Just then, Bi Sain entered.

An Hupyeong ran over and greeted him.

“You’ve arrived?”

The Sado Thirteen Wolves entered behind Bi Sain. They scattered and took up positions around the room. They were places from which they could respond immediately if anything happened to Bi Sain.

After greeting Bi Sain politely, Sachu once again cupped his fist to the Sado Thirteen Wolves in greeting. Once in each direction. Since people did not usually go so far in offering greetings, the First Wolf looked at him closely.

“Have the guests who are coming not arrived yet?”

At Bi Sain’s question after he took his seat, An Hupyeong quickly answered,

“They should be on their way now.”

An Hupyeong looked at Sachu and was about to signal with his eyes for him to go and check.

But Sachu was looking at the Sado Thirteen Wolves and not at him.

“Good grief, that one’s lost his head again, isn’t he?”

Sachu was feeling both emotion and sorrow.

They were people he had truly liked and admired since he was young.

The five characters of “Sado Thirteen Wolves” had remained five characters to him as well.

An unfulfilled dream.

If I had tried harder back then, could I be standing there too?

Then Geom Mugeuk stepped inside.

An Hupyeong, who had been delighted, flinched in surprise. Only Geom Mugeuk and Ian had come.

An Hupyeong ran over to Geom Mugeuk and asked,

“Where is Lady Cha?”

“We agreed to meet here.”

“You told her she must come, didn’t you?”

“She will come.”

It was something he could have said after exchanging greetings first. But his desire to introduce Cha Iran to Bi Sain came first.

The next thing that came first was this introduction.

“This person here is the Young Alliance Leader of the Sado Alliance.”

An Hupyeong puffed out his chest. Lady Ian, I am a man who associates with the Young Alliance Leader of the Sado Alliance.

“This is Lady Ian, who advanced to the finals this time.”

This time, he looked at Bi Sain and put strength into his voice. I am a man who mingles with beauties like this!

“Now, please sit.”

Everyone sat down, and Geom Mugeuk promptly took a seat as well.

“Do you know what kind of gathering this is? How dare a mere guard butt in?”

As if aggrieved, Geom Mugeuk said,

“You said you would feed me liquor and dishes I’d never tasted in my life. You said the person ranked second among the later-generation talents was coming.”

“Has this bastard gone mad?”

Bi Sain asked in a low voice,

“Who is ranked first?”

An Hupyeong panicked and waved his hands.

“This fellow is spouting nonsense!”

Watching that commotion, Sachu frowned. More than any other mistake, only those words reached his ears.

The Sado Thirteen Wolves were standing proudly right there, and yet he had so thoughtlessly said, “a mere guard.”

As if apologizing on his behalf, Sachu bowed his head slightly toward the First Wolf, who stood a little distance away from him.

Then the First Wolf’s voice transmission flew to Sachu.

—Are your younger siblings well?

Sachu stared at the First Wolf in shock. What did that mean?

In that instant, Sachu’s eyes flew wide open in astonishment. In a trembling voice, he sent a voice transmission.

—You remember me.

That day from long ago came to mind.

The days when he had been so young and green, camping out at the entrance of the Sado Alliance just to catch even a glimpse of the Sado Thirteen Wolves.

And on the day he finally met them, Sachu had mustered the greatest courage of his entire life.

He had blocked the path of the Sado Thirteen Wolves. And then he had asked what no one around him had been able to tell him.

“How can I become one of the Sado Thirteen Wolves?”

The one who had stepped forward then and listened to him was this very First Wolf.

“I heard that the greatest martial artists of the Sado Alliance are the Sado Thirteen Wolves. I want to become the greatest martial artist and feed my younger siblings until they are full.”

They had left without saying a word, but astonishingly, the First Wolf remembered him from that day.

—How do you remember me?

—Because you are the only person who ever blocked our path and survived.

Sachu was truly grateful and moved that he remembered him.

But precisely because of that, he could no longer meet the First Wolf’s eyes and lowered his head.

He had once dreamed such a grand dream.

Because now he was reduced to living his life following that scoundrel An Hupyeong around, cleaning up after his messes.

Just then, Ilrang slowly walked over as if keeping watch over their surroundings, and as she passed Sachu, who had his head hanging low, she gave his shoulder a light pat.

It was a gesture more heartening than any words in the world.

Sachu made up his mind.

This garment, the one Ilrang had patted on the shoulder—he would never wash it, but preserve it carefully for the rest of his life.

It was at that very moment.

Grab.

From behind, An Hupyeong seized his shoulder and said,

“Go to the kitchen and see to it yourself. Make sure there are no mistakes.”

Of all places, he had grabbed the very spot Ilrang had touched.

“Why grab me when you could just say it!”

Sachu shouted in a burst of anger. An Hupyeong stared at him in surprise. Everyone’s eyes turned to him.

“Why on earth are you doing this!”

It was the first time Sachu had ever shouted at An Hupyeong. And it was also a cry directed at himself.

His bewilderment lasted only a moment before An Hupyeong asked coldly,

“What’s gotten into you?”

At that moment, over An Hupyeong’s shoulder, Sachu caught sight of Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk raised his hand and made a gesture of covering his mouth, exactly as Sachu had done to him earlier.

Sachu understood. It meant that he should say nothing now.

Until now, everyone had always told him to be quiet.

Shh! They had said they would tear his mouth open if he did not keep quiet.

But the way that mouth was being covered now was different. This was an action being done for his sake.

Just let it pass quietly. Now was not the time. He could feel that intent.

“I’m sorry. I think I must have lost my mind.”

Sachu bowed his head and ran to the kitchen.

Now was not the time to press him, so An Hupyeong turned back to everyone and said,

“This is how difficult it is to manage subordinates these days. You all understand, don’t you?”

Then he laughed aloud.

But no one laughed along with him. In fact, no one was even looking at him.

The Thirteen Sado Wolves each had their gazes fixed straight ahead, while Ian and Bisain were talking about something between themselves.

And Geom Mugeuk was watching Sachu as he walked toward the kitchen.

‘Where is everyone looking?’

In that brief moment, he felt as though he had been cast aside all alone. He had not felt like this even when his father had beaten him.

Just then, someone behind him said,

“Please move aside.”

When An Hupyeong turned around, Cha Iran was standing there. At last, the woman who would save him had appeared.

“Oh! Lady Cha has finally arrived.”

When he greeted her warmly, Cha Iran tilted her head.

“And who might you be?”

“I am An Hupyeong, the young leader of Sinhwabang.”

Instead of introducing herself, Cha Iran smiled sweetly.

“Your esteemed face is quite swollen.”

Cha Iran walked past him.

Bisain and Ian were there as well, but her gaze was fixed only on Geom Mugeuk.

“There are quite a lot of guests, aren’t there? I told you we should meet cozily, just the two of us.”

Just as she reached the place where everyone was seated.

Hum.

The secret coffer inside Geom Mugeuk’s robe reacted faintly.

Cha Iran smiled bewitchingly and said,

“Who knows? Something good might happen when it’s just the two of us.”

Chapter 697: Three Cups of Penalty Wine If You Can't Answer
-----------------------------------------------------------
Cha Iran had arrived, and the secret coffer reacted?

‘Could she possibly have a bead?’

Geom Mugeuk was inwardly startled. Until now, whenever he had met her, it had never reacted once. Today was the first time.

Geom Mugeuk asked the secret coffer in his breast.

‘Do you perhaps absorb things other than beads? Are you the sort of fellow who picks up and eats anything when you’re hungry?’

Of course, Geom Mugeuk knew very well that was not the case. He had clearly seen the symbols of the secret coffer and the beads painted on the mural.

In the end, it meant she had brought a bead.

Of the six beads, only two remained. The red bead, Jeokjeong, and the purple bead, Jajeong.

‘One of those two beads is with Cha Iran?’

Geom Mugeuk’s heart pounded.

He had never once considered that someone might possess one of the beads the secret coffer absorbed. After all, until now, the secret coffer had been stored as though hidden in the most secret of places.

Was it because the secret coffer had reacted?

Who knows? Perhaps something nice will happen when it’s just the two of us.

Even the words she had spoken while walking over a little earlier sounded meaningful now.

Could she know that he had absorbed the energy of the beads? Or was she carrying one by chance for some other purpose, without knowing?

If she had brought it knowingly?

If you can, kill me and take it. Was that what she was thinking?

What would she do if I really did?

Cha Iran naturally sat down beside Geom Mugeuk. Normally, the reaction should have grown stronger, but this time it was different from usual. The moment she came close, the reaction stopped dead.

“Aren’t you going to congratulate me? I made it to the finals.”

“Congratulations.”

“It feels nice to be congratulated, even if I had to prod you in the ribs for it.”

An Hupyeong came over to them and quickly introduced Bi Sain.

“Miss Cha, this is the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance.”

Cha Iran looked at Bi Sain and put on an expression of feigned surprise.

At that reaction, An Hupyeong was inwardly pleased and subtly placed himself on the same level as Bi Sain.

‘Surprised, aren’t you? First you meet the young leader of Shinhwa Gang, and now even the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance?’

Bi Sain was thinking along similar lines.

‘Well? It’s your first time seeing such a beautiful woman, isn’t it?’

Cha Iran smiled bewitchingly and greeted Bi Sain.

“I’m Cha Iran.”

She had her own way of dealing with men by nature. Even without deliberately trying to seduce them, the look in her eyes and the smile she wore when greeting someone made the other party restless and drew them in completely.

Bi Sain silently gazed at her.

Through the letter Geom Mugeuk had sent earlier, he knew this woman intended to kill his master. Yet he revealed no hostility whatsoever.

The fact that Geom Mugeuk had naturally arranged a seat like this meant he trusted him. If he was to become someone who would one day lead the Sado Alliance, he ought to be able to control his emotions to this extent.

Wasn’t that woman, who was about to kill his master, facing him without so much as a change in expression?

“I am Bi Sain.”

At Bi Sain’s belated reply, An Hupyeong was inwardly delighted. He thought Bi Sain had been so captivated by her that his greeting had come late. Naturally, he began to show off.

“For this special gathering today, I rented out this entire place!”

No matter what, he had to be the main figure who had arranged today’s meeting.

“I was determined to introduce Miss Cha to the young alliance leader.”

“To me?”

At Bi Sain’s questioning gaze, An Hupyeong smiled and fawned on him.

“I thought the two of you would suit each other very well.”

He laughed heartily as he said it, but Bi Sain’s reaction was not what he had expected.

“I think this gathering is inappropriate.”

An Hupyeong asked in surprise.

“What do you mean?”

“Is it acceptable for these young ladies to be present at a seat where you are?”

He meant that someone from the tournament’s organizing side should not privately meet with women who had advanced to the finals.

‘What kind of stifling nonsense is that from someone who will become the supreme figure of the evil path!’

That was what he truly wanted to say, but—

“This is a special seat with you present, young alliance leader, so everyone will understand.”

“Are you using me as an excuse?”

“No, absolutely not. That was never my intention.”

Bi Sain did not bother to hide his thoughts or feelings. As though he did not know her identity, he behaved as he usually did. He revealed his natural self to Cha Iran.

She was no ordinary woman. She had come alone to a place where both he and Geom Mugeuk were present. Perhaps she believed they did not know her identity, but even so, she was not ordinarily bold.

Cha Iran looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“Thank you for introducing me to such an esteemed person, but my heart belongs to only one man.”

There was an expression she showed only when looking at Geom Mugeuk.

An Hupyeong could not understand it. What on earth was so good about that guard?

“It seems that guard has some charm I don’t know about.”

Whatever it was, he intended to tear it down somehow.

“You can’t see it? I can see a great deal.”

Cha Iran brought her face close to Geom Mugeuk’s. So close that if she moved just a little nearer, they would touch.

Geom Mugeuk did not retreat and faced her gaze.

“This man’s eyes are filled with death, so how can they be so clear?”

She spoke as if that aura of death was visible to her eyes, but Geom Mugeuk said nothing.

The one who spoke in Geom Mugeuk’s stead was Yi An.

“I have never once felt death in those eyes.”

When Cha Iran slowly turned her head toward her, Yi An added,

“One’s own image is bound to be reflected in another person’s eyes.”

It meant that death, in the end, originated from Cha Iran herself.

A strange smile formed on Cha Iran’s face.

“Miss Yi truly likes this man, doesn’t she?”

“I’m not foolish enough to hate my guard.”

She meant that one could hardly dislike the person who protected one’s life.

An Hupyeong’s expression stiffened as he listened. It sounded as though those words were directed at him, who had clashed with Sachu just a little while ago.

Moreover, when the attention of both women had been focused on Geom Mugeuk from the very beginning, An Hupyeong felt displeased. He had lived a life where he was always the center wherever he went, yet here he kept feeling excluded.

An Hupyeong picked up the wine bottle and tried to fill Bi Sain’s cup.

“Now, young alliance leader. Please accept a cup from me.”

Bi Sain did not even pretend to hear him and only looked at the two women. The wine bottle in his hand suddenly became awkward, and his face flushed. Truly, these people!

Even so, he was still the young leader of Shinhwa Gang in name, and they treated him like this?

‘He’s filthy fond of women.’

If that were not the reason, then it would mean Bi Sain was looking down on him far too much.

In truth, Bi Sain was trying to grasp what kind of person Cha Iran was. In a certain sense, she was a woman more deeply connected to him than to Geom Mugeuk. If she killed his master, then in the end he would ascend as leader of the Sado Alliance.

In other words, she was a woman trying to place him in the position of alliance leader.

‘For what reason, exactly?’

Was there something she hoped to gain by putting him, who was not yet prepared, in the alliance leader’s seat? Or did she have some other intention?

An Hupyeong struggled to suppress his anger and spoke again.

“I cannot decide whom I should support in this final.”

He was trying somehow to display his influence.

An Hupyeong used the word support, not cheer for. He spoke as if he could influence the decision of the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven.

“Which lady do you support, young alliance leader? In truth, your support is the most important.”

This time, Bi Sain answered him.

“What importance does my opinion have? What matters is the will of the judges and the heroes.”

“Are you not in a position where you could overturn all those decisions?”

In his own way, it was meant as flattery toward Bi Sain, but Bi Sain’s response was cold.

“Are you now telling me to do something unfair?”

“No, that is not what I meant.”

“Then what did you mean?”

“I only meant that you are someone of that much greatness.”

At that still-cold gaze, An Hupyeong downed his wine. Somehow, things were not going the way he wanted.

“Why is the food taking so long?”

An Hupyeong rose from his seat and walked toward the kitchen. Even when he stood up, no one looked his way. His anger flared even further.

“What are you doing? Hurry up and bring out the food!”

The moment he entered the kitchen, he shouted. Just as he was about to vent his anger on the innocent cooks—

“Let us return.”

At the voice that came from behind him, he turned and saw Sachu. The man who had yelled at him earlier was standing in a corner of the kitchen.

“What did you say?”

“I said let us go back.”

An Hupyeong walked toward him with an angry face. At any other time, Sachu would have bowed his head deeply and apologized, but today he was staring straight at him.

“Do you know what kind of seat that is?”

“What kind of seat is it?”

“What?”

It was a seat with a clear purpose. A seat meant to curry favor with the young alliance leader! A seat meant to curry favor with Yi An. Sachu knew that too, and yet he was acting like this?

“Are you looking down on me too?”

“If I were looking down on you, I would have stayed as I always have, pretending not to know anything.”

Instinctively, he could feel it. Though he did not know why Sachu was doing this, he knew these words were being said for his sake.

Though Sachu was being rude, among the people here, he was the only one who was not looking down on him.

“That is not a place where you should be, young master.”

All four of them were people An Hupyeong could not handle. Only he himself did not know that fact.

“Hide in an underground tunnel and come out once that typhoon has passed. The reason you are safe right now, young leader, is because you are standing in the eye of the storm.”

“!”

The typhoons had told him, shh, but he had ignored their warning. Still, it was all right. This was the last piece of advice he would give him.

Of course, An Hupyeong did not accept it graciously.

“You arrogant bastard.”

He clearly knew the words were meant for his sake, but he felt offended. He had lived the sort of life where being told the truth made him feel offended.

“You’d better be prepared for later.”

The moment An Hupyeong turned around—

Tap-tap.

Sachu ambushed him from behind and pressed An Hupyeong’s acupoints. Since he had never imagined this would happen, An Hupyeong slipped into sleep.

After calming the startled cooks, Sachu opened the kitchen’s back door and called in the subordinates waiting outside.

“The young leader has drunk too much. Take him back to his residence and lay him on his bed.”

The subordinates carried him on their backs and returned to his quarters.

It was the last consideration he could show him before leaving.

If An Hupyeong remained here in this state of anger, he would ultimately cause a major incident despite the presence of the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance. The An Hupyeong Sachu had experienced until now was that kind of person.

In any case, An Hupyeong would not let him leave peacefully, so this was how he intended to depart.

Just as Sachu was also about to leave through the back door, someone spoke from behind him.

“Was it because of your younger siblings?”

When he turned, Illang was standing there.

His question meant: was he giving up on his dream and staying under An Hupyeong because of his younger siblings?

Sachu confessed honestly.

“No. I merely used my younger siblings as an excuse to make the easier choice.”

If it had truly been because of his siblings, he would have challenged himself again once their hunger had been resolved.

He had earned good money, and following An Hupyeong around had been easy. At least here in Anhui, no one picked a fight with the young leader of Shinhwa Gang.

“I realized something today. There are two kinds of impossibility. One is impossible because you vaguely assume it won’t work. The other is impossible because you tried until you felt you would die, and it still did not work. I have already experienced the easy kind, so now I intend to go challenge the difficult impossibility.”

Sachu intended to newly enter the Sado Alliance.

His monthly pay would be incomparably less than now. Starting over at his age would be several times harder. And he knew that entering now and becoming one of the Sado Thirteen Wolves was also impossible.

Still, he intended to try. He would regard this chance to meet the Sado Thirteen Wolves again as the final opportunity heaven had granted him.

“When we meet again, I will greet you properly then.”

Then he added with an awkward smile,

“Though I probably won’t see you again in my lifetime.”

After respectfully giving a cupped-fist salute, Sachu turned and walked away.

When he was young, he had stood in front of the Sado Thirteen Wolves and thrown out a question. He had thought that was passion and sincerity.

But now, there would be no questions.

Because he had reached an age where he knew that true passion did not ask questions. Because he knew it began before one even asked.

Illang silently watched Sachu’s retreating back grow distant, then went inside.

“Young Gang Leader An has returned ahead of us.”

At those words, no one pressed to ask why he had left first.

Only Geom Mugeuk found it regrettable.

“Ah! The person who was supposed to pay for the drinks left first.”

At his joke, Cha Iran brought up something unexpected.

“Why don’t we wager on the drinks?”

The wager she proposed was truly something none of them had expected.

“Instead of just drinking, let’s each ask one another a question as we drink. If you can’t answer, you drink three penalty cups! The person who drinks the most penalty cups today pays for the wine. Oh, and no expelling the alcohol qi outside your body to keep from getting drunk!”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not the sort of person to refuse such an interesting proposal. Especially when there were things they wanted to ask one another.

He thought she might drink the penalty cups, but she was not someone who would lie. He himself had no intention of lying either.

“Good. Let’s do it.”

Since Geom Mugeuk accepted, Ian and Bisain naturally nodded as well, their eyes gleaming with interest.

“Since I proposed it, I’ll ask the first question.”

At Cha Iran’s words, everyone readily nodded. After they all toasted together and emptied their cups.

“I’ll ask Guard Geom.”

Her gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk.

“You like this young lady here as a woman, don’t you?”

The first question was already powerful. Ian was more startled than Geom Mugeuk. She had never imagined such a question would be asked.

“If you don’t want to answer, you can just drink three penalty cups.”

“There’s no need for that. I’ll answer.”

Geom Mugeuk glanced briefly at Ian and replied.

“I do.”

Cha Iran looked surprised.

“I was sure you’d drink the three penalty cups.”

Ian felt as though she could fly from happiness at Geom Mugeuk’s answer. Even if he had only said it because of the wager, she was happy.

But she was displeased by Cha Iran’s question.

‘So her first question is about me and the Young Cult Leader?’

She wanted to question Cha Iran at once, but Geom Mugeuk moved first.

“I answered the question, so the next question is mine.”

“Go ahead.”

“I’ll ask Miss Cha.”

This time as well, after they all toasted together and emptied their cups, Geom Mugeuk asked Cha Iran.

“Who is the person you trust most?”

It was a question with many possible meanings. She could answer with a family member, or with her leader. She could also answer with someone who had come here with her for this matter.

Cha Iran looked at Geom Mugeuk with a strange gleam in her eyes.

“I asked about someone you like, but you ask about someone I trust. Were you afraid that if you asked who I liked, I’d answer that it was you?”

Smiling brightly like that, she answered the question.

“The person I trust most is……”

A startling statement flowed from her lips.

“Someone you know too.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk stared at her in surprise. He could not tell what she meant by saying that.

“I won’t tell you who it is, so you can die of curiosity. This is punishment for your first answer.”

She drank three penalty cups in succession, then set down her cup and asked,

“Now then, who’s asking the next question?”

Chapter 698: Did You Know I’m a Mean Drunk?
-------------------------------------------
“The person I trust most is someone you know?”

If the force behind her included people who were already dead, there was no telling who it might be.

But he did not think she would have answered that the person she trusted most was dead.

Then, if it was someone alive?

One person naturally came to mind.

The Sword King, Ak Gunhak.

Of the people who could have been the answer to her reply, he was the only one still alive.

He recalled the final moment when he had parted with Ak Gunhak.

The sight of his back as he walked away, hair tied tightly, an iron sword and shoes hanging from his waist.

The sight of him walking barefoot, then turning back toward him from far away.

—My name is Ak Gunhak.

Even his shout, and the way he had smiled brightly with those lonely eyes. Seeing how that image always came to mind, it seemed he must miss him.

“The person you trust most is Ak Gunhak?”

With that answer, Geom Mugeuk understood.

She was telling him.

I know what you know about me.

Until now, whether she had thought so or not, she had not shown it.

The secret coffer’s reaction had not been a coincidence. She was throwing down the gauntlet.

If you can stop me, try.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Cha Iran. Like a martial artist standing face-to-face with an enemy, she was pounding the table and shouting.

“Come on, then! I’ll take any question you throw at me!”

Then Ian stepped forward.

“I’ll ask the next question.”

Since they had agreed to drink before asking a question, all four emptied their cups.

“I’ll ask Miss Cha.”

Ian had been displeased by her first question, when she had asked about Geom Mugeuk’s feelings toward her. If it had been only the two of them, that would have been one thing, but to ask whether he liked her when all the parties involved were present?

It felt as if she were treating Ian’s relationship with Geom Mugeuk, and the feelings within it, like a joke.

But Ian did not respond emotionally. Precisely because she considered that relationship so precious, she found herself becoming overly sensitive like this; and even that would likely be the result the other party wanted.

Ian smiled and asked calmly.

“I’d like to ask about the person you said you trusted most. Does that person know of Miss Cha’s trust?”

Cha Iran’s expression stiffened slightly.

“Why are you asking about that person?”

Because of Geom Mugeuk. If it was someone Geom Mugeuk knew as well, then he must surely be important. She wanted to be of even the slightest help to Geom Mugeuk.

Even if she herself could not discern it, Geom Mugeuk would sense her subtle emotions.

“I’m curious. The Miss Cha I sensed seemed like someone who doesn’t trust people easily, so I wondered what kind of person he must be for you to say you trust him most. Does he know?”

He knows. No, he does not. It was a simple answer, but without hesitating, she drank three penalty cups again. She showed her resolve not to speak at all about anything related to that person.

Ian said with an apologetic expression.

“I won’t ask any more about him.”

“It doesn’t matter. There’s plenty of wine. Now, come at me again!”

Ian’s question had been useful. From Cha Iran’s reaction, Geom Mugeuk realized that her feelings toward Ak Gunhak ran quite deep.

“While digging underground like that, when did you manage to steal the heart of such a beautiful woman?”

Next was Bi Sain’s turn.

“I will ask Miss Cha.”

Cha Iran smiled and said,

“I’m the only one exploding with popularity. Miss Yi must feel left out.”

“I’m not the type to pass by someone who tells me to come at them.”

In truth, aside from asking why she was trying to kill his master, Bi Sain honestly had nothing he wanted to ask her.

Still, he thought that if he learned even one more thing about Cha Iran, it might help with this matter, so he chose her.

“Why are you trying to become the greatest beauty under heaven?”

It seemed like a merely formal question, but in truth it touched upon what he was curious about. She had planned to become the greatest beauty under heaven, approach his master, and launch a surprise attack. So if she were to answer honestly, she would have to say this.

To kill the Sado Alliance Leader.

As Cha Iran hesitated to answer, Ian smiled and joked.

“I jumped in because of the prize money.”

But in the end, Cha Iran did not answer.

“Ah, how nice it would be if my reason were that simple too.”

With that, she drank three more penalty cups.

She could have answered with something like, It’s a dream any woman might have at least once, and that would have been fine.

At the very least, from that penalty drink, they could tell that she was utterly sincere and serious about this wager.

After drinking in succession, the flush of wine rose on her face. It did not look as if she had a particularly high tolerance.

“Are you all right?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s concern, she spoke boldly.

“Don’t tell me you’re worried about me. I haven’t even started yet!”

The questioning order went around once, and it became Cha Iran’s turn again. He thought she would ask Geom Mugeuk a question, but her target was Bi Sain.

“I’ll ask the Young Alliance Leader.”

And the question was truly unexpected.

“What does the Sado Alliance Leader like most?”

For a moment, Bi Sain was startled. He had not expected her to ask such a question.

Ian, who was beside him, also forced down her sudden tension.

“May I ask why you want to know?”

At Bi Sain’s question, Cha Iran replied,

“Aren’t you curious? What sort of thing the supreme one of the unorthodox factions likes?”

She looked at Ian and provoked her.

“If I become the greatest beauty under heaven this time, I’ll meet him in person at the celebration banquet. It would help to know what he likes, wouldn’t it?”

Instead of answering, Bi Sain drank his penalty cup.

“How could I dare speak of the Alliance Leader at a drinking table? Please understand that I must drink the penalty cup.”

He was angry at her shamelessness in asking about his master, but Bi Sain strove not to reveal those feelings.

“I’m sorry. It seems I asked a rude question.”

“It is fine.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. She had thrown out that question to confirm whether they knew she was targeting the Sado Alliance Leader. Her question had been useful as well.

It became Geom Mugeuk’s turn to ask again.

This time, too, he chose Cha Iran.

“I apologize if this feels like a wheel battle, but there is something else I’m curious about, Miss Cha.”

“Questions from Guard Geom are always welcome.”

But it was by no means a welcome question.

“Who is the person you fear most?”

For an instant, he felt Cha Iran flinch.

“Earlier you asked who I trust most, and now you ask who I fear most.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had asked the question, gave his own answer.

“For me, it is my father.”

Aside from the two questions he had asked her earlier, there were likely many questions to which his father would be the answer.

The person he liked most, the person he respected most…

In the end, Cha Iran picked up the penalty cup again.

“Come at me! Come at me!”

Ian followed up on that question.

“Then I’ll ask something a little easier. What does Miss Cha cherish most?”

But Cha Iran was troubled by this question as well.

“Really, you people!”

As if she had never imagined such questions would come flying when she first suggested the wager, she shook her head.

“What I cherish right now is…”

Her gaze turned toward the wine cup.

“This wine!”

She answered, but as if that was not the answer she truly wished to give, she drank another penalty cup.

The sound of her setting down the cup grew louder. Her drunken eyes began to lose focus.

Geom Mugeuk suggested they slow down a little while drinking, then said to her,

“It seems you have lived a very difficult life, Miss Cha. Those were questions others could have answered without much trouble.”

Geom Mugeuk’s and Cha Iran’s gazes tangled in midair.

Soon, a trace of sorrow passed through her eyes, and she sighed.

“It was not a smooth life.”

She did not hide her emotions. He could not tell whether it was sincere, or whether she was acting drunk.

“Now, let’s go again!”

“You should rest a little as we continue.”

“Come at me!”

From then on, they did not ask questions only to Cha Iran.

Ian asked Bi Sain what he regretted most, and Geom Mugeuk asked Ian what book she had read recently. At times the order of questioning changed, and sometimes someone asked twice in a row. They gradually became drunk.

They were still wary of one another, but they fully enjoyed the pleasure of the drinking table. If not on a day like this, when else would they ever ask such things? They threw questions at one another and answered.

As the questions went round and round, the last question of Cha Iran’s that could be understood was this. In a voice thick with drunkenness, she asked Geom Mugeuk,

“What is your greatest secret?”

Her utterly drunken eyes seemed to be asking this.

Who are you, that our organization is being destroyed? What are you, that he spared your life? What are you, that I must go this far? What are you…

Geom Mugeuk raised his cup and spoke in a drunken voice.

“Under the influence of wine, I nearly told you my secret.”

When Geom Mugeuk drank the penalty cup, Cha Iran rejoiced.

“You’re starting to crumble too! Now, let’s keep going!”

The words she said most often at that drinking table that day were these.

All of you, come at me!

* * *

When Cha Iran woke, she was lying on a bed in a guest room prepared separately in Hwamyeong Hall.

When she turned her head, Geom Mugeuk was sitting on a chair beside the bed.

“What did you do to me? You look innocent, but you’re sinister.”

Geom Mugeuk asked with an incredulous expression,

“Are you pretending not to remember, or do you really not remember?”

“Of course!”

Cha Iran smiled bashfully and said,

“I’m pretending not to remember.”

“You vomited, threw a drunken fit, shouted at the top of your lungs, and cursed. Do you remember that?”

“Here and there.”

Let’s drink without expelling the alcohol with our internal energy!

What on earth had she been relying on when she said such a thing?

He did not know how much of it had been intentional, but at the very least, her drunken antics had not been an act.

He had truly experienced many things in the fight against the force behind her, no, in all his life before the regression as well, but this was the first time he had ever seen an enemy’s drunken behavior so nakedly. No, there would likely never be another time in the future either, so it was the first such experience of his life.

“Did you know you had a drunken habit?”

Cha Iran shook her head.

“Yesterday was the first time I ever drank that much.”

To commit the worst overdrinking of her life in front of her enemy, and that, before the young cult leader of the Demon Cult and the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance. When she became dead drunk, how could she know what she might say? Truly, if nothing else, her nerve had to be acknowledged.

And now it was time to speak of the truths they had not shared yesterday.

“Why did you reveal your identity?”

As expected, she did not deny it and ask what he meant.

“You knew about me, didn’t you?”

How had she realized that? He had known about her because he had known her in his life before the regression.

“How did you know?”

“A woman’s intuition.”

This time, she asked. He should have regarded her merely as one beautiful woman among those participating in the competition.

“How did you know about me?”

“A man’s intuition.”

Cha Iran smiled alluringly.

“I feel relieved, rather. Until now, it was truly stifling not being able to speak freely. Why else would I have suggested a wager like that?”

“But the person who drank the most penalty cups was you.”

“I didn’t expect demonic practitioners to ask those kinds of questions. Someone even asked what my childhood dream was, didn’t they? Who on earth asked that?”

“You remember everything.”

Cha Iran slowly raised herself from the bed. Her clothes were completely wrinkled, and from unidentified things to mud, all sorts of filth clung to them. It seemed she had gone outside and rolled all the way across the ground.

“I’d believe it if you said those were martial robes just returned from a battlefield.”

“You underestimate the cleanliness of martial artists far too much.”

She laughed, picked up the water pitcher on the table beside the bed, and gulped it down.

“What if I had put poison in it? How can you drink like that?”

“If you were going to kill me anyway, you would have killed me while I was asleep.”

Geom Mugeuk knew. She was not someone who would fall asleep this defenselessly merely because she was drunk. Then why had she shown such an opening?

“Why didn’t you kill me?”

“You are not someone who would die easily. If an opportunity to kill you easily appeared, wouldn’t that be a trap?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Cha Iran set down the pitcher and smiled.

“You really are as clever as I heard.”

She stretched, then walked to the window and looked outside. It was still early dawn.

“At least, even if you don’t kill me……”

The figure she presented as she looked back at Geom Mugeuk was unbearably sensual. No matter how filthy her clothes were, they could not mar her beauty.

“I thought you’d give in because of the drink. You even had the excuse of changing my clothes for me.”

At her frank words, Geom Mugeuk laughed and said,

“Had you not tried to vomit on me, perhaps I might have.”

With a faint smile on her lips, Cha Iran formally greeted Geom Mugeuk.

“Young Cult Leader, a pleasure.”

Even with her identity exposed, she was still full of confidence.

“I am not the type to spare an enemy and invite future trouble. What were you thinking?”

It sounded as though he meant he had been waiting in order to kill her, but she did not become afraid.

“Then I die.”

Her eyes did not fear death. It was not so much because of courage as because she was certain Geom Mugeuk would not kill her.

“That was what the man who fought you and returned said.”

It was clear that by “that man,” she meant Ak Gunhak.

“That you let him live and sent him away.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“He was the one who spared me and left.”

A smile formed on her lips, as if to say, I knew it.

It was a different sort of smile from the ones she had shown until now. Compared to the alluring smile that always appeared reflexively, this one was slightly stiff and awkward.

But Geom Mugeuk could tell. That smile was her real smile.

And one more thing.

She not only trusted Ak Gunhak the most, she also liked him.

“Has he left?”

Cha Iran was inwardly surprised.

Geom Mugeuk had known he would leave. Ak Gunhak was not the kind of person who would tell others that he was leaving.

‘Just what did he see in you, to even tell you something like that?’

The wind blowing in from outside the window stirred her hair.

“He hasn’t been able to leave yet.”

Geom Mugeuk asked no more and only nodded.

“Why don’t you ask? Why he hasn’t been able to leave.”

If it were an organization one could leave so easily, he would not have met him in its underground chamber. Nor would he have said he was going to leave. How could leaving such a disgusting organization possibly be easy?

Geom Mugeuk bade her farewell.

“Do cure your hangover properly.”

As Geom Mugeuk was about to leave, she asked,

“Why are you leaving without killing me?”

Geom Mugeuk looked back at her. Because I still haven’t found out why you’re trying to kill the leader of the Sado Alliance. And because there is now one more thing I must find out.

“Is this mercy, letting me fight the finals before you kill me?”

At her needless show of weakness, Geom Mugeuk firmly shook his head.

“After seeing that awful sight, I won the bet.”

As he left the room, Geom Mugeuk added,

“I can hardly kill the person who’s going to pay for the drinks, can I?”

Chapter 699: Why Would Father Have Kept the Demon Venerable?
------------------------------------------------------------
Cha Iran stood by the window, watching Geom Mugeuk depart.

As if he had sensed her gaze, Geom Mugeuk, who had been walking some distance away, turned around and waved to her.

Cha Iran did not wave back, but Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and then resumed walking.

Her gaze lifted to the dawn sky. In the brightening daybreak, the stars that had shone all night were fading away.

“Don’t smile like that at someone you’re about to kill.”

At her words, someone revealed himself on the roof of the building across the way.

Dressed to the nines in expensive clothes, even with snow-white makeup on his face, he was Hwa Domyeong.

“Don’t come over. Spectators are supposed to watch from across the street.”

It was a remark aimed at Hwa Domyeong’s claim that he had come this time as a mere spectator.

But Hwa Domyeong lightly launched himself through the air and entered through the window she had been looking out of.

“How could I bear it, when you were so drunk you were crawling on the ground?”

“If the Young Cult Leader catches sight of you, that neck of yours will go clean off.”

“He’s a merciful man who spared you even after seeing that drunken spectacle, isn’t he?”

At Hwa Domyeong’s joke, Cha Iran ended up laughing. It truly must have been an incredible night.

“It’s far too much of a waste just to watch.”

Cha Iran stared at Hwa Domyeong as though glaring at him.

“You meant to get involved in this from the start, didn’t you?”

Hwa Domyeong neither confirmed nor denied it.

“Don’t. If you die too, everything will become even more of a mess. You know that, don’t you? What will happen if it comes to that.”

“We just have to win.”

“They already knew our target was the Evil Dao Alliance Leader. They even refrained from stopping him from coming.”

“They’re keeping us alive because they want to know why we’re after the Evil Dao Alliance Leader?”

Cha Iran nodded.

“If they let their guard down, then—”

Cha Iran cut Hwa Domyeong off.

“They won’t let their guard down.”

After a brief pause, she emphasized it even more.

“The Young Cult Leader won’t let his guard down.”

Hwa Domyeong could tell that she feared Geom Mugeuk.

“You’re only scared now? Even after hearing about all the people who have died until now?”

“I hadn’t seen it with my own eyes.”

Geom Mugeuk as a guard and Geom Mugeuk as the Young Cult Leader felt completely different. After revealing his identity, the gaze with which he looked at her seemed to peer into the very depths of her heart.

“You’ve never failed before, have you?”

“Because this face and body have always worked.”

At her self-deprecating words, Hwa Domyeong spoke in a way that was both clear and unclear.

“Aren’t you even more frightening when they don’t work?”

His makeup was so heavy it was almost ridiculous, but Hwa Domyeong’s eyes were utterly serious.

Then Cha Iran took something from within her robes. It was the very box Hwa Domyeong had given her.

She opened the lid.

Inside the box was a red bead.

“He must have known I had this.”

That was why, when she opened this box, the bead should have been gone. He should have left behind a lump of stone drained of its energy and feigned ignorance. And yet Geom Mugeuk had.

“He resisted.”

Her gaze deepened as she stared at the bead.

“He didn’t even think to open it once.”

She had thought that while he might be able to resist killing his opponent, he would find it difficult to resist the desire to obtain this bead. At the very least, she herself would not have been able to resist.

“So don’t loiter around nearby. When I die at the Young Cult Leader’s hands, I might not want to die alone and point out where you’re hiding.”

A laugh spread from behind the fan.

“Pay the tab here for me. I should at least collect a viewing fee.”

“Where are you going?”

“To wash up and buy new clothes.”

Cha Iran moved toward the door and left the room.

“And cure my hangover.”

Watching her back as she left, Hwa Domyeong gently fanned himself. The painting of a beauty on the fan moved as though it were dancing.

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk returned to his quarters, Ian was awake and waiting for him.

There was always a sense of relief when Geom Mugeuk came back safely, but today that relief felt especially great.

“What about Lady Cha?”

“I saw her wake up a little while ago before I left.”

It meant he had not killed her. When Geom Mugeuk had said he would remain with her, Ian had worried. Not so much about what kind of temptation the dead-drunk Cha Iran might attempt again.

“The more she carried on drunkenly, the more suspicious it felt. I wondered if all of it might be some kind of trap.”

“It probably was a trap.”

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk in surprise.

“It was a trap?”

“She set something up, but it didn’t go the way she wanted. If not, she wouldn’t have shown us that side of herself. And even if she had truly tried to kill me, it wouldn’t have been easy.”

Truly, Cha Iran of the previous night had been more terrifying than any hidden weapon.

Geom Mugeuk lay down on his bed, using his arm as a pillow, and silently stared up at the ceiling. His expression was somehow grave.

“Is something troubling you?”

“Does it look that way?”

When Ian nodded, Geom Mugeuk sprang up from the bed.

“As expected! You’re the only one who knows me best.”

No, anyone could see you had something on your mind!

The fact that he had made it so obvious meant he wanted to discuss the matter with her.

And Geom Mugeuk’s concern was truly unexpected.

“The person she said she trusts the most is Instructor Ak.”

Ian was startled. She knew Ak Gunhak as well. She had taken his lessons at the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall together with Seo Jin. She also knew that his relationship with Geom Mugeuk had deepened afterward.

“It seemed Cha Iran likes him. In truth, that isn’t the problem…”

An unrequited love was not the problem. The problem was—

“You’re worried that Instructor Ak might like that woman.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

What if Ak Gunhak liked her? Then he would not kill Cha Iran. He intended to resolve this matter somehow without killing her.

“May I tell you what I think?”

“Of course.”

Ian carefully posed a question.

“Is Instructor Ak the kind of person who would simply stand by while the woman he likes and his friend fight a life-and-death battle?”

If she were the woman he liked, he would come to find them by any means. Those words might not apply to an ordinary person, but Ian had experienced it too. She knew just how extraordinary a master he was.

“What if he doesn’t know about this situation?”

This was exactly what Geom Mugeuk was worried about.

“You have to find that out through Lady Cha.”

“How?”

“Go to Lady Cha and tell her that you’re friends with that person, Young Cult Leader. That you’re very close. If it’s only her one-sided love and if the two of them like each other, her reaction will definitely be different.”

Ian spoke with confidence.

“If it’s you, Young Cult Leader, I believe you’ll surely be able to tell the difference.”

“You’re clever, Ian!”

“You thought of it too, didn’t you? Weren’t you just asking because you wanted to confirm it once through my mouth?”

“I didn’t. I couldn’t. Once it became my own problem, my head stopped working.”

“Is this the moment my hidden tail finally proves useful?”

“Let’s go. Don’t tuck that tail away just yet.”

“Where?”

As Geom Mugeuk left the room, he said something incomprehensible.

“To become the Most Beautiful Woman Under Heaven.”

* * *

An Hupyeong woke from sleep.

“Huh?”

When he opened his eyes, he was on the bed in his own quarters. He had definitely been drinking with the Young Alliance Leader at Hwamyeong Hall.

“Was it a dream?”

He could not tell whether this was the dream, or whether the drinking had been the dream.

“Sachu! Sachu!”

An Hupyeong shouted for Sachu. Sachu had always opened the door, come in, and asked what was wrong. But Sachu did not enter the room. Instead, another subordinate came in.

“Warrior Sa is not here.”

“Where did he go?”

“After we parted at Hwamyeong Hall yesterday, we haven’t seen him either.”

“Hwamyeong Hall?”

It had not been a dream. Now that he thought about it, his last memory was meeting Sachu in the kitchen yesterday.

“He said you were drunk and told us to escort you back to your quarters.”

“That bastard!”

An Hupyeong took his men and rushed to Hwamyeong Hall. Everyone who had been there yesterday was gone, and Hwamyeong Hall was busy preparing for business.

After hounding the cook and the waiter there, he learned that Sachu had sealed his acupoint and left, and that the remaining four had drunk until late.

“Damn it! I should have been there!”

Rather than the fact that Sachu had left, An Hupyeong regretted missing that drinking party. He had already forgotten how excluded he had felt there.

“Find that bastard! Right now!”

He released his subordinates to look for Sachu, but Sachu had already gone far away.

He went straight to Bi Sain’s quarters first. He had to meet Bi Sain and make an excuse for leaving without a word yesterday.

But Bi Sain was not at his quarters. And he could hardly dare go to the Evil Dao Alliance Leader and ask where the Young Alliance Leader was.

An Hupyeong headed straight for the inn where Ian was staying. If Bi Sain was not there, he thought he should at least recover some points with Ian.

But she, too, and that detestable guard, were both absent.

“Sachu! Where did they all go?”

Out of habit, he called for Sachu as he turned around and shouted.

“Why aren’t you saying anything?”

In that instant, An Hupyeong flinched. Among the subordinates looking at him, Sachu was not there.

Come to think of it, these subordinates’ faces were merely familiar; he did not even know their names. Sachu had always taken care of managing them. Among these fierce-looking men, not one gave him a feeling of trustworthiness.

Then, suddenly, he recalled what Sachu had said to him before leaving.

—Hide in a burrow, and come out after that typhoon passes. The reason you are safe right now, Young Gang Leader, is because you are in the eye of the typhoon.

Was there truly anyone among these men here who would say such a thing to him?

“You must find that bastard! Find him and bring him back alive no matter what!”

* * *

The people An Hupyeong was so desperately searching for were all gathered in one place.

Geom Mugeuk had brought Ian and Bi Sain to the guest hall where the Master of Cheonhwa Pavilion was staying.

“Welcome, Young Cult Leader.”

Geugak Soma could tell. With the final stage two days away, Ian and the Master of Cheonhwa Pavilion were people who should not meet each other.

And yet the fact that they had come meant the moment of decisive battle had arrived. On top of that, Bi Sain was with them as well.

After exchanging greetings with the Master of Cheonhwa Pavilion, Geom Mugeuk first relayed what had happened yesterday.

“Cha Iran revealed her identity herself.”
